Thus internal positive values require the retained signed term, not a silent boundary reset.", "evidence": "UNVERIFIED direct joint derivation, no new verifier call. Let the prescribed schedule be(block_0,b_0,...,block_m), with nonnegative ordinary sums s_i. Choose w_i>=0, T_i=s_i+1+w_i, and A_i=b_i-1-w_i>0. Choose0<beta_i<=A_i/T_(i+1). Define a_i=sqrt(T_(i+1)beta_i/A_i), q_i=sqrt(A_i beta_i/T_(i+1)), alpha_0=1/T_0, alpha_(i+1)=q_i alpha_i, zeta_0=e_0 and zeta_(i+1)=-a_i zeta_i+sqrt(1-a_i^2)e_(i+1) in a Euclidean space with orthonormal e_i. Put omega_i=alpha_i zeta_i; J=max(0,<omega_i,z>:0<=i<=m), f=env_1 J. Set X_0=e_0, X_(i+1)=X_i-(s_i+b_i)omega_i, Z_i=X_i-T_i omega_i. Then Z_(i+1)=Z_i-A_i alpha_i(zeta_i+a_i zeta_(i+1)), because T_(i+1)alpha_(i+1)=A_i alpha_i a_i. This residual increment is orthogonal to every zeta_j with j>=i+1. Thus <omega_j,Z_i>=0 for j>=i. For j<i, let R_l=A_l-T_(l+1)beta_l, P_(j,i)=product_(j<=l<i)beta_l, Q_(j,i)=product_(j<=l<i)A_l/T_(l+1), and H_(j,i)=sum_(l=j)^(i-1)(-1)^(l-j)R_l product_(v=j)^(l-1)beta_v. Direct inner products give <omega_j,Z_i>=-alpha_j^2 H_(j,i) and <omega_j,omega_i>=alpha_j^2(-1)^(i-j)P_(j,i), while alpha_i^2=alpha_j^2 P_(j,i)Q_(j,i).\nAt a query of ordinary block i after cumulative sum u, the proposed proximal point is Z_i+d omega_i, with d=T_i-u-1 in[w_i,s_i+w_i]. The current plane has value d alpha_i^2. The old plane j minus it, divided by alpha_j^2, is -H_(j,i)+d C_(j,i), where C_(j,i)=P_(j,i)[(-1)^(i-j)-Q_(j,i)]. Since this is affine in d and both endpoints are actual queries, its exact necessary-and-sufficient bound is H_(j,i)>=w_i C_(j,i)+s_i[C_(j,i)]_+. Negative H is permitted when the displayed right-hand side is negative; Z_i is not required to be an actual minimizer. Every future plane has value d alpha_i^2(-1)^(j-i)P_(i,j), so it is dominated exactly when its even-lag P_(i,j)<=1 if s_i+w_i>0; odd lag is automatic. The zero plane is dominated by d>=0. Hence these tests certify ALL actual proximal samples of the SAME J. Proximal optimality proves every supplied GD step, and the elementary monotonicity argument gives convex differentiable1-smooth f.\nOrigin is a minimizer and is nearest to e_0: the retained omega_0=e_0/T_0 makes <e_0,z><=0 for every z with J(z)=0, so ||e_0-z||^2>=1. At the endpoint d=w_m, the exact normalized objective is (2w_m+1)/T_0^2 times product_(i<m)A_i beta_i/T_(i+1).\nFor appending a fixed next block/margin w, let A=A_i, T=s_(i+1)+1+w. For old j<=i set p=P_(j,i), q=Q_(j,i), c_old=H_(j,i)/p, sigma=(-1)^(i+1-j), K=sigma-Aq/T, and choose d_endpoint=w+s_(i+1) when K>=0, otherwise w. Empty products are1 and H_(i,i)=0. The identity H_(j,i+1)/p=c_old-sigma(A-T beta) makes the exact old-plane constraint beta[sigma T-d_endpoint K]>=sigma A-c_old. Together with0<beta<=A/T and beta p<=1 on each new even-lag comparison at a past positive-height query, these are finitely many half-line constraints defining the exact next interval. Its coefficient signs must be retained, and its nonemptiness is not presumed. Finally the simplifying assumption A_i>=T_(i+1) is ALGEBRAICALLY equivalent to w_i+w_(i+1)<=b_i-s_(i+1)-2. Hence b_i=s_(i+1)+2 forces both margins zero within that easier subregime. This is not a no-go for the full retained-history criterion, whose extra terms were just displayed.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "workers/max/research/max_nonperiodic_markov_profile_policy.md", "section": "8", "extends": ["59a1dd1dd3c4c735", "b0bf06c26eb801e6"], "status": "unverified; no new verifier allocation", "fact_ids": [], "scope": "exact positive-margin Markov criterion and budget, not a uniform B_n parameter policy"}, "glossary": {"w_i": "Nonnegative positive-level margin; actual checkpoint proximal height is w_i alpha_i^2.", "T_i": "s_i+1+w_i, the block residual offset coefficient in this extension.", "A_i": "b_i-1-w_i, assumed positive in this Markov joining geometry.", "P_(j,i)": "Product of beta_l from j to i-1.", "Q_(j,i)": "Product of A_l/T_(l+1) from j to i-1.", "H_(j,i)": "The complete signed alternating residual sum explicitly defined in the evidence.", "C_(j,i)": "P_(j,i)[(-1)^(i-j)-Q_(j,i)], the old/current plane slope difference in block i."}}
{"id": "4f9c9ce521523bb9", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T13:55:05.480663+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C19 asymmetric nonperiodic extension with unbounded ray-scale ratio: suppose queried secants on each ray lie in [M_sigma/(13/5),M_sigma], and the first fast activation level I satisfies b_(I+1)M_slow<=1 (automatic when M_fast/M_slow>=(13/5)^2). An exact two-state maximum-product recursion forces every nonzero complete H_(I+2) trajectory onto the slow ray, with fast-start/slow-start envelope ratio <=1. This persists at all later levels and yields normalized gap <=(240000/a)(13/5)^(-m)=o(U_(2^m-1)^ZJ), uniformly over arbitrary asymmetric nonperiodic profiles and horizon-dependent knots.", "evidence": "# Arbitrarily separated asymmetric ray scales: an exact two-state envelope\n\nStatus: complete candidate proof, not verifier-accepted. No new verification\ncall is allocated. This proof includes its scalar-product and benchmark\nestimates, so it does not use the unverified C18 finding as a premise.\n\n## 1. The class, the scale-separation condition, and the conclusion\n\nPut c=13/5, a=c^(-17), b_j=a c^j, H_0=(),\nH_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), N_j=2^j-1, and\ns_j=sum H_j=a(c^j-2^j)/(c-2).\n\nLet f be any fixed convex differentiable scalar function with 1-Lipschitz\nderivative g, f(0)=0=min f. Label its two rays slow and fast, and choose\n\n    0<M_slow<=M_fast<=1.\n\nThere is no restriction on which physical ray receives either label. Assume\non ray sigma that\n\n    M_sigma/c <= |g(x)|/|x| <= M_sigma,     x!=0.            (1)\n\nThese secant bands may contain arbitrary nonperiodic variation. No symmetry,\nhomogeneity, local-curvature pattern, knot bound, or prescribed crossing\nhistory is imposed.\n\nLet I be the first level for which b_I M_fast>1, and require\n\n    b_(I+1) M_slow <= 1.                                  (2)\n\nIn particular, (2) holds whenever M_fast/M_slow>=c^2. The ratio of the two\nray scales can be arbitrarily large and can depend on m. All other function\nparameters and its knot count can also depend on m.\n\nFor every m>=0, the GD trajectory under H_m from x_0!=0 satisfies\n\n    2 f(x_(N_m))/x_0^2 <= (60000/a)c^(-m).                 (3)\n\nFor m>=1 a trajectory-local version holds: it is enough that (1) hold at\nthe queried iterates on their respective rays. The rest of the derivative\nis unrestricted except for convexity and smoothness. The resulting bound is\n\n    2 f(x_(N_m))/x_0^2 <= (240000/a)c^(-m).                (4)\n\nA trajectory that reaches a minimizer has terminal objective zero and is\nhandled separately. The two conclusions hold for either initial sign.\nThe supremum of these class errors divided by U_(N_m)^ZJ tends to zero.\nNeither (3) nor (4) is an all-scalar or a multidimensional worst-case bound.\n\n## 2. The exact state-envelope recursion\n\nWrite rows and columns in order (slow,fast). For a step of level j, define\n\n    d_sigma(j)=(1-b_j M_sigma/c)_+,\n    u_sigma(j)=(b_j M_sigma-1)_+,\n\n    B_j = [[d_slow(j),u_fast(j)],\n           [u_slow(j),d_fast(j)]].                       (5)\n\nHere r_+=max(r,0). A same-sign scalar step from ray sigma multiplies its\nradius by at most d_sigma(j); a crossing multiplies it by at most\nu_sigma(j). A zero entry means that branch cannot carry a nonzero point.\nBoth assertions follow from\nx_next=(1-b_j lambda)x with lambda in [M_sigma/c,M_sigma].\n\nFor nonnegative 2 by 2 matrices use maximum-product multiplication\n\n    (A odot B)_(i,j)=max_(v in {slow,fast}) A_(i,v) B_(v,j).\n\nLet E_0 be the identity matrix (ones on its diagonal and zeros elsewhere),\nand define\n\n    E_(j+1)=E_j odot B_j odot E_j.                       (6)\n\nThe entry E_j(i,v) bounds the ending radius divided by the starting radius\nof every nonzero trajectory of H_j from ray v to ray i. This follows by\ninduction, conditioning on the two intermediate rays between its three\npieces. Equivalently, the entries are exactly the maximum products of the\ndisplayed one-step bounds over all permitted sign paths. Thus (6) is an\nexact recursion for the relaxed envelope; it is not an assertion that\nindependently selected bounds all interpolate one legal derivative.\n\nWe will prove that E_(I+2) has zero fast row and that its slow-row entry\nfrom a fast start is no larger than its entry from a slow start. This\nproperty is then preserved by (6) for every later level, even when later\ncheckpoints can cross from the slow ray.\n\n## 3. Two levels force every nonzero path onto the slow ray\n\nSet\n\n    t=b_I M_fast,  s=b_I M_slow,  eta=13/8.\n\nSince b_17=1 and M_fast<=1, I>=18. Its defining minimality and (2) give\n\n    1<t<=c,    0<s<=1/c.                                (7)\n\nAlso s_I/b_I>=eta. To check this, let d=2/c=10/13. Then d^3<1/2 and\nd^2<4/5 imply d^17<1/40, and\ns_I/b_I=(1-d^I)/(c-2)>=13/8.\n\nEvery step in H_I preserves both signs. Define\n\n    p_sigma=product_(h in H_I)(1-h M_sigma/c)>0.\n\nThus E_I is diagonal with entries p_slow,p_fast. At the next level,\n\n    E_(I+1) = [[A,D],[0,F]],\n    A=p_slow^2(1-s/c),\n    D=p_fast p_slow(t-1),\n    F=p_fast^2(1-t/c).                                 (8)\n\nAt level I+1, a nonzero fast-ray point must cross: even the smallest\npermitted secant has b_(I+1)M_fast/c=t>1. A slow-ray point cannot cross,\nbecause b_(I+1)M_slow=cs<=1. Therefore\n\n    B_(I+1)=[[1-s,ct-1],[0,0]],\n\nand direct multiplication in (6) yields\n\n    E_(I+2)=[[A_0,D_0],[0,0]],\n    A_0=A^2(1-s),\n    D_0=A max(D(1-s),F(ct-1)).                          (9)\n\nIn particular all permitted nonzero paths end on the slow ray.\n\nIt remains to prove R=D_0/A_0<=1. For 0<=v<=u<1,\nlog(1-u)-log(1-v)<=-(u-v), by integration of -1/(1-z)<=-1.\nApplying this at each step of H_I gives\n\n    p_fast/p_slow\n       <= exp[-(M_fast-M_slow)s_I/c]\n       <= exp[-eta(t-s)/c].                             (10)\n\nUsing (8)-(10),\n\n    R <= max(R_1(t,s),R_2(t,s)),\n\n    R_1(t,s)=[(t-1)/(1-s/c)] exp[-eta(t-s)/c],\n\n    R_2(t,s)=[(1-t/c)(ct-1)/((1-s/c)(1-s))]\n                                      exp[-2eta(t-s)/c]. (11)\n\nBoth right sides are increasing in s on (0,1/c]. For R_1, the derivative\nof its logarithm with respect to t is\n\n    1/(t-1)-eta/c = 1/(t-1)-5/8 >=0,    1<t<=c.\n\nConsequently\n\n    R_1(t,s) <= [c^2/(c+1)] exp(-18/13)<1.              (12)\n\nIndeed c^2/(c+1)=169/90 and exp(18/13)>1+18/13=31/13>169/90.\n\nFor R_2 and 1<t<c, the derivative of its logarithm in t is\n\n    -1/(c-t)+c/(ct-1)-2eta/c.\n\nThis derivative is strictly decreasing in t. At t=1 its continuous limit\nis 1-5/4=-1/4<0. Thus R_2 decreases in t, and at t=c it is zero. Taking\nthe upper limit t down to 1 and s up to 1/c gives\n\n    R_2(t,s) <= [c^2/(c+1)] exp(-10/13)<1.              (13)\n\nFor the last strict inequality,\nexp(10/13)>1+10/13+(10/13)^2/2=349/169>169/90.\nEquations (11)-(13) prove R<1 uniformly in both ray scales and their ratio.\n\n## 4. Every later complete block is controlled by the slow-ray envelope\n\nSuppose E_j=[[A_j,D_j],[0,0]] with D_j/A_j=R<=1. Equation (6) gives\n\n    A_(j+1)=A_j^2 max(d_slow(j),R u_slow(j)),\n    D_(j+1)=R A_(j+1).                                  (14)\n\nNo fast-ray coefficient appears here: a preceding complete block already\nends on the slow ray, and the following complete block returns every\nnonzero possible sign to that ray. The ratio R is preserved exactly.\n\nFor M in (0,1] define\n\n    q(z)=max(1-z/c,z-1),\n    P_sec(m,M)=product_(j=0)^(m-1)q(a M c^j)^(2^(m-j-1)),\n    P_sec(0,M)=1.\n\nThus P_sec(j+1,M)=P_sec(j,M)^2 q(b_j M). Since R<=1,\n\n    max(d_slow(j),R u_slow(j))<=q(b_j M_slow).            (15)\n\nEvery step in H_(I+2) has h M_slow<=1. Therefore (8)-(9) show\n\n    A_0=P_sec(I+2,M_slow).\n\nInduction in (14)-(15) proves, for every m>=I+2,\n\n    A_m<=P_sec(m,M_slow),  D_m<=P_sec(m,M_slow),\n    |x_(N_m)|<=P_sec(m,M_slow)|x_0|,                    (16)\n\nand every nonzero terminal point is on the slow ray.\n\n## 5. A self-contained uniform estimate for P_sec\n\nFor completeness the analytic product estimate needed in (16) is proved\nhere. For 0<M<=1 let k be the largest integer with y=a M c^k<=13/9.\nThen k>=17, 5/9<y<=13/9, and\n\n    q(a M c^j)=1-a M c^j/c,   j<=k,\n    q(a M c^j)=a M c^j-1,     j>=k+1.\n\nPut d=2/c. The previously proved d^17<1/40 gives\n2(1-d^k)/(c(c-2))>=5/4. For m>=k+1, log(1-u)<=-u on levels j<k gives\n\n    log P_sec(m,M)/2^(m-k-1)\n       <=-5y/4+log(1-y/c)\n            +sum_(ell=1)^(m-k-1)2^(-ell)log(c^ell y-1).   (17)\n\nFor m=k+1 this is at most -25/36. For m>=k+2 retain ell=1 exactly.\nFor ell>=2 the logarithms are positive; extend that tail to infinity and\nuse log(c^ell y-1)<=ell log c+log y. Summing the two geometric series\nbounds (17) by\n\n    E(y)=-5y/4+(1/2)log[c y(c-y)^2(cy-1)].               (18)\n\nOn [5/9,13/9],\n\n    E''(y)=-1/(c-y)^2-1/(2y^2)-c^2/(2(cy-1)^2)<-1/2,\n\nbecause y<3/2, cy-1<3, and 2/9+c^2/18>1/2. At y_0=4/5,\nE'(y_0)=5/216 and\n\n    c y_0(c-y_0)^2(cy_0-1)=113724/15625<(27/10)^2.\n\nSince e>sum_(j=0)^5 1/j!=163/60,\nE(y_0)<log(162/163)<=-1/163. Taylor's upper bound and a completed square\ngive\n\n    E(y)<=E(y_0)+(5/216)(y-y_0)-(y-y_0)^2/4\n          <=E(y_0)+25/46656<-1/200.\n\nThe final rational comparison follows from 25/46656<37/32600.\nThe separate m=k+1 case obeys the same bound. Hence\n\n    P_sec(m,M)<=exp[-2^(m-k-1)/200],   m>=k+1.            (19)\n\nFor m<=k all factors of P_sec are at most one, so\nM P_sec(m,M)^2<=(13/(9a))c^(-m). For m=k+J with J>=1, (19) gives\n\n    M P_sec(m,M)^2\n       <=(13/(9a))c^(-m)c^J exp(-2^J/200).\n\nSince c<4, with z=2^J/200 the last factor is at most\n40000 z^2 exp(-z)<=40000: the maximum of z^2 exp(-z) is 4/e^2<1.\nThus, uniformly in m>=0 and 0<M<=1,\n\n    M P_sec(m,M)^2 <=(520000/(9a))c^(-m)\n                    <(60000/a)c^(-m).                  (20)\n\n## 6. Full areas, early horizons, and query-local hypotheses\n\nUnder the global ray bands (1), integration from the minimizer gives\nf(x)<=M_sigma x^2/2 on ray sigma. For m>=I+2 the terminal ray is slow,\nso (16) and (20) prove (3).\n\nIf m<=I, both signs are preserved and all one-step envelope factors are\nat most one. Moreover M_fast<=c/(a c^I)<=c/(a c^m). Thus the objective\nratio is at most (c/a)c^(-m), smaller than (3).\n\nIf m=I+1, (8) has entries at most max(1,t-1)<=c-1. Consequently the\nobjective ratio is at most\n\n    M_fast(c-1)^2\n       <=[c^2(c-1)^2/a]c^(-m)<(60000/a)c^(-m).\n\nThis completes the global case for every horizon.\n\nFor the query-local variant take m>=1. The last step is always a<1/2,\nso it preserves any nonzero sign. If the last queried ray is sigma,\nmonotonicity and the upper queried secant bound give\n\n    |g(x_(N_m))| <= |g(x_(N_m-1))|\n       <= M_sigma |x_(N_m-1)|\n       <= [M_sigma/(1-a)] |x_(N_m)|.\n\nThe area under a monotone derivative between the minimizer and the\nterminal point is at most |x_(N_m)| |g(x_(N_m))|. Thus every global\nobjective estimate just used is enlarged by at most 2/(1-a)<4.\nThis proves (4). The derivative may behave arbitrarily between or outside\nthe queried points; no omitted area or terminal-gradient assumption has\nbeen introduced.\n\n## 7. Exact benchmark comparison\n\nThe exact benchmark definitions are those in accepted fact b98bb807d514e80d.\nIn shifted variables C_n=S_circle(n)+1 and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1, they read\nC_0=B_0=1 and, for n>=1,\n\n    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) Gamma(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),\n    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n    Gamma(A,B)=2A+B/2+sqrt(B^2+8AB)/2,\n    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nWe include the needed stronger estimate rather than invoking an unverified\nprior bound. By homogeneity and comparison with the positive root of\nz^2-(A+B)z-AB, the inequality\nOmega(A,B)<=(A^(3/4)+B^(3/4))^(4/3) reduces to\n\n    t^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1,   0<=t<=1.\n\nSet u=t^(1/3), v=(1-t)^(1/3). The difference from 1 equals\nu^3v^3[1/(1+u+u^2)+1/(1+v+v^2)-uv]>=0: each reciprocal is at least\n1/3 and uv<=2^(-2/3)<2/3. Induction gives C_n<=(n+1)^(4/3).\nNext Gamma(A,B)<=B+4A by squaring sqrt(B^2+8AB)<=B+4A.\nSince u^(4/3)+v^(4/3)<=(u+v)^(4/3) for nonnegative u,v, induction in\nthe second recurrence gives B_n<=1+4n^(4/3).\n\nDividing (4) by U_(N_m)^ZJ therefore yields at most\n\n    (240000/a)c^(-m)[1+4(2^m-1)^(4/3)] ->0,\n\nbecause c^3=2197/125>16. This comparison is uniform over all permitted\nasymmetric profiles, scale separations, horizons and initial points.\n\n## 8. The remaining interface\n\nThe actual radial derivatives can be specified independently and may be\nnonperiodic with horizon-dependent knot complexity. For instance, choose\narbitrary measurable w_sigma(R) in [M_sigma/c,M_sigma] and define\ng_sigma(R)=integral_0^R w_sigma(t)dt and\nf(sigma R)=integral_0^R (R-t)w_sigma(t)dt. These are globally legal\nexamples with complete areas. They are only a subfamily of the theorem,\nwhich also permits flat pieces of the derivative inside its secant bands.\n\nThe proof's new mechanism is the explicit two-state recursion (6) and the\nuniform ratio bound R<=1 after forced sign collapse. It allows unbounded\nasymmetry between the two rays. Its hypotheses remain substantive: the\nsecants on each ray fit into a factor-c band, and the scale phases obey\n(2). When (2) fails, the low matrices need not collapse to a single row;\nwhen a ray's own secants span more than c, several consecutive levels can\nhave both crossing and noncrossing branches. Neither situation has been\ncontrolled by this proof. A general two-state upper-envelope computation\nwithout a proved quantitative recurrence would not resolve that gap.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "workers/max3/research/c19_separated_ray_sector_obstruction.md", "status": "complete self-contained candidate proof; unverified; no fact_submit allocated", "fact_ids": ["b98bb807d514e80d"], "related_findings": ["3423f78cc0ca8c92", "4d3911499920adbe"], "scope": "per-ray factor-c queried secant bands with separated activation levels; not full scalar class", "is_fragile": false, "justification_type": "calculation", "confidence": 0.95, "suggested_followup": "none", "next_gap": "overlapping activation levels and more than factor-c queried secant variation within a ray"}, "glossary": {"c": "Schedule growth factor 13/5, local to the candidate.", "a": "Schedule amplitude c^(-17), local to the candidate.", "M_slow": "Upper endpoint of the lower ray's queried secant band [M_slow/c,M_slow].", "M_fast": "Upper endpoint of the higher ray's queried secant band [M_fast/c,M_fast], at most1.", "I": "First integer level with b_I M_fast>1.", "t": "Fast activation phase b_I M_fast in (1,c].", "s": "Slow activation phase b_I M_slow in (0,1/c].", "B_j": "Two by two nonnegative one-step envelope matrix defined in equation5.", "E_j": "Maximum-product envelope matrix for the full recursive schedule H_j.", "odot": "Matrix product with maxima in place of sums and ordinary multiplication for terms.", "d_sigma": "One-step same-ray radius bound (1-b_j M_sigma/c)_+.", "u_sigma": "One-step crossing radius bound (b_j M_sigma-1)_+.", "R": "Ratio of the fast-start to slow-start entries in the single nonzero row of E_(I+2), preserved at later levels.", "P_sec": "Scalar product of q(a M c^j) with level multiplicities2^(m-j-1), q(z)=max(1-z/c,z-1)."}}
{"id": "8d147e2cec6dadca", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T13:55:55.091575+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Complete exact one-prefix terminal profile candidate: rho_two(a)=inf_b W2(a,b) has three explicit ranges separated by sqrt2 and sqrt3, with unique optimal final step throughout. Full original-space nonnegative certificates prove all ranges. It also exhibits nonquasiconvexity after final-step elimination and a strict suboptimal local minimum at(sqrt3,3/2).", "evidence": "# Exact optimized final-stage value for every one-step prefix\n\nStatus: self-contained UNVERIFIED candidate. No new verifier allocation.\nThe only accepted predecessors are 1252b8823bb5a290 (interpolation and\nnormalization) and b98bb807d514e80d (Huber and single-cut lower bounds).\nAll new upper certificates and all sign checks are proved below. Neither\nthe previous terminal SDP candidate nor the earlier two-step calibration\nis used as an unverified premise.\n\n## 1. Exact piecewise value and its unique final step\n\nFor a>=0 define\n\n    rho_two(a)=inf_(b>=0) W_2(a,b),\n\nin the project's original normalization over every finite dimension and\nevery convex L-smooth objective with a minimizer, with L>0 arbitrary.\nEvery infimum below is attained at the UNIQUE indicated b.\n\n* If 0<=a<=sqrt(2), put\n\n      t=(-1+sqrt(8a+9))/4,   b=1+t,\n      q=1/(2t+1)^2.\n\n  Then rho_two(a)=q. Here t(2t+1)=a+1 and b=psi(a,0).\n\n* If sqrt(2)<=a<=sqrt(3), put s=a-1 and let t>0 be the unique positive\n  solution of\n\n      s^2 t^2(2a+2t+3)=1.                             (1)\n\n  Then b=1+t and rho_two(a)=q=s^2 t^2.\n\n* If a>=sqrt(3), then\n\n      b=3/2,   rho_two(a)=(a-1)^2/4.                  (2)\n\nThe formulas agree at both boundaries. Zero steps may be inserted anywhere\nwithout changing the values or the unique optimal nonzero final step.\n\n## 2. Lower bounds and uniqueness that will match the certificates\n\nAccepted fact b98bb807d514e80d and the actual scalar quadratic x^2/2 give,\nfor EVERY a,b>=0,\n\n    W_2(a,b)>=max{\n      1/(1+2a+2b),\n      (b-1)_+^2/(a+1)^2,\n      (a-1)_+^2/(1+2b),\n      (a-1)^2(b-1)^2}.                                (3)\n\nIn the first range, b=1+t is the unique intersection of the first and\nsecond bounds at q: t^2/(a+1)^2=1/(1+2a+2+2t)=q.\nThe first bound is strictly larger than q for any smaller b, and the second\nis strictly larger for any larger b. Thus (3) proves the lower value and\nuniqueness in that range once an upper certificate at b=1+t is given.\n\nIn the middle range, equation(1) is precisely the intersection of the first\nand fourth bounds. Its left side is strictly increasing from zero to infinity\nas t increases. Again smaller b makes the first bound strictly larger, while\nlarger b>1 makes the fourth strictly larger. This proves the same claims\nfor that range conditional on its displayed upper certificate.\n\nFor every a>1 the third and fourth bounds jointly have unique minimal maximum\nat b=3/2, with value (a-1)^2/4. Indeed\n\n    (b-1)^2(1+2b)-1=b^2(2b-3),\n\nthe reciprocal branch is strictly decreasing up to 3/2, and the quadratic\nbranch is strictly increasing afterward. This proves the lower value and\nuniqueness for the third range.\n\nThe remaining work is therefore three explicit FULL upper certificates.\n\n## 3. Range one: the primitive-cap boundary appears as a PSD coefficient\n\nUse L=1, optimum zero, x_1=x_0-a g_0, x_2=x_1-b g_1, and the signed Q of\nthe accepted terminal fact. Set r=1/(2t+1), so q=r^2 and t=r(a+1).\nWrite mu_i=lambda_(i,star). All omitted multipliers, including every\nstar-outgoing multiplier and every incoming-terminal multiplier, are zero.\nChoose\n\n    mu_0=2a r^2,   mu_1=2(a+2)r^2,   mu_2=2r,\n    lambda_01=2r^2,\n    lambda_10=2(a+1)r^2,\n    lambda_21=2(1-r).\n\nAll are nonnegative. The finite flows are\nmu_0+lambda_01=lambda_10,\nmu_1+lambda_10=lambda_01+lambda_21,\nmu_2+lambda_21=2. The second follows from\n1-r=2rt=2r^2(a+1).\n\nThe complete identity is\n\n    q||x_0||^2-2f_2+sum lambda_ij Q_ij\n      =||r x_0-a r g_0-(a+2)r g_1-g_2||^2\n                      +r^2(2-a^2)||g_0-g_1||^2.       (4)\n\nFor a coefficient check, the gradient block has diagonals\n2r^2, 2(2a+3)r^2, 1; its off-diagonal entries are\n2r^2(a^2+a-1), ar, (a+2)r. These are exactly the coefficients of\nthe RHS. The x_0 row is fixed by minus mu_i/2, and its diagonal is r^2.\nValue coefficients cancel by the displayed flows. This verifies the\nidentity on the full formal space, including when a=0.\n\nFor 0<=a<=sqrt(2), the remaining square coefficient is nonnegative, so\n(4) proves the required upper value. At a=sqrt(2), that coefficient vanishes\nand lambda_01=2q>0, consistently retaining the hostile calibration's adjacent\nforward edge. No incoming constraint at the queried point1 was discarded.\n\n## 4. Range two: a full rank-one certificate\n\nAssume sqrt(2)<=a<=sqrt(3). Use s=a-1, t from(1), b=1+t,\nr=s t=sqrt(q), and\n\n    d=(1-r)/(a r).\n\nThe defining equations give the useful identities\n\n    r^2(2a+2b+1)=1,\n    a r d=1-r,\n    a(1+2r)d^2=2b.                                  (5)\n\nFor the third identity, note\n2ab r^2=1-3r^2+2r^3=(1-r)^2(1+2r), using\nab=(a-1)(b-1)+a+b-1=r+a+b-1.\n\nSet all unlisted multipliers to zero and choose\n\n    mu_0=2r s d,   mu_1=2r d,   mu_2=2r,\n    lambda_01=d^2+2d-2b = d(2r-s d),\n    lambda_10=d(d-2r),\n    lambda_20=2(d-a t),\n    lambda_21=2(b-d).                                (6)\n\nThe alternative lambda_01 expression follows by using both last identities\nin(5). The terminal outgoing sum is two because\nr+d-a t+b-d=r+b-a t=1. The flow at node1 is\nmu_1+lambda_10=lambda_01+lambda_21. Finally\nmu_0+mu_1+mu_2=2r(a d+1)=2, so node0 also has the required zero flow.\n\nBefore checking signs, verify the complete algebraic identity\n\n    q||x_0||^2-2f_2+sum lambda_ij Q_ij\n                      =||r x_0-s d g_0-d g_1-g_2||^2. (7)\n\nIts x_0 coefficients are immediate from mu. The terminal cross entries\nare s d and d: in the terminal-square parameterization of the predecessor,\nalpha_star=r, alpha_0=d-a t, alpha_1=b-d, which sum to one, and\n\n    V_alpha=r x_0-s d g_0-d g_1.\n\nThe g_1 diagonal is lambda_01+lambda_21=d^2. The g_0 diagonal is\nmu_0+(1-a)lambda_01, which equals s^2d^2 by(5). The coefficient of\n<g_0,g_1> is\n\n    a mu_1+(a-1)lambda_10-lambda_01\n      =-2(1-r)d+(s-1)d^2+2b\n      =2s d^2,\n\nagain by(5). The g_2 diagonal is one by terminal flow. These account for\nevery quadratic coefficient, proving(7) even before any multiplier signs\nare assumed.\n\n### All multiplier signs, analytically\n\nFirst t>=1/2. The left side of(1), at t=1/2, equals\n\n    (a-1)^2(a+2)/2=(a^3-3a+2)/2<=1\n\nfor a<=sqrt(3); use its strict increase in t. Also 0<r<1/2 follows from\nr^2=1/(2a+2b+1) and a>1,b>=3/2.\n\nNext d>=1. Put t_0=1/(a^2-1). At that argument the left side of(1)\nminus one is\n\n    a^2(3-a^2)/[(a^2-1)(a+1)^2]>=0.\n\nThus t<=t_0 and r<=1/(a+1), which is equivalent to d>=1.\nIt follows that lambda_10=d(d-2r)>0. From(5),\nd^2<2b/a<b^2, because a b>=(3/2)sqrt(2)>2.\nHence lambda_21>0. All mu_i are positive.\n\nFor lambda_01, all quantities are continuous in a on this interval; this\nfollows directly from strict monotonicity of the left side of(1) in t,\nor from taking limits of its unique bounded positive roots.\nIf lambda_01=0, its two expressions in(6) give d=2t and\n\n    0=d^2+2d-2b=2(2t-1)(t+1).\n\nTherefore t=1/2,d=1. Equation a r d=1-r and r=(a-1)/2 then force\na^2=3. There is no zero in [sqrt(2),sqrt(3)). At a=sqrt(2),\nt(2t+1)=a+1, r=1/(2t+1), d=a t, and lambda_01=2r^2>0.\nConsequently lambda_01>=0 throughout, vanishing only at sqrt(3).\n\nFor lambda_20 put alpha_0=d-a t. It is also continuous. To locate its\npossible zeros using fully explicit algebra, set c=1/(a+1) and take the\nlegal capped quadratic with derivative min(c,x), starting at1. Its trajectory\nfor (a,b) has gradients (c,c,-ct), terminal normalized value c^2t^2,\nand every Q occurring in(6) is zero EXCEPT\n\n    Q_20=-a b/(a+1)^2.\n\nThe square vector in(7) evaluated there equals -a alpha_0/(a+1): this\nfollows from(5), or from\na alpha_0=r(a+2b)-b. Thus the algebraic identity(7) gives\n\n    a^2 alpha_0^2+2ab alpha_0\n                         =a^2(a^2-2)t^2.              (8)\n\nIn particular alpha_0=0 is possible only at a=sqrt(2), because t>0.\nAt a=sqrt(3), the values t=1/2,d=1 give alpha_0=1-sqrt(3)/2>0.\nBy continuity and absence of another zero, alpha_0>0 for sqrt(2)<a<=sqrt(3)\nand it is zero at sqrt(2). This proves the remaining multiplier sign.\nThe capped quadratic is used only to check the displayed polynomial identity,\nwithout assuming a bound from a certificate before its signs are established.\n\nAll multipliers in(6) are now nonnegative, so the full square(7) proves\nthe middle-range upper value. Combined with(3), this proves that range.\n\n## 5. Range three below2: the active star-outgoing edge changes\n\nLet sqrt(3)<=a<=2, s=a-1, b=3/2, q=s^2/4. In addition to mu_i, now use\nnu_0=lambda_(star,0). Set all unlisted multipliers zero and take\n\n    mu_0=s^2,    mu_1=mu_2=s,\n    nu_0=a^2-3=s^2+2s-2,\n    lambda_10=lambda_20=2-a=1-s,\n    lambda_21=1.\n\nEvery coefficient is nonnegative in this interval. At node0,\nmu_0=nu_0+lambda_10+lambda_20; at node1,\nmu_1+lambda_10=lambda_21; and at node2,\nmu_2+lambda_20+lambda_21=2. The FULL identity is\n\n    q||x_0||^2-2f_2+sum lambda_ij Q_ij\n                 =||(s/2)x_0-s g_0-g_1-g_2||^2.       (9)\n\nTo check its coefficients, the g_0 diagonal is\n(mu_0+nu_0+lambda_10+lambda_20)/2=s^2; the g_1 diagonal is\n(mu_1+lambda_10+lambda_21)/2=1; and the g_2 diagonal is one.\nThe <g_0,g_1> and <g_0,g_2> coefficients both equal\na s+s(1-s)=2s. The <g_1,g_2> coefficient is\n(3/2)s+(3/2)(1-s)+1/2=2. The x_0 cross coefficients are\n-mu_0,-mu_1,-mu_2, exactly as on the RHS. The flow cancels all values.\n\nThis proves the third-range upper bound through a=2. Notice that nu_0\nis strictly positive for sqrt(3)<a<=2. These are optimal certificates for\na fixed earlier step; no claim of global schedule optimality is made.\n\n## 6. Range three above2, and all zero boundaries\n\nFor a>=2, interpolation with the minimizer gives <x_0,g_0>>=||g_0||^2,\nand smoothness gives ||g_0||<=||x_0||. Thus\n\n    ||x_1||^2<=||x_0||^2+a(a-2)||g_0||^2\n                                      <=(a-1)^2||x_0||^2.\n\nAt the last step b=3/2, direct expansion of the one-step identity gives\n\n    (1/4)||x_1||^2-2f_2+Q_(1,star)+Q_(2,star)+Q_(2,1)\n                       =||(1/2)x_1-g_1-g_2||^2.\n\nConsequently W_2(a,3/2)<=(a-1)^2/4, completing the third range.\nThe lower arguments in Section2 include arbitrary b=0 and arbitrary large b.\nAt a=0 the original-coordinate certificate(4) is already PSD. Any other\nzero insertions leave the actual iterates unchanged, as also proved in the\naccepted zero-compression fact. Multiplying normalized functions by L restores\nevery original L>0 without changing either step or normalized error.\n\n## 7. Optimizing the last step does NOT restore convexity in the earlier step\n\nPut\n\n    a_left=3/2,   a_right=sqrt(3),\n    q_local=(2-sqrt(3))/2=1/(4+2sqrt(3)).\n\nThe middle-range formula gives t=sqrt(3)-1 at a_left and therefore final\nb=sqrt(3). At a_right the final b=3/2. In both cases\n\n    rho_two(a_left)=rho_two(a_right)=q_local.           (10)\n\nFor EVERY a strictly between these two endpoints one has\n\n    rho_two(a)>q_local.                               (11)\n\nTo prove this directly, suppose some b had W_2(a,b)<=q_local. The Huber\nbound in(3) forces a+b>=T_0=3/2+sqrt(3), so b>1. The quadratic bound forces\n(a-1)(b-1)<=r_0=(sqrt(3)-1)/2. But\n\n    (a-1)(b-1)>=(a-1)(T_0-a-1)>r_0,\n\nbecause the last quadratic is strictly concave in a and takes exactly r_0\nat both a_left and a_right. This is a contradiction. The already established\nattainment of rho_two makes the inequality in(11) strict for its infimum.\n\nThus even AFTER eliminating the final step exactly, the remaining value is\nnot quasiconvex (and hence not convex) in the earlier step. In particular,\nthe midpoint of the two endpoints in(10) violates the convex-value inequality.\nThis is a sharper closure obstruction than nonconvexity of the unoptimized\nwhole-schedule value.\n\n## 8. A strict suboptimal local minimum and a coordinatewise trap\n\nThe schedule\n\n    (a,b)=(sqrt(3),3/2)\n\nis the UNIQUE minimizer of W_2 on the entire region a>3/2,b>=0, with value\nq_local. Indeed (11) excludes 3/2<a<sqrt(3). For a>sqrt(3), the third/fourth\nbounds in(3) have minimal maximum (a-1)^2/4>q_local. At a=sqrt(3), their\nunique minimizer is b=3/2. Hence this point is a strict local minimum in the\nfull nonnegative two-step domain.\n\nIt is also globally optimal in EACH coordinate separately. The fixed-prefix\nassertion was proved above. With b=3/2 fixed, the Huber bound is strictly\nlarger than q_local when a<sqrt(3), and the quadratic bound is strictly\nlarger when a>sqrt(3). Thus a=sqrt(3) is the unique best first step for that b.\n\nNevertheless it is not a global schedule minimum. The range-one certificate\nat a=sqrt(2), b=(3+sqrt(8sqrt(2)+9))/4 has q<2/15<q_local. To check these\nstrict comparisons without decimals, sqrt(2)>7/5 implies b>37/20, so\na+b>13/4 and q=1/(1+2a+2b)<2/15. Also sqrt(3)<26/15, which follows by\nsquaring, gives q_local>2/15.\n\nThis supplies a concrete full-class obstruction to concluding global optimality\nfrom exact successive one-coordinate minimizations, even when each subproblem\nis solved globally and the full interpolation/PSD certificates are retained.\nIt does not contradict the uniqueness of the separate globally optimal\ntwo-step calibration, and it makes no asymptotic claim.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "artifact": "workers/max2/research/exact_one_prefix_profile.md", "status": "complete_candidate_unverified", "no_verification_allocation": true, "proof_scope": "All a>=0, all dimensions, all L>0, zero insertions; not the all-horizon profile.", "justification_type": "calculation", "is_fragile": false, "fragility_reason": "", "suggested_followup": "none", "scope": "subgoal"}, "glossary": {"rho_two": "inf_(b>=0) W_2(a,b), where the initial step a is held fixed.", "q_local": "(2-sqrt(3))/2, exact candidate value at the suboptimal local minimum (sqrt(3),3/2).", "a_left": "3/2, left endpoint of the explicit nonquasiconvexity interval.", "a_right": "sqrt(3), right endpoint of the explicit nonquasiconvexity interval.", "nu_0": "Interpolation multiplier lambda_(star,0), allowed and explicitly positive in range sqrt(3)<a<=2."}}
{"id": "30f487d74ba2e756", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T13:58:48.978247+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Candidate uniform closure of the ENTIRE retuned two-bridge family with block capacities (4,4,27/7): primitive validity forces T_tot<81/5<9^rho, rho=log_2(1+sqrt2). This excludes the needed primitive amplification threshold for every positive pair of bridge excesses, not only seed29a1f4a5269032b1. No quadratic-balance assumption or cut-saturation assumption is used. The complete proof includes the new two-dimensional interpolation inequality rather than treating its unverified finding as a premise.", "evidence": "# A two-dimensional obstruction at a saturated primitive cut\n\nStatus: complete candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. No solver or verifier\ncall. The only existing fact used is 1252b8823bb5a290, for its explicit finite\nsmooth-convex interpolation construction. Every other identity is proved here.\n\n## 1. Definitions and an energy identity\n\nFor a nonnegative physical schedule h of length n let T_tot=1+sum_i h_i.\nFor normalized smoothness 1 and an optimum jet (0,0,0), put\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n    V_h=2 sum_(i<n) h_i(f_i-f_n)\n          +sum_(i<n) h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n          -T_tot(T_tot-1)||g_n||^2.\n\nHere primitive validity means V_h>=0 on every legal convex 1-smooth GD\ntrajectory. Suppose the sampled data additionally satisfy\n\n    f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2 for every i,\n    x_n=g_n.\n\nThe first condition is exactly Q_(i,star)=0; the second gives\nf_n=||g_n||^2/2. Expanding\n\n    ||x_0||^2-||x_n||^2\n       =2 sum h_i<g_i,x_i>-sum h_i^2||g_i||^2\n\ntherefore gives the exact identity\n\n    V_h=||x_0||^2-T_tot^2||g_n||^2.                 (1)\n\nFor any such data, whether or not yet jointly interpolable, write y_i=x_i-g_i.\nDirect substitution also gives\n\n    Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>.                             (2)\n\n## 2. General two-bridge construction\n\nLet a,b,c be arbitrary finite nonnegative blocks, with capacities\n\n    A=1+sum a_i, B=1+sum b_i, C=1+sum c_i.\n\nTake r_L,r_R>0 and h=(a,1+r_L,b,1+r_R,c). Define\n\n    D_tail=B+C+r_R, T_tot=A+D_tail+r_L,\n    eta_cut=r_L/D_tail, beta_inner=r_R/C,\n    p_aux=-B/r_L.\n\nThe first scalar array is a legal Huber trajectory: start at A and use\nderivative clip(x,-eta_cut,1). During a and at its endpoint the gradient is\n1. Its endpoint is 1. The first bridge lands at -r_L=-eta_cut D_tail.\nEvery remaining query has gradient -eta_cut, and the final point is\n-eta_cut. All the data satisfy the hypotheses of (1), so\n\n    V_base=A^2-T_tot^2 eta_cut^2.                   (3)\n\nThe second scalar array is defined as follows. During a use constant\ngradient p_aux, start at A p_aux, and end at p_aux. The first bridge lands\nat -r_L p_aux=B. On the remaining schedule (b,1+r_R,c) use the ACTUAL\nHuber trajectory with derivative clip(x,-beta_inner,1), starting at B.\nThe b block ends at 1, the second bridge lands at -r_R=-C beta_inner,\nand c ends at -beta_inner. Assign every value by\nf_i=g_i x_i-g_i^2/2. Thus (1) yields\n\n    V_aux=A^2 B^2/r_L^2-T_tot^2 beta_inner^2.        (4)\n\nThe second array is generally NOT interpolable across the first bridge.\nIt is only auxiliary data; its possible violations are accounted for below.\n\nFor epsilon_mix>0, combine both arrays into two-dimensional data\n\n    X_i=(x_i^base,sqrt(epsilon_mix) x_i^aux),\n    G_i=(g_i^base,sqrt(epsilon_mix) g_i^aux),\n    F_i=f_i^base+epsilon_mix f_i^aux.\n\nThe GD equations hold coordinate by coordinate, all Q_(i,star) remain zero,\nand X_n=G_n. Consequently\n\n    V_mix=V_base+epsilon_mix V_aux.                 (5)\n\nHere is a complete interpolation audit. Partition the samples into L (a\nand its endpoint), M (b and its endpoint after the first bridge), and R (c\nand its final endpoint after the second bridge). Overlaps are assigned to\nthe group whose gradient is used at the relevant bridge. The groups are\ndisjoint in original time: L includes the first bridge query, M includes\nthe second bridge query, and R includes the terminal sample.\n\n* Within L all gradients in each scalar array agree, so Q=0.\n* Within M union R the auxiliary data are one legal Huber trajectory;\n  the base gradients agree, so all Q<=0 there.\n* From L to M, the auxiliary target has y_j>=0 and the source gradient\n  p_aux<1, so Q_aux<=0. From M to L, the auxiliary target y_i is a\n  nonnegative multiple of p_aux<0 and 1-p_aux>0, so Q_aux<=0.\n* From L to R, if R_j is the remaining sum of physical c steps after its\n  target sample j, the two residuals are\n\n      Q_base=-(1+eta_cut)eta_cut R_j,\n      Q_aux=beta_inner(-p_aux-beta_inner)R_j.\n\n* From R to L, if H_i is the remaining sum of physical a steps after its\n  target sample i, then\n\n      Q_base=-(1+eta_cut)H_i,\n      Q_aux=(-beta_inner-p_aux)p_aux H_i.\n\nZero remaining sums give zero in both arrays. Thus duplicate positions\ncaused by zero steps do not require a separate limiting argument.\n\nThe optimum-outgoing inequalities hold too: Q_(star,i)=-<G_i,Y_i>,\nand the inner product is nonnegative separately in each scalar array.\nFor the auxiliary L data this is H_i p_aux^2>=0, and in the other groups\nit follows directly from their plateau coordinates. Q_(i,star)=0 already.\n\nWhen r_L r_R<BC, one has -p_aux>beta_inner. The ONLY potentially positive\nauxiliary residuals are L-to-R, and all interpolation inequalities hold for\n\n    0<epsilon_mix<=epsilon_max,\n    epsilon_max=eta_cut(1+eta_cut)\n                    /[beta_inner(-p_aux-beta_inner)].             (6)\n\nWhen r_L r_R>BC the ONLY potentially positive auxiliary residuals are\nR-to-L, and all inequalities hold for\n\n    0<epsilon_mix<=epsilon_max,\n    epsilon_max=(1+eta_cut)\n                    /[(-p_aux)(beta_inner+p_aux)].                 (7)\n\nWhen r_L r_R=BC both cross families are nonpositive for any epsilon_mix.\n\nAll these are complete original-index interpolation checks, including both\ncross directions and both optimum directions.\n\n## 3. One fixed globally legal function\n\nInclude the zero optimum sample and define, on R^2,\n\n    H_mix(v)=max_i { c_i+<X_i,v-G_i>+||v-G_i||^2/2 },\n    c_i=<G_i,X_i>-F_i=||G_i||^2/2,\n    f_mix(x)=sup_v { <x,v>-H_mix(v) }.\n\nThe maximum includes i=star. This is the explicit finite interpolation\nconstruction of accepted fact 1252b8823bb5a290, proof section 2, applied to\nthe entire finite data set. The checked Q inequalities imply that f_mix\ninterpolates every (X_i,G_i,F_i), is convex differentiable and 1-smooth,\nand has f_mix(0)=0=min f_mix. Thus the mixed data are one actual GD\ntrajectory of one fixed function; they are not an adaptive oracle or a\nreset of the function at a bridge.\n\n## 4. A universal quantitative two-cut necessary condition\n\nAny primitive h as above necessarily satisfies\n\n    r_L r_R [A^2+r_L(T_tot+A)] <= A^2 BC.           (8)\n\nFirst assume r_L r_R<BC and choose epsilon_mix=epsilon_max in (6).\nUsing D_tail=B+C+r_R, substitution into (3)-(5) gives\n\n    V_mix = (BC+r_R D_tail)(D_tail+r_L)\n              /[D_tail^2 r_R(BC-r_L r_R)]\n             * {A^2 BC-r_L r_R[A^2+r_L(T_tot+A)]}.\n\nThe prefactor is positive, proving necessity of (8) in this case. For an\nalgebra check, before the final factorization the numerator is\n\n    (BC+r_R D_tail)\n       {A^2[BC(D_tail+r_L)-r_L r_R D_tail]\n                                     -T_tot^2 r_L^2 r_R}.\n\nUse A^2 D_tail+T_tot^2 r_L\n=(D_tail+r_L)[A^2+r_L(T_tot+A)] to obtain the displayed factorization.\n\nIf r_L r_R>BC, set z_aux=-p_aux=B/r_L and beta_inner>z_aux, and use (7).\nEquation (5) becomes\n\n    V_mix=(beta_inner+eta_cut z_aux)/(beta_inner-z_aux)\n             * {A^2-T_tot^2[(1+eta_cut)beta_inner/z_aux-eta_cut]}.\n\nThe bracket is strictly smaller than A^2-T_tot^2<0. Hence no such schedule\nis primitive. If r_L r_R=BC, V_aux=z_aux^2(A^2-T_tot^2)<0, so sufficiently\nlarge epsilon_mix in the fully legal mixed data again gives V_mix<0.\nThis excludes the two remaining cases and completes (8).\n\nNo quadratic-balance assumption about a,b,c appears in this argument.\n\n## 5. Saturated first cut forces its inner tail cut\n\nSuppose in addition r_L T_tot=A D_tail. Then eta_cut=A/T_tot, V_base=0,\nand A^2+r_L(T_tot+A)=A T_tot. Condition (8) reduces to\n\n    T_tot r_L r_R<=ABC,\n    equivalently r_R D_tail<=BC.                   (9)\n\nTherefore a positive defect r_R D_tail>BC in the inner tail cannot be\nrepaired by any signed storage or full collection of interpolation edges\nwhile keeping this outer cut exactly saturated. This statement concerns\nprimitive validity of this actual schedule, not all objective certificates.\n\nThe geometry also explains the conclusion without (8): at outer saturation\n(4) becomes (T_tot/D_tail)^2[B^2-D_tail^2 beta_inner^2], which is strictly\nnegative exactly when the tail cut fails. Any sufficiently small positive\nmixture allowed above then has V_mix<0.\n\n## 6. Explicit refutation candidate for the outstanding eight-step seed\n\nSet u=10/7, C=27/7, A=B=4, and\n\n    D_tail=(165+sqrt(65641))/42,\n    r_R=D_tail-55/7,\n    T_tot=(4+D_tail+sqrt((4+D_tail)^2+16D_tail))/2,\n    r_L=T_tot-4-D_tail.\n\nThe schedule is\n\n    (3/2,3/2,1+r_L,3/2,3/2,1+r_R,10/7,10/7).\n\nDirectly from these root equations,\n\n    r_R D_tail=196/9, r_L T_tot=4D_tail,\n    BC=108/7<196/9.\n\nThus (9) fails. Here is a concrete mixing scale without an unspecified\nsmallness parameter. Define eta_cut=r_L/D_tail, beta_inner=r_R/C,\np_aux=-4/r_L, and\n\n    epsilon_mix=eta_cut(1+eta_cut)\n                      /[2 beta_inner(-p_aux-beta_inner)].          (10)\n\nIt is positive: the first defining polynomial has its positive root in\n(10,101/10), and substituting 5/2 into\nr_L^2+(4+D_tail)r_L-4D_tail shows r_L<5/2. Hence\nr_L r_R<(5/2)(157/70)=157/28<108/7=BC. Formula (10) is half of (6).\n\nFor reference the two position arrays are\n\n    base: (4,5/2,1,-r_L,-r_L+(3/2)eta_cut,\n           -r_L+3eta_cut,-C eta_cut,-(1+u)eta_cut,-eta_cut),\n    aux:  (4p_aux,(5/2)p_aux,p_aux,4,5/2,1,\n           -r_R,-(1+u)beta_inner,-beta_inner).\n\nThe gradient arrays are\n\n    base: (1,1,1,-eta_cut,-eta_cut,-eta_cut,\n                           -eta_cut,-eta_cut,-eta_cut),\n    aux:  (p_aux,p_aux,p_aux,1,1,1,\n                           -beta_inner,-beta_inner,-beta_inner).\n\nValues are g_i x_i-g_i^2/2 in each coordinate. Sections 2-3 show that\ntheir mixed two-dimensional jets come from the explicit fixed f_mix.\nFinally beta_inner D_tail=1372/243=4(343/243), so\n\n    V_mix = epsilon_mix(T_tot/D_tail)^2\n                  [16-(1372/243)^2]\n          = -epsilon_mix(T_tot/D_tail)^2 * 937600/59049 < 0.        (11)\n\nThis gives an exact concrete primitive counterexample for the proposed\neight-step seed. It closes that seed's proposed primitive amplification.\nIt makes no claim that a different direct objective certificate is\nimpossible, and does not settle the unrestricted asymptotic target.\n\n## Literature and verification status\n\nTargeted Matlas and web retrieval supplied no theorem used in this proof.\nThe primitive/Huber regime had already been audited in previous rounds;\nthis continuation uses the accepted finite interpolation construction\nrather than an external black box. All new results above remain candidates\npending a selected verifier allocation. No fact was submitted or written.\n\n## 7. Retuning both bridges does not repair the fixed shortened-tail family\n\nThe obstruction is wider than the exact radical seed. For ANY nonnegative\nblocks of capacities A=B=4 and C=27/7, every primitive schedule\n(a,1+r_L,b,1+r_R,c) with r_L,r_R>0 has\n\n    T_tot<81/5<9^rho, rho=log_2(1+sqrt(2)).         (12)\n\nThis rules out the required primitive amplification seed throughout the\ntwo-parameter bridge family with these three physical blocks, without a\nquadratic-product assumption and without requiring either cut to saturate.\n\nHere is the entire scalar deduction. The first-cut Huber array from section2\ngives T_tot r_L<=A(B+C+r_R). The same construction at the second bridge\n(start on its positive plateau at A+B+r_L, negative slope r_R/C) gives\nT_tot r_R<=C(A+B+r_L). For a fixed T=T_tot set\n\n    R=T-A-B-C,\n    U(T)=A(T-A)/(T+A), S(T)=C(T-C)/(T+C),\n    L(T)=R-S(T).\n\nBoth cuts force L(T)<=r_L<=U(T), with r_R=R-r_L. At\nT0=81/5 these endpoints and their complementary r_R values are\n\n    r_L=L(T0)=128/65,  r_R=S(T0)=216/91,\n    r_L=U(T0)=244/101, r_R=R-U(T0)=6812/3535.\n\nLet Phi_T(z)=z(R-z)[A^2+z(T+A)]. Its derivative is\n\n    Phi_T'(z)=A^2 R+2[(T+A)R-A^2]z-3(T+A)z^2.\n\nSince R>0, this quadratic has one negative and one positive root. Thus\nPhi_T increases and then decreases on (0,R); its minimum on any closed\nsubinterval is attained at an endpoint. The two exact endpoint excesses\nover the necessary bound A^2 BC in (8), at T0, are\n\n    Phi_T0(L(T0))-A^2 BC=26650944/1922375>0,\n    Phi_T0(U(T0))-A^2 BC=97846272/1785175>0.\n\nFor T>=T0, U and S increase, while their derivatives\n2A^2/(T+A)^2 and 2C^2/(T+C)^2 lie in (0,1). Hence L=R-S and R-U\nalso increase. Each endpoint product z(R-z)[A^2+z(T+A)] strictly increases,\nbecause all its positive factors increase. If the cut interval is empty,\nthe cuts already fail; otherwise both endpoint excesses stay positive and\nPhi_T(z)>A^2 BC throughout the interval. This contradicts (8) and proves\nT<T0.\n\nFor the last inequality in (12), let lambda=1+sqrt(2), so 2^rho=lambda.\nOne has 9^6-2^19=7153>0 and\n\n    lambda^19=9369319+6625109 sqrt(2)\n       >9369319+(7/5)6625109\n       =(81/5)^6+8890332269/15625.\n\nTherefore 9^(6rho)>lambda^19>(81/5)^6 and (12) follows.\n\nAll endpoint arithmetic here is exact rational arithmetic for two displayed\nvalues. It is not a reopened schedule search or numerical PEP screen.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["dd3ca4162bb552a3", "29a1f4a5269032b1"], "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "branch_id": "max4_saturated_cut_defect_lifting", "status": "complete_candidate_not_verified", "artifact": "local_memory/saturated_cut_defect_lifting.md"}, "glossary": {"A": "4, capacity of the first nonnegative physical block in the corollary.", "B": "4, capacity of the middle nonnegative physical block in the corollary.", "C": "27/7, capacity of the last nonnegative physical block in the corollary.", "T_tot": "One plus the sum of all physical steps.", "rho": "log_2(1+sqrt(2)), the Silver exponent used in the existing seed-amplification threshold.", "r_L": "Positive excess above one of the first bridge.", "r_R": "Positive excess above one of the second bridge.", "Phi_T": "z(R-z)[A^2+z(T+A)], the necessary two-cut polynomial at fixed total capacity T.", "T0": "81/5, the explicit strict capacity upper bound in the corollary.", "U(T)": "A(T-A)/(T+A), first-cut upper bound for r_L.", "S(T)": "C(T-C)/(T+C), second-cut upper bound for r_R.", "L(T)": "T-A-B-C-S(T), the induced lower bound for r_L.", "R": "T-A-B-C, the sum of the two bridge excesses.", "lambda": "1+sqrt(2) in the final exact power comparison."}}
{"id": "ce8ff75e1f9e9111", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T14:02:00.343792+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Reusable exact TWO-dimensional primitive screen for three arbitrary ordinary blocks: capacities A,B,C, bridges1+r,1+s, T=A+B+C+r+s, k=s/C. Every beta satisfying beta<=r/B, beta*k<=1 and r>=beta[B+(C-1)k(1-rk/B)_+] gives a legal instance with V_h=1-[r(r-B beta)+T^2 r beta k^2/B]/A^2. This provides an explicit extra necessary test beyond all single cuts and full curvature, and rejects the current asymmetric eight-step seed with beta=2/5.", "evidence": "UNVERIFIED self-contained construction. Let A,B,C>=1 be the actual capacities of any finite nonnegative ordinary lists and r,s>0. Define k=s/C and choose beta>0 with beta<=r/B, beta*k<=1, r>=beta[B+(C-1)k(1-rk/B)_+]. On R^2 put p=(1/A,0), q=(-beta/A,sqrt(r*beta/B-beta^2)/A), g_end=-k*q. Define J(z)=max(0,<p,z>,<q,z>,<g_end,z>) and f(x)=min_z[J(z)+||x-z||^2/2]. Starting e_0=A p, constant gradient p through the first ordinary list ends at p; its bridge1+r reaches X=-r p. Put Z=X-Bq, for which <q,Z>=0, <p,Z>=-(r-B beta)/A^2, ||q||^2=r beta/(A^2 B), and ||Z||^2=r(r-B beta)/A^2. The middle list uses constant q, ending at Z+q; its bridge1+s reaches Z-sq=Z+C g_end. The last list uses constant g_end, ending at Z+g_end. At candidate proximal points d p, 0<=d<=A-1, the future q plane is nonpositive and the future g_end/current ratio is beta k<=1. At Z+dq, 0<=d<=B-1, the old p plane is nonpositive and the future plane is negative current; both are dominated. At Z+d g_end, 0<=d<=C-1, the old q plane is nonpositive and the old p inequality is exactly r-B beta>=d beta k(1-rk/B). Its maximum over the displayed interval is the assumed inequality. Thus every old/future plane and zero is checked at all queries, independently of the individual ordinary steps. Active-plane subgradient optimality proves the stated trajectory of the one envelope; the standard two-point monotonicity calculation proves that f is convex differentiable1-smooth. Origin is a minimizer and is closest to e_0 because its retained p plane forces every minimizing point's first coordinate to be nonpositive.\nAt every query f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2. For the physical primitive potential V_h=2sum h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-(T-1)T||g_n||^2, T=1+sum h_i, the GD square-distance telescope therefore gives V_h=||e_0||^2-||x_n+(T-1)g_n||^2. Here x_n=Z+g_end, Z is orthogonal to g_end, and ||g_end||^2=k^2 r beta/(A^2B), so V_h=1-[r(r-B beta)+T^2 r beta k^2/B]/A^2. Primitive validity consequently requires A^2>=r^2+r beta[T^2 k^2/B-B] for every admissible beta. The maximum admissible beta is explicitly beta_max=min{1/k,r/[B+(C-1)k(1-rk/B)_+]}; its second entry is already<=r/B. If Tk>B, it maximizes this necessary test's right-hand side. No individual block is assumed quadratic-balanced or primitive. Passing this test is NOT a certificate.\nFor candidate29a1f4a5269032b1 take A=B=4,C=27/7,beta=2/5. The exact parameter bounds12/5<r<5/2 and k<3/5 make the old-plane inequality strictly legal, as detailed in the full counterexample759b0f50d34487a6. Its primitive energy is less than-406159/1476225.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "workers/max/research/max_eight_step_markov_counterexample.md", "section": "5", "counterexample_id": "759b0f50d34487a6", "target_seed": "29a1f4a5269032b1", "status": "unverified exact symbolic filter; no solver or verifier", "fact_ids": [], "scope": "necessary primitive condition, not a universal objective upper bound"}, "glossary": {"A,B,C": "Actual physical capacities1+ordinary-step sum for three prescribed lists.", "T": "Physical total capacity A+B+C+r+s.", "k": "The ratio s/C.", "beta": "Positive correlation parameter in the displayed two-dimensional construction.", "V_h": "The saturated physical primitive energy explicitly defined in the evidence.", "beta_max": "Largest parameter allowed by the stated complete-plane test."}}
{"id": "563c43a0df633463", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T14:03:29.103247+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Candidate exact defect-transport interface retaining actual function-value slacks: appending at a saturated primitive cut preserves ANY core-ended negative primitive energy exactly; prepending has the explicit nonnegative penalty (kappa-1)(||y_end||^2+2I_end,star+2sum h_i I_i,star)+2T(T-D)delta_end/D. Thus generic negative tail energy cannot be transported after dropping those moments. Preserved-data normalization to a core endpoint exists iff delta_n=min_i delta_i, and a legal negative-energy one-step transcript violates this criterion.", "evidence": "# Exact one-bridge defect lifting and the retained slack penalty\n\nStatus: complete candidate interface, NOT verified. No fact_submit or numerical\nsearch. The proof uses only accepted interpolation fact1252b8823bb5a290 and\nthe direct calculations below, not earlier unverified findings as premises.\n\n## Definitions\n\nLet h have capacity K=1+sum h_i. On a legal normalized GD trajectory with\nminimizer0 define\n\n    I_i_star=-Q_(i,star)=<g_i,x_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2>=0,\n    delta_end=f_n-||g_n||^2/2>=0,\n    y_end=x_n-g_n,\n    V_h=2sum h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                           -K(K-1)||g_n||^2.\n\nThe squared-distance telescope gives\n\n    V_h=||x0||^2-||xn||^2-2sum h_i I_i_star\n                         -2(K-1)f_n-K(K-1)||g_n||^2.             (1)\n\nCall the endpoint core when x_n=g_n. In that case interpolation to/from0\nforces f_n=||g_n||^2/2, and (1) reduces to\n\n    V_h=||x0||^2-K^2||g_n||^2-2sum h_i I_i_star.                (2)\n\nAll sample data and the initial-distance term are retained.\n\n## A. Appending at a saturated outer cut preserves a core-ended defect\n\nLet b be any nonnegative schedule of capacity D with an actual legal\ntrajectory having endpoint x_m=g_m. Its dimension and all earlier values,\npoints, gradients and optimum interpolation slacks are arbitrary.\nLet c be any nonnegative block of capacity C, and r_ext>0 satisfy\n\n    T_tot=D+C+r_ext, r_ext T_tot=DC.                            (3)\n\nIf V_b<0 on that trajectory, then (b,1+r_ext,c) is not primitive.\n\nProof. Use a scalar base trajectory whose derivative is clip(x,-r_ext/C,1),\nstarting at D. The entire b block stays on its positive plateau and ends\nat1; the bridge lands at-r_ext; c remains on its negative plateau and\nends at-r_ext/C. The base energy is D^2-T_tot^2(r_ext/C)^2=0 by(3).\n\nFor the auxiliary vector array retain EVERY supplied b sample. Write\ne=g_m=x_m and append the bridge, which lands at-r_ext e. On c set the\nconstant gradient v_end=-(r_ext/C)e, so its initial point is C v_end\nand its final point v_end. Assign each appended value\nf_i=<v_end,x_i>-||v_end||^2/2. This auxiliary array may violate old/new\ncross interpolation and is not claimed to be legal by itself.\n\nCombine the base and auxiliary arrays in orthogonal coordinates with a\npositive multiplier epsilon_mix on every auxiliary squared norm and value.\nAll base-zero constraints remain valid under this mixture:\n\n* Within the old b samples, the auxiliary data are the original legal data.\n* Within the new c samples, the auxiliary gradient is constant.\n* A new-to-old base constraint can be zero only at an old query with\n  zero remaining b steps, hence at its endpoint x_m=e. The auxiliary\n  Q_(new,m) is zero by direct expansion using x_m=g_m=e and f_m=||e||^2/2.\n* An old-to-new base constraint can be zero only at a new query with zero\n  remaining c steps, hence at x=v_end=g. Direct expansion gives\n  Q_(old,new)=Q_(old,star)<=0.\n* Every auxiliary optimum constraint is valid: old ones by hypothesis;\n  appended incoming ones are zero and outgoing ones are\n  -R_i||v_end||^2<=0, where R_i is the remaining c stepsize sum.\n\nZero steps create only the endpoint duplicates already covered above.\nAll other base cross constraints are strictly negative. If E_bad is the\nfinite set of pairs with Q_aux>0, choose\n\n    epsilon_mix=min(1, min_(ij in E_bad) [-Q_base,ij/(2Q_aux,ij)]),\n\ninterpreting the inner minimum as1 for an empty set. Every pair in E_bad\nhas Q_base<0 by the preceding audit, so epsilon_mix>0. The combined data\nobey all original-index interpolation inequalities. The explicit conjugate\nconstruction in fact1252b8823bb5a290 interpolates them by one fixed legal\nconvex differentiable 1-smooth function with minimizer0.\n\nThe auxiliary core endpoint permits (2). No new I_i_star is added, and\nT_tot^2||v_end||^2=D^2||e||^2 by(3). Therefore\n\n    V_aux=V_b, V_mix=epsilon_mix V_b<0.                         (4)\n\nThis is a true defect inheritance rule for the displayed core-ended\nwitness class. It does not assert that every failure of primitive validity\nadmits such a witness.\n\n## B. Prepending has an explicit nonnegative retained-slack penalty\n\nLet b now be any nonnegative schedule of capacity D and any actual legal\ntrajectory (z_j,g_j,f_j) with minimizer0. Let a have capacity A and let\nr_ext>0 satisfy\n\n    T_tot=A+D+r_ext, r_ext T_tot=AD,\n    kappa_lift=(T_tot/D)^2>1.\n\nPut p_aux=-z_0/r_ext. Before the supplied b trajectory insert constant\ngradient p_aux on a, starting at A p_aux and ending at p_aux, then the\nbridge1+r_ext. Its next point is -r_ext p_aux=z_0, exactly the supplied\ntail start. On the inserted points use f=<p_aux,x>-||p_aux||^2/2.\n\nThe scalar base is clip(x,-r_ext/D,1), starting at A; its primitive energy\nis zero by the saturated-cut identity. The auxiliary old/new data can be\nmixed with this base exactly as in partA, PROVIDED\n\n    <p_aux,y_end><=delta_end.                                  (5)\n\nFor completeness, the active cross checks differ from partA as follows.\nTail-to-inserted-end constraints have Q_aux=Q_(tail,star)<=0 because the\ninserted endpoint has x=g=p_aux and f=||p_aux||^2/2. Inserted-to-tail-end\nconstraints have the common value <p_aux,y_end>-delta_end, which is\nnonpositive exactly by(5). Every other possibly positive cross constraint\nhas strictly negative base slack. The same finite positive minimum\ntherefore gives a legal mixture, including all zero-step duplicates and\nall optimum constraints. This proves interpolation, not negativity yet.\n\nLet Sigma_I=sum_(j<m) b_j I_j_star be the weighted ACTUAL tail optimum\ninterpolation slack. Applying (1) separately to the tail and auxiliary\nfull array, and using A^2/r_ext^2=kappa_lift, gives the exact identity\n\n    V_aux=kappa_lift V_b+Penalty,\n\n    Penalty=(kappa_lift-1)\n                  [||y_end||^2+2 I_m_star+2 Sigma_I]\n              +[2T_tot(T_tot-D)/D] delta_end.                  (6)\n\nEvery term of Penalty is nonnegative. Here\nI_m_star=<g_m,y_end>-delta_end; substitution of\nz_m=g_m+y_end into (1) verifies (6) term by term. In particular, a negative\ntail V_b alone does NOT imply V_aux<0. A valid defect-lifting certificate\nmust keep this complete penalty and require kappa_lift V_b+Penalty<0.\n\nAt a core endpoint, (5) is automatic and delta_end=I_m_star=0, so\n\n    V_aux=kappa_lift V_b+2(kappa_lift-1)Sigma_I.                (7)\n\nWhen additionally every weighted incoming optimum slack vanishes,\nSigma_I=0, the negative energy scales exactly by kappa_lift. The new\ntwo-cut Huber construction is in this special class. Discarding Sigma_I\noutside it would repeat the same wrong-sign loss of value information\nthat obstructed the earlier mixed-moment state.\n\n## C. The exact criterion for moving the minimum to the final proximal point\n\nThere is a precise normalization test; a reset cannot be presumed. For any\nfinite fully legal transcript without fixing a particular optimum, define\ndelta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2 for every sample, including the terminal one. Add\nthe proposed optimum jet\n\n    x_star_new=x_n-g_n, g_star_new=0, f_star_new=delta_n.\n\nKeeping ALL original samples, a convex 1-smooth interpolant with this new\noptimum exists if and only if\n\n    delta_n<=delta_i for every i.                              (8)\n\nNecessity and sufficiency are both explicit. The new interpolation edges\nare Q_(i,star_new)=Q_(i,n)<=0 and\nQ_(star_new,i)=delta_n-delta_i. The former already hold in the full\ntranscript; the latter are exactly(8). When they all hold, translate the\nnew optimum to(0,0,0) and use the finite interpolation construction of\nfact1252b8823bb5a290. Translation changes no GD displacement or primitive\nenergy and makes x_n=g_n. This supplies a core-ended witness with exactly\nthe original V_h. When (8) fails, such a preserved-data normalization is\nimpossible.\n\nFor a concrete failure, f(x)=x^2/4, h=(5), x0=1 has\nx1=-3/2, g0=1/2, g1=-3/4, f0=1/4, f1=9/16. Thus\ndelta0=1/8 and delta1=9/32. The proposed new optimum is at-3/4 with\nvalue9/32, and its edge to sample0 violates interpolation by\ndelta1-delta0=5/32>0. The transcript has V_h=-25<0, so even a genuinely\nnegative primitive-energy example need not admit this particular\nnormalization. This does NOT exclude a different core-ended counterexample\nfor the same schedule; indeed this one-step schedule has others.\n\n## Scope and useful next test\n\nThese interfaces give necessary inheritance rules at saturated primitive\ncuts, and an exact penalty away from the special all-tight tail class.\nThey retain all actual prefix interpolation and an explicit fixed-function\nrealization. They neither provide an improved objective certificate nor\nprove that arbitrary primitive failures have core-ended witnesses. The\npreserved-data normalization is now exactly characterized by(8), and can\nfail on a negative-energy instance. Existence of some OTHER core-ended\nwitness for every nonprimitive schedule is still a separate closure claim;\nno all-schedule induction may assume it.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "complete_candidate_not_verified", "is_fragile": false, "fragility_reason": "", "suggested_followup": "none", "scope": "subgoal"}, "glossary": {"I_i_star": "Nonnegative actual incoming optimum slack <g_i,x_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2.", "delta_end": "Terminal value f_n-||g_n||^2/2 after normalization of the optimum to zero.", "y_end": "Terminal proximal point x_n-g_n in the original global coordinates.", capacity of the prepended block in partB.", "C": "Physical capacity of the appended block in partA.", "T_tot": "Physical capacity of the full one-bridge joined schedule.", "r_ext": "Positive excess above one of the new bridge stepsize.", "kappa_lift": "(T_tot/D)^2 in partB.", "Sigma_I": "sum b_j I_j_star, the weighted actual optimum slacks inside the supplied tail.", "Penalty": "The complete auxiliary gradient in partB.", "v_end": "-(r_ext/C)g_m, the auxiliary terminal gradient in partA.", "E_bad": "Finite set of ordered auxiliary interpolation pairs with positive Q_aux.", "delta_i":
