{"id": "cd21b59c480a46f9", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T14:05:15.430458+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Terminal sharpness NECESSITY candidate: for every nonnegative prefix h of sum S, rho_last(h)=q_S iff W(h,psi(S,0))=q_S iff the FULL intrinsic primitive energy V_h is nonnegative on every legal trajectory iff V_h has a complete finite-pair nonnegative/PSD certificate. Equality forces unique final b, terminal support star/last, no star-outgoing prefix multipliers, and exact star-incoming weights.", "evidence": "# Terminal sum-guard equality is equivalent to intrinsic primitivity\n\nStatus: complete UNVERIFIED candidate. No additional fact_submit is allocated.\nThe accepted predecessors are1252b8823bb5a290 and b98bb807d514e80d. The\nknown primitive-energy functional is already used in max4's shared research;\nthe new point here is NECESSITY from sharpness of the terminal sum guard.\nNo unverified terminal minimax or guard candidate is a proof premise.\n\n## 1. Definitions and the equivalence\n\nFix k>=0 and a finite nonnegative prefix h_pre=(h_0,...,h_(k-1)). Put\nS=sum h_pre and define\n\n    t=(-1+sqrt(8S+9))/4,\n    b_S=1+t,\n    r=1/(2t+1),\n    q_S=r^2=1/[1+2(S+b_S)].                            (1)\n\nThus t(2t+1)=S+1, t=r(S+1), and 1-r=2r^2(S+1).\nLet rho_last=inf_(b>=0) W_(k+1)(h_pre,b), in the original project\nnormalization. It is always at least q_S.\n\nFor a smoothness-one trajectory of the prefix, with arbitrary initial\npoint and any minimizer, define the translation-invariant intrinsic energy\n\n    V_prim(h_pre)=2sum_(i<k) h_i(f_i-f_k)\n       +sum_(i<k)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n       -S(S+1)||g_k||^2.                              (2)\n\nHere f_i are function values, g_i are gradients, and the GD recursion is\nx_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i. In arbitrary smoothness L, use f_i/L and g_i=grad f(x_i)/L.\nThe value in(2) is unchanged by a common additive shift of all function values.\n\nThe following four statements are equivalent:\n\n(A) rho_last(h_pre)=q_S.\n\n(B) W_(k+1)(h_pre,b_S)=q_S.\n\n(C) V_prim(h_pre)>=0 on EVERY finite-dimensional legal convex 1-smooth\n    trajectory with a minimizer.\n\n(D) There are nonnegative coefficients eta_ij on distinct finite-prefix\n    pairs i,j in {0,...,k} such that\n\n      V_prim(h_pre)+sum_(i!=j)eta_ij Q_ij                (3)\n\n    is PSD on the FULL formal space of (g_0,...,g_k), after substituting\n    the prefix GD positions. All value coefficients cancel. Equivalently,\n    the finite-node flow of eta is\n\n      out_i-in_i=-2h_i,       i<k,\n      out_k-in_k=2S.\n\nWhen (A) holds, b_S is the unique optimal final step. Leading, interior,\ntrailing and all-zero prefixes are included in the equivalence.\n\n## 2. The universal lower guard and its equality location\n\nAccepted fact b98bb807d514e80d gives, for every b>=0,\n\n    W_(k+1)(h_pre,b)\n       >=max{1/[1+2(S+b)], (b-1)_+^2/(S+1)^2}.         (4)\n\nThe first term decreases strictly; the second increases strictly for b>1.\nThey meet at b=b_S and value q_S, by(1). Thus rho_last>=q_S, and (B)\nimplies (A) with the asserted unique step.\n\nTo justify (A) implies (B) without using the unverified minimax result,\ntake a sequence b_j with W(h_pre,b_j)->q_S. The continuous lower envelope\nin(4) has unique minimizer b_S and tends to infinity as b tends to infinity,\nso b_j->b_S. The function W(h_pre,b) is lower semicontinuous: for every\nfixed actual prefix instance its last objective is a continuous function\nof b, and W is the supremum of these functions in the same normalization.\nTherefore W(h_pre,b_S)<=liminf_j W(h_pre,b_j)=q_S. The reverse follows\nfrom(4), proving (B). This argument needs no prior existence theorem for\nan optimal final step.\n\n## 3. Sufficiency of the intrinsic energy: an explicit identity\n\nNormalize the minimizer to zero. Append b_S, write n=k+1, and put\n\n    V=r[x_k-(S+2)g_k].\n\nThe following algebraic identity holds on every formal trajectory:\n\n    q_S||x_0||^2-2f_n\n      +2q_S sum_(i<k)h_i Q_(i,star)\n      +2q_S(S+2) Q_(k,star)\n      +2r Q_(n,star)+2(1-r)Q_(n,k)\n          =||g_n-V||^2+q_S V_prim(h_pre).             (5)\n\nOne derivation is to apply the terminal-square expansion in accepted\nfact1252b8823bb5a290 with alpha_star=r, alpha_k=1-r and every other\nterminal weight zero. It gives V=x_n-(1-r)(x_k-g_k), which equals the\ndisplayed V because b_S-1+r=t+r=r(S+2). After expanding its remaining\nprefix terms and using\n\n    ||x_0||^2-||x_k||^2\n       =sum_(i<k)[2h_i<x_i,g_i>-h_i^2||g_i||^2],\n\nthe star terms cancel every <x_i,g_i>, and the remaining expression is\nexactly q_S times(2). This checks (5) without any assumption that its\nprefix remainder is PSD on unrestricted data.\n\nIf (C) holds, the RHS of(5) is nonnegative on every actual trajectory.\nAll Q terms on the LHS are nonpositive with nonnegative coefficients.\nThus 2f_n<=q_S||x_0||^2 and W(h_pre,b_S)<=q_S. Equation(4) gives equality.\nThis proves (C)=>(B) directly, without needing a finite multiplier\nrepresentation in advance. The implication (D)=>(C) follows from(3)\nbecause all finite Q are nonpositive on actual trajectories.\n\n## 4. Necessity: two explicit equality instances force the terminal support\n\nFirst assume all k prefix steps are positive, allowing k=0, and assume (B).\nBy accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290, select a tight full nonnegative-flow PSD\ncertificate at level q_S with no incoming terminal multipliers. Write its\noutgoing probabilities as alpha_j=lambda_(n,j)/2 for j in {star,0,...,k}.\n\nConsider the scalar Huber function with derivative clamp(x,[-q_S,q_S]),\nstarted at x_0=1. If P_i=sum_(u<i)h_u, its prefix points and gradients are\n\n    x_i=1-q_S P_i,    g_i=q_S.\n\nThe final point is 1-q_S(S+b_S), still strictly in its positive linear\nregion, and its normalized error is q_S. Moreover, at EVERY prefix sample,\n\n    delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2\n            =q_S[1-q_S(P_i+1)]>0.                    (6)\n\nThe inequality uses P_i<=S and q_S(S+1)=r t=(1-r)/2<1.\nEvery star-outgoing prefix residual Q_(star,i)=-delta_i is strictly negative.\nComplementarity in the accepted full certificate therefore forces\n\n    lambda_(star,i)=0,       0<=i<=k.                  (7)\n\nNext use the scalar capped quadratic with derivative min(c,x), c=1/(S+1),\nalso started at1. Its whole prefix remains on the right affine branch:\n\n    x_i=1-cP_i,   g_i=c,   x_k=c.\n\nThe appended step sends it to x_n=-ct and gradient g_n=-ct. Its normalized\nerror is c^2t^2=q_S. For i<k the outgoing terminal residual is exactly\n\n    Q_(n,i)=-b_S c^2(S-P_i)<0,                        (8)\n\nwhile Q_(n,k)=Q_(n,star)=0. To check(8), start from Q_(n,k)=0, then increase\nthe old point from c to x_i=c+c(S-P_i). Its old value increases by c times\nthat displacement while its pairing with g_n changes by -ct times that\ndisplacement. The resulting difference is -(1+t)c^2(S-P_i).\nStrict positivity of the prefix steps makes S-P_i>0 for every i<k.\nComplementarity now forces alpha_i=0 for every i<k.\n\nThus only alpha_star and alpha_k=1-alpha_star remain. Evaluate the terminal\nsquare on the Huber equality trajectory. The accepted fact gives g_n=V_alpha,\nso\n\n    q_S=alpha_star(1-Sq_S)-(t+alpha_star)q_S.\n\nEquivalently,\n\n    alpha_star[1-(S+1)q_S]=(t+1)q_S.\n\nThe bracket is positive, and(1) gives its unique solution\n\n    alpha_star=r,   alpha_k=1-r.                      (9)\n\nAll these restrictions hold for every selected optimal certificate of\nthe stated no-incoming-terminal type. They were deduced from actual scalar\nequality functions, not from a rank guess or an assumed sparse prefix.\n\n## 5. Full PSD forces the remaining optimum multipliers\n\nThe accepted terminal remainder is\n\n    R_alpha=q_S||x_0||^2-2(1-r)delta_k\n             -r^2||x_k-(S+2)g_k||^2\n             +sum_(i!=j in {star,0,...,k})lambda_ij Q_ij.\n\nIt is PSD on the FULL original prefix formal space. By(7), no multiplier\npoints out of star. Let mu_i=lambda_(i,star). Its x_0-square coefficient\nis q_S-r^2=0. A PSD matrix with a zero diagonal entry has a zero row:\non any two-coordinate restriction, a nonzero off-diagonal entry would make\nthe quadratic negative for one sufficiently small signed second coordinate.\nTherefore every x_0--gradient cross coefficient in R_alpha vanishes.\n\nFinite-pair Q_ij have no x_0 cross term after the GD substitution, because\nx_j-x_i does not contain x_0. The star terms contribute -mu_i<x_0,g_i>.\nExpansion of the negative square then forces\n\n    mu_i=2q_S h_i,       i<k,\n    mu_k=2q_S(S+2).                                  (10)\n\nNo interpolation multiplier on a finite queried-prefix pair is discarded.\nSubstitute(10), divide by q_S, and use the telescoping calculation in(5).\nThe result is exactly\n\n    R_alpha/q_S=V_prim(h_pre)\n           +sum_(i!=j in {0,...,k})(lambda_ij/q_S)Q_ij.\n\nThis is PSD on the full gradient formal space, and each eta_ij=lambda_ij/q_S\nis nonnegative. All value coefficients cancel because they already cancel\nin R_alpha. Their coefficients give precisely the finite flows in(D).\nThus (B)=>(D), completing the equivalence for positive prefixes.\n\n## 6. Zero-step lifting and the empty prefix\n\nDelete zero prefix steps and select as representatives each original\npositive-step query index, with original index k representing the last\nprefix query. Actual coincident queries have identical gradients and values:\nadding their two Q inequalities gives ||g_i-g_j||^2<=0, and their values\nthen agree. The original and compressed schedules therefore have identical\nS, b_S, q_S, W and rho_last.\n\nThe energy(2) is also unchanged by this compression. Terms at zero steps\nhave coefficient zero, while the final gradient is the selected original\ng_k. Hence condition(C) is equivalent on the two schedules.\n\nIf (B) holds, apply the positive-prefix proof to the compressed schedule\nand lift its finite eta multipliers by the representative-index map.\nAll other eta vanish. Every mapped finite Q is the corresponding compressed\nQ as an identity in independent original formal vectors, because the\nomitted h coefficients are zero. The lifted quadratic(3) is the congruence\nof the compressed PSD matrix with the coordinate-selection map. Unused\ngradient columns are zero; no equality between duplicated formal gradients\nis needed for PSD. This proves(D) in the full ORIGINAL formal space.\n\nFor an empty or all-zero prefix, S=0, V_prim=0, b_S=3/2, q_S=1/4, and\neta=0 is a certificate in(D). The sufficiency identity(5) proves the upper\nvalue, and(4) proves the lower value and uniqueness. These cases therefore\nrequire no strictness or Slater assumption.\n\n## 7. What this does and does not close\n\nThe result characterizes exactly WHEN the sum-based terminal lower guard\nis sharp: the complete prefix must satisfy the full intrinsic primitive\nenergy inequality, equivalently its full finite-pair PSD certificate.\nIt supplies a necessity statement for the single terminal ConPD bridge\npsi(S,0), not merely the familiar sufficiency of composing a primitive\nprefix with an empty terminal block.\n\nThe primitive energy itself is not new; it is already present in shared\nfindings504a69a1e000d6aa and2973c2322cdee545. Their numerical probes and\nunverified composition statements are not premises here. The accepted\nterminal reduction is what forces(7)-(10) and the complete finite certificate.\n\nAn arbitrary globally optimal longer schedule need not attain this particular\nsum-only guard. A dominant terminal block can have several steps, and its\nearlier prefix is not thereby primitive. Nor does this equivalence bound\nthe largest sum of a primitive prefix of a fixed length. Those are distinct\nunresolved bridges to the original asymptotic target.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "plan_id": "eb2fc9dd3153b351", "related_existing_energy": ["504a69a1e000d6aa", "2973c2322cdee545"], "artifact": "workers/max2/research/terminal_sharpness_primitive_equivalence.md", "status": "complete_candidate_unverified", "no_verification_allocation": true}, "glossary": {"V_prim": "Intrinsic primitive energy 2sum_(i<k)h_i(f_i-f_k)+sum_(i<k)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||²-S(S+1)||g_k||².", "eta_ij": "Nonnegative multipliers on ALL finite-prefix Q_ij whose sum with V_prim is a full PSD quadratic.", "q_S": "Terminal sum-guard value1/[1+2(S+psi(S,0))].", "b_S": "The prescribed terminal bridge psi(S,0)."}}
{"id": "20c6d21ee60a4cb0", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T14:09:15.474402+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Quantitative defect-to-terminal-gain construction candidate: ANY negative intrinsic primitive-energy witness can be converted into one fixed legal function with inf_b>=0 normalized terminal error >=q_S+epsilon Delta/2>q_S. The exact gain is q_S+epsilon Delta-epsilon²D/(1-epsilon), with explicit epsilon keeping EVERY remote anchor inactive and a fixed orthogonal test gradient.", "evidence": "# A quantitative terminal gain from any negative primitive-energy witness\n\nStatus: self-contained UNVERIFIED candidate. No new fact_submit is allocated.\nThe only accepted predecessor used is1252b8823bb5a290. This directly constructs\none legal function resisting ALL appended final steps; it does not assume the\nunverified terminal SDP or primitive-equivalence candidates.\n\n## 1. Input and output\n\nFix a nonnegative prefix h_pre of length k and sum S. Write\n\n    t=(-1+sqrt(8S+9))/4,  b_0=1+t,\n    r=1/(2t+1),  q=r^2,  c=1/(S+1).\n\nThus t=r(S+1), c t=r, and q=1/[1+2(S+b_0)]. Define on a smoothness-one\nprefix trajectory\n\n    V_prim=2sum_(i<k)h_i(f_i-f_k)\n       +sum_(i<k)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-S(S+1)||g_k||^2.   (1)\n\nSuppose a legal finite-dimensional prefix witness has V_prim<0. The following\nfinite construction gives a normalized legal prefix and one fixed convex\n1-smooth function F whose trajectory under h_pre satisfies, for EVERY b>=0,\n\n    2F(x_k-b g_k) >= q+epsilon Delta/2 > q.             (2)\n\nAll of epsilon and Delta are explicit in the supplied witness and finite\nGram/jet operations below. Therefore\n\n    inf_(b>=0) W_(k+1)(h_pre,b) >= q+epsilon Delta/2.    (3)\n\nThe bound retains every old anchor. Its constants depend on the actual\nnegative-energy witness, its normalization, and the prefix geometry; no\nhorizon-uniform comparison with the exact Zhang--Jiang profile is claimed.\n\nFirst compress zero steps. Energy(1) is unchanged, since a zero step's\ncoefficient vanishes and the final query can be retained as representative.\nThe all-zero case has V_prim=0, so a negative witness leaves a nonempty\npositive prefix. Work with that prefix until restoring duplicates at the end.\n\n## 2. Saturating every outgoing optimum constraint\n\nWe may first arrange that the k+1 prefix gradients are linearly independent,\nwhile retaining a negative energy. If they are not, take a direct sum with a\nsmall positively weighted actual quadratic prefix whose gradients are independent.\nSuch an auxiliary prefix is obtained from k+1 distinct eigenvalues in(0,1):\nthe gradients evaluate z P_i(z), where P_i(z)=product_(u<i)(1-h_u z), and\nthe P_i have distinct degrees0,...,k with nonzero leading coefficients.\nAfter division of each row by its nonzero eigenvalue this is an invertible\npolynomial evaluation matrix. Choose all initial coordinates nonzero.\n\nEnergy(1) is linear in the Gram/value data of a direct sum. If the original\nenergy is -D_0<0 and the auxiliary energy is E_aux, a weight\n0<zeta<=D_0/[2(D_0+|E_aux|)] keeps the combined energy at most -D_0/2.\nIts gradient Gram matrix is positive definite. This direct sum is an actual\nsmooth convex function and supplies every finite-prefix Q inequality.\n\nFor this independent-gradient prefix choose\n\n    f_*<min_(0<=i<=k)(f_i-||g_i||^2/2).\n\nSolve the finite linear system for z_*:\n\n    <g_i,z_*>=f_*-f_i+<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2,\n                         0<=i<=k.                    (4)\n\nIndependence guarantees a solution, for example in the span of the gradients\nusing their positive definite Gram matrix. Append the jet(z_*,0,f_*).\nThen EVERY outgoing optimum residual Q_(i,*) is zero, while EVERY incoming\none is strictly negative:\n\n    Q_(*,i)=f_*-f_i+||g_i||^2/2<0.\n\nAll finite-prefix inequalities are unchanged. Translate z_* to zero and\nsubtract f_* from all values. The interpolation construction in the accepted\nfact gives a globally convex 1-smooth function with exactly those jets and\nminimum zero. Its initial distance R=||x_0-z_*|| is positive; if it were zero,\nthe nonzero independent g_0 would be the gradient at a minimizer.\nScale vectors by1/R and values by1/R^2. Denote this normalized prefix by B.\nIt now satisfies\n\n    ||x_0^B||=1,  Q_(i,star)^B=0 for every i,\n    E_B:=V_prim(B)<0.                                 (5)\n\nThe chosen minimizer is allowed to differ from the original witness's\nminimizer. Every interpolation direction has been checked before making\nthat change; no function value or normalization is independently reset.\n\n## 3. One explicit two-dimensional reference prefix\n\nPut omega=1/(1+q t). Let A be the even Huber function with threshold q,\nand let C have derivative min(c,x) and value C(0)=0. Use the fixed product\n\n    F_A(z_1,z_2)=omega A(z_1/sqrt(omega))\n                 +(1-omega)C(z_2/sqrt(1-omega)),\n    x_0^A=(sqrt(omega),sqrt(1-omega)).\n\nBoth coordinate functions are convex differentiable and 1-smooth; hence\nso is F_A, with minimum zero. For P_i=sum_(u<i)h_u its full prefix is\n\n    x_i^A=x_0^A-P_i g_A,\n    g_A=(sqrt(omega)q,sqrt(1-omega)c).\n\nThe first scalar coordinate stays above q because q(S+1)<1; the second\nstays at least c because c(S+1)=1. Thus these gradients and positions are\nthe actual full trajectory for the prescribed order of h_pre.\nDefine its prefix proximal data y_i^A=x_i^A-g_A and\ndelta_i^A=F_A(x_i^A)-||g_A||^2/2. At its terminal point for b_0, put\n\n    w_A=(sqrt(omega)q,-sqrt(1-omega)c t).\n\nDirect scalar evaluation gives the following identities:\n\n    <g_A,w_A>=0,\n    2<x_k^A,w_A>-||w_A||^2=q,\n    delta_k^A=<y_k^A,w_A>,\n    x_k^A-w_A=b_0 g_A+(1-r)y_k^A.                     (6)\n\nFor the orthogonality, use q=c^2t^2 and 1-omega=omega q t.\nFor the last identity, the second coordinate is immediate and the first\nreduces to r[1-q(S+1)]=(1+t)q. The remaining identities follow by evaluating\nthe two scalar values, each of which has normalized terminal error q and\nzero outgoing interpolation residual to the optimum.\n\nThe two vectors g_A and y_k^A are independent: y_k^A has positive first\ncoordinate and zero second coordinate, while g_A has positive second\ncoordinate. For each earlier i<k the exact inactive-plane margin is\n\n    margin_i:=delta_i^A-<y_i^A,w_A>\n          =(1-omega)b_0 c^2(S-P_i)>0.                 (7)\n\nThis follows by evaluating the capped-coordinate terminal-to-i residual;\nthe Huber coordinate's corresponding residual is zero. Positive compressed\nsteps guarantee S-P_i>0.\n\n## 4. The negative energy provides a positive perturbation slope\n\nFor prefix B set\n\n    y_i^B=x_i^B-g_i^B,  delta_i^B=f_i^B-||g_i^B||^2/2,\n    V_B=x_k^B-b_0 g_k^B-(1-r)y_k^B,\n    Phi_B=2(1-r)delta_k^B+||V_B||^2,\n    Delta=-q E_B>0.\n\nThe following identity is obtained by expanding V_B and telescoping\n||x_0^B||^2-||x_k^B||^2 along GD:\n\n    q||x_0^B||^2-Phi_B\n      +2q sum_(i<k)h_i Q_(i,star)^B\n      +2q(S+2)Q_(k,star)^B=q E_B.\n\nIndeed V_B=r[x_k^B-(S+2)g_k^B], because t+r=r(S+2). Expansion cancels\nall x_i--g_i pairings against the star terms and leaves precisely(1).\nUsing the normalization and saturation in(5), it follows that\n\n    Phi_B=q+Delta.                                    (8)\n\nLet u_1 be the unique vector in span(g_A,y_k^A) solving\n\n    <g_A,u_1>=-<g_k^B,V_B>,\n    <y_k^A,u_1>=delta_k^B-<y_k^B,V_B>.                 (9)\n\nIts existence follows from the positive definite two-vector Gram matrix\nidentified after(6). Put D=||u_1||^2>=0 and for i<k put\n\n    remote_i=delta_i^B-<y_i^B,V_B>-<y_i^A,u_1>.\n\nChoose explicitly\n\n    epsilon=min{1/2, Delta/[2(D+Delta)],\n                    min_(i<k) margin_i/[2(margin_i+|remote_i|)]}. (10)\n\nEvery entry is positive, so 0<epsilon<=1/2. Computing u_1 requires only\na two-by-two linear system; no optimization or numerical sweep is involved.\n\n## 5. Retaining all prefix constraints and all terminal anchors\n\nTake the actual direct-sum prefix C with weights1-epsilon and epsilon:\n\n    x_i^C=(sqrt(1-epsilon)x_i^A,sqrt(epsilon)x_i^B),\n    g_i^C=(sqrt(1-epsilon)g_A,sqrt(epsilon)g_i^B),\n    f_i^C=(1-epsilon)f_i^A+epsilon f_i^B.\n\nIt is realized by the corresponding scaled product of the two legal\nfunctions. Its initial squared norm is one, its minimum value is zero,\nand every ordered prefix Q is the same convex combination of the two\nvalid Q values. Thus it retains the complete prefix, not just selected edges.\nDefine one FIXED terminal test vector\n\n    w_C=(sqrt(1-epsilon)[w_A+epsilon u_1/(1-epsilon)],\n                                          sqrt(epsilon)V_B).\n\nEquation(9) gives <g_k^C,w_C>=0. For the last prefix plane its other\nequation gives delta_k^C-<y_k^C,w_C>=0. For EVERY earlier plane,\n\n    delta_i^C-<y_i^C,w_C>\n        =(1-epsilon)margin_i+epsilon remote_i\n                                      >=margin_i/2>0, (11)\n\nwhere the last inequality follows from(10). The optimum plane has value zero.\nConsequently the maximum of the prefix conjugate affine planes at w_C is\nEXACTLY zero. No earlier plane can defeat the displayed terminal lower bound.\n\n## 6. An exact gain expression for one globally legal function\n\nBy accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 the maximal prefix interpolant\n\n    F=[v -> ||v||^2/2+max_j(<y_j^C,v>-delta_j^C)]^*\n\nis a globally convex differentiable 1-smooth function with minimum zero,\ninterpolating EVERY prefix jet C. Its trajectory under h_pre is therefore\nexactly C. The test-vector orthogonality and(11) imply, for EVERY b>=0,\n\n    2F(x_k^C-b g_k^C)\n       >=2<x_k^C,w_C>-||w_C||^2=Gamma_epsilon.\n\nExpanding this final expression, then using(6) and(9), gives\n\n    Gamma_epsilon\n       =(1-epsilon)q+epsilon Phi_B\n                             -epsilon^2 D/(1-epsilon)\n       =q+epsilon Delta-epsilon^2 D/(1-epsilon).        (12)\n\nFor clarity, the cross term from the A block is\n2epsilon<b_0 g_A+(1-r)y_k^A,u_1>. Combined with the B block this equals\n\n    epsilon[2<x_k^B,V_B>-||V_B||^2\n       -2b_0<g_k^B,V_B>\n       +2(1-r)(delta_k^B-<y_k^B,V_B>)]=epsilon Phi_B,\n\nby the definition of V_B. This checks every term in(12).\n\nThe second bound in(10) gives\n\n    epsilon D/(1-epsilon)<=Delta D/(2D+Delta)<=Delta/2.\n\nSubstituting in(12) proves(2)-(3). F is fixed before the final step is chosen;\nthe same single test vector proves its bound on the entire nonnegative ray.\n\nRestore zero steps by repeating their actual query jets. The duplicate\nconjugate planes add nothing to the finite maximum, and every trajectory\nidentity is unchanged. The actual normalized initial distance remains one.\nFor any prescribed L>0, replace F with L F and divide its objective by L.\n\nThe construction turns ANY concrete failure of intrinsic primitivity into\nan explicit strictly stronger final-stage lower bound. Its gain can be small\nbecause the saturation radius, independent-gradient perturbation, and remote\nanchor margins can deteriorate. Controlling those constants along a whole\nschedule remains an additional quantitative problem.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "artifact": "workers/max2/research/primitive_defect_terminal_gain.md", "related_candidate": "cd21b59c480a46f9", "status": "complete_candidate_unverified", "no_verification_allocation": true, "proof_method": "independent-gradient perturbation, optimum-jet saturation, explicit two-dimensional correction, maximal prefix interpolant", "not_used_as_premise": ["ca309246ba02a2da", "cd21b59c480a46f9", "3e06d97b1142c19c"]}, "glossary": {"E_B": "Negative intrinsic primitive energy of the saturated, norm-one prefix B.", "Delta": "Positive perturbation slope -q_S E_B.", "u_1": "Unique vector in the reference two-dimensional span satisfying the two inner-product corrections in equation9.", "margin_i": "Positive inactive-plane margin of each earlier reference prefix anchor at w_A.", "remote_i": "Explicit bad-prefix plane offset minus its reference correction.", "Gamma_epsilon": "Exact all-final-step conjugate lower certificate q_S+epsilon Delta-epsilon²||u_1||²/(1-epsilon)."}}
{"id": "154e28491664f3f1", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T14:17:24.524997+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Constructive positive-level terminal policy for ANY legal zero-level Markov prefix: the complete retained history gives an exact scalar feasible interval [T_min,T_max] and a closed-form maximizing T_opt, beta_opt and normalized payoff. No parameter existence or scale is hidden. Positive levels can repair a join with NO zero-level continuation of the same frozen prefix; an arbitrary-block family exhibits negative residuals at unqueried points while every actual plane comparison holds. Internal-margin contributions also telescope to an explicit two-endpoint identity.", "evidence": "# Exact positive-level terminal policy for a retained Markov prefix\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint proof candidate. No verifier or numerical solver call.\nThis is a conditional constructive theorem for every prefix in the stated\nclass, not an assertion that every prescribed schedule has a prefix in this\nclass with the required benchmark payoff. All new algebra is proved here.\n\n## 1. The retained prefix and its full data\n\nWork with smoothness one in a Euclidean space. Let a prescribed prefix be\npartitioned into nonnegative ordinary blocks of sums s_0,...,s_m, separated\nby checkpoints b_0,...,b_(m-1). Put C_i=s_i+1. Choose\n\n    a_i=b_i-1>0,  0<beta_i<=a_i/C_(i+1),\n    alpha_0=1/C_0,\n    alpha_(i+1)^2=(a_i beta_i/C_(i+1)) alpha_i^2,\n    g_0=alpha_0 e_0,\n    g_(i+1)=-beta_i g_i+v_(i+1),\n\nwhere v_(i+1) is orthogonal to the entire previously used space and has\nsquared norm alpha_i^2 beta_i(a_i/C_(i+1)-beta_i). The e_0 vector is unit.\nSet X_0=e_0, X_(i+1)=X_i-(s_i+b_i)g_i, and\n\n    Z_i=X_i-C_i g_i.\n\nFor j<i define\n\n    P_(j,i)=product_(j<=l<i) beta_l,\n    Q_(j,i)=product_(j<=l<i) a_l/C_(l+1),\n    R_l=a_l-C_(l+1)beta_l,\n    H_(j,i)=sum_(l=j)^(i-1) (-1)^(l-j) R_l P_(j,l),\n\nwhere empty products equal one. Direct induction gives\n\n    ||g_i||^2=alpha_i^2,\n    <g_j,g_i>=(-1)^(i-j) alpha_j^2 P_(j,i),\n    alpha_i^2=alpha_j^2 P_(j,i)Q_(j,i),\n    <g_j,Z_i>=-alpha_j^2 H_(j,i)  (j<i),\n    <g_j,Z_i>=0                   (j>=i).           (1)\n\nFor the last equality, Z_0=0 and\nZ_(i+1)-Z_i=-a_i g_i-C_(i+1)g_(i+1). Its inner product\nwith g_(i+1) is zero by the displayed gradient recurrence. Every later\ngradient is a scalar multiple of g_(i+1) plus a vector orthogonal to this\nincrement. The formula for an earlier gradient follows by substituting\nits signed product correlation and summing these increments. This also\nproves all identities in (1), without citing an unverified finding.\n\nAssume the following explicitly checkable prefix inequalities hold:\n\n    H_(j,i)>=s_i P_(j,i)[(-1)^(i-j)-Q_(j,i)]_+\n                                                   for j<i,           (2)\n    P_(j,i)<=1             for j<i, i-j even, s_j>0.                  (3)\n\nHere [t]_+=max(t,0). In particular every H_(j,i) is nonnegative.\nDefine J_old(z)=max(0,<g_0,z>,...,<g_m,z>). The actual queries in\nblock i, after an ordinary partial sum u in [0,s_i], have position\nZ_i+(C_i-u)g_i and proposed proximal point Z_i+(s_i-u)g_i.\nIts selected plane g_i has value (s_i-u)alpha_i^2. Equations (1)-(2)\ncompare every old plane with it; (1) and (3) compare every future plane.\nThe zero plane is dominated too. Thus g_i is a subgradient of J_old at\nthis proximal point and the complete prescribed prefix is realized by\nf_old=env_1 J_old. Every endpoint Z_i has value zero. The intermediate\npartial sums require no extra condition because each comparison is\naffine in u and its endpoints are included.\n\nFor completeness, for any finite maximum J of linear functions including\nzero, its proximal minimizer z(x) is unique. If g=x-z(x), the subgradient\noptimality condition is g in partial J(z(x)). Monotonicity of partial J\ngives ||g(x)-g(y)||^2<=<g(x)-g(y),x-y>. Comparing the two minimizing\nexpressions in the envelope and using this Lipschitz bound shows\nenv_1 J is differentiable with gradient g and is convex and 1-smooth.\nThis supplies the fixed smooth function used below.\n\n## 2. Append one whole terminal block, preserving every old query\n\nAppend a checkpoint of size b=1+A with A>0, followed by an arbitrary\nnonnegative block of sum C-1, where C>=1. The bridge uses the existing\nendpoint gradient g_m, so the next start is X_new=Z_m-A g_m.\nChoose a level parameter T>=C and beta with 0<beta<=A/T. In one new\northogonal direction set\n\n    g_new=-beta g_m+v_new,\n    ||v_new||^2=alpha_m^2 beta(A/T-beta),\n    Z_new=X_new-T g_new.\n\nSet J=max(J_old,<g_new,z>) and f=env_1 J. In the new ordinary block\nthe proposed proximal points have form Z_new+d g_new with\nT-C<=d<=T-1. The final proximal height is\n(T-C)||g_new||^2, and the squared gradient is\n\n    ||g_new||^2=(A beta/T)alpha_m^2.                 (4)\n\nOnly old indices j<m with m-j odd can obstruct the extension. For each\nsuch j retain\n\n    p_j=P_(j,m), q_j=Q_(j,m), c_j=H_(j,m)/p_j>=0,\n    D_j=A q_j, E_j=A-c_j.\n\nDefine the future-plane cap\n\n    U=min_{j<m, m-j odd, s_j>0} 1/p_j,             (5)\n\nwith empty minimum +infinity. At old positive-height queries the new\nplane is dominated exactly when beta<=U. Indeed (1) makes its intercept\nzero there and its relative positive slope is beta p_j. At old zero\nheight queries all new-plane values are zero. Old indices of the other\nparity give negative slopes and no additional condition.\n\nFor an index j of the obstructing parity, subtracting the selected new\nplane at Z_new+d g_new and dividing by alpha_j^2 p_j gives exactly\n\n    -c_j+A-T beta+d beta(1-D_j/T).                 (6)\n\nConsequently define, for T>=C,\n\n    F_j(T)=C+D_j-C D_j/T,             if T<=D_j,\n           1+D_j-D_j/T,               if T>=D_j.  (7)\n\nThe formulas agree at T=D_j whenever this point belongs to the domain.\nThe maximum of (6) over d in [T-C,T-1] is nonpositive exactly when\n\n    beta F_j(T)>=E_j.                             (8)\n\nFor all other old indices, the new signed residual is the old\nnonnegative residual plus p_j(A-T beta), and the new correlation has\nnegative sign. Their comparisons therefore hold automatically. This\nincludes g_m. The zero plane is dominated because T-C>=0.\n\nThus (5), (8), and 0<beta<=A/T are NECESSARY AND SUFFICIENT for the\nspecified new samples to come from the same fixed J while retaining all\nprefix samples. There is no removal of a queried interpolation condition.\n\nOrigin remains a closest minimizer to e_0. In fact J>=0, J(0)=0, and\nthe retained g_0=e_0/C_0 plane forces <e_0,z><=0 at every zero of J.\nThe envelope has the same zero set: a zero minimum in its defining\nnonnegative sum requires z=x and J(z)=0. Hence every minimizer is at\ndistance at least one from e_0, with equality attained at origin. The\nEXACT normalized final objective is\n\n    V(T,beta)=alpha_m^2 A beta(2T-2C+1)/T.         (9)\n\n## 3. Eliminate all continuous feasibility choices explicitly\n\nIndices with E_j<=0 give no constraint in (8), since F_j is positive.\nFor each remaining index, 0<E_j<=A. Its square-root endpoint is\n\n    T_j^+ = [A(1+D_j)\n                +sqrt(A^2(1+D_j)^2-4 A D_j E_j)]/(2E_j).             (10)\n\nThe radicand is at least A^2(D_j-1)^2. The PSD upper choice beta<=A/T\ncan satisfy (8) exactly when T<=T_j^+, for T>=C. To prove this, if\nC<=T<=D_j, then\n\n    F_j(T)-T=-(T-C)(T-D_j)/T>=0,\n\nso A F_j(T)>=E_j T holds. For T>=max(C,D_j), the same inequality is\n\n    E_j T^2-A(1+D_j)T+A D_j<=0.\n\nThis convex quadratic is nonpositive at both 1 and D_j, since E_j<=A.\nThus its smaller root is no greater than min(1,D_j), and on the stated\ndomain the condition is precisely T<=T_j^+. Also T_j^+>=max(1,D_j),\nso the first range creates no missing case.\n\nThe future cap can satisfy (8) exactly when F_j(T)>=z_j:=E_j/U, where\nz_j=0 if U=+infinity. F_j is continuous and strictly increasing, with\nlimit 1+D_j. If z_j>=1+D_j, no finite T is feasible. Otherwise its\nexact lower endpoint is\n\n    T_j^- = C,                         if z_j<=F_j(C),\n            C D_j/(C+D_j-z_j),         if F_j(C)<z_j<=D_j,\n            D_j/(1+D_j-z_j),           otherwise.                  (11)\n\nThe middle case automatically implies C<D_j. The last case has a\npositive denominator by the previously checked strict inequality.\n\nSet\n\n    T_min=max(C, max_j T_j^-),\n    T_max=min_j T_j^+,                                      (12)\n\nover indices with E_j>0. Empty maxima contribute no bound and an empty\nminimum is +infinity. There is an extension exactly when no index has\nz_j>=1+D_j and T_min<=T_max. Its ENTIRE set of possible T is the closed\ninterval [T_min,T_max]. At each T in this interval the allowed beta is\n\n    max(0,max_j E_j/F_j(T)) <= beta <= min(A/T,U),  beta>0.             (13)\n\nThis is an explicit policy from the actual retained history, rather than\nan assertion that feasible scales exist. Positive finite T_min is always\nattained. If T_max is infinite, the optimum below is still finite.\n\n## 4. Closed-form best payoff for this frozen prefix\n\nAt fixed T, (9) increases in beta, so choose beta_max=min(A/T,U).\nPut d=2C-1, and interpret A/U=0 when U=+infinity. Before imposing the\ninterval (12), the payoff is increasing up to\n\n    T_peak=max(d,A/U)\n\nand decreasing thereafter. Indeed for T<=A/U it is\nalpha_m^2 A U(2-d/T), a strictly increasing function. For T>=A/U it\nis alpha_m^2 A^2(2T-d)/T^2, whose derivative has the sign of d-T.\nSince C>=1, d>=C, so T_peak lies in the original domain. The exact\nconstrained optimizer is therefore\n\n    T_opt=min(T_max,max(T_min,T_peak)),\n    beta_opt=min(A/T_opt,U),\n    V_opt=alpha_m^2 A beta_opt(2T_opt-2C+1)/T_opt.       (14)\n\nAll quantities in (10)-(14) are obtained by finitely many scalar\noperations on already retained data. No optimization oracle, parameter\nlimit, dimension-dependent normalization, or hidden vanishing scale is\nused. The prefix may have any finite number of checkpoints and arbitrary\nnonnegative ordinary blocks. Its physical boundary magnitude alpha_m is\nretained in (14), rather than silently normalized away.\n\n## 5. Positive levels can repair a genuinely impossible zero-level join\n\nFor any M>1 take three arbitrary ordinary blocks each of sum M-1, and\nthe two checkpoints 4M+1 and 2M+1. On the first two blocks choose\nbeta_0=2, alpha_0=1/M. Then\n\n    ||g_1||^2=8/M^2, R_0=2M,\n    p_0=2, q_0=4, c_0=M,\n    A=2M, C=M, D_0=8M, E_0=M, U=1/2.\n\nThe old prefix is legal: its only earlier-plane residual is 2M>=0 and\nthe future correlation is negative. At T=C=M, inequality (8) requires\nbeta>=1, while the old-query future bound requires beta<=1/2. Thus NO\nzero-level new block is legal for this exact frozen prefix.\n\nEquations (10)-(14) instead give\n\n    T_min=8M/7,\n    T_max=1+8M+sqrt(64M^2+1),\n    T_opt=4M, beta_opt=1/2,\n    V_opt=12/M+2/M^2.                                  (15)\n\nAt the optimum the terminal gradient is g_new=-g_1/2. In particular the\nold frozen prefix needs no change and no new dimension at this optimal\nfinal join. There is also a legal endpoint choice T=T_min=8M/7 and\nbeta=1/2. There the unqueried residual is H_(0,2)=-6M/7<0, while\nP_(0,2)=1, Q_(0,2)=7 and w_2=M/7. Its exact comparison is\nH_(0,2)=w_2(1-7); the positive-part term is zero. Its normalized payoff\nis 2/M+7/M^2. Thus enforcing that every unqueried Z_i remain a\nminimizer would incorrectly reject this legal positive-level continuation.\n\nThis example is an arbitrary-block parametric family, not a new uniform\nbenchmark comparison. The earlier nonperiodic comparison in shared\nfinding b0bf06c26eb801e6 is a separate complete joint candidate. The\npresent result supplies an exact arbitrary-prefix terminal policy that\ncan replace an existential endpoint choice in future work.\n\n## 6. A separate algebraic invariant for internal positive margins\n\nThe preceding interval theorem assumes all retained blocks have zero\nproximal endpoint value. For further iteration, internal positive\nmargins cannot be erased. The following exact cancellation describes\ntheir location in the full state.\n\nFor a chain with margins w_i>=0, define T_i=s_i+1+w_i and\nA_i=b_i-1-w_i>0, and keep the gradient recurrence with these parameters.\nFor fixed beta_i put\n\n    R_l^w=b_l-1-w_l-(s_(l+1)+1+w_(l+1))beta_l,\n    R_l^0=b_l-1-(s_(l+1)+1)beta_l.\n\nLet H^w and H^0 be the corresponding signed residual sums. Every\ninterior w_l contribution cancels between the two adjacent summands, so\n\n    H^w_(j,i)=H^0_(j,i)-w_j+(-1)^(i-j)P_(j,i)w_i.      (16)\n\nLet Q^w_(j,i)=product_(j<=l<i) A_l/T_(l+1). At the actual proximal\nsegment d in [w_i,s_i+w_i], the exact old-plane comparison is\n\n    H^w_(j,i)>=w_i P_(j,i)[(-1)^(i-j)-Q^w_(j,i)]\n           +s_i P_(j,i)[(-1)^(i-j)-Q^w_(j,i)]_+.\n\nSubstituting (16) cancels the parity-dependent endpoint term and gives\n\n    H^0_(j,i)-w_j+w_i P_(j,i)Q^w_(j,i)\n          >=s_i P_(j,i)[(-1)^(i-j)-Q^w_(j,i)]_+.        (17)\n\nThe nonnegative margin itself is not disposable: it remains at both\nendpoints of (17), and every intermediate margin remains in Q^w through\nthe true squared-gradient ratios. The example in (15) illustrates a\nnegative H^w at an unqueried residual point with valid positive-height\nsamples. The interval theorem does not assert a greedy optimum over\nfuture internal cells or an arbitrary-schedule lower bound.\n\n## Retrieval and status\n\nThe fact graph was searched before this subgoal. No accepted fact there\nprovides this positive-level policy. A targeted Matlas query about\nMoreau support functions and polygon projections returned projection and\nFrank-Wolfe results that are not used. The derivation above is elementary\nand does not rely on any new external black box. No fact has been\nsubmitted because the current TASK allocates no additional verifier call.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "workers/max/research/max_exact_positive_terminal_policy.md", "extends": ["5e110ec38dadffb4", "b0bf06c26eb801e6"], "status": "unverified; no new verifier allocation", "fact_ids": [], "scope": "all legal zero-level Markov prefixes, exact new positive terminal block; not uniform B_n comparison"}, "glossary": {"C": "One plus the sum of the new ordinary terminal block; C>=1.", "A": "Excess above one of the new checkpoint, assumed positive.", "T": "C plus the nonnegative proximal endpoint margin in the new block.", "beta": "Positive new anti-correlation coefficient in g_new=-beta g_m+v_new.", "alpha_m": "Actual retained boundary gradient norm, not normalized away.", "P_(j,i)": "Product of the prefix anti-correlation parameters beta_l.", "Q_(j,i)": "Product of a_l/C_(l+1), the scale ratios after factoring out P.", "H_(j,i)": "Full alternating signed residual sum defined in proof section 1.", "p_j,q_j,c_j": "Retained quantities P_(j,m),Q_(j,m),H_(j,m)/P_(j,m) for old odd-lag indices.", "D_j,E_j": "A q_j and A-c_j, respectively.", "U": "Exact cap on beta from the new plane at every old positive-height query, equation (5).", "F_j(T)": "Explicit positive increasing piecewise rational function (7) encoding old plane j at all new queries.", "T_min,T_max": "Exact lower and upper endpoints from equations (10)-(12).", "T_opt,beta_opt,V_opt": "Actual closed-form constrained optimizer and exact normalized score in (14).", "H^w,H^0": "Signed residual sums with positive margins and with those margins formally zeroed at fixed beta, respectively."}}
{"id": "a141afe16d306715", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T14:19:20.181281+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Exact candidate core-witness reduction: EVERY negative primitive-energy trajectory for a finite nonnegative schedule has a core-ended realization in one extra dimension with identical primitive energy. Add a zero-energy affine-ray Huber companion large enough to make the terminal adjusted value minimal, then replace the optimum. This closes the explicit missing hypothesis left in563c43a0df633463. The same complete proof gives arbitrary-prefix heredity at a saturated appended primitive bridge. The arbitrary-tail/prepending penalty remains. No verifier allocation used; not a fact.", "evidence": "# Exact Huber padding gives a core-ended realization of every primitive defect\n\nStatus: complete candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. No numerical PEP or\nfact_submit call. The only fact used is 1252b8823bb5a290, specifically its\nfinite smooth-convex interpolation construction. This file includes every\nnew deduction; earlier shared findings are context, not premises.\n\n## 1. Statement and definitions\n\nFix a finite nonnegative stepsize schedule h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)). Put\ns=sum_i h_i, T=1+s, and R_i=sum_(t=i)^(n-1) h_t for 0<=i<=n.\nThus R_n=0. Consider any actual finite convex differentiable 1-smooth\ntrajectory x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i, with g_i=grad f(x_i), f_i=f(x_i).\nLet\n\n  delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2,\n  Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n  V_h=2 sum_(i<n) h_i(f_i-f_n)\n       +sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-T(T-1)||g_n||^2.\n\nThen there exists another actual trajectory for the SAME h, in at most\none additional dimension, on an everywhere finite convex differentiable\n1-smooth function with minimizer0, such that\n\n  x_n_new=g_n_new, f_n_new=||g_n_new||^2/2,\n  V_h_new=V_h.\n\nIn particular, h has a negative primitive-energy witness if and only if it\nhas a witness whose endpoint equals its terminal gradient relative to a\nminimizer. No preservation of the normalized objective error is asserted.\n\nHere and below a core endpoint means precisely x_n=g_n with optimum0.\n\n## 2. Choose one finite Huber companion\n\nIf R_i=0, nonnegativity of h forces every later step to be zero.\nConsequently x_i=x_n, g_i=g_n, f_i=f_n and delta_i=delta_n.\nChoose any c>0 such that\n\n  c^2 >= max_(i:R_i>0) (delta_n-delta_i)_+/R_i,                 (1)\n\nwith empty maximum0 and (a)_+=max(a,0). This is a finite maximum.\n\nDefine the fixed scalar Huber function\n\n  H_c(z)=z^2/2                         if |z|<=c,\n         c|z|-c^2/2                   if |z|>=c.\n\nStart its GD run at z_0=T c. Every step of the given schedule stays on\nits positive affine ray:\n\n  z_i=(1+R_i)c, H_c'(z_i)=c,\n  H_c(z_i)=(R_i+1/2)c^2.                                    (2)\n\nThese formulas include its final point z_n=c. The derivative is\ncontinuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz, so H_c is a fixed legal\nconvex differentiable 1-smooth function.\n\nRun the direct sum f(x)+H_c(z). Its exact sampled data are\n\n  X_i=(x_i,(1+R_i)c),\n  G_i=(g_i,c),\n  F_i=f_i+(R_i+1/2)c^2.                                    (3)\n\nThe original-index GD equations hold, including zero steps.\n\nThe companion's finite interpolation residual is zero for every ordered\npair: its two gradients are equal and its sampled values are affine.\nTherefore\n\n  Q_ij_new=Q_ij_original<=0.                                (4)\n\nIts adjusted values obey\n\n  Delta_i=F_i-||G_i||^2/2=delta_i+R_i c^2>=delta_n=Delta_n.    (5)\n\nFor R_i>0 this follows from(1); for R_i=0 it follows from the duplicate\nsample observation above. No strictness or limiting argument is needed.\n\n## 3. Add the new optimum, retaining every query jet\n\nReplace the old optimum by the new jet\n\n  X_star_new=X_n-G_n=(x_n-g_n,0),\n  G_star_new=0,\n  F_star_new=Delta_n=delta_n.                               (6)\n\nThe previous optimum, if one was specified, is not retained. All query\njets(3) are retained exactly.\n\nFor every query i, direct expansion gives\n\n  Q_(i,star_new)=Q_(i,n)_new=Q_(i,n)_original<=0,\n  Q_(star_new,i)=Delta_n-Delta_i<=0.                         (7)\n\nAt i=n both quantities are zero. Equations(4),(7) check every ordered\ninterpolation pair in the enlarged finite data set.\n\nBy the finite interpolation construction in fact1252b8823bb5a290, these\ndata are interpolated by one everywhere finite convex differentiable\n1-smooth function with the new optimum jet. Concretely, after translating\nthe new optimum to0 and its value to0, form\n\n  H(v)=max_(j in {star,0,...,n})\n       {<G_j,X_j>-F_j+<X_j,v-G_j>+||v-G_j||^2/2},\n  F_new(x)=sup_v {<x,v>-H(v)}.\n\nHere X_j,F_j in the displayed formula denote the translated data.\nThis is the accepted fact's explicit construction, with the whole\nfinite set treated as interpolation input. It interpolates every\noriginal-index query and the zero optimum. Translation preserves all\nGD differences. At the last query,\n\n  x_n_new=G_n=(g_n,c),\n  f_n_new=F_n-delta_n=||G_n||^2/2.                           (8)\n\nThe initial displacement is explicitly\n\n  x_0_new=(x_0-x_n+g_n,Tc),\n\nso it is nonzero since c>0.\n\n## 4. Exact preservation of primitive energy\n\nThe energy is unchanged by a common translation of positions and a\ncommon subtraction from all function values. It is additive under the\northogonal direct sum(3). The Huber companion has exactly zero energy:\nfrom(2),\n\n  V_companion/c^2\n    =2 sum_i h_i R_i+sum_i h_i(1-h_i)-s(s+1)=0,\n\nbecause\n\n  2 sum_i h_i R_i=s^2+sum_i h_i^2.\n\nThus V_h_new=V_h, without an approximation or an unproved inequality.\n\nThis supplies a core-ended realization of every negative witness.\nThe converse follows because a core-ended witness is itself legal.\nFor n=0 or an all-zero schedule the construction still works, and V_h=0.\n\n## 5. What happens to the retained optimum slacks\n\nAfter the normalization, the incoming optimum slack is exactly\n\n  I_i_star_new=-Q_(i,n)_original>=0.                         (9)\n\nThus the construction DOES NOT remove queried-to-terminal interpolation\nslack. Define d=x_0-x_n and Sigma_n=-sum_(i<n)h_i Q_(i,n).\nExpansion of the definitions gives\n\n  V_h+2 Sigma_n=||d+g_n||^2-T^2||g_n||^2.                    (10)\n\nEquivalently, the core realization has\n\n  ||x_0_new||^2-T^2||g_n_new||^2\n     =||d+g_n||^2-T^2||g_n||^2,\n\nindependent of c. Padding removes the terminal-adjusted-value obstruction\nbut leaves exactly the old query-to-terminal slack ledger. This is why\nthe result does not, by itself, prove tail heredity at a prepended\nsaturated bridge.\n\n## 6. Exact hostile check: the old direct-reset counterexample\n\nTake f(x)=x^2/4, h=(5), x_0=1. The original data are\n\n  (x_0,x_1)=(1,-3/2),\n  (g_0,g_1)=(1/2,-3/4),\n  (f_0,f_1)=(1/4,9/16),\n  (delta_0,delta_1)=(1/8,9/32),\n  V_h=-25.\n\nA direct preserved-data core reset fails because delta_1>delta_0.\nChoose c=1/4. Then c^2=1/16>=(delta_1-delta_0)/5=1/32.\nAfter padding and normalizing, the data are\n\n  x_0_new=(7/4,3/2),  g_0_new=(1/2,1/4), f_0_new=5/16,\n  x_1_new=(-3/4,1/4), g_1_new=(-3/4,1/4), f_1_new=5/16.\n\nThey satisfy x_1_new=x_0_new-5g_0_new and\nx_1_new=g_1_new. Their finite Q residuals are unchanged from the legal\noriginal pair; their optimum residuals follow(7). Direct substitution\nin the energy gives\n\n  V_h_new=5(1-5)(5/16)-30(5/8)=-25.\n\nThus the very example that defeats an unpadded reset is repaired\nexactly by one extra equality-mode coordinate.\n\n## 7. Saturated append heredity, with the entire proof included\n\nLet b and c_block be any nonnegative physical blocks, with capacities\nD=1+sum b_i and C=1+sum(c_block)_i. Let r>0 solve\n\n  T_join=D+C+r, r T_join=DC.                               (11)\n\nIf the joined schedule (b,1+r,c_block) is primitive, then b is primitive.\nThis is a necessary prefix heredity theorem at an exactly saturated\nprimitive bridge. No primitive assumption about c_block is needed.\n\nProof by contrapositive. If b is not primitive, sections1-4 turn an\nactual negative witness into a legal core-ended witness for b, with\nendpoint e=x_m=g_m and value||e||^2/2. Denote its energy V_b<0.\n\nConstruct a scalar base Huber run with gradient clip(z,-r/C,1), initial\npoint D. During b it has gradient1 and ends at1; the bridge lands at-r;\nduring c_block its gradient is v_base=-r/C and it ends atv_base.\nAll incoming optimum slacks are zero and the final point equals its\ngradient. Squared-distance telescoping therefore gives\n\n  V_base=D^2-T_join^2(r/C)^2=0                             (12)\n\nby(11).\n\nFor an auxiliary vector array, retain ALL supplied core-ended b samples.\nThe new bridge lands at-r e. During c_block use constant gradient\n\n  v_aux=-(r/C)e.\n\nIts starting point is C v_aux, and its terminal point is v_aux.\nAssign each new value <v_aux,x>-||v_aux||^2/2. This array is not presumed\nlegal across the join; the following audit supplies that legality after\nmixing.\n\nFor epsilon>0, combine base and auxiliary points and gradients in\northogonal coordinates with auxiliary scale sqrt(epsilon), and combine\ntheir values with scale epsilon. All GD equations are exact.\n\nEvery active base interpolation constraint has nonpositive auxiliary\nresidual:\n\n* Old-old constraints: the auxiliary data are the original legal b run.\n* New-new constraints: constant auxiliary gradient makes them zero.\n* New-to-old constraints: the base residual can be zero only at an old\n  target whose remaining b sum is0. That target is the old core endpoint,\n  including any zero-step duplicates. At this target, direct expansion\n  gives Q_aux(new,m)=0.\n* Old-to-new constraints: the base residual can be zero only at a new\n  target whose remaining c_block sum is0. That target has x=g=v_aux,\n  so Q_aux(old,new)=Q_aux(old,star)<=0.\n* Incoming optimum constraints: old ones are legal; new ones are0.\n  Outgoing optimum constraints: old ones are legal; new ones are\n  minus the remaining c_block sum times||v_aux||^2, hence nonpositive.\n\nFor every auxiliary pair with Q_aux>0 the corresponding base residual\nis therefore strictly negative. The finite choice\n\n  epsilon=min(1, min_(Q_aux>0) [-Q_base/(2Q_aux)])\n\nis positive, interpreting the inner minimum as1 when there are no such\npairs. The combined data obey all interpolation constraints, including\nboth optimum directions and every original zero-step index. The\nconstruction in fact1252b8823bb5a290 realizes one fixed legal function.\n\nFor any core-ended legal trajectory of capacityK, distance telescoping\ngives\n\n  V=||x_0||^2-K^2||g_end||^2-2sum h_i I_i_star.              (13)\n\nThe auxiliary full trajectory is core-ended, adds no new incoming\noptimum slack, and its bridge query was already a core endpoint.\nEquation(11) gives T_join^2||v_aux||^2=D^2||e||^2.\nUsing(13) for the old b run and the full auxiliary array yields\n\n  V_aux=V_b.\n\nTogether with(12), the fully legal combined trajectory has energy\nepsilon V_b<0. This proves the stated contrapositive.\n\n## 8. Scope and the next unmatched condition\n\nThe missing existence of SOME core-ended negative witness is settled\nby this candidate construction; the old preserved-data reset criterion\nremains correct but was not a universal obstruction after dimensional\npadding. The saturated-append converse now covers arbitrary primitive\nfailures in its prefix.\n\nThis does not establish a corresponding arbitrary-tail converse for a\nprepended saturated bridge. Its complete value/optimum-slack penalty\nremains, and(9) identifies the retained quantity exactly. Neither the\noriginal normalized objective ratio nor the unrestricted asymptotic\nZhang-Jiang target follows from this primitive-energy reduction.\n\nNo new statement here is in the fact graph. {"id": "3d2994746c1d983e", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T14:20:25.764576+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C21 removes the remaining relative-phase restriction in the two-ray scalar sector obstruction. On Hsc_m, every fixed scalar convex1-smooth function whose queried secants on EACH ray fit its own factor13/5 band has normalized objective O((13/5)^(-m)), uniformly over arbitrarily different ray scales, all phases, nonperiodic profiles, and horizon-dependent knots. The exact-profile ratio tends to zero. This is not an all-scalar bound.", "evidence": "# All relative phases of two scalar secant bands\n\nStatus: complete candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. No verifier request\nis allocated by the current TASK or master guidance. Every new estimate used\nin this document is proved here. Earlier C18/C19 findings are motivation,\nnot premises. The only accepted fact cited is b98bb807d514e80d for the exact\nbenchmark definitions. No external theorem or numerical search is used.\n\n## 1. Statement\n\nSet c_sc=13/5 and a_sc=c_sc^(-17). Define\n\n    Hsc_0=(), Hsc_(j+1)=(Hsc_j,bsc_j,Hsc_j),\n    bsc_j=a_sc c_sc^j, Nsc_j=2^j-1,\n    ssc_j=sum Hsc_j=a_sc(c_sc^j-2^j)/(c_sc-2).\n\nLet f be a fixed differentiable convex function on the real line with\n1-Lipschitz derivative g, f(0)=0=min f. On the two rays choose arbitrary\nconstants M_slow,M_fast with 0<M_slow<=M_fast<=1. Either physical ray can\nbe called slow. Suppose each nonzero queried point on ray sigma satisfies\n\n    M_sigma/c_sc <= |g(x_t)|/|x_t| <= M_sigma.             (1)\n\nThe constants, derivative, and initial point can depend on the horizon m.\nNo relation between the two scales is imposed. The derivative may be\nnonperiodic and asymmetric, with any number of knots or repeated visits.\nOnly the finitely many queried secants are constrained by (1).\n\nPut K_sc=192*27^4 and C_sc=200000*c_sc^3*K_sc^2/a_sc. Then GD under the\ncomplete word Hsc_m, starting from any x_0!=0, satisfies\n\n    2 f(x_(Nsc_m))/x_0^2 <= C_sc c_sc^(-m).              (2)\n\nIn particular the supremum over this entire class, divided by the exact\nU_(Nsc_m)^ZJ, tends to zero. Each ray's secants must fit in its own band of\nwidth factor13/5, but the two bands can have arbitrary relative scale and\nphase. This closes the phase restriction in C19. It is still a restricted\nscalar class, not all scalar functions and not unrestricted W.\n\nWithin the proof abbreviate c_sc,a_sc,bsc_j,ssc_j by c,a,b_j,S_j. These\nare local aliases, unrelated to the Huber slopes c,a in the project glossary.\nAll matrix products below use maximum and multiplication, not addition.\n\n## 2. Exact two-state upper envelope\n\nFor ray sigma put\n\n    d_sigma(j)=(1-b_j M_sigma/c)_+,\n    u_sigma(j)=(b_j M_sigma-1)_+,\n    B_j=[[d_slow(j),u_fast(j)],[u_slow(j),d_fast(j)]].\n\nRows denote the output ray, columns the input ray. A scalar step with\nqueried secant lambda on ray sigma has multiplier1-b_j lambda. Its\nsame-ray magnitude is at most d_sigma(j), and its opposite-ray magnitude\nat most u_sigma(j). A zero coefficient excludes that nonzero transition.\n\nDefine (A odot B)_(i,j)=max_k A_(i,k)B_(k,j), and let E_0 be the identity\nmatrix. The exact recurrence for the relaxed sign-path envelope is\n\n    E_(j+1)=E_j odot B_j odot E_j.                       (3)\n\nInduction by the two intermediate signs in the word decomposition shows\nthat E_j(output,input) bounds every actual radius ratio. This proves an\nupper bound even though independently maximizing its entries need not\nproduce one legal function. No such realization is asserted.\n\nLet I be the first integer with b_I M_fast>1. Write\n\n    t=b_I M_fast, s=b_I M_slow.\n\nThen I>=18, 1<t<=c, and 0<s<=t. Every checkpoint before I has upper\nsecant product at most1, so E_I is diagonal. Its entries are\n\n    p_s=p(s), p_f=p(t),\n    p(z)=product_(h in Hsc_I)(1-h z/(c b_I)).             (4)\n\nAll these factors are positive. With eta=13/8, the sum formula gives\nS_I/b_I>=eta: (2/c)^3<1/2 and (2/c)^2<4/5 imply (2/c)^17<1/40.\nConsequently\n\n    p(t)/p(s) <= exp[-5(t-s)/8].                         (5)\n\nThis follows termwise from log(1-u)-log(1-v)<=-(u-v) for 0<=v<=u<1.\nWe shall also use, for 0<z<=c,\n\n    p(z) <= exp[-5z/8-z^2/70],  p(z)>1/27.             (6)\n\nFor the first inequality, log(1-v)<=-v-v^2/2 and the level counts give\n\n    sum_(h in Hsc_I) h^2/b_I^2\n       =[1-(2/c^2)^I]/(c^2-2).\n\nSince 2/c^2<1/3 and I>=18, the coefficient of z^2 in the logarithm is\nlarger than (26/27)/(2c^2(c^2-2))>1/70. For the lower bound, every\nv=h z/(c b_I) is at most1/c, and their sum is at most5/3. Integration\ngives log(1-v)>=-v/(1-1/c), so log p(z)>=-65/24>-3. The elementary\nfactorial-series bound e<3 gives p(z)>1/27.\n\n## 3. A common-band product estimate proved from scratch\n\nFor M in (0,1] set\n\n    q_sec(z)=max(1-z/c,z-1),\n    P_sec(m,M)=product_(j=0)^(m-1)\n                     q_sec(a M c^j)^(2^(m-j-1)).\n\nThe empty product is1. We prove\n\n    M P_sec(m,M)^2 <=(60000/a)c^(-m).                    (7)\n\nLet k be the largest integer with y=a M c^k<=13/9. Then k>=17 and\n5/9<y<=13/9. The first branch of q_sec holds through k; its second\nbranch holds afterwards. For m>=k+1, log(1-v)<=-v on levels below k\ngives\n\n    log P_sec(m,M)/2^(m-k-1)\n      <=-5y/4+log(1-y/c)\n          +sum_(ell=1)^(m-k-1)2^(-ell)log(c^ell y-1).    (8)\n\nFor m=k+1 this is at most-25/36. Otherwise keep ell=1 exactly, extend\nthe positive terms ell>=2 to infinity, and replace their logarithms by\nell log c+log y. The result is\n\n    E(y)=-5y/4+(1/2)log[c y(c-y)^2(cy-1)].               (9)\n\nOn the indicated interval E''(y)<-1/2. Indeed its three negative terms\nare -1/(c-y)^2, -1/(2y^2), and -c^2/(2(cy-1)^2); the last two alone\nhave total magnitude greater than2/9+c^2/18>1/2. At y=4/5,\nE'=5/216 and c y(c-y)^2(cy-1)=113724/15625<(27/10)^2. Since\ne>163/60, E(4/5)<log(162/163)<=-1/163. The tangent parabola therefore\ngives E(y)<=-1/163+25/46656<-1/200. Thus\n\n    P_sec(m,M)<=exp[-2^(m-k-1)/200]  for m>=k+1.         (10)\n\nFor m<=k every factor is at most1 and M<=(13/(9a))c^(-m). For\nm=k+J, J>=1, equation(10) bounds M P_sec^2 by\n(13/(9a))c^(-m)c^J exp(-2^J/200). As c<4 and z^2 exp(-z)<=1 for\nz>=0, this is at most (520000/(9a))c^(-m), proving(7).\n\n## 4. Separated activation: s<=1/c\n\nThis case is included to keep the statement independent of unverified C19.\nAt level I write\n\n    A=p_s^2(1-s/c), D=p_s p_f(t-1), F=p_f^2(1-t/c).\n\nThen E_(I+1)=[[A,D],[0,F]]. At I+1 a fast input must cross and a slow\ninput cannot cross. Hence\n\n    E_(I+2)=[[A^2(1-s), A max(D(1-s),F(ct-1))],[0,0]].\n\nIts right/left nonzero-entry ratio is at most max(R_1,R_2), where\n\n    R_1=(t-1)/(1-s/c) *exp[-5(t-s)/8],\n    R_2=(1-t/c)(ct-1)/[(1-s/c)(1-s)] *exp[-5(t-s)/4].\n\nBoth increase with s. R_1 increases with t up to c; R_2 decreases with\nt from1, since its logarithmic derivative is\n-1/(c-t)+c/(ct-1)-5/4, is decreasing, and equals-1/4 at t=1.\nThus R_1<=(169/90)exp(-18/13)<1 and\nR_2<=(169/90)exp(-10/13)<1. The latter inequalities follow respectively\nfrom exp(18/13)>31/13>169/90 and\nexp(10/13)>349/169>169/90.\n\nIf E_j=[[A_j,R A_j],[0,0]] with R<=1, recurrence(3) preserves R and\n\n    A_(j+1)=A_j^2 max(d_slow(j),R u_slow(j))\n           <=A_j^2 q_sec(b_j M_slow).\n\nIts initial A_(I+2) is P_sec(I+2,M_slow). Every later nonzero endpoint\nis therefore on the slow ray, with radius at most P_sec(m,M_slow)|x_0|.\nEquation(7) supplies the required bound in this case.\n\n## 5. Overlapping activation: one off-diagonal entry controls all levels\n\nNow assume 1/c<s<=t<=c. At level I use all four entries\n\n    E_(I+1)=[[A,D],[C,F]],\n    A=p_s^2(1-s/c), D=p_s p_f(t-1),\n    C=p_s p_f(s-1)_+, F=p_f^2(1-t/c).\n\nAt I+1 put d=(1-s)_+, u_s=cs-1>0, u_f=ct-1>0. Thus\nB_(I+1)=[[d,u_f],[u_s,0]]. The two relations\n\n    C d=0,  C u_f<=D u_s                              (11)\n\nhold: for s<=1 the first entry C vanishes, while for s>1 the second\ninequality reduces to (t-1)/(s-1)>=(ct-1)/(cs-1).\n\nSet L=max(A d,D u_s). Direct multiplication in(3) gives new entries\n\n    A_0=A L,                F_0=F D u_s,\n    D_0=max(A D d,D^2 u_s,A F u_f),\n    C_0=max(A F u_s,C^2 u_f),\n    E_(I+2)=[[A_0,D_0],[C_0,F_0]].                     (12)\n\nThese entries satisfy, without any division by a potentially zero entry,\n\n    A_0 F_0<=C_0 D_0,  C_0 u_f<=D_0 u_s.             (13)\n\nFor the first inequality use D_0>=D L and C_0>=A F u_s. For the\nsecond, the two possibilities in C_0 are bounded by D_0 u_s/u_f:\nD_0>=A F u_f treats the first, and D_0>=D^2 u_s together with(11)\ntreats the second.\n\nFor every j>=I+2 both rays must cross at checkpoint j, even at the\nsmallest permitted secants. Write u_s(j)=b_j M_slow-1 and\nu_f(j)=b_j M_fast-1. Their ratio u_f(j)/u_s(j) decreases with j.\nSuppose E_j=[[A_j,D_j],[C_j,F_j]] satisfies\n\n    A_j F_j<=C_j D_j, C_j u_f(j)<=D_j u_s(j), D_j>0.\n\nMultiplication by the pure crossing B_j yields exactly\n\n    D_(j+1)=D_j^2 u_s(j),\n    A_(j+1)=A_j D_j u_s(j),\n    F_(j+1)=F_j D_j u_s(j),\n    C_(j+1)=max(A_j F_j u_s(j),C_j^2 u_f(j)).           (14)\n\nThe first product inequality persists because the term A_j F_j u_s(j)\noccurs in C_(j+1). The ratios A_j/D_j and F_j/D_j are constant, while\nC_(j+1)/D_(j+1)<=C_j/D_j. Together with decreasing u_f/u_s, this proves\nthe second product inequality at the next level. Equation(13) starts\nthe induction. The full two-state envelope is therefore controlled by\nthe scalar recurrence for D_j in(14).\n\nFor clarity its starting value in(12) is the maximum of three terms:\n\n    T_1=p_s^2 p_f^2 (t-1)^2(cs-1),\n    T_2=p_s^3 p_f (1-s/c)(t-1)(1-s)_+,\n    T_3=p_s^2 p_f^2 (1-s/c)(1-t/c)(ct-1).              (15)\n\nThe positivity D_0>0 and a uniform ratio bound follow as follows. Every\nentry in(12) is less than16. If t<=2, T_3>=(1/27^4)(72/845)>\n1/(12*27^4). If t>=2 and s<=3/4, T_2>=(1/27^4)(37/208) exceeds that\nsame bound. If t>=2 and s>=3/4, T_1>=(1/27^4)(19/20) does so. Thus\n\n    max(A_0,D_0,C_0,F_0)/D_0 <= K_sc=192*27^4.         (16)\n\nBy(14) this ratio bound holds at every later level.\n\n## 6. The off-diagonal recurrence decays uniformly\n\nWe prove, writing J=m-I-2>=0,\n\n    D_m <= exp[-2^J/400].                             (17)\n\nHere D_(I+2)=D_0 in the notation of(12). To avoid ambiguity, recurrence(14)\npropagates each of the three starting terms in(15) independently; their\nmaximum is the propagated D_0 because positive powers commute with max.\n\nThe future factors are c^ell s-1 with ell=2,3,..., and are all greater\nthan1. For any starting term T, their complete infinite logarithmic\nbudget, divided by 2^J, is bounded above by\n\n    log T + log(c^2)+(1/2)log[s(c^2 s-1)].              (18)\n\nTo derive(18), index future factors by k=1,2,..., so they equal\nc^(k+1)s-1. Keep the first correction log(1-1/(c^2 s)) exactly with\nweight1/2 and drop the later negative corrections. The sums of 2^(-k)\nand k2^(-k) are1 and2, giving(18). Extending a finite tail is valid\nbecause all added original logarithms are positive.\n\n### Case 1/c<s<=1\n\nIn this range p_s contains exactly the low factors of P_sec(I+2,M_slow),\nwhose base value is\n\n    p_s^4 (1-s/c)^2 q_sec(cs).\n\nFrom(5),\n\n    p_f(t-1)/[p_s(1-s/c)] <= c/e<1.                    (19)\n\nIndeed the logarithmic expression increases with s and t on s<=1,\nt<=c and equals c/e at s=1,t=c. Thus T_1 and T_2 are at most this\nbase value. At all future levels q_sec(c^ell s)=c^ell s-1. The index k\nin(10) is either I or I+1, so their propagated values are at most\nexp[-2^J/400].\n\nFor T_3 use p_s^2 p_f^2<=exp[-5(s+t)/4]. For fixed s the logarithm\nof exp(-5t/4)(1-t/c)(ct-1) decreases for t>=1. Replacing t by1 in\n(18) therefore gives the upper bound\n\n    F_low(s)=-5(s+1)/4+log[(c-s)(c-1)^2]\n                         +(1/2)log[s(c^2 s-1)].        (20)\n\nOn [1/c,1], F_low''<-1: the last two negative curvature terms alone\nhave magnitudes greater than1/2+1/2. At s=2/3,\n\n    F_low'(2/3)=-407/7627,\n    (c-s)^2(c-1)^4 s(c^2s-1)\n       <(163/60)^4(13/12)<exp(49/12).\n\nThe last upper comparison uses e>163/60 and exp(1/12)>13/12. Thus\nF_low(2/3)<-1/24. Since (407/7627)^2/2<1/600, its tangent parabola\nproves F_low(s)<-1/25 throughout the interval. This is stronger than\nthe exponent required by(17).\n\n### Case 1<=s<=t<=c\n\nT_2=0. First, the function p(t)^2(1-t/c)(ct-1) is decreasing for\nt>=1. The derivative of 2log p is at most-5/4, and the remaining\nlogarithmic derivative is -1/(c-t)+c/(ct-1). Therefore\n\n    T_3<=p_s^4(1-s/c)^2(cs-1)\n         <=P_sec(I+2,M_slow).\n\nThe index k in(10) is I-1 or I, so the same propagation proves(17)\nfor this term.\n\nFor T_1 use the quadratic improvement in(6). Its bound in(18) is\n\n    F_high(s,t)=-5(s+t)/4-(s^2+t^2)/35+2log(t-1)\n                  +log(cs-1)+log(c^2)\n                  +(1/2)log[s(c^2s-1)].               (21)\n\nThe part depending on t has second derivative at most-25/32. Its\nderivative at t=c is-26/175. Completing the tangent parabola gives\n\n    F_high(s,t)<=F_high(s,c)+1/60.                     (22)\n\nThe function s->F_high(s,c) has second derivative less than-2/5.\nFor example the four positive magnitudes have sum bounded below by\n\n    2/35+c^2/(c^2-1)^2+1/(2c^2)\n                              +c^4/(2(c^3-1)^2)>2/5.\n\nAt s=7/4 its derivative is -213097/10765020, with magnitude less\nthan1/40. The argument of its combined logarithm is\n\n    (c-1)^2 c^2 (cs-1)*sqrt[s(c^2s-1)] <268<exp(28/5).\n\nThe first inequality follows on squaring rational quantities. The\nsecond follows from\n(163/60)^5[1+3/5+(3/5)^2/2+(3/5)^3/6]>268.\nIts tangent-parabola correction is less than1/1000. Hence(22) is\nstrictly less than\n\n    28/5-87/16-3929/14000+1/60+1/1000\n       =-211/2100<-1/10.\n\nThis proves(17) for T_1 and completes all cases. Every displayed\nrational comparison is a finite arithmetic inequality; none asserts\na numerical scan or an optimized trajectory.\n\n## 7. Radius, complete objective area, and early horizons\n\nIn the overlapping case equations(16)--(17) give\n\n    |x_(Nsc_m)|<=K_sc exp[-2^(m-I-2)/400]|x_0|,\n                                                    m>=I+2. (23)\n\nFirst suppose the bands hold everywhere on their rays. Then integration\nfrom the minimizer gives f(x)<=M_sigma x^2/2. In(23) use M_sigma<=M_fast\nand M_fast=t/(a c^I)<=c/(a c^I). With J=m-I-2, the normalized error is\nat most\n\n    (c^3 K_sc^2/a)c^(-m)c^J exp(-2^J/200)\n       <=(40000 c^3 K_sc^2/a)c^(-m).                   (24)\n\nFor m<=I the signs cannot change, all envelope factors are at most1,\nand M_fast<=(c/a)c^(-m). For m=I+1 every entry of E_(I+1) is at most\nc-1, giving the bound c^2(c-1)^2 c^(-m)/a. These are smaller than(24).\nThe separated case was bounded in section4 by(7), including its own\ntwo early ranges by the same estimates. Thus the global-band assertion\nholds at every horizon.\n\nFor the stated queried-only hypotheses take m>=1. The final step of\nHsc_m is a<1/2. Smoothness and g(0)=0 ensure it preserves any nonzero\nray. Write x_pre for the last query and x_end for the terminal point.\nIf a query or iterate already has zero gradient, the remaining objective\nis zero. Otherwise monotonicity and(1) give, on its ray sigma,\n\n    |g(x_end)|<=|g(x_pre)|<=M_sigma |x_pre|\n                    <=M_sigma |x_end|/(1-a).\n\nThe entire area between0 and x_end obeys\n\n    f(x_end)=integral_0^|x_end| |g(sigma r)| dr\n                <=|x_end| |g(x_end)|.\n\nThus the preceding endpoint estimate with M_sigma x_end^2 is enlarged\nby at most2/(1-a)<4. Formula(2), with the chosen C_sc, follows. This\nretains all function-value area, even if the derivative is arbitrary\nbetween or outside the queries. At m=0, 1-smoothness directly gives\n2f(x_0)<=x_0^2, also smaller than(2).\n\n## 8. Exact benchmark comparison and scope\n\nThe accepted fact b98bb807d514e80d defines the exact benchmark. Write\nC_n=S_circle(n)+1 and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1. Its shifted recursions are\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) Gamma(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),\n    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n    Gamma(A,B)=2A+B/2+sqrt(B^2+8AB)/2.\n\nHere is an elementary stronger growth estimate, proved rather than\nimported from an unverified finding. Homogeneity and the defining\nquadratic of Omega show that\nOmega(A,B)<=(A^(3/4)+B^(3/4))^(4/3) is equivalent to\nt^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1 on[0,1]. Put u=t^(1/3),\nv=(1-t)^(1/3). The difference from1 equals\n\n    u^3 v^3[1/(1+u+u^2)+1/(1+v+v^2)-uv]>=0,\n\nbecause each reciprocal is at least1/3 and uv<=2^(-2/3)<2/3.\nInduction gives C_n<=(n+1)^(4/3). Also Gamma(A,B)<=B+4A by squaring\nthe square-root comparison, and induction using\nu^(4/3)+v^(4/3)<=(u+v)^(4/3) gives B_n<=1+4n^(4/3).\n\nConsequently(2), divided by U_(Nsc_m)^ZJ=1/B_(Nsc_m), is at most\n\n    C_sc c_sc^(-m)[1+4(2^m-1)^(4/3)] ->0,\n\nsince c_sc^3=2197/125>16. All constants are uniform over the function,\nthe two secant scales, their relative phase, and horizon-dependent knots.\n\nThe newly closed gap is arbitrary relative phase of two bounded secant\nbands. The proof makes no claim when a single ray's queried secants\nspan more than13/5. The independent factor-four relaxation already\nhas divergent illegal trajectories; dropping (1) requires new fixed-profile\ninformation, not merely rerunning the matrix recursion. No fact graph\nnode has been created by this document.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "workers/max3/research/c21_all_ray_sector_phases.md", "fact_ids": ["b98bb807d514e80d"], "finding_ids": ["59b568f9b03563db", "3423f78cc0ca8c92", "4f9c9ce521523bb9", "75a0fbef6b918dff"], "status": "complete self-contained candidate; NOT verifier-accepted; no fact_submit allocation", "new_mechanism": "After two activation levels E has AF<=CD and C*u_fast<=D*u_slow; one off-diagonal entry follows D_next=D^2*u_slow exactly, with uniform negative logarithmic product bounds.", "scope": "All relative scales and phases of two per-ray queried secant bands of width13/5; not full scalar class.", "is_fragile": false, "fragility_reason": "", "suggested_followup": "none", "justification_type": "calculation", "confidence": 0.95}, "glossary": {"c_sc": "13/5, the ruler growth constant in this finding.", "a_sc": "c_sc^(-17), the smallest step of the scalar ruler word.", "Hsc_j": "Recursive scalar schedule Hsc_0=(), Hsc_(j+1)=(Hsc_j,bsc_j,Hsc_j).", "bsc_j": "a_sc c_sc^j, the level-j checkpoint step.", "Nsc_j": "2^j-1, the number of GD steps in Hsc_j.", "ssc_j": "Sum of the steps in Hsc_j.", "M_slow": "Smaller of the two positive upper secant bounds on the physical rays.", "M_fast": "Larger of the two positive upper secant bounds on the physical rays.", "K_sc": "192*27^4, a uniform bound on envelope-entry ratios.", "C_sc": "200000*c_sc^3*K_sc^2/a_sc, an explicit uniform objective constant.", "q_sec": "Scalar one-step envelope max(1-z/c_sc,z-1).", "P_sec": "Weighted product of q_sec over all levels of the ruler word.", "E_j": "In this finding only, the two-sign maximum-product envelope; all entries and operations are defined explicitly in the evidence.", "F_low": "One-variable logarithmic product upper bound in equation20.", "F_high": "Two-variable logarithmic product upper bound in equation21."}}
{"id": "9c3632026c522612", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T14:25:51.606972+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Exact one-cell closure for arbitrary POSITIVE-VALUE affine-envelope prefixes: projecting a final constant gradient into the old geometric span preserves every queried jet and improves normalized payoff by exactly (2S+1)||v_perp||^2/R^2. On the reversed boundary ray, including ALL affine offsets and orthogonal innovations, the global optimum reduces to an explicit intersection of quadratic-root intervals plus finitely many concave-quadratic vertices. This retains actual G and closest-minimizer distance R. Consequently greedy immediate-cell maximization never creates fresh dimensions; their benefit must repay the displayed cost in later cells.", "evidence": "# Exact terminal affine-cell policy and the cost of a new direction\n\nStatus: self-contained UNVERIFIED candidate. No fact submission or numerical\nsolver. This concerns an explicitly described one-cell continuation of a\nfixed prefix, not all smooth-convex extensions or the uniform ZJ target.\n\n## 1. General positive-value prefix\n\nLet\n\n    J_old(z)=max_{j in P} (eta_j+<a_j,z>),\n\nwhere P is a finite index set containing the zero plane (eta_*=0,a_*=0).\nSuppose J_old(0)=0 and 0 is a closest zero to the initial point x_init.\nWrite R=||x_init||>0. Thus every eta_j<=0. Let f_old=env_1 J_old.\nFor a prescribed finite GD prefix retain EVERY queried jet (x_k,g_k,f_k),\nits proximal point z_k=x_k-g_k, and height H_k=J_old(z_k).\nThe prefix may have arbitrary positive heights, arbitrary affine offsets,\nnon-Markov gradients, and arbitrary prescribed nonnegative steps.\n\nAfter a chosen checkpoint applied to the last retained query, let X be\nthe resulting next position. Append any nonnegative ordinary list of sum\nS, and put C=S+1>=1 and d=2C-1. We seek a single new affine plane\n\n    ell(z)=a+<p,z>, a<=0,\n    J=max(J_old,ell), f=env_1 J,\n\nwhose gradient p is used throughout the whole new ordinary block,\nincluding its final sample. The data before X are kept exactly.\n\nThe prefix is preserved exactly when\n\n    a+<p,z_k><=H_k                              for every old query k. (1)\n\nThe proposed new queries after partial mass u in [0,S] have position\nX-u p and proximal point X-(u+1)p. The new plane dominates old plane j\nat such a point exactly when\n\n    (u+1)||p||^2-<p,X+(u+1)a_j>\n                    +eta_j+<a_j,X>-a <=0.                          (2)\n\nThis expression is affine in u. Hence it suffices, and is necessary,\nto check (2) at u=0 and u=S; both are actual endpoint samples. This\nalso checks the zero plane because it is included in P. An active new\nplane supplies the required subgradient, so the proximal optimality\ncondition gives the same p at every supplied intermediate query. All\nsteps of the given ordinary list therefore occur in their actual order.\n\nThe envelope is convex, differentiable and 1-smooth: its proximal point\nis the unique minimizer of J(z)+||x-z||^2/2; the subgradient monotonicity\ninequality gives ||p(x)-p(y)||^2<=<p(x)-p(y),x-y>, where p(x)=x-z(x).\nComparing the two minimizing expressions yields derivative p(x).\nThus this construction is one fixed smooth-convex function.\n\nThe minimum and normalization remain exact. J>=0 and J(0)=0 since\na<=0. Its zero set is a subset of the old zero set and still contains\n0, so 0 remains closest to x_init at distance R. The envelope has the\nsame zero set as J, since both nonnegative terms in its minimizing\nexpression must vanish at a zero. At the final query, the exact score is\n\n    2 f_end/R^2 = [2a+2<p,X>-d||p||^2]/R^2.                         (3)\n\n## 2. Orthogonal innovations have an exact immediate price\n\nLet V be the linear span of X, all z_k, all old normals a_j, and x_init.\nDecompose p=p_parallel+p_perp orthogonally with p_parallel in V.\nReplace the new plane by a+<p_parallel,z>.\n\nEvery old-query test (1) is unchanged. In each new-query test (2), the\nlinear terms are unchanged while the squared norm decreases by\n||p_perp||^2. Thus every test remains valid. The same a<=0 still retains\nthe closest minimizer. Equation (3) shows the EXACT increase in score:\n\n    score_projected-score_original=d||p_perp||^2/R^2.                (4)\n\nThis is strict when p_perp is nonzero. It is valid for the whole new\nconstant-gradient block, not just an unqueried terminal sample. No old\nposition, gradient, value, or affine plane has been discarded.\n\nConsequently no maximizer of this immediate one-cell objective has a\ncomponent in a fresh direction orthogonal to the complete old geometry.\nFor example, when old data are Markov and p=-beta g+v_perp with\n||v_perp||^2=||g||^2 beta(A/T-beta), the immediate penalty is exactly\n\n    d||g||^2 beta(A/T-beta)/R^2.                                    (5)\n\nCreating new directions may still be useful for later blocks. Formula\n(4) says that an immediate-cell maximization policy never chooses them;\nit does not say that their globally optimal use is impossible. Starting\nfrom a one-dimensional prefix and repeatedly maximizing only this\nimmediate objective therefore cannot create a new dimension.\n\n## 3. Explicit exact optimization on the reversed boundary ray\n\nLet g be the nonzero gradient at the retained checkpoint query, set\nG2=||g||^2, and suppose\n\n    A=-<g,X>/G2>0.\n\nAllow all new planes of the form\n\n    ell(z)=G2 theta+<-beta g+v_perp,z>,\n    beta>=0, theta<=0,\n\nwhere v_perp is orthogonal to V. By (4), optimizing this entire family\nreduces exactly to v_perp=0. No Markov or zero-height prefix assumption\nis made. Define the retained dimensionless numbers\n\n    u_k=H_k/G2, v_k=<g,z_k>/G2                    for old queries k,\n    u_*=v_*=0                                  for an extra index *,\n    r_j=(eta_j+<a_j,X>)/G2,\n    c_j=<a_j,g>/G2                              for old planes j.\n\nThe extra * among the query bounds imposes theta<=0; it is separate\nfrom the zero plane already included among the old planes. Conditions\n(1)-(2) become exactly\n\n    theta<=u_k+v_k beta                           for every k,*;\n    theta>=t beta^2+(t c_j-A)beta+r_j              for every j,\n                                                     t in {1,C}.  (6)\n\nSet U_aff(beta)=min_k(u_k+v_k beta). Feasibility is equivalent to all\nthe explicit convex quadratic inequalities\n\n    t beta^2+(t c_j-A-v_k)beta+(r_j-u_k)<=0,\n                            j in P, t in {1,C}, k in queries union{*}. (7)\n\nEach has a positive leading coefficient. If its discriminant is\nnegative the family is infeasible. Otherwise it gives the closed\ninterval between its two real roots. Intersect those finitely many\nroot intervals with beta>=0. This is the exact feasible interval I,\npossibly empty. The pair zero-plane/* with t=C also gives\n\n    0<=beta<=A/C,                                                   (8)\n\nso every nonempty I is compact. These formulas include equality and\nzero-step degeneracies. If beta=0 is feasible, (6) forces theta=0\nthrough the zero plane and theta<=0, and the terminal block is already\nat a minimizer. Positive score requires beta>0.\n\nFor beta in I, the largest allowed theta is U_aff(beta), and the exact\nbest score at that beta is\n\n    V(beta)=(G2/R^2)[2U_aff(beta)+2A beta-d beta^2].                  (9)\n\nThis is a strictly concave piecewise quadratic function on I. It has an\nexplicit finite maximization rule. For each query bound k, let\n\n    I_k=I intersect {beta: u_k+v_k beta<=u_l+v_l beta for every l}.\n\nThe set I_k is a closed interval, possibly empty, determined by affine\nhalf-line intersections. On a nonempty I_k, clamp (A+v_k)/d to I_k and\ncall the result beta_k. Compare the finitely many values V(beta_k).\nAny largest one is the global optimum; set theta_opt=U_aff(beta_opt).\nEvery optimizing parameter, scale, intercept, and normalized value is\ntherefore explicitly determined by retained finite data and quadratic\nroots. No continuous optimizer or presumed feasible parameter appears.\n\nThe frozen prefix can have positive internal values and nonzero offsets.\nIn particular this rule does not replace them by zero, and it retains\nboth the true boundary magnitude G2 and the actual distance R.\n\n## 4.
