A nonzero affine offset improves a frozen multidimensional prefix\n\nThis quantitative example distinguishes the affine rule from merely\nchoosing a positive homogeneous terminal level. For any M>1 prescribe\nthree arbitrary nonnegative ordinary blocks, each of sum M-1, with\ncheckpoints 4M+1 and 2M+1. Work in R^2 with x_init=(1,0), and set\n\n    p=(1/M,0), q=(-2/M,2/M), J_old(z)=max(0,<p,z>,<q,z>).\n\nOn the first block use gradient p. Its positions run from (1,0) to p;\nall its proximal points are d p, 0<=d<=M-1. The q plane is negative\nthere. The checkpoint 4M+1 leads to X_1=(-4,0). Put\n\n    Z_1=X_1-Mq=(-2,-2).\n\nOn the second block use gradient q. Its proximal points are Z_1+d q,\n0<=d<=M-1. The q plane has value d||q||^2, while the p plane has\nvalue -2/M-2d/M^2<=0. Thus both supplied prefix blocks and their\ncheckpoint queries are legal. The second checkpoint 2M+1 leads to\n\n    X=X_1-3Mq=(2,-6), G2=8/M^2, A=2M, C=M.\n\nOrigin is closest because the retained p plane forces the first\ncoordinate of every old zero to be nonpositive. Thus R=1.\n\nFor the new plane G2 theta+<-beta q,z>, its exact old-query upper\nbound is\n\n    theta<=min(0, k(1-2beta)), k=(M-1)/8.                           (10)\n\nIndeed on the first block the normalized inequality is\ntheta<=(1-2beta)d/8 for 0<=d<=M-1. On the second it is\ntheta<=d(1+beta), whose minimum is zero. The zero endpoints are\nactual retained queries.\n\nFor beta>=1/2 the unconstrained optimizer of (9) with (10) is\n\n    beta_aff=(7M+1)/[4(2M-1)],\n    theta_aff=(M-1)(1-2beta_aff)/8<0.                               (11)\n\nHere is the complete new-plane feasibility check, not just an objective\nstationarity calculation. Put d=2M-1>1, so\n\n    beta_aff=(7d+9)/(8d),\n    theta_aff=-3(d-1)(d+3)/(64d),\n    7/8<beta_aff<2.\n\nAmong the terminal checks for the zero plane, the larger t=C is the\nonly stronger one. Its lower bound for theta is\n\n    L_0=C beta_aff(beta_aff-2).\n\nThe exact slack is\n\n    theta_aff-L_0\n       =(d-1)(57d^2+126d+81)/(128d^2)>0.                            (12)\n\nFor old plane q, the strongest lower bound is\nL_q=C(beta_aff+1)(beta_aff-2)<=L_0. For old plane p it is\n\n    L_p=C(beta_aff^2-9beta_aff/4+1/4),\n\nbecause beta_aff>1/4 makes the t=C bound stronger than t=1. Its slack\nis\n\n    theta_aff-L_p=(55d^3+85d^2-27d-81)/(128d^2)>0.                  (13)\n\nThe numerator is at least 28d+4 for d>=1, using d^3>=d and d^2>=1.\nThus every old plane, the zero plane, and all past queries are checked.\nThe chosen beta maximizes the full piecewise quadratic upper bound:\nbelow 1/2 that bound is increasing, and above 1/2 its vertex is (11).\nIts feasibility proves global optimality within this affine ray family.\n\nThe exact normalized score is\n\n    V_aff=(57M^2+2M+5)/[2M^2(2M-1)].                              (14)\n\nFor a homogeneous new plane theta=0, (10) forces beta<=1/2. The\nobjective 2A beta-(2C-1)beta^2 increases on that interval. Beta=1/2\nis feasible by the same checks, so the homogeneous optimum is\n\n    V_hom=12/M+2/M^2.\n\nThe exact gain from retaining the affine-value freedom is\n\n    V_aff-V_hom=9(M+1)^2/[2M^2(2M-1)]>0.                           (15)\n\nNo new direction is used at the terminal cell; the earlier two-\ndimensional prefix is preserved. This example illustrates the general\noptimizer and does not supply a new all-schedule or asymptotic result.\n\n## 5. What remains for iteration\n\nEquations (1)-(3) apply to any proposed internal affine cell too, but\nmaximizing its endpoint objective is not a proved optimal policy for the\nremaining schedule. Equation (4) makes the lost mechanism quantitative:\nevery immediate optimum removes fresh orthogonal information, while a\nlater-block policy may need that information. A constructive uniform\ncomparison must therefore control the later return on the exact cost\n(4), or use a different family. The present interval and affine formulas\nsolve the terminal one-cell choice; they do not assume the needed\ncontinuation value or hide it inside a feasibility claim.\n\n## Sources and verification status\n\nThe fact graph and shared memory were searched first. The existing scalar\nconstant-tail candidate 3dd8ba794379225a was read; it preserves scalar\ngradient knots and areas. The result here concerns a fixed finite\nmultidimensional affine-envelope prefix with all values and planes\nretained, and proves the orthogonal projection cost and affine-ray\nquadratic-root policy directly. No result from that unverified candidate\nis used as a premise. Targeted Matlas retrieval returned no theorem\nused in this proof. No external black box or solver computation is used.\nNo verifier request is allocated in the current TASK, so this is not a\nfact in the graph and must not be treated as one.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "workers/max/research/max_affine_terminal_cell_policy.md", "extends": ["154e28491664f3f1", "5e110ec38dadffb4"], "compared_with": ["3dd8ba794379225a"], "status": "unverified; no new verifier allocation", "fact_ids": [], "scope": "one final affine cell on a frozen full prefix; exact ray optimizer and innovation-cost invariant, not arbitrary-schedule B_n comparison"}, "glossary": {"J_old": "Finite maximum of the retained affine planes eta_j+<a_j,z>, including zero, with minimum zero at the closest minimizer origin.", "R": "Actual distance ||x_init|| from the initial point to the closest retained minimizer origin.", "z_k,H_k": "Retained proximal point x_k-g_k and its nonsmooth value J_old(z_k).", "X": "Fixed post-checkpoint starting point of the new terminal ordinary block.", "S,C,d": "Sum S of the new nonnegative ordinary steps, C=S+1, and d=2C-1.", "a,p": "Intercept and gradient of the one new affine plane, with a<=0.", "V": "Linear span of X, all old proximal points, all old plane normals, and the initial point.", "p_parallel,p_perp": "Orthogonal components of the new plane normal inside and perpendicular to V.", "G2": "True squared norm ||g||^2 of the retained boundary gradient g.", "A": "-<g,X>/G2, assumed positive for the reversed-ray policy.", "beta,theta": "Nonnegative reversed-gradient magnitude and nonpositive new-plane intercept divided by G2.", "u_k,v_k": "H_k/G2 and <g,z_k>/G2; the extra zero pair imposes theta<=0.", "r_j,c_j": "(eta_j+<a_j,X>)/G2 and <a_j,g>/G2.", "U_aff": "Minimum of the retained affine upper bounds u_k+v_k beta on the new intercept.", "I,I_k": "Exact feasible beta interval from all quadratic roots, and the subinterval where affine upper bound k is active."}}
{"id": "1afc93b8e38d3cfc", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T14:28:28.003591+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Constructive quantitative companion to max2's terminal-sharpness link: ANY negative primitive-energy witness can be converted into a fixed compact-projection-function witness with W(h,psi(sum h,0))>q_S, with explicit surplus -q_S*epsilon*V_aux/(D^2+epsilon||X0_aux||^2)>0. The full candidate proof includes a scaled-epigraph reduction showing projection functions universally test primitive validity, an exact zero-energy Huber balance of the terminal mixed moment, every old/new edge, and an explicit finite epsilon. No uniform asymptotic surplus is established; unverified, no fact_submit.", "evidence": "# Projection realization and a constructive terminal objective gap\n\nStatus: complete candidate, NOT verifier-accepted. No numerical solver or\nfact_submit call. The only accepted predecessor used is1252b8823bb5a290,\nfor the explicit finite smooth-convex interpolation construction. All new\nlemmas and calculations are proved within this file. This is a constructive,\nquantitative companion to max2's unverified terminal-sharpness equivalence\ncd21b59c480a46f9; that candidate is NOT a premise.\n\n## 1. Definitions and result\n\nFix a finite nonnegative prefix schedule h=(h_0,...,h_(k-1)), put\ns=sum h_i and D=s+1, and define its intrinsic primitive energy by\n\n  V_h=2sum_(i<k)h_i(f_i-f_k)\n       +sum_(i<k)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sD||g_k||^2.             (1)\n\nAll functions below have smoothness1, and an optimum at0 of value0\nunless a translation is explicitly mentioned. Write\n\n  Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n  y_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2,\n  I_i_star=<g_i,x_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2=-Q_(i,star).\n\nA projection function is\n\n  F_K(X)=max_(G in K){<X,G>-||G||^2/2}\n        =(||X||^2-dist(X,K)^2)/2,                           (2)\n\nwhere K is a nonempty closed convex set containing0. Its gradient is the\nnearest-point projection P_K. Such a function is convex differentiable\n1-smooth, minimized at0, and EVERY incoming optimum slack is zero.\n\nWe prove two statements.\n\n(P) If h has any legal witness with V_h<0, it has one on a projection\nfunction F_K; K may be chosen as the convex hull of0 and its k+1 sampled\ngradients. One extra ambient coordinate suffices before taking that hull.\nThus projection functions universally test primitive validity.\n\n(T) Define\n\n  t=(-1+sqrt(8D+1))/4, b=1+t,\n  r=1/(2t+1), q=r^2=1/[1+2(s+b)].                          (3)\n\nEvery V_h<0 witness can be converted to a fixed legal projection-function\ninstance for the schedule(h,b) whose normalized objective error is\n\n  q + [-q epsilon V_aux]/[D^2+epsilon||X_0_aux||^2] > q.     (4)\n\nHere the finite auxiliary prefix, its negative V_aux, and epsilon>0 are\nconstructed explicitly below. At most three extra ambient coordinates\nbeyond the supplied witness suffice. The final K has at most k+3 listed\nvertices, including0.\n\nThis is a strict excess over the sum-based terminal guard at its prescribed\nbridge. No uniform positive excess over the exact asymptotic benchmark is\nclaimed.\n\n## 2. Elementary properties of projection functions\n\nThe maximum(2) has a unique maximizer because the objective equals\n||X||^2/2-||X-G||^2/2 and K is closed convex and nonempty. Existence follows\nby restricting a minimizing sequence for distance to a bounded ball.\n\nThe projection normal inequality is\n\n  <X-P_K(X),G-P_K(X)><=0 for every G in K.\n\nUsing it at X and X' gives\n\n  <X-X',P_K(X)-P_K(X')>>=||P_K(X)-P_K(X')||^2.\n\nHence P_K is1-Lipschitz. The two maximizing inequalities yield\n\n  0<=F_K(X+U)-F_K(X)-<P_K(X),U>\n    <=<P_K(X+U)-P_K(X),U><=||U||^2.\n\nThis proves differentiability and gradient P_K; the Lipschitz property\nthen proves1-smoothness. Convexity follows from the supremum of affine\nfunctions of X. Since0 belongs to K, F_K>=0 and F_K(0)=0.\n\nAt any query with G=P_K(X), the maximizing value is\nF_K(X)=<G,X>-||G||^2/2. Thus I_i_star=0, and substitution gives\n\n  Q_ij=<G_i-G_j,Y_j>, Y_j=X_j-G_j.                          (5)\n\nConversely, if finite data satisfy all Q<=0, all I_i_star=0, and both\noptimum directions, let K_fin=conv{0,G_0,...,G_m}. Equation(5) and the\noutgoing optimum inequalities say\n\n  <Y_j,G-G_j><=0 for every vertex G of K_fin.\n\nThey say the same for every G in K_fin by convexity. Therefore G_j is\nthe projection of X_j onto K_fin. Formula(2) realizes ALL those finite\nvalues and gradients, with one fixed compact polytope.\n\n## 3. Replace the supplied function by its finite maximal interpolant\n\nStart from the given negative witness and retain all its queried jets,\nincluding the terminal one and the zero optimum. Put\n\n  Phi(v)=max_(j in {star,0,...,k}){<y_j,v>-delta_j}.          (6)\n\nHere the star plane is0. Optimum interpolation gives delta_j>=0, so\nPhi>=0 and Phi(0)=0. Phi is finite, convex and globally Lipschitz with\nconstant M=max_j||y_j||. In particular,\n\n  0<=Phi(v)<=M||v||.\n\nThe interpolant supplied in fact1252b8823bb5a290 is exactly\n\n  F(x)=max_v {<x,v>-||v||^2/2-Phi(v)}.                     (7)\n\nIndeed, its conjugate lower quadratics expand as\n||v||^2/2+<y_j,v>-delta_j. The fact therefore shows that F is convex\ndifferentiable1-smooth with minimizer0 and interpolates every retained\nquery. Running h on F from x_0 reproduces the supplied finite trajectory\nand its negative energy exactly. No full original function is discarded\nwithout preserving the required finite data.\n\n## 4. A scaled epigraph turns the whole primitive defect into projection data\n\nFor a positive parameter Lambda define the closed convex polyhedron\n\n  K_Lambda={(v,z): Lambda z>=Phi(v)}.                      (8)\n\nIt contains0. Run h on F_(K_Lambda) from\n\n  X_0^Lambda=(x_0,-Lambda).\n\nWrite its query points as (u_i,w_i) and projected gradients as (v_i,z_i).\nInductively w_i=-Lambda-a_i/Lambda with a_i>=0. Since w_i<0 and\nPhi(v)>=0, for fixed v the maximizing vertical coordinate in(2) is its\nsmallest feasible one. Consequently\n\n  z_i=Phi(v_i)/Lambda,\n  a_0=0, a_(i+1)=a_i+h_i Phi(v_i),                         (9)\n  F_(K_Lambda)(u_i,w_i)\n   =max_v {<u_i,v>-||v||^2/2-Phi(v)\n             -[a_i Phi(v)+Phi(v)^2/2]/Lambda^2}.           (10)\n\nThese are exact formulas for one fixed function, not time-dependent\noracle choices.\n\nHere is a uniform finite-horizon error estimate. Let\n\n  B=max(1,||x_0||) product_(i<k)(1+2h_i),\n  P=2MB, C=(s+1/2)P^2,\n  U=sqrt(2C) product_(i<k)(1+h_i).\n\nThe objective in(10) at v=0 equals0, so its maximizing v_i satisfies\n<u_i,v_i>-||v_i||^2/2>=0 and hence ||v_i||<=2||u_i||.\nThe GD recursion proves ||u_i||<=B. Thus Phi(v_i)<=P and\n0<=a_i<=sP for every query, including the terminal one.\n\nThe maximizer g_F(u) in(7) has norm at most||u|| because grad F is\n1-Lipschitz and grad F(0)=0. Evaluating(10) at g_F(u_i) and using\nPhi(g_F(u_i))<=P gives\n\n  0<=F(u_i)-F_(K_Lambda)(u_i,w_i)<=C/Lambda^2.\n\nThe objective in(7) is1-strongly concave in v. To spell out the needed\nbound, its negative is ||v||^2/2+Phi(v)-<u_i,v>; subtract its value at\nits minimizer g_F(u_i), and use convexity of Phi and the minimizer\nsubgradient to obtain at least||v-g_F(u_i)||^2/2.\nThe preceding value comparison therefore yields\n\n  ||v_i-g_F(u_i)||<=sqrt(2C)/Lambda.\n\nLet the original interpolated run of F have points x_i and gradients g_i.\nUsing the1-Lipschitz property of grad F in the GD recursions gives\n\n  ||u_i-x_i||<=sqrt(2C)[product_(j<i)(1+h_j)-1]/Lambda,\n  ||v_i-g_i||<=U/Lambda.                                 (11)\n\nThe original points also lie in the ball of radiusB. On this ball\n||grad F||<=B, so\n\n  |F_(K_Lambda)(u_i,w_i)-f_i|<=BU/Lambda+C/Lambda^2,\n  |(||v_i||^2+z_i^2)-||g_i||^2|\n      <=3BU/Lambda+P^2/Lambda^2.                          (12)\n\nThese bounds include C=0, when all displayed errors are zero.\n\nDefine\n\n  H_abs=sum_(i<k)h_i|1-h_i|+sD,\n  C_1=BU(4s+3H_abs), C_2=4sC+H_abs P^2.\n\nSubstituting(12) into(1) gives the explicit bound\n\n  |V_h^Lambda-V_h|<=C_1/Lambda+C_2/Lambda^2.                (13)\n\nFor V_h=-v_def<0, choose any finite\n\n  Lambda>max{1,2(C_1+C_2)/v_def}.\n\nThen V_h^Lambda<-v_def/2<0. This proves the existence of a negative\nprojection-function witness. No numerical optimization or limit verdict\nis used: the bound supplies a finite admissible parameter.\n\nFinally, let K_fin be the convex hull of0 and the k+1 gradients actually\nencountered in this run. It is a subset of K_Lambda containing all these\ngradients. The projection normal inequalities remain true on K_fin,\nso(2) with K_fin realizes exactly the same finite run. This proves(P).\n\nThe large coordinate changes the initial-distance normalization. The\nargument transfers intrinsic primitive energy, not the original\nnormalized objective ratio.\n\n## 5. One zero-energy companion enforces the precise terminal balance\n\nWe now start with the legal negative projection prefix just constructed,\nso I_i_star=0 for every prefix query. Let its terminal data be(x,g,f),\nput y=x-g, and note delta=f-||g||^2/2=<g,y>>=0.\n\nThe identities following from(3) are\n\n  t(2t+1)=D, rD=t, 1-r=2r^2D.                             (14)\n\nDefine the signed terminal balance\n\n  J=r||y||^2-(t+1)<g,y>.                                 (15)\n\nIf J=0 no additional coordinate is needed. If J is nonzero, add one\nscalar Huber trajectory with constant positive gradient c, starting at\n(D+a)c and ending at(1+a)c, for parameters a>0,c>0 specified below.\nAt prefix query i its point is (1+a+R_i)c, where R_i is the remaining\nstepsize sum. All its incoming optimum slacks and finite Q residuals\nare zero. Its intrinsic primitive energy is exactly zero, because\n\n  2sum h_i R_i+sum h_i(1-h_i)-sD=0.\n\nIts contribution to J is\n\n  a[ra-(t+1)]c^2.\n\nChoose:\n\n* If J>0: a=(t+1)/(2r), c^2=J/{a[(t+1)-ra]}.\n* If J<0: a=2(t+1)/r, c^2=(-J)/{a[ra-(t+1)]}.\n\nEvery displayed denominator is strictly positive. The direct sum now\nhas J=0, every incoming optimum slack still0, and the same negative\nprimitive energy. Denote this complete auxiliary prefix by\n(X_i,G_i,F_i), its initial point X_0_aux, and its energy V_aux<0.\nFor its last prefix sample write X,G,Y=X-G,delta=<G,Y>.\n\nThus the exact inherited boundary condition is\n\n  r||Y||^2=(t+1)<G,Y>.                                   (16)\n\nThe function-value moment delta was retained and balanced; it was not\nreplaced by a bound of the wrong sign.\n\n## 6. Append the prescribed bridge and specify every new terminal datum\n\nAppend b=1+t and define\n\n  Z=X-bG=Y-tG,\n  P_term=r[Y-DG],\n  Y_term=Z-P_term=(1-r)Y,\n  F_term=<P_term,Z>-||P_term||^2/2.                       (17)\n\nBy(16),\n\n  <P_term-G,Y>=r||Y||^2-(rD+1)<G,Y>=0.                  (18)\n\nHere rD=t. In particular\n\n  F_term-||P_term||^2/2\n      =<P_term,Y_term>=(1-r)delta>=0.\n\nThe terminal incoming optimum residual is0 and its outgoing optimum\nresidual is nonpositive.\n\nBecause all incoming optimum residuals in the prefix are0, equation(5)\ngives for every old query i\n\n  Q_(i,term)=<G_i-P_term,Y_term>\n     =(1-r)<G_i-G,Y>=(1-r)Q_(i,k)<=0.                    (19)\n\nThe other cross direction is\n\n  Q_(term,i)=<P_term-G_i,Y_i>.                           (20)\n\nAt i=k this is0 by(18). It is also0 at any old query with R_i=0,\nbecause those queries coincide with the last prefix sample. Other\nresiduals(20) may be positive; we keep them instead of assuming legality.\n\n## 7. Exact finite slack mixing supplies the remaining inequalities\n\nUse the scalar base function with derivative min(1,z), with value0 at0.\nExplicitly, it is z^2/2 for z<=1 and z-1/2 for z>=1. It is convex\ndifferentiable1-smooth and minimized at0.\n\nStart it atD. During the entire prefix it has gradient1 and point1+R_i,\nso its final prefix point is1. The appended bridge reaches-t, with\ngradient-t and value t^2/2. Every incoming optimum residual is0.\nIts finite prefix residuals and old-to-terminal residuals are0, while\n\n  Q_base(term,i)=-(1+t)R_i.                              (21)\n\nThus whenever an auxiliary residual(20) is positive, its base residual\nis strictly negative. Define the finite positive coefficient\n\n  epsilon=min(1,\n      min_(i:Q_aux(term,i)>0) [(1+t)R_i/(2Q_aux(term,i))]), (22)\n\nwith an empty inner minimum interpreted as1.\n\nCombine base and auxiliary points and gradients in orthogonal\ncoordinates, scaling the auxiliary coordinates by sqrt(epsilon) and\nits values by epsilon. The GD equations remain exact. All prefix\nconstraints are legal; all old-to-terminal constraints are nonpositive\nby(19); new-to-old constraints are nonpositive by(21),(22).\nBoth optimum directions are nonpositive. Zero-step duplicates have\nalready been treated explicitly.\n\nEvery combined incoming optimum residual is0. By section2, one compact\npolytope K=conv{0, all combined sampled gradients} realizes the entire\nsupplied schedule through the fixed function F_K in(2).\nThis K has at most k+3 listed vertices. Thus no terminal feasibility\ncondition is hidden in the construction.\n\n## 8. The exact normalized objective surplus\n\nFor any prefix whose incoming optimum slacks are all0, distance\ntelescoping gives\n\n  V_h=||X_0||^2-||X+sG||^2\n     =||X_0||^2-||Y+DG||^2.                             (23)\n\nFor completeness, insert F_i=<G_i,X_i>-||G_i||^2/2 into(1).\nThe sum 2sum h_i<G_i,X_i>-sum h_i^2||G_i||^2 equals\n||X_0||^2-||X||^2, and the remaining terms combine to\n-2s<X,G>-s^2||G||^2. This proves(23).\n\nEquations(14),(17),(18) give\n\n  2F_term=||P_term||^2+2(1-r)delta\n         =r^2||Y-DG||^2+4r^2D<G,Y>\n         =q||Y+DG||^2.\n\nTherefore the exact auxiliary terminal objective deficit is\n\n  q||X_0_aux||^2-2F_term=q V_aux<0.                       (24)\n\nThe base has initial norm squaredD^2 and final twice-value t^2=qD^2.\nAfter the fully legal mixture,\n\n  ||X_0_mix||^2=D^2+epsilon||X_0_aux||^2,\n  2F_mix(term)-q||X_0_mix||^2=-q epsilon V_aux>0.\n\nDividing proves(4). This is an actual positive, finite, algebraically\nspecified objective surplus for a fixed legal function.\n\n## 9. A finite exact check\n\nFor h=(3/2), take the scalar quadratic f(x)=x^2/2 from x_0=1.\nIts prefix endpoint and gradient are both-1/2, every incoming optimum\nslack is0, and\n\n  D=5/2, V_h=1-D^2/4=-9/16.\n\nHere Y=0, so J=0. With t=(sqrt(21)-1)/4, the terminal point and gradient\nare t/2. No auxiliary new-to-old violation occurs, so epsilon=1 is\npermitted in(22). Mixing with the stated scalar base gives precisely\n\n  2F_mix(term)/||X_0_mix||^2=(125/116)q>q.\n\nThe surplus9q/116 agrees exactly with(4):\n(-q V_h)/(D^2+1)=9q/116.\n\n## 10. Scope, related work, and remaining gap\n\nThis construction realizes, rather than merely infers, a strict failure\nof the terminal sum guard whenever the prefix is not primitive. It\ncomplements max2's dual certificate characterization rather than\nclaiming that equivalence as a new independent target solution.\n\nAll signed function-value information in the original witness is stored\nin Phi and then in the geometry of K_Lambda. The subsequent Huber\ncompanion balances the exact mixed terminal moment(16), and the finite\nepsilon retains every queried-prefix and new/old interpolation edge.\n\nThe surplus(4) can be small: Lambda, the moment-balancing coordinate and\nthe active-constraint mixing scale all affect its denominator and\nepsilon. No bound uniform over arbitrary schedules, horizons, or defects\nhas been obtained. An improved asymptotic schedule or the exact-profile\nlower theorem therefore does not follow.\n\nThe original target remains open. No claim in this file has entered the\nfact graph, and no new verifier or numerical allocation was consumed.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "finding_ids": ["cd21b59c480a46f9", "a141afe16d306715"], "branch_id": "max4_projection_terminal_defect", "artifact": "local_memory/projection_terminal_defect.md", "status": "complete_candidate_not_verified", "scope": "subgoal", "is_fragile": false, "fragility_reason": "", "suggested_followup": "none"}, "glossary": {"V_h": "Intrinsic primitive energy of the supplied prefix, defined in equation1.", "F_K": "Projection function max_(G in K)(<X,G>-||G||^2/2), with gradient the nearest-point projection.", "P_K": "Nearest-point projection onto the closed convex set K.", "Phi": "Finite nonnegative convex support function max_j(<y_j,v>-delta_j), not the project's scalar bridge phi.", "K_Lambda": "Scaled epigraph {(v,z):Lambda*z>=Phi(v)} in the projection reduction.", "Lambda": "Positive finite epigraph scale chosen using the explicit error bound13.", "B": "Finite uniform bound on horizontal trajectory coordinates in section4.", "M": "Maximum norm of the original proximal points y_j; Lipschitz bound for Phi.", "P": "2MB, a bound on encountered values of Phi.", "C": "(s+1/2)P^2, the finite objective perturbation constant in section4.", "U": "sqrt(2C)*product_(i<k)(1+h_i), the gradient/point error constant.", "C_1": "BU(4s+3H_abs), coefficient of1/Lambda in the energy error bound.", "C_2": "4sC+H_abs*P^2, coefficient of1/Lambda^2 in the energy error bound.", "H_abs": "sum h_i|1-h_i|+s(s+1), the absolute gradient-coefficient sum.", "v_def": "-V_h>0 for the original negative witness.", "D": "1+sum h_i, prefix physical capacity.", "t": "Positive root of t(2t+1)=D.", "b": "1+t=psi(sum h_i,0), the appended terminal step.", "r": "1/(2t+1).", "q": "r^2=1/[1+2(sum h_i+b)], the canonical terminal objective guard.", "J": "Signed terminal balance r||y||^2-(t+1)<g,y>.", "a": "Nonnegative final affine-ray offset of the Huber companion used to balance J.", "c": "Positive constant Huber gradient in the moment-balancing companion.", "P_term": "Auxiliary terminal gradient r(Y-DG).", "Y_term": "Auxiliary terminal proximal point (1-r)Y.", "V_aux": "Negative primitive energy after the projection reduction and zero-energy moment-balancing companion.", "epsilon": "Positive finite cross-interpolation mixing coefficient in equation22.", "X_0_aux": "Initial point of the complete auxiliary prefix before the final scalar base is mixed in.", "R_i": "Remaining nonnegative prefix stepsize sum at query i.", "K_fin": "Convex hull of0 and the sampled projection gradients, preserving the finite run."}}
{"id": "08c9c955dad85bba", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T14:29:53.111397+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Complete multidimensional constant-gradient-tail reduction for an arbitrary legal smooth-convex prefix: form J_min from every queried proximal supporting plane; adding ONE affine plane with two endpoint comparison families characterizes ALL smooth-convex constant-gradient tails preserving the full prefix and optimum. The exact finite concave maximization is attained when feasible, and every optimizer lies in the old geometric span. A retained active-normal certificate gives the actual closest-minimizer normalization. The anti-boundary-ray restriction has the explicit quadratic-root optimizer in the same joint proof.", "evidence": "# Exact terminal affine-cell policy and the cost of a new direction\n\nStatus: self-contained UNVERIFIED candidate. No fact submission or numerical\nsolver. This concerns an explicitly described one-cell continuation of a\nfixed prefix, not all smooth-convex extensions or the uniform ZJ target.\n\n## 1. General positive-value prefix\n\nLet\n\n    J_old(z)=max_{j in P} (eta_j+<a_j,z>),\n\nwhere P is a finite index set containing the zero plane (eta_*=0,a_*=0).\nSuppose J_old(0)=0 and 0 is a closest zero to the initial point x_init.\nWrite R=||x_init||>0. Thus every eta_j<=0. Let f_old=env_1 J_old.\nFor a prescribed finite GD prefix retain EVERY queried jet (x_k,g_k,f_k),\nits proximal point z_k=x_k-g_k, and height H_k=J_old(z_k).\nThe prefix may have arbitrary positive heights, arbitrary affine offsets,\nnon-Markov gradients, and arbitrary prescribed nonnegative steps.\n\nAfter a chosen checkpoint applied to the last retained query, let X be\nthe resulting next position. Append any nonnegative ordinary list of sum\nS, and put C=S+1>=1 and d=2C-1. We seek a single new affine plane\n\n    ell(z)=a+<p,z>, a<=0,\n    J=max(J_old,ell), f=env_1 J,\n\nwhose gradient p is used throughout the whole new ordinary block,\nincluding its final sample. The data before X are kept exactly.\n\nThe prefix is preserved exactly when\n\n    a+<p,z_k><=H_k                              for every old query k. (1)\n\nThe proposed new queries after partial mass u in [0,S] have position\nX-u p and proximal point X-(u+1)p. The new plane dominates old plane j\nat such a point exactly when\n\n    (u+1)||p||^2-<p,X+(u+1)a_j>\n                    +eta_j+<a_j,X>-a <=0.                          (2)\n\nThis expression is affine in u. Hence it suffices, and is necessary,\nto check (2) at u=0 and u=S; both are actual endpoint samples. This\nalso checks the zero plane because it is included in P. An active new\nplane supplies the required subgradient, so the proximal optimality\ncondition gives the same p at every supplied intermediate query. All\nsteps of the given ordinary list therefore occur in their actual order.\n\nThe envelope is convex, differentiable and 1-smooth: its proximal point\nis the unique minimizer of J(z)+||x-z||^2/2; the subgradient monotonicity\ninequality gives ||p(x)-p(y)||^2<=<p(x)-p(y),x-y>, where p(x)=x-z(x).\nComparing the two minimizing expressions yields derivative p(x).\nThus this construction is one fixed smooth-convex function.\n\nThe minimum and normalization remain exact. J>=0 and J(0)=0 since\na<=0. Its zero set is a subset of the old zero set and still contains\n0, so 0 remains closest to x_init at distance R. The envelope has the\nsame zero set as J, since both nonnegative terms in its minimizing\nexpression must vanish at a zero. At the final query, the exact score is\n\n    2 f_end/R^2 = [2a+2<p,X>-d||p||^2]/R^2.                         (3)\n\n## 2. Orthogonal innovations have an exact immediate price\n\nLet V be the linear span of X, all z_k, all old normals a_j, and x_init.\nDecompose p=p_parallel+p_perp orthogonally with p_parallel in V.\nReplace the new plane by a+<p_parallel,z>.\n\nEvery old-query test (1) is unchanged. In each new-query test (2), the\nlinear terms are unchanged while the squared norm decreases by\n||p_perp||^2. Thus every test remains valid. The same a<=0 still retains\nthe closest minimizer. Equation (3) shows the EXACT increase in score:\n\n    score_projected-score_original=d||p_perp||^2/R^2.                (4)\n\nThis is strict when p_perp is nonzero. It is valid for the whole new\nconstant-gradient block, not just an unqueried terminal sample. No old\nposition, gradient, value, or affine plane has been discarded.\n\nConsequently no maximizer of this immediate one-cell objective has a\ncomponent in a fresh direction orthogonal to the complete old geometry.\nFor example, when old data are Markov and p=-beta g+v_perp with\n||v_perp||^2=||g||^2 beta(A/T-beta), the immediate penalty is exactly\n\n    d||g||^2 beta(A/T-beta)/R^2.                                    (5)\n\nCreating new directions may still be useful for later blocks. Formula\n(4) says that an immediate-cell maximization policy never chooses them;\nit does not say that their globally optimal use is impossible. Starting\nfrom a one-dimensional prefix and repeatedly maximizing only this\nimmediate objective therefore cannot create a new dimension.\n\n## 3. Explicit exact optimization on the reversed boundary ray\n\nLet g be the nonzero gradient at the retained checkpoint query, set\nG2=||g||^2, and suppose\n\n    A=-<g,X>/G2>0.\n\nAllow all new planes of the form\n\n    ell(z)=G2 theta+<-beta g+v_perp,z>,\n    beta>=0, theta<=0,\n\nwhere v_perp is orthogonal to V. By (4), optimizing this entire family\nreduces exactly to v_perp=0. No Markov or zero-height prefix assumption\nis made. Define the retained dimensionless numbers\n\n    u_k=H_k/G2, v_k=<g,z_k>/G2                    for old queries k,\n    u_*=v_*=0                                  for an extra index *,\n    r_j=(eta_j+<a_j,X>)/G2,\n    c_j=<a_j,g>/G2                              for old planes j.\n\nThe extra * among the query bounds imposes theta<=0; it is separate\nfrom the zero plane already included among the old planes. Conditions\n(1)-(2) become exactly\n\n    theta<=u_k+v_k beta                           for every k,*;\n    theta>=t beta^2+(t c_j-A)beta+r_j              for every j,\n                                                     t in {1,C}.  (6)\n\nSet U_aff(beta)=min_k(u_k+v_k beta). Feasibility is equivalent to all\nthe explicit convex quadratic inequalities\n\n    t beta^2+(t c_j-A-v_k)beta+(r_j-u_k)<=0,\n                            j in P, t in {1,C}, k in queries union{*}. (7)\n\nEach has a positive leading coefficient. If its discriminant is\nnegative the family is infeasible. Otherwise it gives the closed\ninterval between its two real roots. Intersect those finitely many\nroot intervals with beta>=0. This is the exact feasible interval I,\npossibly empty. The pair zero-plane/* with t=C also gives\n\n    0<=beta<=A/C,                                                   (8)\n\nso every nonempty I is compact. These formulas include equality and\nzero-step degeneracies. If beta=0 is feasible, (6) forces theta=0\nthrough the zero plane and theta<=0, and the terminal block is already\nat a minimizer. Positive score requires beta>0.\n\nFor beta in I, the largest allowed theta is U_aff(beta), and the exact\nbest score at that beta is\n\n    V(beta)=(G2/R^2)[2U_aff(beta)+2A beta-d beta^2].                  (9)\n\nThis is a strictly concave piecewise quadratic function on I. It has an\nexplicit finite maximization rule. For each query bound k, let\n\n    I_k=I intersect {beta: u_k+v_k beta<=u_l+v_l beta for every l}.\n\nThe set I_k is a closed interval, possibly empty, determined by affine\nhalf-line intersections. On a nonempty I_k, clamp (A+v_k)/d to I_k and\ncall the result beta_k. Compare the finitely many values V(beta_k).\nAny largest one is the global optimum; set theta_opt=U_aff(beta_opt).\nEvery optimizing parameter, scale, intercept, and normalized value is\ntherefore explicitly determined by retained finite data and quadratic\nroots. No continuous optimizer or presumed feasible parameter appears.\n\nThe frozen prefix can have positive internal values and nonzero offsets.\nIn particular this rule does not replace them by zero, and it retains\nboth the true boundary magnitude G2 and the actual distance R.\n\n## 4. A nonzero affine offset improves a frozen multidimensional prefix\n\nThis quantitative example distinguishes the affine rule from merely\nchoosing a positive homogeneous terminal level. For any M>1 prescribe\nthree arbitrary nonnegative ordinary blocks, each of sum M-1, with\ncheckpoints 4M+1 and 2M+1. Work in R^2 with x_init=(1,0), and set\n\n    p=(1/M,0), q=(-2/M,2/M), J_old(z)=max(0,<p,z>,<q,z>).\n\nOn the first block use gradient p. Its positions run from (1,0) to p;\nall its proximal points are d p, 0<=d<=M-1. The q plane is negative\nthere. The checkpoint 4M+1 leads to X_1=(-4,0). Put\n\n    Z_1=X_1-Mq=(-2,-2).\n\nOn the second block use gradient q. Its proximal points are Z_1+d q,\n0<=d<=M-1. The q plane has value d||q||^2, while the p plane has\nvalue -2/M-2d/M^2<=0. Thus both supplied prefix blocks and their\ncheckpoint queries are legal. The second checkpoint 2M+1 leads to\n\n    X=X_1-3Mq=(2,-6), G2=8/M^2, A=2M, C=M.\n\nOrigin is closest because the retained p plane forces the first\ncoordinate of every old zero to be nonpositive. Thus R=1.\n\nFor the new plane G2 theta+<-beta q,z>, its exact old-query upper\nbound is\n\n    theta<=min(0, k(1-2beta)), k=(M-1)/8.                           (10)\n\nIndeed on the first block the normalized inequality is\ntheta<=(1-2beta)d/8 for 0<=d<=M-1. On the second it is\ntheta<=d(1+beta), whose minimum is zero. The zero endpoints are\nactual retained queries.\n\nFor beta>=1/2 the unconstrained optimizer of (9) with (10) is\n\n    beta_aff=(7M+1)/[4(2M-1)],\n    theta_aff=(M-1)(1-2beta_aff)/8<0.                               (11)\n\nHere is the complete new-plane feasibility check, not just an objective\nstationarity calculation. Put d=2M-1>1, so\n\n    beta_aff=(7d+9)/(8d),\n    theta_aff=-3(d-1)(d+3)/(64d),\n    7/8<beta_aff<2.\n\nAmong the terminal checks for the zero plane, the larger t=C is the\nonly stronger one. Its lower bound for theta is\n\n    L_0=C beta_aff(beta_aff-2).\n\nThe exact slack is\n\n    theta_aff-L_0\n       =(d-1)(57d^2+126d+81)/(128d^2)>0.                            (12)\n\nFor old plane q, the strongest lower bound is\nL_q=C(beta_aff+1)(beta_aff-2)<=L_0. For old plane p it is\n\n    L_p=C(beta_aff^2-9beta_aff/4+1/4),\n\nbecause beta_aff>1/4 makes the t=C bound stronger than t=1. Its slack\nis\n\n    theta_aff-L_p=(55d^3+85d^2-27d-81)/(128d^2)>0.                  (13)\n\nThe numerator is at least 28d+4 for d>=1, using d^3>=d and d^2>=1.\nThus every old plane, the zero plane, and all past queries are checked.\nThe chosen beta maximizes the full piecewise quadratic upper bound:\nbelow 1/2 that bound is increasing, and above 1/2 its vertex is (11).\nIts feasibility proves global optimality within this affine ray family.\n\nThe exact normalized score is\n\n    V_aff=(57M^2+2M+5)/[2M^2(2M-1)].                              (14)\n\nFor a homogeneous new plane theta=0, (10) forces beta<=1/2. The\nobjective 2A beta-(2C-1)beta^2 increases on that interval. Beta=1/2\nis feasible by the same checks, so the homogeneous optimum is\n\n    V_hom=12/M+2/M^2.\n\nThe exact gain from retaining the affine-value freedom is\n\n    V_aff-V_hom=9(M+1)^2/[2M^2(2M-1)]>0.                           (15)\n\nNo new direction is used at the terminal cell; the earlier two-\ndimensional prefix is preserved. This example illustrates the general\noptimizer and does not supply a new all-schedule or asymptotic result.\n\n## 5. What remains for iteration\n\nEquations (1)-(3) apply to any proposed internal affine cell too, but\nmaximizing its endpoint objective is not a proved optimal policy for the\nremaining schedule. Equation (4) makes the lost mechanism quantitative:\nevery immediate optimum removes fresh orthogonal information, while a\nlater-block policy may need that information. A constructive uniform\ncomparison must therefore control the later return on the exact cost\n(4), or use a different family. The present interval and affine formulas\nsolve the terminal one-cell choice; they do not assume the needed\ncontinuation value or hide it inside a feasibility claim.\n\n## 6. Completeness for a fixed smooth-convex prefix and constant-gradient tail\n\nThere is also a precise completeness statement for the finite program\n(1)-(3). It needs a minimal old envelope, rather than arbitrary additional\nold planes. Let the old jets and the optimum jet (0,0,0) be any legal\nsmooth-convex data at smoothness one. For every old query define\n\n    z_k=x_k-g_k,\n    H_k=f_k-||g_k||^2/2,\n    eta_k=H_k-<g_k,z_k>,\n    J_min(z)=max(0, eta_k+<g_k,z>: old queries k).                  (16)\n\nThe smooth-convex interpolation inequality in accepted fact\n1252b8823bb5a290 gives, for every ordered pair of old queries,\n\n    H_i>=H_j+<g_j,z_i-z_j>.\n\nThe optimum inequalities give H_i>=0 and eta_i<=0. Hence J_min(z_i)=H_i,\ng_i is a subgradient there, and env_1 J_min realizes every old jet and\nthe optimum at origin. This uses the accepted necessary interpolation\ninequality; the actual realizing envelope has just been constructed.\n\nAmong ALL convex 1-smooth functions preserving these jets and minimum\nzero at origin, impose the additional restriction that every sample in\nthe appended ordinary block, including its terminal sample, has the\nsame gradient p. Then (1)-(2), with the old planes from (16), are an\nexact finite characterization of the possible tails. For the reverse\ndirection, equal gradients at two samples force\n\n    f_i-f_j=<p,x_i-x_j>\n\nby applying the accepted interpolation inequality in both directions.\nThus if f_start is the value at X, the tail value after partial mass u\nis f_start-u||p||^2. Every tail supporting affine plane in its\nnonsmooth interpolation data has the SAME intercept\n\n    a=f_start-<p,X>+||p||^2/2.\n\nThe optimum inequality gives a<=0. Applying the interpolation\ninequality between each old sample and each tail sample gives (1)-(2).\nTheir necessity has therefore been proved directly from arbitrary\nsmooth-convex data, without presuming a particular old global function.\nTheir sufficiency is the envelope construction of Section 1. In\nparticular the old function outside the queried samples may change;\nthe complete queried jets and optimum remain fixed.\n\nThe finite maximization of 2a+2<p,X>-d||p||^2 is attained whenever this\nconstant-tail family is nonempty. It is a closed feasible problem. Its\nzero-plane t=C inequality and a<=0 imply\n\n    C||p||^2<=<p,X>, ||p||<=||X||/C,\n    -||X||^2/(4C)<=a<=0.\n\nThus its parameter set is compact. Every optimizer lies in the span of\nthe old geometric data by the strict improvement (4). The extra\nrestriction p=-beta g reduces it to the explicit ray optimizer of\nSection 3; that restriction is not claimed to contain the unrestricted\nvector optimizer.\n\nFor true closest-minimizer normalization one must additionally establish\nthat origin is closest to x_init among the zeros of J_min. This is\nalready an assumption in Section 1 and holds in Section 4. A concrete\nsufficient certificate is\n\n    x_init=sum_k lambda_k g_k,\n    lambda_k>=0, lambda_k eta_k=0.                                (17)\n\nFor any zero z of J_min this gives <x_init,z><=0, hence\n||x_init-z||^2>=||x_init||^2, with equality at z=0. Any minimizer of\nANY other interpolating smooth-convex function must also satisfy all\neta_k+<g_k,z><=0, by applying interpolation to that minimizer. Thus\n(17), or a separately computed genuine distance profile, supplies the\nsame true normalization throughout the exact constant-tail class.\n\nThe case C=1 is a terminal-sample specialization. For C>1 this is an\nexact reduction only for tails whose gradient stays constant at all\ntheir samples. The program can be empty for an incompatible long block;\nno arbitrary-schedule feasibility or benchmark bound is inferred.\n\n## Sources and verification status\n\nThe fact graph and shared memory were searched first. The existing scalar\nconstant-tail candidate 3dd8ba794379225a was read; it preserves scalar\ngradient knots and areas. The result here concerns a fixed finite\nmultidimensional affine-envelope prefix with all values and planes\nretained, and proves the orthogonal projection cost and affine-ray\nquadratic-root policy directly. No result from that unverified candidate\nis used as a premise. Targeted Matlas retrieval returned no theorem\nused in this proof. No external black box or solver computation is used.\nNo verifier request is allocated in the current TASK, so this is not a\nfact in the graph and must not be treated as one.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "workers/max/research/max_affine_terminal_cell_policy.md", "section": "6 with self-contained sections1-5", "finding_ids": ["9c3632026c522612", "154e28491664f3f1"], "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "status": "unverified; no additional verifier request", "scope": "exact complete constant-gradient-tail class, NOT all remaining trajectories or uniform B_n comparison"}, "glossary": {"J_min": "max(0,eta_k+<g_k,z>) over every old queried gradient, with eta_k=f_k-<g_k,x_k>+||g_k||^2/2.", "z_k,H_k": "Old proximal points x_k-g_k and values f_k-||g_k||^2/2.", "a,p": "Intercept and constant gradient of the single new terminal supporting plane.", "C,d": "One plus total nonnegative tail step mass, and d=2C-1.", "X": "Fixed initial query of the appended tail after the prescribed checkpoint.", "R": "The genuine closest-minimizer distance, when certified by the retained geometry.", "lambda_k": "Nonnegative coefficients certifying x_init=sum lambda_k g_k with lambda_k eta_k=0.", "V": "Span of X, old proximal points, old normals, and initial point; terminal orthogonal projection strictly improves objective outside this span."}}
{"id": "4ad60ca9f3ec3e2e", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T14:35:05.271151+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Exact finite projection-lift candidate: every negative intrinsic primitive witness can be converted, by an explicit slack coordinate and a fixed 2D ball-projection repair, to a compact projection-polytope witness with at most n+2 listed vertices and dimension at most n+1. Exact energy identity V_new=V_original+[K_rep V_ball-(s I_n-J_n)^2]/Lambda^2 supplies a finite negative choice. Therefore primitive validity is equivalent to ||x_n+s P_K(x_n)||<=||x_0|| for all such polytopes. This removes the limiting interpolation step from the prior projection route while retaining ALL original values/slacks in its geometry. No objective-normalization preservation or asymptotic claim; unverified.", "evidence": "# A finite exact projection lift of arbitrary primitive defects\n\nStatus: complete candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. No solver or\nfact_submit. The argument below is elementary and self-contained. It does\nnot use another unverified finding as a premise. It gives an alternative\nfinite-data implementation of the projection reduction in1afc93b8e38d3cfc.\n\n## 1. Input, notation and conclusion\n\nLet h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be any finite nonnegative schedule. Put\ns=sum h_i and D=1+s. Take any actual convex differentiable1-smooth\nGD data(x_i,g_i,f_i), 0<=i<=n, with a minimizer normalized to0 of value0.\nWrite\n\n  Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n  I_i=<g_i,x_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2>=0,\n  delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2>=0,\n  J_i=sum_(t<i)h_t I_t,\n  V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)\n       +sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sD||g_n||^2.\n\nAssume V_h=-v_def<0. We construct explicit finite data in dimension d+3,\non a fixed projection function associated with a compact polytope K\ncontaining0, such that its primitive energy is still negative and all its\nincoming optimum slacks are exactly0.\n\nThe constructed K has at most n+2 listed vertices:0 and the n+1 sampled\ngradients. After an optional orthogonal compression, its ambient dimension\nis at most n+1. Every quantity is obtained by finite arithmetic, square roots,\na fixed two-dimensional recurrence, and finite maxima. No limiting\ninterpolant or numerical PEP evaluation is required.\n\nAs a consequence, primitive validity of h is equivalent to\n\n  ||X_n+s P_K(X_n)||<=||X_0||                             (1)\n\nfor every finite-dimensional compact polytope K containing0 and every\nGD run X_(i+1)=X_i-h_i P_K(X_i). It suffices in testing failure to use\npolytopes with at most n+2 listed vertices.\n\n## 2. One slack coordinate, with its interpolation errors retained exactly\n\nFor Lambda>0 define auxiliary finite data\n\n  X_i^a=(x_i,-Lambda-J_i/Lambda),\n  G_i^a=(g_i,I_i/Lambda),\n  F_i^a=<G_i^a,X_i^a>-||G_i^a||^2/2\n       =f_i-[I_i J_i+I_i^2/2]/Lambda^2.                  (2)\n\nThe GD equations hold exactly because J_(i+1)-J_i=h_i I_i.\nEvery incoming optimum residual of this auxiliary array is0 by its\nassigned value. We do NOT assume its other interpolation constraints\nare legal. They are exactly\n\n  Q_ij^a=Q_ij+A_ij/Lambda^2,\n  A_ij=-(I_i-I_j)(J_j+I_j),                              (3)\n  Q_(star,i)^a=-delta_i+B_i/Lambda^2,\n  B_i=I_i(J_i+I_i)>=0.                                  (4)\n\nTo verify(3), when incoming slacks are0 the finite residual equals\n<G_i^a-G_j^a,X_j^a-G_j^a>. Its original-coordinate part is\nQ_ij+I_i-I_j. The added-coordinate part is\n-(I_i-I_j)-(I_i-I_j)(J_j+I_j)/Lambda^2.\nThe constant terms cancel, leaving(3). Equation(4) follows directly\nfrom(2).\n\nIf the sum of h between indices i and j is0, every step between them\nis zero. The original queries, gradients and values are identical;\nalso I_i=I_j and J_i=J_j. Thus A_ij=0 for those duplicate queries.\n\n## 3. One explicit strict projection trajectory repairs every other edge\n\nLet e=(1,0) in R^2 and take the closed Euclidean ball\n\n  K_ball={z:||z-e||<=2}.\n\nIt contains0 in its interior. Define the fixed projection function\n\n  F_ball(x)=max_(g in K_ball){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.\n\nRun the SAME schedule h from x_0^ball=e+(0,L), where L=6s+4.\nIts gradients always have norm at most3. Hence\n\n  ||x_i^ball-e||>=L-3s=3s+4>2.\n\nEvery query stays strictly outside the ball, so, setting\nz_i=x_i^ball-e, u_i=z_i/||z_i||,\n\n  g_i^ball=e+2u_i,\n  y_i^ball=x_i^ball-g_i^ball=(||z_i||-2)u_i.              (5)\n\nThis is an exact finite recurrence of arithmetic and square roots.\nAll incoming optimum slacks are0. The outgoing optimum slack is\n\n  delta_i^ball=<g_i^ball,y_i^ball>\n       =(||z_i||-2)(2+<e,u_i>)>0.                       (6)\n\nDifferent positive cumulative times have different unit directions u_i.\nHere is an explicit proof, including arbitrary large finite steps.\nThe recursion for z_i is\n\n  z_(i+1)=mu_i z_i-h_i e,\n  mu_i=1-2h_i/||z_i||>0,\n\nbecause h_i<=s and ||z_i||>=3s+4>2s.\nThe second coordinate z_(i,2) stays strictly positive.\nFor the ratio of first to second coordinates,\n\n  z_(i+1,1)/z_(i+1,2)\n    =z_(i,1)/z_(i,2)-h_i/(mu_i z_(i,2)).\n\nIt decreases strictly at every positive step and is unchanged at a\nzero step. Thus u_i!=u_j whenever the cumulative step between i and j\nis positive.\n\nThe finite projection residuals are therefore\n\n  Q_ij^ball=2(||z_j||-2)(<u_i,u_j>-1)<0                 (7)\n\nfor every such pair, and they are0 at zero-step duplicates. This\nchecks BOTH ordered directions of every pair.\n\nFor completeness, projection functions are legal without invoking a\nnew external theorem. The nearest-point normal inequalities imply\n<P_K(x)-P_K(x'),x-x'>>=||P_K(x)-P_K(x')||^2, hence\nP_K is1-Lipschitz. The maximizing expression for F_K implies\n\n  0<=F_K(x+d)-F_K(x)-<P_K(x),d>\n     <=<P_K(x+d)-P_K(x),d><=||d||^2.\n\nThus grad F_K=P_K, so F_K is convex differentiable1-smooth. Since0\nbelongs to K, its minimum is F_K(0)=0. At every query\nF_K(x)=<P_K(x),x>-||P_K(x)||^2/2, giving the asserted zero incoming\nslack and the formula for finite Q.\n\n## 4. A finite repair coefficient gives exact full interpolation\n\nLet the finite coefficient K_rep be\n\n  max{0,\n      max_(0<=i<=n) B_i/delta_i^ball,\n      max_(i!=j, positive cumulative step)\n                  (A_ij)_+/(-Q_ij^ball)}.               (8)\n\nEvery denominator is strictly positive by(6),(7). The last maximum is\nomitted if its index set is empty. All quantities are explicitly\ndetermined by the original finite data and the fixed ball run.\n\nFor any Lambda>0 combine the auxiliary array(2) and the ball array in\northogonal coordinates with ball-coordinate scale sqrt(K_rep)/Lambda:\n\n  X_i=(X_i^a,(sqrt(K_rep)/Lambda)x_i^ball),\n  G_i=(G_i^a,(sqrt(K_rep)/Lambda)g_i^ball),\n  F_i=F_i^a+(K_rep/Lambda^2)f_i^ball.                     (9)\n\nWhen K_rep=0 these extra coordinates are simply zero. Every GD\nequation remains exact, and all incoming optimum residuals remain0.\nFor distinct finite samples,\n\n  Q_ij_new=Q_ij+[A_ij+K_rep Q_ij^ball]/Lambda^2<=0,\n\nusing(8); the duplicate-query case follows from A_ij=Q_ij^ball=0.\nFor outgoing optimum residuals,\n\n  Q_(star,i)_new=-delta_i+\n              [B_i-K_rep delta_i^ball]/Lambda^2<=0.\n\nThese checks account for EVERY original-index interpolation constraint.\nNo positivity has been assumed merely from a small-parameter argument.\n\nDefine the compact polytope\n\n  K=conv{0,G_0,...,G_n}.\n\nBecause all incoming optimum residuals are0, the checked finite\ninequalities state\n\n  <X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j><=0,\n\nand the outgoing optimum inequalities give the same condition at vertex0.\nConvex combinations give the condition for every point of K. It is\nprecisely the nearest-point normal condition, so P_K(X_j)=G_j.\nConsequently the fixed function\n\n  F_K(X)=max_(G in K){<X,G>-||G||^2/2}\n\ninterpolates all the constructed points, gradients and values. It is\na fixed globally legal function, with no adaptive resetting.\n\n## 5. Exact primitive energy and a finite negative choice\n\nFor arbitrary original legal data, telescoping the GD squared distances\nand using the definitions of I_i gives\n\n  V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_n+s g_n||^2+2(sI_n-J_n).             (10)\n\nFor the auxiliary array all incoming slacks are0, so the same algebra,\nwhich does not require prior legality, gives\n\n  V_aux=||X_0^a||^2-||X_n^a+s G_n^a||^2\n       =V_h-(sI_n-J_n)^2/Lambda^2.                      (11)\n\nIndeed X_0^a has added coordinate-Lambda, while X_n^a+sG_n^a has added\ncoordinate-Lambda+(sI_n-J_n)/Lambda. Expanding these squares and then\nusing(10) proves(11).\n\nLet V_ball be the intrinsic primitive energy of the fixed ball\ntrajectory, calculated by(1)'s energy definition, or equivalently by\n\n  V_ball=||x_0^ball||^2\n                  -||x_n^ball+s g_n^ball||^2.\n\nEnergy is additive in orthogonal coordinates. Therefore the fully legal\ndata(9) have EXACTLY\n\n  V_new=V_h+\n        [K_rep V_ball-(sI_n-J_n)^2]/Lambda^2.             (12)\n\nChoose, for example,\n\n  Lambda^2=1+2K_rep max(V_ball,0)/v_def.                 (13)\n\nThis is positive and finite. If K_rep max(V_ball,0)=0, (12) is at most\n-v_def. Otherwise the positive contribution in(12) is strictly below\nv_def/2. In all cases\n\n  V_new<-v_def/2<0.\n\nThe output is thus a concrete negative witness on a compact projection\nfunction, produced by finite exact operations.\n\nThe final dimension can be reduced to at most n+1. Let W be the linear\nspan of the n+1 output gradients, hence of K. Project every query onto W.\nThe GD equations, gradient pairings and function values are unchanged;\nprojection onto K ignores the component orthogonal to W. The orthogonal\ncomponent of every query is the same, so it also cancels from the two\nsquared norms in the energy identity. This preserves V_new exactly.\n\n## 6. The lossless primitive operator criterion and its limitation\n\nFor a projection function, every I_i is0, so(10) is exactly\n\n  V_h=||X_0||^2-||X_n+s P_K(X_n)||^2.\n\nIf h is primitive, it is nonnegative on every such trajectory, proving(1).\nConversely, if h is not primitive, sections2-5 produce a compact polytope\nwith at most n+2 listed vertices and a negative energy, so(1) fails.\nThis proves the asserted equivalence with the original intrinsic\nprimitive inequality.\n\nAll old function values remain encoded: they determine I_i and hence\nthe additional coordinates, the repair coefficient and the final\npolytope. The reduction is not a permission to delete queried-history\nconstraints or optimum terms from an arbitrary full certificate.\n\nThe initial norm is changed by the added -Lambda coordinate and the\nball coordinates. The construction does NOT preserve normalized\nobjective error and does not by itself settle the asymptotic\nZhang-Jiang target. It supplies an exact finite projection realization\nfor intrinsic primitive defects and a precise geometry in which to\npursue that target's remaining primitive-capacity questions.\n\nNo new result in this file has been verified or written as a fact.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["1afc93b8e38d3cfc"], "branch_id": "max4_exact_projection_lift", "artifact": "local_memory/exact_projection_lift.md", "status": "complete_candidate_not_verified", "scope": "subgoal", "is_fragile": false, "fragility_reason": "", "suggested_followup": "none"}, "glossary": {"V_h": "Intrinsic primitive energy defined in section1.", "I_i": "Actual incoming optimum slack <g_i,x_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2.", "J_i": "Weighted accumulated incoming optimum slack sum_(t<i)h_t I_t.", "v_def": "Positive magnitude -V_h of the original primitive defect.", "X_i^a": "Auxiliary lifted query (x_i,-Lambda-J_i/Lambda).", "G_i^a": "Auxiliary lifted gradient (g_i,I_i/Lambda).", "F_i^a": "Assigned auxiliary value <G_i^a,X_i^a>-||G_i^a||^2/2.", "A_ij": "Exact pairwise interpolation error coefficient -(I_i-I_j)(J_j+I_j).", "B_i": "Exact outgoing optimum error coefficient I_i(J_i+I_i).", "K_ball": "Euclidean radius2 ball centered at e=(1,0) in R^2.", "L": "6s+4, vertical starting coordinate of the strict ball-projection repair run; smoothness is fixed1 throughout this proof.", "z_i": "Ball-run query displacement x_i^ball-e.", "u_i": "Unit direction z_i/||z_i|| in the strict ball run.", "mu_i": "1-2h_i/||z_i||>0, coefficient in its displacement recursion.", "K_rep": "Finite maximum of exact error-to-slack ratios in equation8.", "Lambda": "Positive lift scale, fixed by equation13 when preserving a negative defect.", "V_ball": "Primitive energy of the one fixed ball-projection repair trajectory.", "K": "Compact convex hull of0 and all output gradients.", "F_K": "Projection function max_(G in K)(<X,G>-||G||^2/2).", "P_K": "Nearest-point projection onto K.", "W": "Linear span of the output gradients in the optional dimension compression."}}
{"id": "e7b04732b4b3738a", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T14:36:59.658275+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Exact two-cell innovation policy for ANY retained affine-envelope prefix: fix the old component q_0, two offsets, and next reversal beta; every old/future-plane constraint is affine in r=||q||^2, giving an explicit feasible interval. The two-block payoff is affine with coefficient K=2 beta(S_1+b)-(2S_2+1)beta^2, so its exact optimum is a specified interval endpoint. Immediate cost and later gain are both explicit. A fully checked arbitrary-block family repays the fresh-direction cost by a factor [(4tau+2)M+1]/(8M-4), unbounded in the next bridge factor tau, while preserving the true closest minimizer.", "evidence": "# Exact two-cell budget for a fresh orthogonal direction\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED self-contained proof candidate. No solver or verifier\ncall. This gives an exact conditional choice of innovation energy for two\nspecified cells, not a uniform comparison for arbitrary complete schedules.\n\n## 1. Fixed old geometry and the two future cells\n\nLet J_old(z)=max_j(eta_j+<a_j,z>) be a finite maximum including zero,\nwith minimum zero at origin. Assume origin is a true closest minimizer\nto the initial point, at distance R>0. Retain all old queried proximal\npoints z_k and their values H_k=J_old(z_k). These determine the old\nsmooth-envelope jets. Let X be the fixed next starting position after\nthe prescribed checkpoint from this retained prefix.\n\nPrescribe a first nonnegative ordinary list of sum S_1, then a checkpoint\nb>=0, then a second nonnegative ordinary list of sum S_2. Define\n\n    C_1=S_1+1, C_2=S_2+1, L=S_1+b.\n\nTake q_0 in the old geometric span, offsets a_1,a_2<=0, and beta>0.\nAll these are fixed parameters in the result below. In a new orthogonal\ndirection e define\n\n    q=q_0+sqrt(r-||q_0||^2)e,  r>=||q_0||^2,\n    ell_1(z)=a_1+<q,z>,\n    ell_2(z)=a_2-<beta q,z>,\n    J_r=max(J_old,ell_1,ell_2), f_r=env_1 J_r,\n    h=<q_0,X>.\n\nThe old span contains X, all old normals, all old proximal points and\nthe initial point. Thus every inner product of q with old data equals\nthe corresponding product with q_0, while ||q||^2=r. We intend to use\ngradient q on the first ordinary block and gradient -beta q on the\nsecond. The new direction is chosen before the actual run. No adaptive\nchange of the global function occurs.\n\n## 2. All legality conditions are affine in r\n\nAt old queried proximal points, preserving both new planes requires\n\n    a_1+<q_0,z_k><=H_k,\n    a_2-beta<q_0,z_k><=H_k.                                      (1)\n\nThese conditions do not involve r. At a first-block proximal point\nX-tq, t in [1,C_1], comparing ell_1 with old plane j gives\n\n    a_1+h-eta_j-<a_j,X>+t<a_j,q_0>-t r>=0.                       (2)\n\nComparing it with the future plane ell_2 gives\n\n    a_1-a_2+(1+beta)h-(1+beta)t r>=0.                            (3)\n\nThe first-block query using the checkpoint is included at t=C_1.\nAfter that checkpoint the second block starts at X-Lq. Its proximal\npoints are X-(L-t beta)q, t in [1,C_2]. Comparing ell_2 with old\nplane j gives\n\n    a_2-beta h-eta_j-<a_j,X>\n       +(L-t beta)<a_j,q_0>+beta(L-t beta)r>=0.                   (4)\n\nComparing it with ell_1 gives\n\n    a_2-a_1-(1+beta)h+(1+beta)(L-t beta)r>=0.                     (5)\n\nThe zero plane is already one of the old planes in (2) and (4).\nEvery expression is affine in t, so (2)-(3) need be checked only at\nt=1,C_1 and (4)-(5) only at t=1,C_2. These are exactly the actual\nendpoint samples of the ordinary blocks. Intermediates are convex\ncombinations of endpoint inequalities even if a block contains very\nunequal nonnegative steps or zero steps.\n\nConsequently, after (1), the EXACT allowed set for r is the intersection\nof r>=||q_0||^2 with the finitely many affine half-line conditions\n(2)-(5) at these endpoints. It is a closed interval [r_min,r_max],\npossibly empty. A zero coefficient in any inequality requires checking\nits constant term; it must not be divided away. Its endpoints are\nexplicit maxima and minima of the corresponding scalar ratios.\n\nIf it is nonempty, it is bounded: the zero-plane condition (2) at\nt=C_1 gives r<=(a_1+h)/C_1. Thus no unbounded variance or limiting\nparameter is being used. Every allowed r is realized by the displayed\nsingle new coordinate, and all samples belong to one fixed J_r.\n\nHere is the smooth-function justification. When the selected affine\nplane is maximal at a proposed proximal point z, its normal is a\nsubgradient of J_r at z. Since x-z is that normal, z uniquely minimizes\nJ_r(z)+||x-z||^2/2. This produces precisely the claimed gradient of\nthe envelope at the queried x. Subgradient monotonicity proves the\nenvelope gradient is 1-Lipschitz, and the envelope is convex and\ndifferentiable. The position recursions therefore follow the complete\nprescribed schedule. Adding the two nonpositive-intercept planes keeps\norigin a minimizer and shrinks the old zero set. Since origin was\nalready closest, the actual distance remains exactly R.\n\n## 3. The exact lookahead policy and its immediate cost\n\nAt the first block endpoint, the normalized objective is\n\n    V_1(r)=[2a_1+2h-(2C_1-1)r]/R^2.                            (6)\n\nAt the second block endpoint, it is\n\n    V_2(r)=[2a_2-2beta h+K r]/R^2,\n    K=2beta L-(2C_2-1)beta^2.                                  (7)\n\nThus the exact optimal innovation energy over this entire family is\n\n    r_opt=r_max if K>0,\n          r_min if K<0,\n          any point of the interval if K=0.                     (8)\n\nThis is a concrete choice from retained geometry and the supplied\nfuture schedule. It does not assume that the feasible interval exists;\nthe interval test gives the exact answer. All gradient magnitudes and\nthe true distance R appear in the formulas.\n\nFor r_2>=r_1 in the feasible interval, the immediate price and subsequent\ngain are exactly\n\n    V_1(r_1)-V_1(r_2)=(2C_1-1)(r_2-r_1)/R^2,\n    V_2(r_2)-V_2(r_1)=K(r_2-r_1)/R^2.                            (9)\n\nIf 0<beta<=L/C_2, all the second-block coefficients of r in (4)-(5)\nare nonnegative, and K>=beta L/C_2>0 when L>0. Thus first-block\nlegality puts upper bounds on the fresh energy, the second block puts\nlower bounds (with zero-coefficient exceptions checked), and lookahead\nchooses the largest allowed amount. This contrasts with (6), which\nstrictly prefers the smallest amount whenever both are feasible. The\nratio of future gain to immediate cost is exactly K/(2C_1-1).\n\nFor larger beta no monotonicity shortcut is made: the signed half-line\nintersection and the sign of K in (8) still give the exact solution.\n\n## 4. A full arbitrary-block family that repays the cost\n\nChoose M>1 and tau>=2. Start at x_init=(1,0), put p=(1/M,0), and\ntake J_old(z)=max(0,<p,z>). First apply ANY nonnegative block of sum\nM-1 with gradient p, followed by checkpoint 4M+1. Its endpoint is\nX=(-4,0), and all of its proximal samples are d p, 0<=d<=M-1.\nIts minimum zero is closest at origin, since every zero has first\ncoordinate nonpositive; R=1.\n\nIn Sections 1-3 take\n\n    q_0=(-2/M,0), a_1=a_2=0, beta=1/2,\n    S_1=S_2=M-1, b=tau M+1, L=(tau+1)M, h=8/M.\n\nThe new planes at every old positive proximal point are respectively\nnegative and identical to its existing p-plane, so (1) holds.\nThe exact feasible innovation interval is\n\n    4/M^2<=r<=8/M^2.                                             (10)\n\nThe lower endpoint is the PSD condition r>=||q_0||^2. The zero-plane\nfirst-block endpoint gives the upper endpoint r<=h/M=8/M^2. The\nold p-plane bound in (2) is\n\n    r<=-2/M^2+12/(Mt), t=1,M,\n\nwhich is at least 10/M^2 at its more restrictive endpoint. Condition\n(3) gives exactly the same bound as the zero plane. Thus every r in\n(10) passes all first-block and future-plane tests.\n\nFor the second block, beta<=L/C_2=tau+1, so every test improves as r\nincreases. It remains to check the lower endpoint. There q=q_0 is\nscalar, the second block starts at (2tau-2,0), and its gradient\n-q/2=p is exactly the retained old positive normal. Its proximal\npoints have first coordinates between 2tau-3 and 2tau-2-1/M, all\npositive for tau>=2. The p plane is maximal, and the q plane is\nnegative. This checks every second-block test at the lower endpoint;\ntheir affine monotonicity proves them for the whole interval. Hence\n(10) is exact, and every internal and terminal query is legal.\n\nAt the two ends of (10), the first-block scores are\n\n    V_1(4/M^2)=8/M+4/M^2,\n    V_1(8/M^2)=8/M^2.\n\nThe second-block scores are\n\n    V_2(4/M^2)=(4tau-6)/M+1/M^2,\n    V_2(8/M^2)=(8tau-4)/M+2/M^2.                               (11)\n\nThus spending a fresh squared component 4/M^2 costs\n8/M-4/M^2 immediately and earns\n\n    (4tau+2)/M+1/M^2\n\nat the next block endpoint. The exact ratio is\n\n    [(4tau+2)M+1]/(8M-4),                                      (12)\n\nwhich is greater than one for tau>=2 and is unbounded as tau grows.\nAll three ordinary blocks may have completely different nonperiodic\nstep patterns; only their prescribed equal total masses are used.\nThere is no dimension or closest-minimizer loss in (11)-(12).\n\nThis example is a test of the general innovation budget, not a new\nuniform B_n theorem. Its main point is that the direction discarded\nby a greedy immediate-cell optimizer has an exact, demonstrably useful\nfuture value. The arbitrary-prefix interval in Sections 1-3 supplies\nthe actual legality budget for such decisions.\n\n## Status and next interface\n\nThe fact graph and shared findings were searched first. Targeted Matlas\nretrieval gave no useful external theorem, and none is used. All new\nformulas, old/future-plane checks, and normalization arguments are in\nthis joint proof. No unverified predecessor is invoked as a fact.\n\nThe remaining issue is choosing the old parallel vector q_0, offsets,\nand reversal beta over many cells so that the complete exact B_n bound\nholds for every prescribed schedule. The innovation amount is now an\nexplicit endpoint choice conditional on those parameters; their global\nchoice is not replaced by an existence assertion here.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "workers/max/research/max_two_cell_innovation_budget.md", "related_findings": ["9c3632026c522612", "08c9c955dad85bba"], "status": "unverified; no new verifier allocation", "fact_ids": [], "scope": "explicit conditional two-cell parameter policy and exact lookahead budget; not uniform B_n comparison"}, "glossary": {"J_old": "Retained finite maximum of affine planes, including zero, with origin an actual closest minimizer.", "R": "The genuine initial distance to that closest minimizer.", "X": "Fixed starting position of the first new ordinary block.", "S_1,S_2": "Prescribed nonnegative ordinary step sums in the two new cells.", "C_1,C_2,L": "S_1+1, S_2+1, and S_1+b, where b is the intervening prescribed checkpoint.", "q_0": "Fixed first new gradient component inside the old geometric span.", "r": "Squared norm of the first new gradient q, obtained by one orthogonal innovation.", "a_1,a_2": "Fixed nonpositive offsets of the two new affine planes.", "beta": "Fixed positive reversal coefficient, so the second new gradient is -beta q.", "h": "The retained scalar inner product <q_0,X>.", "r_min,r_max": "Exact endpoints of the affine half-line feasibility intersection from every old/future plane.", "K": "2 beta L-(2C_2-1)beta^2, the coefficient of fresh squared-gradient energy in the second endpoint payoff.", "M,tau": "Parameters M>1 and tau>=2 of the explicit arbitrary-block test family."}}
{"id": "1a537d9464d4c619", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T14:37:27.668826+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C23 exact new nonperiodic scalar class result: with a common quadratic core of curvature reciprocal to any ruler checkpoint, outer logarithmic derivative growth <=1/2, and arbitrary two-ray shapes within factor13/8, EVERY trajectory on Hsc_m that ever crosses the minimizer subsequently hits it exactly. The complete class worst-case objective is attained by a Huber function and equals mu_core/(1+2 mu_core ssc_m), hence is uniformly o(U_(Nsc_m)^ZJ). Unlike C21, radial secants may vary without bound.", "evidence": "# Exact last-crossing capture for nonperiodic scalar profiles\n\nStatus: complete candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. No fact_submit is\nallocated. All new ingredients are proved in this document; earlier scalar\nfindings are not premises. This is a scoped obstruction with an exact\nworst-case value, not an all-scalar upper bound.\n\n## 1. A class with unbounded secant variation\n\nSet c_sc=13/5, a_sc=c_sc^(-17), bsc_j=a_sc c_sc^j, and\n\n    Hsc_0=(), Hsc_(j+1)=(Hsc_j,bsc_j,Hsc_j),\n    Nsc_j=2^j-1,\n    ssc_j=sum Hsc_j=a_sc(c_sc^j-2^j)/(c_sc-2).\n\nChoose any integer ell>=17 and put mu_core=1/bsc_ell<=1. Choose a radius\nR_core>0. On each of the positive and negative rays choose a locally\nabsolutely continuous function G_sigma:[0,infinity)->[0,infinity) such that\n\n    G_sigma(R)=R                         for 0<=R<=1,\n    0<=R G_sigma'(R)<=G_sigma(R)/2       for almost every R>1. (1)\n\nThe two ray profiles can differ and need not be periodic. Require only\n\n    8/13 <= G_+(R)/G_-(R) <= 13/8        for R>=1.          (2)\n\nDefine one fixed physical derivative, before running the algorithm, by\n\n    g(x)= mu_core R_core G_+(x/R_core)   for x>=0,\n    g(x)=-mu_core R_core G_-(-x/R_core)  for x<=0,\n    f(x)=integral_0^x g(u)du.\n\nThese are globally convex differentiable1-smooth functions with unique\nminimizer0. In fact their derivatives are mu_core-Lipschitz. To see this,\nintegrating(1) gives 1<=G_sigma(R)<=sqrt(R) for R>=1 and\n0<=G_sigma'(R)<=1/(2sqrt(R))<=1/2 there. On the core the derivative is1;\nacross0 use G_+(R)+G_-(T)<=R+T. Thus the normalized radial functions\nare monotone and1-Lipschitz on their whole domains.\n\nFor each fixed mu_core and every horizon m, the exact supremum over this\nclass, over R_core and all nonzero initial points, is\n\n    sup 2f(x_(Nsc_m))/x_0^2\n         = mu_core/(1+2 mu_core ssc_m).                 (3)\n\nMore specifically, every trajectory with a strict sign crossing ends\nexactly at the minimizer. The remaining trajectories never change sign\nand obey the upper bound in(3). Both statements use all actual GD steps.\n\nThe class permits arbitrarily many horizon-dependent knots and secants\ntending to zero at infinity. For example any measurable elasticity\nw(R) in[0,1/2] specifies an even member by\nG(R)=exp(integral_1^R w(t)dt/t) outside its core. In particular constant\nw=1/4 gives secants G(R)/R=R^(-3/4), with unbounded variation.\n\nA nonperiodic asymmetric example is obtained by defining, for z>=0,\n\n    eta(z)=(1/8)sin(log(1+z)),\n    G_+(R)=R^(1/4)exp( eta(log R)/2),\n    G_-(R)=R^(1/4)exp(-eta(log R)/2),       R>=1.\n\nHere |eta|<=1/8, |eta'|<=1/8, so the two elasticities lie in[3/16,5/16]\nand their ratio lies in[exp(-1/8),exp(1/8)] inside[8/13,13/8]. The latter\nfollows, for example, from exp(1/8)<=1/(1-1/8)=8/7<13/8. Arbitrary\npiecewise absolutely continuous eta with the same bounds is also allowed,\nwith any finite number of breakpoints chosen as a function of m.\n\nThe complete function values in normalized coordinates are\n\n    F(sigma R)=R^2/2                         for R<=1,\n    F(sigma R)=1/2+integral_1^R G_sigma(u)du  for R>=1,\n    f(x)=mu_core R_core^2 F(x/R_core).                    (4)\n\nNo function-value area is omitted in(3) or(4).\n\n## 2. An exact bound for the crossing cost\n\nFix either one of the radial functions G in(1). Define its increasing\nflow clock on positive radii by\n\n    Clock_G(R)=integral_1^R du/G(u).\n\nThis equals log R on the core. For R>=1 put q(R)=R/G(R). By(1),\n\n    q'(R)=[1-RG'(R)/G(R)]/G(R)>=1/[2G(R)],\n\nand q(1)=1. Consequently\n\n    Clock_G(R)<=2(q(R)-1),             R>=1.            (5)\n\nWe claim that for every h>=2, every r>0, and every positive overshoot\nz=hG(r)-r, one has\n\n    Clock_G(z)<=h-2.                                    (6)\n\nIf z<1, its clock is negative and(6) holds. Suppose z>=1. If r<=1,\nthen z=(h-1)r<=h-1. Since G>=1 beyond1, its clock is at most z-1<=h-2.\n\nNow let r>=1 and write u=G(r), q=r/u. If z>=r, monotonicity and(5)\ngive\n\n    Clock_G(z)<=2(q-1)+(z-r)/u\n                  =2(q-1)+h-2q=h-2.\n\nIf 1<=z<=r, integration of the elasticity bound in(1) gives\nG(z)>=G(r)sqrt(z/r). Therefore(5) gives\n\n    Clock_G(z)<=2(z/G(z)-1)\n       <=2(sqrt(rz)/u-1)\n       =2sqrt(q(h-q))-2<=h-2.\n\nThe last inequality is q(h-q)<=h^2/4. This proves(6) for all cases,\nwith no homogeneity, curvature constancy, or choice of knots at runtime.\n\nFor a crossing from source ray sigma to target ray tau, the landing\nradius is still z=hG_sigma(r)-r. If z>=1, condition(2) implies\n\n    Clock_(G_tau)(z)\n       <=(13/8) Clock_(G_sigma)(z)\n       <=(13/8)(h-2).                                 (7)\n\nIf z<1 its target clock is negative, so(7) remains valid for h>=2.\n\nFor a noncrossing step r'=r-hG_sigma(r)>0 on the same ray,\n\n    Clock_(G_sigma)(r)-Clock_(G_sigma)(r')\n      =integral_(r')^r du/G_sigma(u)\n      >=(r-r')/G_sigma(r)=h.                           (8)\n\nThese are bounds on the actual fixed derivative, not independent-secant\nchoices. Equations(7)--(8) are the needed asymmetric flow-state law.\n\n## 3. The actual suffix forces capture after the last crossing\n\nNormalize positions by R_core and the objective by mu_core R_core^2.\nThe GD steps become h'=mu_core h. Write\n\n    b'_j=mu_core bsc_j=c_sc^(j-ell),\n    S'_j=mu_core ssc_j.\n\nIn particular b'_ell=1, and the normalized gradient is exactly x on the\nunit core. A step of size b'_ell therefore sends every core point to0.\n\nFor all j>=17,\n\n    S'_j/b'_j=[1-(2/c_sc)^j]/(c_sc-2)>=13/8.            (9)\n\nIndeed (2/c_sc)^3<1/2 and (2/c_sc)^2<4/5 imply its seventeenth power\nis less than1/40. Also\n\n    S'_ell=[1-(2/c_sc)^ell]/(c_sc-2)<5/3.              (10)\n\nSince G_sigma(r)<=r everywhere, a strict crossing can occur only at a\nstep h'>1. Thus it has some level j>=ell+1>=18 and h'=b'_j>=c_sc>2.\nIf the trajectory reaches0 anywhere it remains there. In the other case,\nchoose its last strict crossing and call its level j and target ray tau.\nBy(7) its landing clock is at most(13/8)(b'_j-2).\n\nEvery occurrence of level j in the ruler word is immediately followed\nby the complete word Hsc_j. Since j>=ell+1, that suffix contains a\nstep of level ell. The steps after the last such step in Hsc_j form\nexactly Hsc_ell. Therefore the physical normalized step sum after the\nchosen crossing and before that cancelling step is at least\n\n    S'_j-1-S'_ell.                                     (11)\n\nThere are no subsequent strict crossings by choice of the last crossing.\nIf no iterate has yet reached0, all these steps move inward on ray tau,\nso(8) adds their exact time lengths. At the input to the cancelling step,\nits clock is at most\n\n    (13/8)(b'_j-2)-(S'_j-1-S'_ell)\n      <= S'_ell-9/4 < -7/12 <0.                      (12)\n\nNegative clock means radius less than1. The next actual step has size1\nand gradient equal to its position, so it sends this iterate exactly to0.\nAll remaining steps keep it there. This proves the last-crossing assertion.\nIt also covers a landing in the core directly. If m<=ell+1, all steps\nare at most1 in normalized units and no strict crossing is possible.\n\n## 4. Exact objective on the surviving paths\n\nConsider a trajectory that never crosses, has nonzero endpoint of radius\nR_end, and normalized endpoint gradient magnitude u. All queried radii\nare at least R_end, so all queried gradient magnitudes are at least u.\nWriting S'=mu_core ssc_m and R_init=|x_0|/R_core, the exact position\ntelescope gives\n\n    R_init>=R_end+S' u.                               (13)\n\nEvery G_sigma is monotone1-Lipschitz and vanishes at0. Its complete\narea up to R_end is at most the area under min(r,u):\n\n    F(sigma R_end)<=u R_end-u^2/2.                     (14)\n\nUse(13) in(14) and complete the scalar square:\n\n    2F(sigma R_end)\n       <=2u R_init-(2S'+1)u^2\n       <=R_init^2/(2S'+1).                            (15)\n\nMultiplying the normalized ratio by mu_core proves the upper bound(3).\nCrossing trajectories have value0 by section3 and satisfy it too.\n\nFor equality take both profiles G_sigma(R)=min(R,1), which satisfy(1)\nand(2), and take normalized initial radius R_init=2S'+1. Every iterate\nthen remains on the positive plateau and the endpoint is R_end=S'+1.\nIts exact area is F(R_end)=S'+1/2, yielding equality in(15). This proves\nthe asserted supremum is attained, including m=0. The core scale and\nphysical normalization in(4) give precisely(3), not a gradient-norm proxy.\n\n## 5. Exact benchmark comparison and limits of the result\n\nUniformly in ell and R_core,\n\n    mu_core/(1+2 mu_core ssc_m)<=1/(1+2ssc_m),          (16)\n\nbecause mu_core<=1. Equality in this further supremum is attained at\nell=17, where mu_core=1. For m>=3 the sum formula and\n(2/c_sc)^m<1/2 give\n\n    1/(1+2ssc_m) <= (c_sc-2)c_sc^(-m)/a_sc.\n\nFor completeness, the comparison uses the exact recursions in accepted\nfact b98bb807d514e80d, not a numerical asymptotic constant. In shifted\nvariables C_n=S_circle(n)+1 and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1 they are\nC_0=B_0=1,\n\n    C_n=max_j Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_j Gamma(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),\n    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n    Gamma(A,B)=2A+B/2+sqrt(B^2+8AB)/2.\n\nThe needed bound B_n<=1+4n^(4/3) has a short independent proof.\nThe positive-root formula gives\nOmega(A,B)<=(A^(3/4)+B^(3/4))^(4/3) once\nt^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1 on[0,1]. With\nu=t^(1/3), v=(1-t)^(1/3), the difference is\nu^3v^3[1/(1+u+u^2)+1/(1+v+v^2)-uv]>=0, because both reciprocals\nare at least1/3 and uv<=2^(-2/3)<2/3. Induction yields\nC_n<=(n+1)^(4/3). Then Gamma(A,B)<=B+4A and the power-sum inequality\ngive B_n<=1+4n^(4/3) by induction. Consequently the ratio of(16) to\nU_(Nsc_m)^ZJ is at most\n\n    ((c_sc-2)/a_sc)c_sc^(-m)[1+4(2^m-1)^(4/3)] ->0,\n\nsince c_sc^3>16. This comparison is uniform over all admitted profiles,\ncore levels, radii, knots, initial points, and horizon-dependent choices.\n\nThe exact quadratic core is load-bearing: its reciprocal curvature must\noccur as a physical ruler step. The argument does not replace that step\nby an approximate cancellation. It also uses outer elasticity at most1/2\nand the factor13/8 comparison of the two radial derivatives. The result is\ntherefore not an all-scalar obstruction. Removing core resonance, allowing\nfaster outer growth, or allowing much larger changing ray asymmetry are\nseparate unresolved mechanisms.\n\nThe local paper2103.01338 was checked for the motivation that nonlinear\nscalar profiles can behave differently from quadratics. Its log-cosh\nexample concerns fractal Chebyshev steps and is described there as a\nsimulation; it supplies no theorem for this ruler word. Matlas returned\nno applicable theorem for(6).
