No external result is used in the proof.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "workers/max3/research/c23_last_crossing_capture.md", "fact_ids": ["b98bb807d514e80d"], "finding_ids": ["825ee1d20fda8097", "3d2994746c1d983e", "1a79eef5f894e223"], "status": "complete self-contained candidate; NOT verifier-accepted; no fact_submit allocation", "new_mechanism": "Exact fixed-profile crossing clock <=h-2; comparable target clock <=(13/8)(h-2); last-crossing suffix returns inside core before an actual cancelling step.", "scope": "Arbitrary nonperiodic/sub-square-root outer radial profiles, unbounded secant variation and horizon-dependent knots; resonant common quadratic core and bounded ray ratio remain required.", "is_fragile": false, "fragility_reason": "", "suggested_followup": "none", "justification_type": "calculation", "confidence": 0.95}, "glossary": {"mu_core": "1/bsc_ell for a selected integer ell>=17; the shared exact core curvature.", "R_core": "Positive common radius of the exact quadratic core.", "G_sigma": "Normalized radial derivative, equal to R on[0,1] with 0<=R G_sigma'(R)<=G_sigma(R)/2 beyond1.", "Clock_G": "The increasing flow clock integral_1^R du/G(u), equal to log R in the core.", "ssc_m": "Total physical step sum a_sc(c_sc^m-2^m)/(c_sc-2).", "Hsc_m": "The fixed ruler word with growthc_sc=13/5 and first stepa_sc=c_sc^(-17)."}}
{"id": "1560c4b6a302dc15", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T14:43:55.204491+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C24 enlarges the exact nonperiodic capture class: outer elasticity may be any p_grow in[1/2,2/3], with ray-comparison factor kappa_ray<=13/[8(p_grow/(1-p_grow))^p_grow]. A reciprocal-checkpoint quadratic core still forces every crossing trajectory to hit0, and the exact full-class objective supremum is mu_core/(1+2mu_core ssc_m). The proof independently derives its crossing clock, all suffix timing and full areas.", "evidence": "# The core-resonance boundary and a larger capture class\n\nStatus: two self-contained unverified results. No verifier allocation.\nThe counterexample concerns exact annihilation, not the objective upper\nbound. The second result extends the capture class without using C23 as\nan unverified premise.\n\n## 1. Removing exact resonance breaks annihilation on the actual ruler word\n\nLet c_sc=13/5, a_sc=c_sc^(-17), bsc_j=a_sc c_sc^j and\nHsc_(j+1)=(Hsc_j,bsc_j,Hsc_j), with Hsc_0 empty and Nsc_j=2^j-1 its\nlength. Take the single fixed\nfunction\n\n    f(x)=x^2/4                 for |x|<=1,\n    f(x)=|x|/2-1/4            for |x|>=1.\n\nIts derivative is g(x)=(1/2)max(-1,min(x,1)), globally monotone and\n1/2-Lipschitz, and its unique minimizer is0. Its two outer radial\nprofiles are constant, so every outer growth and symmetry requirement\nfrom the capture proposal holds. Its core curvature is mu=1/2.\n\nStart at x_0=1 and use the complete actual word Hsc_19. Each step in\nHsc_18 is at most bsc_17=1. Thus while in the core it has multiplier\n1-h/2 in[1/2,1), preserving sign and decreasing radius. Define the exact\npositive product\n\n    P18=product_(j=0)^17\n             (1-bsc_j/2)^(2^(17-j)).\n\nThe first Hsc_18 sends x_0 to P18. The next actual checkpoint is\nbsc_18=13/5, with core multiplier1-13/10=-3/10. The final Hsc_18\nagain remains in the core and multiplies by P18. Hence\n\n    x_(Nsc_19)=-(3/10)P18^2 !=0,\n    2f(x_(Nsc_19))/x_0^2=(9/200)P18^4>0.\n\nAll intermediate points are in the core, and the central step is a\nstrict crossing. This is a fully legal counterexample to the extension\n\"every crossing trajectory ends exactly at0\" after dropping the\nresonance assumption. No physical ruler step equals2: for j>=17 it is\n(13/5)^(j-17), and prime factorization excludes equality with2; steps\nbefore17 are less than1. Thus no core multiplier is zero.\n\nThe example supplies no benchmark-scale lower bound: its exact payoff\nis small and no asymptotic separation is asserted. It identifies a\nspecific invalid extension of the capture argument, not instability\nof the actual scalar problem. Nonresonance is necessary to escape this\nannihilation mechanism, but this example does not establish that it is\nsufficient to construct a hard profile.\n\n## 2. A continuous tradeoff between outer growth and ray asymmetry\n\nFix any p_grow in[1/2,2/3], put beta_grow=1-p_grow and\n\n    K_grow=(p_grow/beta_grow)^(p_grow),\n    1<=kappa_ray<=13/(8 K_grow).\n\nChoose an integer ell>=17 and mu_core=1/bsc_ell<=1, a core radius\nR_core>0, and two locally absolutely continuous radial functions with\n\n    G_sigma(R)=R                           on[0,1],\n    0<=R G_sigma'(R)<=p_grow G_sigma(R)     almost everywhere beyond1,\n    1/kappa_ray<=G_+(R)/G_-(R)<=kappa_ray   for R>=1.\n\nSet g(sigma R_core R)=sigma mu_core R_core G_sigma(R) and integrate\nfrom0 to define f. These are legal fixed convex differentiable\nmu_core-smooth functions: 1<=G_sigma(R)<=R^(p_grow)<=R beyond1,\nand G_sigma'<=p_grow R^(p_grow-1)<=1. Across the origin the same\nLipschitz bound follows from G_sigma(R)<=R. All complete values are\n\n    f(sigma R_core R)/(mu_core R_core^2)\n       =R^2/2                         for R<=1,\n       =1/2+integral_1^R G_sigma(u)du  for R>=1.\n\nFor this entire class, for every m,\n\n    sup 2f(x_(Nsc_m))/x_0^2\n       =mu_core/(1+2 mu_core ssc_m),                    (1)\n\nwhere ssc_m=a_sc(c_sc^m-2^m)/(c_sc-2). Again every path with a strict\ncrossing ends exactly at0. The class allows arbitrary nonperiodic\nelasticities in the specified range and horizon-dependent knot counts.\n\n### Standalone crossing estimate\n\nFor one radial profile write Clock(R)=integral_1^R du/G(u) and\nq(R)=R/G(R). For R>=1,\n\n    q'(R)>=beta_grow/G(R),\n    Clock(R)<=(q(R)-1)/beta_grow.                      (2)\n\nFor a crossing z=hG(r)-r>0 at h>=2 we claim\n\n    Clock(z)<=K_grow h-1/beta_grow.                    (3)\n\nFirst, beta_grow K_grow=p_grow^(p_grow) beta_grow^(beta_grow)\nis at least1/2 on the stated range. The logarithm of twice this\nquantity is0 at p_grow=1/2 and has derivative\nlog(p_grow/beta_grow)>=0. Consequently K_grow>=1/(2 beta_grow).\nThe right side of(3) is nonnegative for h>=2. If z<1 this proves(3).\nIf r<=1<=z, monotonicity gives Clock(z)<=z-1<=h-2, which is at most\nthe right side because beta_grow<=1/2.\n\nNow suppose r,z>=1 and put u=G(r), q=r/u. When z>=r we have q<=h/2;\nusing(2) and monotonicity,\n\n    Clock(z)<=(q-1)/beta_grow+(z-r)/u\n      =h+(1/beta_grow-2)q-1/beta_grow\n      <=h/(2 beta_grow)-1/beta_grow\n      <=K_grow h-1/beta_grow.\n\nWhen z<=r, integration of the growth bound gives\nG(z)>=u(z/r)^(p_grow). Hence\n\n    z/G(z)<=q^(p_grow)(h-q)^(beta_grow)\n       <=p_grow^(p_grow) beta_grow^(beta_grow) h.\n\nThe last maximum occurs at q=p_grow h; differentiating its logarithm\nestablishes it on0<q<h. Substitution into(2) proves(3).\n\nFor a crossing to the other ray with z>=1, the target clock is at most\nkappa_ray times its source clock. For z<1 the target clock is negative.\nThus the target cost is bounded by\n\n    kappa_ray(K_grow h-1/beta_grow).                   (4)\n\nAny noncrossing step decreases its own ray clock by at least h, since\nthe integral of1/G between its new and old radii is at least its\ndisplacement divided by the old gradient.\n\n### The actual last crossing and the cancelling step\n\nNormalize the physical GD steps by mu_core. Put b'_j=c_sc^(j-ell)\nand S'_j=mu_core ssc_j. The level-ell step has size1 and cancels every\nunit-core point. Since G_sigma(R)<=R, any strict crossing has level\nj>=ell+1 and size b'_j>=c_sc>2. Its immediately following Hsc_j\ncontains a final level-ell step, with trailing suffix Hsc_ell.\n\nFor j>=17,\n\n    S'_j/b'_j=[1-(2/c_sc)^j]/(c_sc-2)>=13/8,\n    S'_ell<5/3.                                       (5)\n\nThe first inequality follows from (2/c_sc)^17<1/40, itself implied by\n(2/c_sc)^3<1/2 and (2/c_sc)^2<4/5.\n\nAfter the last strict crossing, if no point has yet reached0, all\nsubsequent steps move inward on the same ray. The time before the\nspecified cancelling step is S'_j-1-S'_ell. Its input clock is therefore\nless than or equal to\n\n    (kappa_ray K_grow-13/8)b'_j\n                         +1+S'_ell-kappa_ray/beta_grow. (6)\n\nBecause the coefficient of b'_j is nonpositive, use b'_j>=c_sc and\nS'_ell<5/3. The resulting upper bound increases with kappa_ray, since\nK_grow c_sc-1/beta_grow>=0. At its allowed maximum it becomes\n\n    8/3-13/(8 beta_grow K_grow)<0.                    (7)\n\nTo check the strict sign without a decimal estimate, the quantity\nbeta_grow K_grow increases with p_grow and at2/3 equals4^(1/3)/3.\nSince4^(1/3)<8/5, it is less than8/15. The bound in(7) is therefore\nless than8/3-195/64=-73/192. Negative clock places the iterate inside\nthe quadratic core, and the next actual size1 step sends it to0.\nThis proves the annihilation assertion for every crossing path.\n\n### Complete value bound and sharpness\n\nFor a surviving noncrossing path let u be its terminal normalized\ngradient magnitude and R_end its radius. With S'=mu_core ssc_m,\nits exact position telescope yields R_init>=R_end+S'u. Monotonicity\nand1-smoothness bound the full function area by uR_end-u^2/2. Hence\n\n    2F_end<=2uR_init-(2S'+1)u^2<=R_init^2/(2S'+1).\n\nRescaling proves the upper bound(1). It is attained by the symmetric\nHuber choice G_sigma(R)=min(R,1), starting at R_init=2S'+1; its entire\ntrajectory stays on the plateau and has R_end=S'+1 and\nF_end=S'+1/2. Empty horizons are included.\n\nThis proves the continuous tradeoff independently of C23. In particular,\neven outer profiles can have arbitrary elasticity up to2/3, with no\nperiodicity, while the p_grow=1/2 endpoint allows ray ratio13/8. A\nmatching core checkpoint remains indispensable to this exact capture\nargument, as the legal Hsc_19 example in section1 demonstrates.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "workers/max3/research/c24_core_resonance_boundary.md", "finding_ids": ["825ee1d20fda8097", "1a537d9464d4c619"], "status": "complete self-contained unverified candidate; no fact_submit allocated", "scope": "Arbitrary nonperiodic/horizon-dependent outer profiles within explicit growth/asymmetry tradeoff and resonant core; not all scalar functions.", "new_mechanism": "Clock(hG(r)-r)<=K_grow*h-1/(1-p_grow); the actual last-crossing suffix produces clock<-73/192 before the cancelling step.", "is_fragile": false, "fragility_reason": "", "suggested_followup": "none", "justification_type": "calculation", "confidence": 0.95}, "glossary": {"p_grow": "Upper bound in[1/2,2/3] on R G_sigma'(R)/G_sigma(R) outside the quadratic core.", "beta_grow": "1-p_grow.", "K_grow": "(p_grow/beta_grow)^p_grow, the proved crossing-clock slope.", "kappa_ray": "Two-sided bound for the ratio of radial derivatives; 1<=kappa_ray<=13/(8K_grow).", "mu_core": "Reciprocal of the physical checkpointbsc_ell, for an integerell>=17.", "R_core": "Arbitrary positive radius of the common exact quadratic core."}}
{"id": "b647bce3e53eb9bb", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T14:46:29.377103+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C25 stronger chronological quadratic bound: EVERY prefix of EVERY Hsc_m has scalar multiplier magnitude<=1 for every curvature in[0,1], without checkpoint resonance. Therefore any globally smooth-convex function that is exactly quadratic on a ball is indistinguishable from that quadratic when started in the ball: all actual iterates stay inside, even in arbitrary finite dimension. Full normalized objective <=(c_sc/a_sc)c_sc^(-m), uniformly o(U_(Nsc_m)^ZJ).", "evidence": "# Every ruler prefix preserves a quadratic neighborhood\n\nStatus: complete unverified candidate, no verifier allocation. This result\ndoes not assume a core curvature reciprocal to a schedule step. It excludes\ninitial points already inside a quadratic core; it does not exclude an\noutside initial point entering a core during a partial block.\n\n## Statement\n\nPut c_sc=13/5, a_sc=c_sc^(-17), bsc_j=a_sc c_sc^j,\nHsc_0=(), Hsc_(j+1)=(Hsc_j,bsc_j,Hsc_j), and Nsc_m=2^m-1. For every\nlambda in[0,1], every m>=0, and EVERY chronological prefix h_0,...,h_(t-1)\nof Hsc_m, including the empty prefix,\n\n    |product_(i=0)^(t-1)(1-lambda h_i)|<=1.             (1)\n\nWriting Q_m(lambda)=|product_(h in Hsc_m)(1-lambda h)|, also\n\n    lambda Q_m(lambda)^2 <= (c_sc/a_sc)c_sc^(-m).      (2)\n\nConsequently let f be any globally convex differentiable1-smooth function\non a finite-dimensional Euclidean space with minimizer0 and f(0)=0.\nSuppose that on the closed ball of radius R_core it is exactly\nf(x)=<x,A_core x>/2, where A_core is a real symmetric matrix satisfying\n0<=A_core<=I. For every nonzero initial point in that ball, the entire\nGD trajectory on Hsc_m stays in the same ball and sees only that\nquadratic. Thus\n\n    2f(x_(Nsc_m))/||x_0||^2\n         <=(c_sc/a_sc)c_sc^(-m).                       (3)\n\nThe nonlinear continuation outside the ball is arbitrary and can depend\non the horizon. It never influences the trajectory. Scalar cores of\nevery curvature lambda in(0,1], including nonresonant ones, are included.\n\n## 1. Scalar proof, including every intermediate prefix\n\nAbbreviate c=c_sc, a=a_sc, b_j=bsc_j and eta=13/8. The case lambda=0\nhas every product equal to1 and zero objective. Assume lambda>0 and let\nI be the first level with t=lambda b_I>1. Then I>=18 and 1<t<=c.\nEvery step of Hsc_I has lambda h<=1, so every one of its prefix products\nhas magnitude at most1. Its complete product is a number P in[0,1].\n\nThe physical sum is\n\n    ssc_I=a(c^I-2^I)/(c-2),\n    ssc_I/b_I=[1-(2/c)^I]/(c-2)>=eta.\n\nFor the last inequality, (2/c)^3<1/2 and (2/c)^2<4/5 imply\n(2/c)^17<1/40. The bound1-z<=exp(-z) on[0,1] therefore gives\n\n    P<=exp(-lambda ssc_I)<=exp(-eta t).                (4)\n\nThe word Hsc_(I+1) is (Hsc_I,b_I,Hsc_I). Before its middle step the\namplitude is at most P. At and after that step it is at most\n(t-1)P<1, since (t-1)P<=(c-1)exp(-eta)<1. The last strict inequality\nfollows from exp(eta)>1+eta>c-1. Thus every prefix of Hsc_(I+1) also\nhas amplification at most1.\n\nIts complete multiplier magnitude is Q_(I+1)=P^2(t-1). We need the\nstronger bound\n\n    Q_(I+1)<=1/[c lambda b_(I+1)].                     (5)\n\nSince b_(I+1)=c b_I, multiplying(5) by its denominator and using(4)\nreduces it to c^2 t(t-1)exp(-2eta t)<=1. The elementary exponential\nseries gives exp(2eta t)> (2eta t)^3/6, so the left side is at most\n\n    [3c^2/(4eta^3)](t-1)/t^2\n       <=3c^2/(16eta^3)<1.                            (6)\n\nHere (t-1)/t^2<=1/4 follows from (t-2)^2>=0, and the last inequality\nis a strict rational comparison at c=13/5 and eta=13/8. Zero factors\nin P cause no difficulty and only strengthen these inequalities.\n\nNow induct on j>=I+1. Suppose every prefix of Hsc_j has amplification\nat most1 and its complete multiplier satisfies Q_j<=1/(c lambda b_j).\nPut z=lambda b_j>1. In Hsc_(j+1) the first complete block has output\namplitude at most Q_j relative to its input. Its central checkpoint\ntherefore produces amplitude at most\n\n    (z-1)Q_j <=(z-1)/(c z)<1/c.\n\nEvery prefix of the following Hsc_j multiplies this by at most1. This\nproves(1) at the next level. Moreover the exact recursion gives\n\n    Q_(j+1)=Q_j^2(z-1)\n       <=(z-1)/(c^2 z^2)<1/(c^2 z)\n       =1/[c lambda b_(j+1)].                         (7)\n\nEquations(5)--(7) prove the induction. Earlier horizons m<=I already\nhave every lambda h<=1, completing the proof of(1) for all horizons.\n\n## 2. Uniform complete-block objective\n\nFor m<=I, equation(1) gives lambda Q_m^2<=lambda, while\nlambda=t/b_I<=c/b_I<=c/b_m. For m>=I+1, equation(7) gives\n\n    lambda Q_m^2<=1/[c^2 lambda b_m^2]\n       <1/(c^3 b_m)<=c/b_m,\n\nbecause lambda b_m=c^(m-I)t>c. As c/b_m=(c/a)c^(-m), this proves(2).\nThe bound is uniform over lambda, including horizon-dependent choices.\n\n## 3. Arbitrary nonlinear continuation is invisible from inside the core\n\nDiagonalize the real symmetric matrix A_core orthogonally. In each\neigendirection of eigenvalue lambda, quadratic GD multiplies by exactly\nthe scalar prefix product in(1). Hence every quadratic prefix map has\nEuclidean operator norm at most1, and its iterates stay within the\ninitial radius. At each such point the actual gradient of f is A_core x,\nincluding the boundary by differentiability and continuity. Induction\non the actual GD steps therefore identifies the actual iterates with\nthe quadratic iterates throughout the entire word.\n\nIn that orthonormal eigenbasis the exact final objective is the sum of\nlambda Q_m(lambda)^2 times the corresponding squared initial coordinate.\nUsing(2) termwise proves(3). This is full objective area and the original\nsquared-distance normalization. A semidefinite zero eigenspace is harmless.\n\n## 4. Exact-profile comparison\n\nUse the benchmark definitions from accepted fact b98bb807d514e80d:\nC_n=S_circle(n)+1 and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1 obey the positive-root joins\n\n    C_n=max_j Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_j Gamma(C_j,B_(n-j-1)), C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n    Gamma(A,B)=2A+B/2+sqrt(B^2+8AB)/2.\n\nTo make the comparison independent of earlier unverified findings,\nOmega(A,B)<=(A^(3/4)+B^(3/4))^(4/3) follows from\nt^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1. For u=t^(1/3) and\nv=(1-t)^(1/3), the difference is\nu^3v^3[1/(1+u+u^2)+1/(1+v+v^2)-uv]>=0: both reciprocals are at\nleast1/3 and uv<=2^(-2/3)<2/3. Induction gives C_n<=(n+1)^(4/3).\nThe bound Gamma(A,B)<=B+4A and the power-sum inequality then give\nB_n<=1+4n^(4/3). Thus the ratio of(3) to U_(Nsc_m)^ZJ is at most\n\n    (c_sc/a_sc)c_sc^(-m)[1+4(2^m-1)^(4/3)] ->0,\n\nbecause c_sc^3>16.\n\n## Scope\n\nChanging an arbitrary outside profile cannot create a hard instance\nfrom an initial point inside its exact quadratic neighborhood, even\nwhen the core curvature is off the checkpoint grid. To exploit nonlinear\ngeometry one must start outside that neighborhood, or use a function\nwith no exact quadratic neighborhood. Entrance during a partial block\nis different: this proof bounds prefixes of complete Hsc_j words, not\nevery suffix from every possible entrance time. No all-scalar claim is\nmade, and no new fact-graph node has been created.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "workers/max3/research/c25_quadratic_core_invariance.md", "fact_ids": ["b98bb807d514e80d"], "finding_ids": ["1ac286467a5e6ab4", "1a537d9464d4c619"], "status": "complete self-contained candidate; unverified; no new verifier allocation", "scope": "All starting points inside an exact quadratic neighborhood, arbitrary continuation outside; not outside starts, not arbitrary suffixes, not all scalar or all multidimensional functions.", "new_mechanism": "Before first activation all factors contract; thereafter Q_j<=1/(c_sc lambda bsc_j) makes every middle-step excursion<1/c_sc, preserved by exact word recursion.", "is_fragile": false, "fragility_reason": "", "suggested_followup": "none", "justification_type": "calculation", "confidence": 0.96}, "glossary": {"Q_m": "Absolute complete quadratic multiplier product_(h in Hsc_m)(1-lambda h).", "A_core": "Real symmetric positive semidefinite matrix with eigenvalues in[0,1], giving the function's exact quadratic expression on the core ball.", "R_core": "Positive radius of that exact quadratic ball.", "Hsc_m": "Actual ruler word Hsc_(j+1)=(Hsc_j,a_sc c_sc^j,Hsc_j), c_sc=13/5,a_sc=c_sc^(-17)."}}
{"id": "8f4d904e885084d7", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T14:47:05.230665+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Complete candidate prepend extension with retained support geometry: at a saturated bridge, the best constant-gradient auxiliary prefix is the attained convex minimization C_min over the explicit terminal halfspace of max{||ell*v-beta*x||^2,beta^2||x||^2+ell^2[sigma_K(v)-<x/D,v>]}. If C_min<||y+Tg||^2, an explicit finite slack mixture gives a legal negative primitive witness. Every cross edge and optimum edge is checked. Exact affine calibration and a negative 2D example show the earlier forced-core-prefix penalty can hide an available extension. General arbitrary-tail heredity remains unproved; no solver/verifier call.", "evidence": "# Prepending a saturated bridge with the actual supporting gradient hull\n\nStatus: complete candidate interface, NOT verifier-accepted. No solver or\nfact_submit. All calculations and interpolation checks are included here;\nno unverified result is used as a premise.\n\n## 1. Supplied data and the exact extension functional\n\nLet b be any nonnegative tail schedule of capacity D=1+sum b_i, with\nactual legal convex1-smooth sampled data(x_j,g_j,f_j), minimizer0 and\n\n  f_j=<g_j,x_j>-||g_j||^2/2                              (1)\n\nat every sample. Thus every incoming optimum slack is0. Define\n\n  K=conv{0,g_0,...,g_m}, sigma_K(v)=max_(g in K)<g,v>,\n  x=x_0, g=g_m, y=x_m-g_m, delta=<g,y>>=0.\n\nLet a be any nonnegative prefix block of capacity A. Choose r>0 with\n\n  T=A+D+r, rT=AD,\n  beta=T/D=A/r>1, ell=A+r=(beta-1)D.                    (2)\n\nFor v in the closed halfspace\n\n  <v,y><=delta+<x,y>/r,                                (3)\n\nput\n\n  C_ext(v)=max{\n    ||ell v-beta x||^2,\n    beta^2||x||^2+ell^2[sigma_K(v)-<x/D,v>]\n  }.                                                   (4)\n\nThis is a finite continuous convex coercive function. The halfspace is\nnonempty: in the equivalent variable p=v-x/r, p=0 is feasible because\ndelta>=0. Hence its minimum C_min is attained.\n\nWe prove the concrete implication\n\n  C_min<||y+Tg||^2  ==>  (a,1+r,b) is not primitive.     (5)\n\nThe construction retains the full tail gradient hull in sigma_K and\nthe actual terminal value delta in(3). No condition saying that missing\ninterpolation constraints are feasible is assumed: they are checked\nbelow. Formula(4) is the exact smallest auxiliary starting norm in the\nconstant-prefix-gradient extension family described next.\n\n## 2. The new prefix gradient and its supporting condition\n\nFix any v satisfying(3), put p=v-x/r, and define\n\n  z^2=max{0,\n    [sigma_K(x+rp)-<p,x>-r||p||^2]/r}.                   (6)\n\nTake the nonnegative square root z. Embed the whole supplied tail with\none additional zero coordinate. Give every new prefix query the same\ngradient P=(p,z). The last prefix query has point\n\n  X_L=(x,0)+(1+r)P,\n\nand the first query has point\n\n  X_start=(x,0)+(A+r)P.\n\nThe bridge step1+r lands exactly at the supplied tail initial point(x,0).\nAll individual steps of a are realized by this constant gradient,\nincluding zero steps. Assign every new value\nF=<P,X>-||P||^2/2, so its incoming optimum slack is0.\n\nLet Y_L=(x,0)+rP. Equation(6) says exactly\n\n  <(g_j,0)-P,Y_L><=0 for every tail gradient and for0.    (7)\n\nThe gradient0 condition gives <P,Y_L>>=0. Earlier prefix queries have\nproximal points Y_L+HP, with H their remaining prefix step sum, and\noutgoing optimum slacks <P,Y_L>+H||P||^2>=0.\n\nThe new-prefix-to-final-tail residual is\n\n  <P-(g,0),(y,0)>=<p,y>-delta<=0                       (8)\n\nby(3). Every tail query with zero remaining steps is identical to the\nfinal one, so(8) also handles all terminal zero-step duplicates.\n\nAt this stage the auxiliary array is not assumed legal at all other\ncross edges.\n\n## 3. Why the starting norm is exactly(4)\n\nFor fixed p, condition(7) is equivalent to the lower bound on z^2 in(6).\nThe squared starting norm is\n\n  ||x+ell p||^2+ell^2 z^2.\n\nIt increases with z^2, so(6) is its minimum. Expanding the maximum gives\n\n  max{||x+ell p||^2,\n      ||x||^2+ell(r-A)<p,x>/r+ell^2 sigma_K(p+x/r)}.\n\nSubstitute v=p+x/r. The first term becomes||ell v-beta x||^2.\nThe constant in the second term becomes beta^2||x||^2.\nFrom(2), ell(r-A)/r=-ell^2/D. Therefore the second term is\nbeta^2||x||^2+ell^2[sigma_K(v)-<x/D,v>], proving(4).\n\nThe halfspace(3) is precisely the remaining active condition(8).\nThus(4) is not a relaxation of these active constraints: it is the exact\nminimum starting norm over the single-extra-coordinate extensions of\nthis specified kind. Any number of added orthogonal coordinates has\nthe same best value, since only their total squared gradient norm enters.\n\n## 4. Every remaining cross edge is supplied by an explicit equality base\n\nSet eta=r/D. Use the scalar Huber derivative clip(t,-eta,1), starting atA.\nIts prefix gradient is1 and the prefix ends at1; the bridge lands at-r.\nIts tail gradient is-eta throughout, ending at-eta.\nIts primitive energy is\n\n  A^2-T^2 eta^2=0                                      (9)\n\nby(2). Its incoming optimum residuals all vanish.\n\nFor a prefix target with remaining prefix sumH and a tail target with\nremaining tail sumR, the base cross residuals are\n\n  Q_base(tail,prefix)=-(1+eta)H,\n  Q_base(prefix,tail)=-(1+eta)eta R.                    (10)\n\nThus a tail-to-prefix base residual is zero only at the last prefix\nquery or its zero-step duplicates; the auxiliary constraint there is\nnonpositive by(7). A prefix-to-tail base residual is zero only at the\nlast tail query or its zero-step duplicates; the auxiliary constraint\nthere is nonpositive by(8). All within-prefix residuals are0, and all\nwithin-tail auxiliary residuals are legal by hypothesis. Both optimum\ndirections are legal by(1),(7) and the stated prefix slack calculation.\n\nConsequently every positive auxiliary residual has a strictly negative\nbase residual. The finite coefficient\n\n  epsilon=min(1,min_(Q_aux>0)[-Q_base/(2Q_aux)])>0        (11)\n\nis well-defined, with an empty inner minimum interpreted as1.\nCombine the scalar base and the auxiliary data in orthogonal coordinates,\nscaling the auxiliary vectors by sqrt(epsilon) and its values by epsilon.\nEvery original-index interpolation inequality is now nonpositive.\nAll incoming optimum slacks still vanish.\n\nTo see directly that this is one fixed legal function, let K_out be\nthe convex hull of0 and all the combined sampled gradients. For data\nwith(1), finite residuals are <G_i-G_j,X_j-G_j>. The checked finite\nand optimum residuals are precisely the nearest-point normal\ninequalities against every vertex of K_out. Hence each G_j is the\nprojection of X_j onto K_out, and the fixed function\n\n  F_out(X)=max_(G in K_out){<X,G>-||G||^2/2}\n\nrealizes every value and gradient. The projection normal inequalities\ngive its gradient's1-Lipschitz property; the maximum representation\ngives convexity and differentiability. It is minimized at0.\n\n## 5. Exact energy and the strict rejection criterion\n\nFor any array satisfying(1) and the GD equations, distance telescoping\ngives, for its full capacityT,\n\n  V=||X_start||^2-||X_end+(T-1)G_end||^2.                (12)\n\nThis identity is algebraic and does not require prior cross feasibility.\nThe auxiliary endpoint has zero added coordinate, so its energy is\n\n  V_aux=C_ext(v)-||y+Tg||^2.\n\nIf this is negative, equations(9),(11) produce a legal mixture of energy\nepsilon V_aux<0. Taking a minimizer of(4) proves(5).\n\nAt v=0 this recovers the old forced-core-prefix choice p=-x/r,z=0,\nwhen that choice satisfies(3), and its starting cost is beta^2||x||^2.\nFormula(4) can be strictly smaller; that earlier choice need not be\noptimal even for a fixed supplied tail.\n\n## 6. Sharp calibration on a retained affine tail\n\nLet every tail gradient equal1 on the scalar projection function for\nK=[0,1]. Start at x=D+w with any w>0, so the endpoint is1+w,\ny=w and delta=w. The admissibility condition in p is p<=1.\n\nFor p<=-x/r, the support is0 and the cost is at least beta^2 x^2.\nFor -x/r<=p<=1, formula(6) is\n\n  z^2=(1-p)(x+rp)/r,\n\nand the squared starting cost is affine in p with slope\n\n  ell^2(1-x/D)=-ell^2 w/D<0.\n\nIt is therefore minimized at p=1,z=0, with\n\n  C_min=(T+w)^2=||y+Tg||^2.\n\nThe forced-core-prefix choice instead has the strictly larger cost\n\n  beta^2(D+w)^2-(T+w)^2\n    =(beta-1)w[2T+(beta+1)w]>0.\n\nThis recovers the exact zero-energy affine trajectory without losing\nits nonzero terminal value. It shows that the earlier penalty was a\npenalty of a specified extension, not an optimal-extension obstruction.\n\n## 7. A negative witness hidden by that forced-prefix penalty\n\nTake the tail h=(3/2), D=5/2, and the fixed two-dimensional function\n\n  f(u,v)=u^2/2+H_1(v),\n\nwhere H_1 is the ordinary Huber function of threshold1. Start at\nx=(1,D+1). Its endpoint and gradient are\n\n  x_m=(-1/2,2), g=(-1/2,1), y=(0,1), delta=1.\n\nEvery incoming optimum slack is0 and V_tail=-9/16. Use an empty prefix\nA=1 and the saturated bridge\n\n  r=(sqrt(89)-7)/4, T=(7+sqrt(89))/4,\n  beta=T/D=(7+sqrt(89))/10>8/5.\n\nThe forced-core-prefix choice has energy\n\n  beta^2||x||^2-||y+Tg||^2\n     =87 beta^2/16-5beta-1>0.\n\nIndeed the polynomial is increasing for beta>=8/5 and its value at8/5\nis123/25>0. That particular inherited-slack penalty therefore prevents\nthis supplied tail witness from refuting the joined schedule.\n\nThe new choice p=(-1/r,1),z=0 is admissible:\nY_L=(0,D+1+r), so every supplied tail gradient has the same inner\nproduct with Y_L as p, and the terminal constraint is equality.\nIts starting point is(-beta,T+1), with energy exactly\n\n  beta^2-T^2/4=-9 beta^2/16<0.\n\nIn fact here the original fixed function u^2/2+H_1(v) already realizes\nthe entire extension, so mixing is unnecessary. The first bridge sends\n(-1/r,T+1) to(1,D+1), and the tail takes the displayed final step.\nThis is an exact negative calibration for the broader extension rule.\n\nIt does not give a new asymptotic schedule or prove arbitrary-tail\nheredity. Such a theorem would still need to show the strict criterion(5)\nfrom a general negative tail energy, which is not assumed here. The\nfull supporting hull and terminal halfspace remain explicit.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["563c43a0df633463", "a141afe16d306715", "4ad60ca9f3ec3e2e"], "branch_id": "max4_supporting_hull_prepend", "artifact": "local_memory/supporting_hull_prepend.md", "status": "complete_candidate_not_verified", "is_fragile": false, "fragility_reason": "", "suggested_followup": "none", "scope": "subgoal"}, "glossary": {"K": "Convex hull of0 and all gradients of the supplied tail.", "sigma_K": "Support function max_(g in K)<g,v> of the actual tail gradient hull.", "x": "Initial point x_0 of the supplied tail.", "g": "Final gradient of the supplied tail.", "y": "Actual terminal proximal point x_m-g_m.", "delta": "Actual terminal adjusted value <g,y>, using the all-incoming-tight hypothesis.", "A": "Physical capacity of the prepended nonnegative block.", "D": "Physical capacity of the supplied nonnegative tail.", "T": "A+D+r, the full joined capacity.", "r": "Positive bridge excess satisfying rT=AD.", "beta": "T/D=A/r>1 in this construction.", "ell": "A+r=(beta-1)D.", "v": "Shifted new-prefix gradient parameter p+x/r.", "p": "New constant prefix gradient in the old tail coordinates.", "z": "Additional orthogonal prefix-gradient coordinate determined by equation6.", "C_ext": "Exact squared starting norm functional in equation4.", "C_min": "Its attained minimum over the terminal halfspace3.", "Y_L": "Proximal point at the last new prefix query.", "eta": "r/D, negative gradient magnitude of the scalar equality base.", "epsilon": "Finite interpolation-safe mixing coefficient in equation11.", "K_out": "Convex hull of0 and the combined gradients, realizing the whole joined trajectory.", "w": "Positive affine-tail terminal offset in section6.", "H_1": "Scalar Huber function with threshold1 in the exact negative calibration."}}
{"id": "e2dad1cf3b95b8d7", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T14:56:04.062767+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Exact terminal-completion candidate with a quantitative full-profile gain at EVERY n>=24578: two arbitrary constant-gradient blocks with growing bridge factor have a four-anchor formula V=2A epsilon^2-a^2(2epsilon-1/M)_+^2/D and true closest distance1. Choosing six Chebyshev copies, A=M^(1/12), epsilon=M^(-1/3), gives V>=M^(-7/12)>1/B_n, while ALL quadratics, every specified single-cut Huber test, and the universal complex lens are O(M^(-5/6))=o(1/B_n). The old homogeneous envelope has terminal value ZERO; the accepted maximal completion preserves every queried jet and supplies the displayed gain. The same first-block scalar class with any constant second gradient is also O(M^(-5/6)).", "evidence": "# A quantitative exact terminal completion on nonperiodic schedules\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint proof candidate. No verifier or numerical-solver\nrequest. This is an explicit family at every sufficiently large horizon,\nnot the unrestricted all-schedule comparison. Every new calculation used\nbelow is included here; no unverified finding is a premise.\n\nVerified predecessors used:\n\n- `1252b8823bb5a290`: the complete smooth-convex interpolation inequality\n  and the pointwise maximal completion at a genuinely unqueried terminal.\n- `dfde2fa326c908bf`: the exact Chebyshev steps, their sum and polynomial,\n  and the universal complex-lens construction and polynomial estimate.\n- `b98bb807d514e80d`: the single-cut functional K_n, the exact benchmark\n  recursions, and B_n <= 1+4 n^p for p=log_2(3).\n\nWe use smoothness one and initial distance one throughout. Thus the\nnormalized objective is 2f(x_n). General smoothness follows by scaling.\n\n## 1. An arbitrary-block prefix with a completely specified fresh direction\n\nLet M>=2 and A>=4. Prescribe ANY two nonnegative ordinary lists of sum\nM-1 each, separated by the checkpoint AM+1. A final step b will be added\nafter the second list. Work in R^2 and put\n\n    nu=sqrt(2A-4),\n    p=(1,0)/M,                  q=(-2,nu)/M,\n    J(z)=max(0,<p,z>,<q,z>),\n    f_old=env_1 J,              x_init=(1,0).\n\nThe squared boundary gradient is exactly ||q||^2=2A/M^2. The fresh\ncomponent of q has squared norm (2A-4)/M^2; it is not dropped from the\nstate or normalization.\n\nIn the first block every gradient is p. To check this, a query after\nordinary partial mass u has proposed proximal point\n\n    z=(1-(u+1)/M,0),       0<=u<=M-1.\n\nHere <p,z>>=0 and <q,z>=-2<p,z>. Thus p is an active subgradient of J.\nAt the checkpoint query the position is p and its proximal point is 0.\nThe checkpoint AM+1 sends the trajectory to X=(-A,0).\n\nIn the second block every gradient is q. Write t=u+1 in [1,M]. Its\nproposed proximal point is X-tq. The current height is\n\n    <q,X-tq>=(2A/M)(1-t/M)>=0.\n\nIts excess over the p-plane is\n\n    <q-p,X-tq>=3A/M-(2A+2)t/M^2 >= (A-2)/M >0.\n\nThis checks both other planes at every actual query, even if an\nindividual ordinary step is very large. The comparisons are affine in\nt, and the displayed bounds hold on the whole interval. The old q-plane\nwas already checked at every first-block query, so both cross directions\nand every queried sample belong to the same fixed J.\n\nThe envelope justification is elementary: if g is an active-plane\nsubgradient at z and x=z+g, its subgradient inequality makes z the unique\nminimizer of J(w)+||x-w||^2/2. Adding the inequalities for two such\nproximal pairs proves\n\n    ||g(x)-g(x')||^2 <= <g(x)-g(x'),x-x'>.\n\nThe minimizing inequalities identify g as the derivative of the convex\nenvelope, which is consequently 1-smooth. All claimed GD updates and\nsample values follow from this fixed envelope.\n\nAt the last query, just before the final step b, the proximal point and\nposition are respectively\n\n    y=X-Mq=(2-A,-nu),         x_last=y+q.\n\nOne has <q,y>=0, <p,y><0, f_old(x_last)=||q||^2/2. At ALL old\nqueries the value and gradient are retained in the completion below.\n\n## 2. The exact four-anchor maximal interpolant\n\nSet\n\n    a=1-1/M,       z_A=(a,0),       delta_A=a/M,\n    z_B=X-q,\n    delta_B=(M-1)||q||^2.\n\nFor a retained jet (x,g,f), its proximal point z=x-g and height\ndelta=f-||g||^2/2 give the conjugate quadratic\n\n    ||v||^2/2 + <z,v> - delta.\n\nThis is exactly the quadratic H_j in fact 1252b8823bb5a290 after\nexpansion. In the first block (z,delta) traces the line segment from\n(z_A,delta_A) to (0,0). Both endpoints are actual queried samples. In\nthe second block it traces the segment from (z_B,delta_B) to (y,0),\nagain with both endpoints queried. Therefore the maximum of ALL retained\nquadratics, including the optimum jet, is exactly\n\n    H(v)=||v||^2/2 + max(0,\n                        <z_A,v>-delta_A,\n                        <y,v>,\n                        <y,v>+(M-1)(<q,v>-||q||^2)).             (1)\n\nNo constraint is deleted: intermediate quadratics are convex\ncombinations of these endpoint quadratics. By the accepted fact,\n\n    F(x)=sup_v [<x,v>-H(v)]                                    (2)\n\nis one fixed finite convex 1-smooth function, preserves every old query,\nhas F(0)=0=min F, and is the pointwise maximal such interpolant.\nIt is selected before running the complete prescribed schedule.\n\nThe actual closest-minimizer distance stays ONE. The initial jet is\n\n    x_init=(1,0),  g_init=p,  F(x_init)=1/M-1/(2M^2).\n\nFor any minimizer z of F, the accepted interpolation inequality between\nthis jet and (z,0,0) gives\n\n    0 >= F(x_init)+<p,z-x_init>+||p||^2/2 = <p,z>.\n\nHence z has first coordinate <=0 and ||x_init-z||>=1. Origin attains\ndistance one. This proves the genuine normalization for F, not merely\nfor the old envelope.\n\n## 3. Closed formula at the genuinely unqueried terminal\n\nLet\n\n    b=1+M epsilon,    0<=epsilon<=1,\n    d=(2,-nu)=-Mq,\n    e=z_A-y=(A-2+a,nu),\n    D=||e||^2=(A-2+a)^2+2A-4.\n\nThe actual terminal point is\n\n    x_n=x_last-bq = y+epsilon d.\n\nDefine\n\n    tau=a(2epsilon-1/M)_+/D,\n    v=epsilon d-tau e.                                       (3)\n\nHere 0<=tau<=2epsilon/D<=1/2 because D>=(A-2)^2>=4.\nThe elementary products that will be used are\n\n    ||d||^2=2A,   <d,e>=2a,   <y,d>=0,\n    <y,e>=-(A-2)(A+a)<0.                                    (4)\n\nWe now check ALL four affine terms in (1) at v.\n\nIf tau=0, the y-term and zero term both equal zero, and the z_A-term\nis a(2epsilon-1/M)<=0. If tau>0, then\n\n    <e,v>=2a epsilon-tau D=a/M,\n\nso the y-term and z_A-term tie. The y-term is nonnegative because\n<y,v>=-tau<y,e>>=0. Finally\n\n    <q,v>=-[2A epsilon-2a tau]/M <=0,\n\nsince tau<=2epsilon/D and 2A-4/D>0. Consequently the z_B-term is\nstrictly below the y-term, its difference being\n(M-1)(<q,v>-||q||^2)<0. This also handles epsilon=0.\n\nFor tau=0, y is an active subgradient of the max-affine part of H.\nFor tau>0, y+tau e is the convex combination (1-tau)y+tau z_A\nof its active slopes. In both cases\n\n    x_n=v+y+tau e\n\nis a subgradient of H at v. Strong convexity of H therefore makes v\nthe unique maximizer in (2), and it is the ACTUAL terminal gradient.\nSubstituting (3)-(4) gives the exact normalized value\n\n    V(M,A,epsilon) := 2F(x_n)\n       = 2A epsilon^2\n         - a^2(2epsilon-1/M)_+^2/D.                         (5)\n\nEvery parameter and endpoint is finite, and epsilon=0 is included.\nIn particular, since a<=1 and D>=4,\n\n    A epsilon^2 <= V(M,A,epsilon) <= 2A epsilon^2.           (6)\n\nThe precise relative loss from the upper bound is at most\n2/(A D)<=2/[A(A-2)^2], which tends to zero as A grows.\nEquation (5), rather than that limit, is used in the comparisons below.\n\nThere is a substantial completion effect here. For A>=4 and epsilon<=1,\nboth <p,x_n> and <q,x_n> are nonpositive. Thus J(x_n)=0 and\nf_old(x_n)=0, whereas (5) is positive for epsilon>0. No queried value\nhas changed: only the behavior away from the retained samples differs.\nThe original old function's unqueried zero set does not constrain the\nmaximal interpolant. Its actual closest minimizer remains at distance one.\n\nThe upper bound in (6) also has an intrinsic interpretation. Every\ninterpolant of the last query and optimum has F(y)=0 by the unit-step\ndescent inequality, because F(y+q)=||q||^2/2. Smoothness then gives\n2F(x_n)<=||x_n-y||^2=2A epsilon^2. The exact completion loses only\nthe displayed finite old-query correction from this upper bound.\n\n## 4. A fixed horizon-dependent choice\n\nFor each integer N>=4096 let Chi_N be the increasing list\n\n    c_j=sec^2((2j-1)pi/[2(2N+1)]),       1<=j<=N.\n\nWrite L_N for THREE successive copies of this list, and define\n\n    M=2N(N+1)+1,       A=M^(1/12),       epsilon=M^(-1/3),\n    h^(N)=(L_N, AM+1, L_N, 1+M^(2/3)),\n    n_N=6N+2.                                                 (7)\n\nThe exact sum formula in dfde2fa326c908bf makes each L_N have mass\nM-1. Also M>=4^12, so A>=4 and the preceding construction applies\nwithout a limiting argument. It gives the explicit value (5), and\n\n    W_(n_N)(h^(N)) >= V(M,A,epsilon) >= M^(-7/12).             (8)\n\nTo obtain a family at EVERY integer horizon n>=24578, take\nN=floor((n-2)/6), and insert n-(6N+2) zero steps at the very beginning\nof (7). These duplicate a retained query, change no trajectory or\nvalue, and preserve the same formula. No new terminal jet is queried\nbefore the final nonzero step. Denote this length-n schedule by h^[n].\n\nThe first bridge is of order M^(13/12), the final bridge is of order\nM^(2/3), and the ordinary lists depend on the horizon. This is not a\nfixed periodic schedule or a bounded-length legality example.\n\n## 5. Every quadratic and every specified single-cut Huber test is smaller\n\nLet Q_n(h) denote the maximum normalized terminal error of all convex\nquadratics with curvature in [0,1], over every dimension. By orthogonal\ndiagonalization this is\n\n    Q_n(h)=max_(0<=lambda<=1) lambda product_j(1-h_j lambda)^2.\n\nSet T=(2N+1)^2=2M-1, and let P_N be the polynomial of Chi_N. The\nverified Chebyshev identity implies\n\n    |P_N(lambda)|^2 <= min(1,1/(T lambda)),   0<lambda<=1.      (9)\n\nFor the first bound, put sqrt(lambda)=sin(theta): the numerator of\nP_N is, up to sign, sin((2N+1)theta), and\n|sin(k theta)|<=k|sin(theta)| follows by telescoping k unit complex\nnumbers. The second bound follows from |Cheb_(2N+1)(sqrt(lambda))|<=1.\n\nThere are six complete copies of Chi_N in (7). For z=T lambda, its\nquadratic error is at most\n\n    T^(-1) z (1+A z)^2(1+z)^2 min(1,z^(-6)).                 (10)\n\nIndeed (AM+1)/T<=A and (1+M^(2/3))/T<=1 for M>=2. If z<=1,\nthe expression following T^(-1) is <=16A^2. If z>=1, it is\n<=16A^2/z<=16A^2. Therefore\n\n    Q_(n_N)(h^(N)) <=16 A^2/T <=16 M^(-5/6).                 (11)\n\nNow consider the exact K_n in fact b98bb807d514e80d. We check every\nindex, not merely the two selected bridges. In the increasing Chi_N,\nthe first entry is <=4/3, and the ratio of successive entries is <=9.\nFor the latter write c_j=csc^2(theta_j), where\ntheta_j=(N-j+1)pi/(2N+1). Consecutive positive angles have ratio <=2,\nand concavity of sin on [0,pi/2] gives c_(j+1)/c_j<=4<=9.\nThe weaker constant 9 is retained in the bounds below. Thus every\nordinary entry satisfies h_j<=9(1+P_j), with P_j the full preceding\nmass. This remains true at the beginning of each repeated copy.\nAlso max c_j=csc^2(pi/(2N+1))<=T/4<M, by sin(t)>=2t/pi.\n\n- A cut in the first L_N has tail mass at least AM. Its single-cut\n  term is at most 81/(2AM) <=41 M^(-5/6).\n- At the bridge AM+1 the prefix mass is M-1 and the tail mass is\n  M-1+b. Its term is A^2/[1+2(M-1+b)]<=A^2/M=M^(-5/6).\n- A cut in the second L_N has prefix mass at least (A+1)M and tail\n  mass at least b>=M^(2/3). Its term is at most\n  1/[2A^2 M^(2/3)] = M^(-5/6)/2.\n- At the final bridge b=1+M^(2/3), one plus the prefix mass is\n  (A+2)M. Its term is M^(-2/3)/(A+2)^2<=M^(-5/6).\n- The global Huber term is <=1/M<=M^(-5/6).\n\nConsequently\n\n    K_(n_N)(h^(N)) <=41 M^(-5/6).                              (12)\n\nInserted zero steps contribute zero cut terms and change no mass, so\n(11)-(12) hold for h^[n] as well. The second-block estimate only needs\nthe maximum individual step, and hence survives arbitrary permutations\nthere. The first-block ordering assumption is used explicitly above.\n\n## 6. The universal complex lens also misses this family\n\nLet Lens_n and Gap_h be exactly those in accepted fact dfde2fa326c908bf.\nSince n>=N, Lens_n is contained in Lens_N. The Chebyshev ellipse\ncalculation in that fact gives\n\n    |z||P_N(z)|^2 <= cosh^2(2)/T,       z in Lens_n.\n\nFor T|z|<=1, the product formula instead gives\n\n    |P_N(z)| <= exp(s(Chi_N)|z|) <= exp(1/6) <= cosh(2).\n\nThe last inequality follows, for example, from exp(1/6)<=6/5 and\ncosh(2)>=3. Thus, with C=cosh(2),\n\n    |P_N(z)|^2 <= C^2 min(1,1/(T|z|)).                         (13)\n\nThe accepted upper bound on a legal polynomial transcript is\nGap_h(z)<=(2Re z-|z|^2)|Poly_n(z)|^2<=2|z||Poly_n(z)|^2.\nUsing (13) and the same two ranges as in (10), now with T|z| as the\nnonnegative scalar variable, proves\n\n    sup_(z in Lens_n) Gap_(h^[n])(z)\n        <=32 cosh^12(2) M^(-5/6).                            (14)\n\nThis bounds ONLY the universal-lens channel; it makes no assertion\nabout the full schedule-dependent legal complex region.\n\n## 7. Comparison with the actual full B_n sequence\n\nNo asymptotic profile constant or limit is assumed. First, the verified\nupper bound B_n<=1+4n^p, p=log_2(3), yields the explicit estimates\n\n    K_n(h^[n])/U_n^ZJ <=41(1+4n^p)/M^(5/6),\n    Q_n(h^[n])/U_n^ZJ <=16(1+4n^p)/M^(5/6),\n    [sup_(z in Lens_n) Gap_(h^[n])(z)]/U_n^ZJ\n        <=32 cosh^12(2)(1+4n^p)/M^(5/6).                      (15)\n\nHere M>=2N^2, n<=6N+7<=8N, and p<5/3 because 27<32. Every\nright-hand side tends to zero. Thus these three established channels\nall miss the exact benchmark on the very same family.\n\nFor a pointwise lower comparison, set s=1+sqrt(2) and\nd_N=floor(log_2 N). In the verified primitive mass recurrence,\n\n    C_j=S_circle(j)+1,\n    C_j=max_(k<j) Omega(C_k,C_(j-k-1)),\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,\n\none has C_j>=C_(j-1), and the balanced choice gives\nC_(2^r-1)>=s^r. The dominant recurrence with its final empty block\ngives B_n>=2C_(n-1)-1. Since n-1>=2^(d_N+2)-1,\n\n    B_n>=2s^(d_N+2)-1>=s^(d_N+2).                            (16)\n\nOn the other hand N<2^(d_N+1) and M<=5N^2, so\n\n    B_n V(M,A,epsilon)\n      >= [s^2/20^(7/12)] [s/2^(7/6)]^d_N\n      > (18/25)(16/15)^d_N >1,                               (17)\n\nfor d_N>=12. For the elementary constants: s>12/5,\n2^(7/6)<9/4 since 2^7<(9/4)^6, and 20^(7/12)<8.\nFinally (16/15)^d_N >=1+d_N/15>=9/5 for d_N>=12, so the\nlast product is at least 162/125>1.\n\nEquations (5), (15), and (17) concern the actual B_n at EVERY integer\nn>=24578, not just a dyadic comparison constant. They prove for this\nexplicit family\n\n    W_n(h^[n]) >= V(M,A,epsilon) > U_n^ZJ,\n    max(K_n(h^[n]),Q_n(h^[n]),sup_Lens_n Gap_(h^[n]))\n        = o(U_n^ZJ).                                         (18)\n\nThe ratio supplied by (17) in fact diverges. This is a concrete gain\nfrom retaining the two-dimensional geometry and completing the free\nterminal. It is not a lower bound of this strength for all schedules.\n\n## 8. A scalar comparison with the same retained first block\n\nThere is a useful further explanation of the dimensional mechanism.\nKeep the first block and all of its scalar jets fixed, including\nx_init=1, gradient 1/M, and optimum 0. After the bridge AM+1 the\nscalar position is -A. Consider ANY legal scalar continuation whose\ngradient is a constant -v throughout the second ordinary block,\nincluding its last query. Convexity gives v>=0. Its last queried\nposition is x_E=-A+(M-1)v. Smoothness and the optimum force\nx_E<=-v if v>0, hence 0<=v<=A/M (the v=0 case has value zero).\n\nInterpolation against the optimum bounds the last queried value by\n\n    f(x_E)<=Av-(M-1/2)v^2 <= A^2/[4(M-1/2)].\n\nAfter the final step b>=1 the position is x_T=-A+(M-1+b)v.\nIf x_T<=0, monotonicity on the negative ray gives f(x_T)<=f(x_E).\nIf x_T>=0, then x_T<=A epsilon. When A epsilon<=1, the retained\ninitial derivative and convexity imply 0<=f'(x)<=1/M on [0,x_T],\nso f(x_T)<=A epsilon/M. Therefore EVERY such scalar continuation has\n\n    2f(x_T)<=max(A^2/(2M-1), 2A epsilon/M).                     (19)\n\nFor (7), A epsilon=M^(-1/4)<=1, and (19) is <=2M^(-5/6).\nThe two-dimensional construction gives >=M^(-7/12), larger by at\nleast M^(1/4)/2. The second scalar block's values and gradient may be\nchosen freely subject to legality; this is more than a comparison with\none specific scalar example. It is still a constant-gradient block\nrestriction, and does not upper-bound every one-dimensional trajectory\non the full schedule.\n\n## Scope and remaining task\n\nThis joint proof supplies an exact arbitrary-block terminal policy, a\nfour-anchor active-set formula, all old/new comparisons, the actual\nboundary gradient, true distance normalization, and a quantitative\nfamily at every large horizon that defeats several baseline channels.\nIt does not assert that every schedule has an advantageous two-block\npartition, that the completed function remains adverse after an\narbitrary further suffix, or that a greedy intermediate completion is\nglobally optimal. The all-schedule exact-profile comparison remains open.\n\nTargeted Matlas searches returned no external theorem used in the proof.\nThe exact accepted predecessors above were read. A search hit for an\nolder lower-bound paper returned HTTP 503 and was not relied on. All\nnew results remain candidates because the current assignment allocates\nno additional verifier requests.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "dfde2fa326c908bf", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "plan_id": "775bb3bb4cb22319", "artifact": "research/max_terminal_completion_profile_gap.md", "status": "unverified joint proof candidate", "scope": "Explicit family and arbitrary-two-block formula;
