its actual squared magnitude is 2A/M^2.", "J": "The old homogeneous nonsmooth function max(0,<p,z>,<q,z>) used only to certify the retained prefix.", "f_old": "The Moreau envelope of J at parameter one.", "F": "The pointwise maximal smooth-convex interpolant of all retained prefix jets and the optimum, defined by conjugating H.", "H": "The maximum of all retained conjugate quadratics, exactly the four-anchor function in equation(1).", "a": "The dimensionless scalar 1-1/M used in the initial proximal anchor.", "z_A": "Initial queried proximal point (a,0), of height delta_A=a/M.", "z_B": "First queried proximal point of the second block X-q, of height delta_B=(M-1)||q||^2.", "delta_A": "Proximal value a/M at z_A.", "delta_B": "Proximal value (M-1)||q||^2 at z_B.", "X": "The second-block start (-A,0).", "y": "Last queried proximal point (2-A,-nu), forced to be a minimizer of every retained interpolant.", "epsilon": "Nonnegative final jump displacement parameter (b-1)/M in [0,1]; in the horizon family M^(-1/3).", "b": "Final unqueried step 1+M epsilon.", "d": "The vector (2,-nu)=-Mq.", "e": "The anchor difference z_A-y=(A-2+a,nu).", "D": "The squared norm ||e||^2.", "tau": "Active anchor mixture a(2epsilon-1/M)_+/D; unrelated to the Huber threshold in other findings.", "v": "Actual terminal gradient epsilon d-tau e.", "V(M,A,epsilon)": "The exact normalized value in equation(5).", "Chi_N": "The increasing order of the accepted Chebyshev reciprocal-root schedule of length N.", "L_N": "Three consecutive copies of Chi_N, with total mass M-1.", "h^(N)": "The complete length6N+2 schedule (L_N,AM+1,L_N,1+M^(2/3)).", "h^[n]": "For N=floor((n-2)/6), the schedule h^(N) padded at its beginning with n-(6N+2) zeros.", "Q_n": "Worst normalized objective among all convex quadratic functions, equal to the scalar spectral maximum in Section5.", "T": "The scalar (2N+1)^2=2M-1 in the polynomial estimates.", "P_N": "The terminal polynomial of Chi_N from accepted fact dfde2fa326c908bf.", "d_N": "floor(log_2 N), used only in the pointwise lower estimate on the exact B_n.", "s": "The scalar 1+sqrt(2) in Section7.", "C": "The scalar cosh(2) in the universal-lens estimate.", "x_E": "Last scalar queried point in the comparison of Section8.", "x_T": "Scalar point after the final step in Section8."}}
{"id": "bc4ce8b7c164f3b4", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T15:06:32.116359+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Complete continuous direction policy candidate for TWO UNEQUAL prescribed constant-gradient blocks and ANY final nonnegative step: the entire homogeneous zero-level second-gradient family is one parameter kappa in[0,A], including dimension-collapse endpoints. Its exact maximal terminal value is a concave min-affine function; an explicit scalar-endpoint/interior rule globally optimizes kappa with no solver or assumed feasibility. On the SAME all-large-horizon family e2dad1cf3b95b8d7 the optimized direction improves the constructive payoff to >=(1/2)M^(-1/2), a growing-factor improvement, while every queried jet, actual gradient magnitude and true closest distance are retained.", "evidence": "# A quantitative exact terminal completion on nonperiodic schedules\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint proof candidate. No verifier or numerical-solver\nrequest. This is an explicit family at every sufficiently large horizon,\nnot the unrestricted all-schedule comparison. Every new calculation used\nbelow is included here; no unverified finding is a premise.\n\nVerified predecessors used:\n\n- `1252b8823bb5a290`: the complete smooth-convex interpolation inequality\n  and the pointwise maximal completion at a genuinely unqueried terminal.\n- `dfde2fa326c908bf`: the exact Chebyshev steps, their sum and polynomial,\n  and the universal complex-lens construction and polynomial estimate.\n- `b98bb807d514e80d`: the single-cut functional K_n, the exact benchmark\n  recursions, and B_n <= 1+4 n^p for p=log_2(3).\n\nWe use smoothness one and initial distance one throughout. Thus the\nnormalized objective is 2f(x_n). General smoothness follows by scaling.\n\n## 1. An arbitrary-block prefix with a completely specified fresh direction\n\nLet M>=2 and A>=4. Prescribe ANY two nonnegative ordinary lists of sum\nM-1 each, separated by the checkpoint AM+1. A final step b will be added\nafter the second list. Work in R^2 and put\n\n    nu=sqrt(2A-4),\n    p=(1,0)/M,                  q=(-2,nu)/M,\n    J(z)=max(0,<p,z>,<q,z>),\n    f_old=env_1 J,              x_init=(1,0).\n\nThe squared boundary gradient is exactly ||q||^2=2A/M^2. The fresh\ncomponent of q has squared norm (2A-4)/M^2; it is not dropped from the\nstate or normalization.\n\nIn the first block every gradient is p. To check this, a query after\nordinary partial mass u has proposed proximal point\n\n    z=(1-(u+1)/M,0),       0<=u<=M-1.\n\nHere <p,z>>=0 and <q,z>=-2<p,z>. Thus p is an active subgradient of J.\nAt the checkpoint query the position is p and its proximal point is 0.\nThe checkpoint AM+1 sends the trajectory to X=(-A,0).\n\nIn the second block every gradient is q. Write t=u+1 in [1,M]. Its\nproposed proximal point is X-tq. The current height is\n\n    <q,X-tq>=(2A/M)(1-t/M)>=0.\n\nIts excess over the p-plane is\n\n    <q-p,X-tq>=3A/M-(2A+2)t/M^2 >= (A-2)/M >0.\n\nThis checks both other planes at every actual query, even if an\nindividual ordinary step is very large. The comparisons are affine in\nt, and the displayed bounds hold on the whole interval. The old q-plane\nwas already checked at every first-block query, so both cross directions\nand every queried sample belong to the same fixed J.\n\nThe envelope justification is elementary: if g is an active-plane\nsubgradient at z and x=z+g, its subgradient inequality makes z the unique\nminimizer of J(w)+||x-w||^2/2. Adding the inequalities for two such\nproximal pairs proves\n\n    ||g(x)-g(x')||^2 <= <g(x)-g(x'),x-x'>.\n\nThe minimizing inequalities identify g as the derivative of the convex\nenvelope, which is consequently 1-smooth. All claimed GD updates and\nsample values follow from this fixed envelope.\n\nAt the last query, just before the final step b, the proximal point and\nposition are respectively\n\n    y=X-Mq=(2-A,-nu),         x_last=y+q.\n\nOne has <q,y>=0, <p,y><0, f_old(x_last)=||q||^2/2. At ALL old\nqueries the value and gradient are retained in the completion below.\n\n## 2. The exact four-anchor maximal interpolant\n\nSet\n\n    a=1-1/M,       z_A=(a,0),       delta_A=a/M,\n    z_B=X-q,\n    delta_B=(M-1)||q||^2.\n\nFor a retained jet (x,g,f), its proximal point z=x-g and height\ndelta=f-||g||^2/2 give the conjugate quadratic\n\n    ||v||^2/2 + <z,v> - delta.\n\nThis is exactly the quadratic H_j in fact 1252b8823bb5a290 after\nexpansion. In the first block (z,delta) traces the line segment from\n(z_A,delta_A) to (0,0). Both endpoints are actual queried samples. In\nthe second block it traces the segment from (z_B,delta_B) to (y,0),\nagain with both endpoints queried. Therefore the maximum of ALL retained\nquadratics, including the optimum jet, is exactly\n\n    H(v)=||v||^2/2 + max(0,\n                        <z_A,v>-delta_A,\n                        <y,v>,\n                        <y,v>+(M-1)(<q,v>-||q||^2)).             (1)\n\nNo constraint is deleted: intermediate quadratics are convex\ncombinations of these endpoint quadratics. By the accepted fact,\n\n    F(x)=sup_v [<x,v>-H(v)]                                    (2)\n\nis one fixed finite convex 1-smooth function, preserves every old query,\nhas F(0)=0=min F, and is the pointwise maximal such interpolant.\nIt is selected before running the complete prescribed schedule.\n\nThe actual closest-minimizer distance stays ONE. The initial jet is\n\n    x_init=(1,0),  g_init=p,  F(x_init)=1/M-1/(2M^2).\n\nFor any minimizer z of F, the accepted interpolation inequality between\nthis jet and (z,0,0) gives\n\n    0 >= F(x_init)+<p,z-x_init>+||p||^2/2 = <p,z>.\n\nHence z has first coordinate <=0 and ||x_init-z||>=1. Origin attains\ndistance one. This proves the genuine normalization for F, not merely\nfor the old envelope.\n\n## 3. Closed formula at the genuinely unqueried terminal\n\nLet\n\n    b=1+M epsilon,    0<=epsilon<=1,\n    d=(2,-nu)=-Mq,\n    e=z_A-y=(A-2+a,nu),\n    D=||e||^2=(A-2+a)^2+2A-4.\n\nThe actual terminal point is\n\n    x_n=x_last-bq = y+epsilon d.\n\nDefine\n\n    tau=a(2epsilon-1/M)_+/D,\n    v=epsilon d-tau e.                                       (3)\n\nHere 0<=tau<=2epsilon/D<=1/2 because D>=(A-2)^2>=4.\nThe elementary products that will be used are\n\n    ||d||^2=2A,   <d,e>=2a,   <y,d>=0,\n    <y,e>=-(A-2)(A+a)<0.                                    (4)\n\nWe now check ALL four affine terms in (1) at v.\n\nIf tau=0, the y-term and zero term both equal zero, and the z_A-term\nis a(2epsilon-1/M)<=0. If tau>0, then\n\n    <e,v>=2a epsilon-tau D=a/M,\n\nso the y-term and z_A-term tie. The y-term is nonnegative because\n<y,v>=-tau<y,e>>=0. Finally\n\n    <q,v>=-[2A epsilon-2a tau]/M <=0,\n\nsince tau<=2epsilon/D and 2A-4/D>0. Consequently the z_B-term is\nstrictly below the y-term, its difference being\n(M-1)(<q,v>-||q||^2)<0. This also handles epsilon=0.\n\nFor tau=0, y is an active subgradient of the max-affine part of H.\nFor tau>0, y+tau e is the convex combination (1-tau)y+tau z_A\nof its active slopes. In both cases\n\n    x_n=v+y+tau e\n\nis a subgradient of H at v. Strong convexity of H therefore makes v\nthe unique maximizer in (2), and it is the ACTUAL terminal gradient.\nSubstituting (3)-(4) gives the exact normalized value\n\n    V(M,A,epsilon) := 2F(x_n)\n       = 2A epsilon^2\n         - a^2(2epsilon-1/M)_+^2/D.                         (5)\n\nEvery parameter and endpoint is finite, and epsilon=0 is included.\nIn particular, since a<=1 and D>=4,\n\n    A epsilon^2 <= V(M,A,epsilon) <= 2A epsilon^2.           (6)\n\nThe precise relative loss from the upper bound is at most\n2/(A D)<=2/[A(A-2)^2], which tends to zero as A grows.\nEquation (5), rather than that limit, is used in the comparisons below.\n\nThere is a substantial completion effect here. For A>=4 and epsilon<=1,\nboth <p,x_n> and <q,x_n> are nonpositive. Thus J(x_n)=0 and\nf_old(x_n)=0, whereas (5) is positive for epsilon>0. No queried value\nhas changed: only the behavior away from the retained samples differs.\nThe original old function's unqueried zero set does not constrain the\nmaximal interpolant. Its actual closest minimizer remains at distance one.\n\nThe upper bound in (6) also has an intrinsic interpretation. Every\ninterpolant of the last query and optimum has F(y)=0 by the unit-step\ndescent inequality, because F(y+q)=||q||^2/2. Smoothness then gives\n2F(x_n)<=||x_n-y||^2=2A epsilon^2. The exact completion loses only\nthe displayed finite old-query correction from this upper bound.\n\n## 4. A fixed horizon-dependent choice\n\nFor each integer N>=4096 let Chi_N be the increasing list\n\n    c_j=sec^2((2j-1)pi/[2(2N+1)]),       1<=j<=N.\n\nWrite L_N for THREE successive copies of this list, and define\n\n    M=2N(N+1)+1,       A=M^(1/12),       epsilon=M^(-1/3),\n    h^(N)=(L_N, AM+1, L_N, 1+M^(2/3)),\n    n_N=6N+2.                                                 (7)\n\nThe exact sum formula in dfde2fa326c908bf makes each L_N have mass\nM-1. Also M>=4^12, so A>=4 and the preceding construction applies\nwithout a limiting argument. It gives the explicit value (5), and\n\n    W_(n_N)(h^(N)) >= V(M,A,epsilon) >= M^(-7/12).             (8)\n\nTo obtain a family at EVERY integer horizon n>=24578, take\nN=floor((n-2)/6), and insert n-(6N+2) zero steps at the very beginning\nof (7). These duplicate a retained query, change no trajectory or\nvalue, and preserve the same formula. No new terminal jet is queried\nbefore the final nonzero step. Denote this length-n schedule by h^[n].\n\nThe first bridge is of order M^(13/12), the final bridge is of order\nM^(2/3), and the ordinary lists depend on the horizon. This is not a\nfixed periodic schedule or a bounded-length legality example.\n\n## 5. Every quadratic and every specified single-cut Huber test is smaller\n\nLet Q_n(h) denote the maximum normalized terminal error of all convex\nquadratics with curvature in [0,1], over every dimension. By orthogonal\ndiagonalization this is\n\n    Q_n(h)=max_(0<=lambda<=1) lambda product_j(1-h_j lambda)^2.\n\nSet T=(2N+1)^2=2M-1, and let P_N be the polynomial of Chi_N. The\nverified Chebyshev identity implies\n\n    |P_N(lambda)|^2 <= min(1,1/(T lambda)),   0<lambda<=1.      (9)\n\nFor the first bound, put sqrt(lambda)=sin(theta): the numerator of\nP_N is, up to sign, sin((2N+1)theta), and\n|sin(k theta)|<=k|sin(theta)| follows by telescoping k unit complex\nnumbers. The second bound follows from |Cheb_(2N+1)(sqrt(lambda))|<=1.\n\nThere are six complete copies of Chi_N in (7). For z=T lambda, its\nquadratic error is at most\n\n    T^(-1) z (1+A z)^2(1+z)^2 min(1,z^(-6)).                 (10)\n\nIndeed (AM+1)/T<=A and (1+M^(2/3))/T<=1 for M>=2. If z<=1,\nthe expression following T^(-1) is <=16A^2. If z>=1, it is\n<=16A^2/z<=16A^2. Therefore\n\n    Q_(n_N)(h^(N)) <=16 A^2/T <=16 M^(-5/6).                 (11)\n\nNow consider the exact K_n in fact b98bb807d514e80d. We check every\nindex, not merely the two selected bridges. In the increasing Chi_N,\nthe first entry is <=4/3, and the ratio of successive entries is <=9.\nFor the latter write c_j=csc^2(theta_j), where\ntheta_j=(N-j+1)pi/(2N+1). Consecutive positive angles have ratio <=2,\nand concavity of sin on [0,pi/2] gives c_(j+1)/c_j<=4<=9.\nThe weaker constant 9 is retained in the bounds below. Thus every\nordinary entry satisfies h_j<=9(1+P_j), with P_j the full preceding\nmass. This remains true at the beginning of each repeated copy.\nAlso max c_j=csc^2(pi/(2N+1))<=T/4<M, by sin(t)>=2t/pi.\n\n- A cut in the first L_N has tail mass at least AM. Its single-cut\n  term is at most 81/(2AM) <=41 M^(-5/6).\n- At the bridge AM+1 the prefix mass is M-1 and the tail mass is\n  M-1+b. Its term is A^2/[1+2(M-1+b)]<=A^2/M=M^(-5/6).\n- A cut in the second L_N has prefix mass at least (A+1)M and tail\n  mass at least b>=M^(2/3). Its term is at most\n  1/[2A^2 M^(2/3)] = M^(-5/6)/2.\n- At the final bridge b=1+M^(2/3), one plus the prefix mass is\n  (A+2)M. Its term is M^(-2/3)/(A+2)^2<=M^(-5/6).\n- The global Huber term is <=1/M<=M^(-5/6).\n\nConsequently\n\n    K_(n_N)(h^(N)) <=41 M^(-5/6).                              (12)\n\nInserted zero steps contribute zero cut terms and change no mass, so\n(11)-(12) hold for h^[n] as well. The second-block estimate only needs\nthe maximum individual step, and hence survives arbitrary permutations\nthere. The first-block ordering assumption is used explicitly above.\n\n## 6. The universal complex lens also misses this family\n\nLet Lens_n and Gap_h be exactly those in accepted fact dfde2fa326c908bf.\nSince n>=N, Lens_n is contained in Lens_N. The Chebyshev ellipse\ncalculation in that fact gives\n\n    |z||P_N(z)|^2 <= cosh^2(2)/T,       z in Lens_n.\n\nFor T|z|<=1, the product formula instead gives\n\n    |P_N(z)| <= exp(s(Chi_N)|z|) <= exp(1/6) <= cosh(2).\n\nThe last inequality follows, for example, from exp(1/6)<=6/5 and\ncosh(2)>=3. Thus, with C=cosh(2),\n\n    |P_N(z)|^2 <= C^2 min(1,1/(T|z|)).                         (13)\n\nThe accepted upper bound on a legal polynomial transcript is\nGap_h(z)<=(2Re z-|z|^2)|Poly_n(z)|^2<=2|z||Poly_n(z)|^2.\nUsing (13) and the same two ranges as in (10), now with T|z| as the\nnonnegative scalar variable, proves\n\n    sup_(z in Lens_n) Gap_(h^[n])(z)\n        <=32 cosh^12(2) M^(-5/6).                            (14)\n\nThis bounds ONLY the universal-lens channel; it makes no assertion\nabout the full schedule-dependent legal complex region.\n\n## 7. Comparison with the actual full B_n sequence\n\nNo asymptotic profile constant or limit is assumed. First, the verified\nupper bound B_n<=1+4n^p, p=log_2(3), yields the explicit estimates\n\n    K_n(h^[n])/U_n^ZJ <=41(1+4n^p)/M^(5/6),\n    Q_n(h^[n])/U_n^ZJ <=16(1+4n^p)/M^(5/6),\n    [sup_(z in Lens_n) Gap_(h^[n])(z)]/U_n^ZJ\n        <=32 cosh^12(2)(1+4n^p)/M^(5/6).                      (15)\n\nHere M>=2N^2, n<=6N+7<=8N, and p<5/3 because 27<32. Every\nright-hand side tends to zero. Thus these three established channels\nall miss the exact benchmark on the very same family.\n\nFor a pointwise lower comparison, set s=1+sqrt(2) and\nd_N=floor(log_2 N). In the verified primitive mass recurrence,\n\n    C_j=S_circle(j)+1,\n    C_j=max_(k<j) Omega(C_k,C_(j-k-1)),\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,\n\none has C_j>=C_(j-1), and the balanced choice gives\nC_(2^r-1)>=s^r. The dominant recurrence with its final empty block\ngives B_n>=2C_(n-1)-1. Since n-1>=2^(d_N+2)-1,\n\n    B_n>=2s^(d_N+2)-1>=s^(d_N+2).                            (16)\n\nOn the other hand N<2^(d_N+1) and M<=5N^2, so\n\n    B_n V(M,A,epsilon)\n      >= [s^2/20^(7/12)] [s/2^(7/6)]^d_N\n      > (18/25)(16/15)^d_N >1,                               (17)\n\nfor d_N>=12. For the elementary constants: s>12/5,\n2^(7/6)<9/4 since 2^7<(9/4)^6, and 20^(7/12)<8.\nFinally (16/15)^d_N >=1+d_N/15>=9/5 for d_N>=12, so the\nlast product is at least 162/125>1.\n\nEquations (5), (15), and (17) concern the actual B_n at EVERY integer\nn>=24578, not just a dyadic comparison constant. They prove for this\nexplicit family\n\n    W_n(h^[n]) >= V(M,A,epsilon) > U_n^ZJ,\n    max(K_n(h^[n]),Q_n(h^[n]),sup_Lens_n Gap_(h^[n]))\n        = o(U_n^ZJ).                                         (18)\n\nThe ratio supplied by (17) in fact diverges. This is a concrete gain\nfrom retaining the two-dimensional geometry and completing the free\nterminal. It is not a lower bound of this strength for all schedules.\n\n## 8. A scalar comparison with the same retained first block\n\nThere is a useful further explanation of the dimensional mechanism.\nKeep the first block and all of its scalar jets fixed, including\nx_init=1, gradient 1/M, and optimum 0. After the bridge AM+1 the\nscalar position is -A. Consider ANY legal scalar continuation whose\ngradient is a constant -v throughout the second ordinary block,\nincluding its last query. Convexity gives v>=0. Its last queried\nposition is x_E=-A+(M-1)v. Smoothness and the optimum force\nx_E<=-v if v>0, hence 0<=v<=A/M (the v=0 case has value zero).\n\nInterpolation against the optimum bounds the last queried value by\n\n    f(x_E)<=Av-(M-1/2)v^2 <= A^2/[4(M-1/2)].\n\nAfter the final step b>=1 the position is x_T=-A+(M-1+b)v.\nIf x_T<=0, monotonicity on the negative ray gives f(x_T)<=f(x_E).\nIf x_T>=0, then x_T<=A epsilon. When A epsilon<=1, the retained\ninitial derivative and convexity imply 0<=f'(x)<=1/M on [0,x_T],\nso f(x_T)<=A epsilon/M. Therefore EVERY such scalar continuation has\n\n    2f(x_T)<=max(A^2/(2M-1), 2A epsilon/M).                     (19)\n\nFor (7), A epsilon=M^(-1/4)<=1, and (19) is <=2M^(-5/6).\nThe two-dimensional construction gives >=M^(-7/12), larger by at\nleast M^(1/4)/2. The second scalar block's values and gradient may be\nchosen freely subject to legality; this is more than a comparison with\none specific scalar example. It is still a constant-gradient block\nrestriction, and does not upper-bound every one-dimensional trajectory\non the full schedule.\n\n## 9. Exact optimization of the whole homogeneous second-gradient class\n\nThe parallel component need not be fixed at -2/M. There is an explicit\noptimal choice, and it applies to TWO UNEQUAL prescribed block masses.\nThis section includes its own prefix, active-set, and optimization proof.\n\nLet the two ordinary sums be M_0-1 and M_1-1, with M_0>1, M_1>=1.\nLet the first bridge be A M_0+1 for ANY A>0, and let the final step be\nb>=0. Start from (1,0), use p=(1,0)/M_0 in the first block, and for\n0<=kappa<=A put\n\n    q_kappa=(-kappa,sqrt(kappa(A-kappa)))/M_1,\n    X=(-A,0),\n    y=X-M_1 q_kappa.\n\nThen ||q_kappa||^2=A kappa/M_1^2 and <q_kappa,y>=0. This parameter\ncovers, up to an orthogonal rotation fixing the first coordinate, EVERY\npossible q in any finite dimension that satisfies\n\n    <q,X-M_1 q>=0.\n\nIndeed that equation forces q_1=-M_1||q||^2/A<=0. Setting\nkappa=-M_1 q_1 gives the displayed norm and fresh squared component\nkappa(A-kappa)/M_1^2, which is nonnegative exactly for kappa in [0,A].\nThus the class being optimized is precisely the homogeneous new-plane\nclass whose second block ends at proximal value zero, with this first\nblock fixed. It does not include arbitrary affine intercepts or positive\nendpoint levels.\n\nAll prefixes are legal for J=max(0,<p,z>,<q_kappa,z>). At an old\nfirst-block proximal point the q-plane is a nonpositive multiple of\nthe p-plane. At a second-block proximal point X-tq_kappa, 1<=t<=M_1,\nthe q-plane has value\n\n    A kappa/M_1 * (1-t/M_1)>=0,\n\nwhile its first coordinate -A+t kappa/M_1 is nonpositive. Hence the\np-plane is <=0. These verify every actual old/new comparison. For\nkappa=0 the second block is already at a minimizer and has gradient\nzero; no strict positivity is presumed at that endpoint.\n\nLet\n\n    c=1/M_0,  a=1-c,  L=A+2a,\n    epsilon=(b-1)/M_1,\n    d=(kappa,-sqrt(kappa(A-kappa))),\n    z_A=(a,0),  e=z_A-y,\n    D=(A-kappa)L+a^2.\n\nFor epsilon>=0 the terminal point is y+epsilon d. The complete retained\nconjugate maximum is again exactly four terms:\n\n    H_kappa(v)=||v||^2/2+max(0,\n                   <z_A,v>-ac,\n                   <y,v>,\n                   <y,v>+(M_1-1)(<q_kappa,v>-||q_kappa||^2)). (20)\n\nFor M_1=1 the last term duplicates the preceding term. The endpoint\ndata produce (20) directly: first-block proximal heights are <p,z>,\nand second-block proximal heights are <q_kappa,z>. All intermediate\nquadratics are convex combinations of the two endpoints of their own\nblock. Accepted fact 1252b8823bb5a290 gives the fixed maximal interpolant\nF_kappa=H_kappa^*. The initial-jet inequality <p,z><=0 at every\nminimizer proves the actual closest distance is one, for every kappa.\n\nDefine clip_[0,1](u)=max(0,min(1,u)), and put\n\n    tau=clip_[0,1](a(kappa epsilon-c)/D),\n    v=epsilon d-tau e.                                      (21)\n\nHere D>=a^2>0. The useful identities are\n\n    ||d||^2=A kappa,    <d,e>=a kappa,\n    <y,d>=0,           <y,e>=-(A-kappa)(A+a)<=0.              (22)\n\nIf tau=0, then kappa epsilon<=c, the y-term and zero term tie,\nand the z_A-term is <=0. If 0<tau<1, the y-term and z_A-term tie,\nsince <e,v>=ac, and the y-term is >=0. If tau=1, then\n<e,v>>=ac, so the z_A-term dominates the y-term. It is also >=0:\nthe inequality a(kappa epsilon-c)>=D implies\n\n    v_1=-(A-kappa)+kappa epsilon-a\n        >= (A-kappa)(A/a+1)+c >= c.\n\nFor 0<kappa<=A, the fourth term never exceeds the y-term. In the\ninterior case,\n\n    <q_kappa,v>=-kappa(A epsilon-a tau)/M_1<=0,\n\nbecause a tau<=a^2 kappa epsilon/D<=A epsilon; the last inequality\nuses a^2 kappa<=A D. For tau=0 it is immediate. For tau=1, the\ncondition above gives kappa epsilon>=D/a+c>=a+c=1, hence\nA epsilon>=1>a. At kappa=0 this fourth term simply equals the y-term.\n\nFor 0<tau<1, y+tau e is an active convex combination of slopes;\nat tau=0 or1 the appropriate endpoint is active. Since\nx_n=v+y+tau e, this proves the exact terminal gradient and value for\nEVERY kappa and EVERY epsilon>=0. The value is\n\n    V_kappa = min_(0<=t<=1) J_kappa(t),\n    J_kappa(t)=A kappa epsilon^2\n               -2t a(kappa epsilon-c)\n               +t^2[(A-kappa)L+a^2].                        (23)\n\nThis also proves that V_kappa is concave in kappa: it is a minimum of\naffine functions of kappa. There is no numerical or existence step in\nthe following optimization.\n\n### Exact global optimizer in kappa\n\nSet\n\n    epsilon_low=L/[A M_0(A+a)],\n    epsilon_high=L/A.\n\nWhen epsilon_low<epsilon<epsilon_high, choose\n\n    kappa_opt=A(A+a)/L + a/[M_0 epsilon(A+a)],\n    tau_opt=epsilon A/L.                                    (24)\n\nThese inequalities ensure 0<kappa_opt<A and 0<tau_opt<1. This is the\nglobal optimizer over the ENTIRE kappa interval. To prove it, fix\nt=tau_opt in (23). The coefficient of kappa is\n\n    A epsilon^2-2a epsilon t-L t^2=0.\n\nConsequently (23) bounds every V_kappa by the SAME number\n\n    V_opt=epsilon^2 [A(A+a)/L]^2 + 2epsilon Aac/L.             (25)\n\nSubstitution of (24) in the unconstrained minimizer of (23) gives\nexactly tau_opt, so this bound is attained. The argument is an explicit\nupper certificate on this parameter class, paired with its displayed\nrealizing direction; it assumes no minimax interchange.\n\nOutside that open epsilon interval, kappa_opt=A is a global optimizer.\nHere is a direct proof including cases when the interval is empty.\nAt kappa=A let\n\n    t_A=clip_[0,1]((A epsilon-c)/a).\n\nIf epsilon<=epsilon_low, then t_A<=epsilon A/L. If\nepsilon>=epsilon_high, then t_A<=1<=epsilon A/L. In either case the\ncoefficient A epsilon^2-2a epsilon t_A-L t_A^2 of kappa in\nJ_kappa(t_A) is nonnegative. For 0<=kappa<=A it follows that\n\n    V_kappa<=J_kappa(t_A)<=J_A(t_A)=V_A.\n\nThus the endpoint A attains the global maximum, and both transition\nequalities cause no loss. If epsilon_low>=epsilon_high, these two\noutside ranges cover every epsilon>=0 and no interior case exists.\n\nFor this scalar endpoint write x=A epsilon. Its exact value is\n\n    V_A=x^2,                   0<=x<=c,\n        2cx-c^2,               c<=x<=1,\n        (x-a)^2+2ac,           x>=1.                          (26)\n\nThis follows either from (23) or directly from the three possible\nclipped values of t_A. Formulas (24)-(26) are a complete constructive\nsolution for every finite b>=1, unequal M_0,M_1, and A>0.\n\nIf 0<=b<=1, the final point lies on the segment from y+q_kappa to y.\nEvery retained interpolant has F(y)=0 and gradient zero there, and has\ngradient q_kappa and value ||q_kappa||^2/2 at y+q_kappa. The unit\nLipschitz bound and the triangle inequality force gradient t q_kappa\nat y+t q_kappa for 0<=t<=1: the distances to gradients 0 and q_kappa\nare at most t||q_kappa|| and (1-t)||q_kappa||, whose sum is their\nmutual distance. Equality forces the collinear point. Integrating\ngives the exact final value\n\n    (1-b)^2 A kappa/M_1^2.\n\nIt is maximized by kappa=A. This handles zero final steps and b=1.\n\nFor the remaining degenerate first mass M_0=1, its sole nonzero\ninitial jet has gradient (1,0) and proximal point zero. Taking kappa=A\nmakes y=0 and reduces the maximal terminal function on the positive\naxis to x^2/2, because every negative-side retained conjugate affine\nterm is nonpositive there. It attains A^2(b-1)^2/M_1^2 for b>=1.\nFor any kappa the forced minimizer y and smoothness bound give the\nupper bound A kappa(b-1)^2/M_1^2, proving optimality. The b<=1\nsegment proof gives the same formula. Thus M_0=1 needs no division\nby a or omitted limiting argument.\n\n### Quantitative improvement on the same full-horizon family\n\nIn (7) take M_0=M_1=M, A=M^(1/12), epsilon=M^(-1/3). The strict\ninterior regime applies: epsilon<1<epsilon_high, and\n\n    epsilon_low <= (A+2)/(A^2 M) <= 2/(AM) < epsilon\n\nfor A>=4 and M>=4^12. Hence (24) is an explicit legal direction on\nEXACTLY THE SAME schedules, and its error is (25). Since\n(A+a)/(A+2a)>=5/6 for A>=4 and 0<a<=1,\n\n    V_opt >= (25/36) A^2 epsilon^2 >= (1/2) M^(-1/2).         (27)\n\nThis exceeds the earlier bound M^(-7/12) by at least A/2, which\ntends to infinity. All bounds (11)-(17) on the actual schedules stay\nunchanged. In particular (27) exceeds 1/B_n at every n>=24578,\nwhile the three baseline ratios still tend to zero. Against every\nscalar continuation in Section8 its gain is at least M^(1/3)/4.\n\nThe uncompleted envelope still has terminal value zero. Indeed, for\nthese parameters A epsilon=M^(-1/4)<=1/64 and a>=1/2. Also\n\n    A-kappa_opt = Aa/(A+2a)-a/[M epsilon(A+a)] >= a/2,\n\nusing A/(A+2a)>=2/3 and 1/[M epsilon(A+a)]<=M^(-3/4)<=1/6.\nThus epsilon kappa_opt<=A epsilon<=a/2<=A-kappa_opt. Both old\nplanes are nonpositive at the terminal point. This checks the claimed\ncompletion effect after optimizing the angle, rather than inferring\nit from the earlier fixed-angle example.\n\nFor an arbitrary supplied schedule, each choice of an earlier checkpoint\nb_0>1 and its final step determines M_0,M_1,A=(b_0-1)/M_0. Equations\n(24)-(26), the b<=1 formula, and the M_0=1 case therefore give a\nconcrete two-checkpoint lower bound whenever those blocks exhaust the\nschedule. Maximizing over the finitely many possible earlier checkpoints\nis another valid lower-bound functional; the formulas fully solve its\ncontinuous direction parameter. No comparison with B_n for every\nschedule is claimed. In particular, this zero-level second-block class\nhas value zero when the final step is exactly one. Positive endpoint\nlevels or a different prefix remain necessary for that part of the\nall-schedule problem.\n\n## 10. The unit-final-step obstruction survives positive levels and offsets\n\nThe zero value at b=1 in Section9 is not repaired at the benchmark scale\nmerely by allowing a positive second-block proximal level. A bound for\nthe ENTIRE constant-second-gradient class is available without choosing\nan envelope representation.\n\nLet an arbitrary convex 1-smooth f have minimum zero at the origin. Let\nthe start of its last ordinary block be X, let that block have sum M_1-1,\nand suppose its gradient is the same vector q at every actual sample in\nthat block, including its last query. Append a unit final step. Its last\nqueried position is x_E=X-(M_1-1)q. Interpolation with the optimum and\nthe unit-step descent inequality give, respectively,\n\n    f(x_E)<=<q,x_E>-||q||^2/2,\n    f(x_E-q)<=f(x_E)-||q||^2/2.\n\nConsequently\n\n    2f(x_E-q) <=2<q,X>-2M_1||q||^2\n       = ||X||^2/(2M_1)-2M_1||q-X/(2M_1)||^2\n       <= ||X||^2/(2M_1).                                   (28)\n\nThis holds in every dimension, with arbitrary positive values, arbitrary\naffine intercepts in any realizing envelope, and arbitrary behavior\naway from the retained samples. The constant-gradient hypothesis is\nthe only special restriction on the final ordinary block.\n\nFor the first-block geometry of Section9, X=(-A,0), and all retained\nfirst jets ensure the true closest initial distance is one. The single\ncut at the preceding checkpoint AM_0+1, on the same schedule ending in\na unit step, already gives exactly\n\n    K_cut=A^2/(2M_1+1).\n\nEquation (28) is only a factor 1+1/(2M_1) larger. Thus this whole class,\neven after freeing every second-block level, direction, intercept and\nthe maximal terminal completion, cannot beat that baseline by a growing\nfactor when the final step is one. On the modified schedules (7) with\nthe last step replaced by one, its bound is M^(-5/6)/2=o(1/B_n), using\nthe same exact benchmark upper estimate as in (15).\n\nThis closes only the proposed extension by positive levels INSIDE a\nconstant-gradient last block. It leaves varying gradients inside that\nblock, more chronological cells, and the unrestricted worst case open.\nIt identifies a precise missing mechanism for extending the new\nterminal gain to arbitrary schedules.\n\n## Scope and remaining task\n\nThis joint proof supplies an exact arbitrary-block terminal policy, a\nfour-anchor active-set formula, all old/new comparisons, the actual\nboundary gradient, true distance normalization, and a quantitative\nfamily at every large horizon that defeats several baseline channels.\nIt does not assert that every schedule has an advantageous two-block\npartition, that the completed function remains adverse after an\narbitrary further suffix, or that a greedy intermediate completion is\nglobally optimal. The all-schedule exact-profile comparison remains open.\n\nTargeted Matlas searches returned no external theorem used in the proof.\nThe exact accepted predecessors above were read. A search hit for an\nolder lower-bound paper returned HTTP 503 and was not relied on. All\nnew results remain candidates because the current assignment allocates\nno additional verifier requests.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "dfde2fa326c908bf", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "prior_finding": "e2dad1cf3b95b8d7", "artifact": "research/max_terminal_completion_profile_gap.md", "sections": "9-10 add new derivations; {"id": "f7cee3034df8cb7f", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T15:10:54.475588+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Exact saturated-cut intrinsic certificate interface: all cross-block multipliers are forced, the full PSD matrix splits into prefix and tail blocks, and the tail retains the signed credit E_theta=V_tail-theta sum b_i Q_(i,terminal), theta=1-(D/T)^2. An explicit fully nonnegative three-step parent certificate violates the proposed tail coefficient floor. This refutes automatic direct restriction, not existence of a suitable re-selection or arbitrary-tail heredity.", "evidence": "# Saturated-cut certificates retain a signed terminal-interpolation credit\n\nStatus: self-contained candidate, NOT verifier-accepted. No solver or\nfact_submit was used. The full initial-distance objective theorem is not\nbeing restricted here: the statement concerns the explicitly specified\nintrinsic primitive energy. The only accepted background used is the\nnecessity of the interpolation inequalities in fact 1252b8823bb5a290.\n\n## 1. Definitions and an exact identity\n\nWork at smoothness one. On a GD trajectory for a list h of sum s and\ncapacity C=1+s, write g_i=grad f(x_i), f_i=f(x_i), and\n\n  Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <= 0,\n  V_h=2 sum_(i<n) h_i(f_i-f_n)\n          +sum_(i<n) h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sC||g_n||^2.\n\nTake two lists a,b of capacities A,D, respectively, and set\n\n  T=A+D+r,  r>0,  rT=AD,\n  kappa=(T/D)^2=(A/r)^2,\n  theta=1-1/kappa.\n\nIn particular 0<r<min(A,D), kappa>1 and 0<theta<1. The joined\nschedule is h=(a,1+r,b). Denote its bridge query by L and the tail\nqueries by j=0,...,m; z=m is the final query. Tail indices in Q always\nmean the corresponding original joined indices. Define\n\n  J_b=sum_(j<m) b_j Q_(j,z),\n  E_theta(b)=V_b-theta J_b,\n  C_cross=sum_(j<m) b_j Q_(j,L)+Q_(z,L)+r Q_(L,z).\n\nThe exact identity, including every value term, is\n\n  V_h = V_a + kappa E_theta(b) - (kappa-1) C_cross.       (1)\n\nAll coefficients in -C_cross are nonnegative on actual inequalities\n-Q. Thus universal nonnegativity of V_a and E_theta(b) is a sufficient\ncondition for universal nonnegativity of V_h. It is not necessary here\nto assume V_b>=0. Whether this genuinely enlarges the allowable tail\nclass is a separate question.\n\nHere is a direct audit of (1). Put p=g_L, q=g_z, and\nH=sum_(j<m)b_j g_j. Then x_tail,0=x_L-(1+r)p and x_z=x_tail,0-H.\nFor C=C_cross+J_b the value part is\n\n  C_f=2sum_(j<m)b_j f_j+(r-D)f_L+(2-D-r)f_z.\n\nExpand each Q and use\n2sum_j b_j<g_j,sum_(i<j)b_i g_i>=||H||^2-sum_j b_j^2||g_j||^2.\nThe gradient part reduces exactly to\n\n  C_g=sum_(j<m)b_j(1-b_j)||g_j||^2\n            +[(D-r)/2-r^2]||p||^2+(D+r)||q||^2/2.\n\nAll mixed p,H,q products cancel. The identities\n\n  (kappa-1)(D-r)=2(A+r),\n  kappa r^2=A^2,  kappa D^2=T^2\n\nthen show V_h=V_a+kappa V_b-(kappa-1)C coefficient by coefficient:\nthe f_L coefficient is 2(1+r), the f_z coefficient is -2(T-1),\nthe p-square coefficient is -r(1+r), and the q-square coefficient\nis -T(T-1). Substituting kappa theta=kappa-1 proves (1).\n\n## 2. Full intrinsic certificates force the bridge coefficients\n\nFor this part assume the entries of a and b are strictly positive;\neither list may be empty. Suppose an explicit intrinsic certificate is\ngiven:\n\n  M_h=V_h+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij,\n  lambda_ij>=0,\n\nwhere values cancel and M_h is PSD in the full independent formal\ngradient vector. Allowing the optimum index and x_0 initially does not\nenlarge this class. Indeed V_h has no x_0-square coefficient, so PSD\nforces the x_0 row to vanish. Its cross coefficients are -lambda_i,star,\nwhich forces every lambda_i,star=0. Summing the value-flow equalities\nthen gives sum_i lambda_star,i=0, so these multipliers also vanish.\nThis argument relies on the absence of an initial-distance term and\ndoes NOT apply to a full objective certificate.\n\nThere are two explicit equality trajectories for V_h. A scalar Huber\ntrajectory staying on one affine ray has every gradient equal to 1,\nall finite Q zero, and V_h=0. A second trajectory uses the derivative\n\n  f'(x)=min(1,max(x,-eta)),  eta=r/D,\n\nstarting at A. The prefix gradient is 1, its last query is 1, the\nbridge lands at -r, and the tail gradient is -eta until its endpoint\n-eta. Every incoming optimum residual is zero. Distance telescoping\ngives V_h=A^2-T^2 eta^2=0.\n\nFor completeness, if R_i is a prefix query's remaining prefix sum\nand R_j is a tail query's remaining tail sum, this second trajectory\nhas\n\n  Q_(prefix i,tail j)=-(1+eta)eta R_j,\n  Q_(tail j,prefix i)=-(1+eta)R_i.                       (2)\n\nEvery within-block Q is zero. Since M_h>=0 and all lambda Q<=0,\nequality of V_h forces multiplier complementarity and M_h to vanish\non each equality gradient vector. Those vectors span the two block\nindicators e_A,e_B, so both indicators lie in the kernel of M_h.\n\nBy (2), the only potentially nonzero cross multipliers are\n\n  nu_i=lambda_(prefix i,z),  mu_j=lambda_(tail j,L).\n\nFor a prefix index i<L, the corresponding row of M_h e_B is\n-D nu_i/2. Thus nu_i=0 for every i<L. Write nu=nu_L.\nFor a nonterminal tail index j, the corresponding row of M_h e_A\nis [r mu_j-nu b_j]/2; at z it is [r mu_z-nu]/2.\nConsequently\n\n  mu_j=(nu/r)b_j  (j<m),  mu_z=nu/r.\n\nThe sum of the prefix value-flow equations is\nsum_j mu_j-nu=2(A+r). Therefore all cross multipliers are fixed:\n\n  lambda_(j,L)=(kappa-1)b_j (j<m),\n  lambda_(z,L)=kappa-1,\n  lambda_(L,z)=r(kappa-1),                              (3)\n\nand every other cross multiplier is zero. The mixed gradient block\nof the full matrix vanishes, not merely its value on equality data.\n\n## 3. Exact cone interface and the sign that cannot be discarded\n\nLet lambda^A,lambda^B be the two within-block multiplier arrays of\nthe given full certificate. Define a signed tail array by\n\n  lambda_hat_ij=lambda^B_ij/kappa\n                 -theta b_i * 1_(i<m,j=z).             (4)\n\nCombining (1) with (3) yields the full block-diagonal identity\n\n  M_h = M_a + kappa M_b,\n  M_a=V_a+sum lambda^A_ij Q_ij,\n  M_b=V_b+sum lambda_hat_ij Q_ij.                       (5)\n\nEach summand has its own value cancellation, since its values are in\na disjoint block. Full PSD is equivalent to PSD of both summands.\nThe prefix multipliers are nonnegative. The exact tail constraints are\n\n  lambda_hat_ij>=0 except possibly i<m,j=z,\n  lambda_hat_(i,z)>=-theta b_i.                        (6)\n\nConversely, any prefix intrinsic certificate and any PSD tail identity\nwith the value cancellation and bounds (6) give a nonnegative full\ncertificate by (3),(4). Equivalently, the tail has an ordinary\nnonnegative certificate for E_theta(b), not necessarily for V_b.\n\nThus direct restriction to an ordinary primitive tail certificate would\nrequire the stronger bounds\n\n  lambda^B_(i,z)>=(kappa-1)b_i.                        (7)\n\nThey are not implied for each full certificate. The next exact example\nproves this, including full nonnegativity and PSD.\n\n## 4. Exact full certificate violating the proposed coefficient floor\n\nUse the empty prefix A=1, tail b=(1,1) of capacity D=3, and\n\n  r=sqrt(7)-2, T=sqrt(7)+2, kappa=(11+4sqrt(7))/9.\n\nThe full schedule is h=(sqrt(7)-1,1,1). Use original indices 0,1,2,3,\nwhere 0 is the bridge query and 1,2,3 are the tail queries. Set\n\n  lambda_10=lambda_20=lambda_30=kappa-1,\n  lambda_03=r(kappa-1),\n  lambda_12=5kappa/2, lambda_21=17kappa/4,\n  lambda_23=37kappa/4-1, lambda_32=12kappa,\n  lambda_13=3kappa/4-1,\n\nwith every unlisted multiplier zero. All are nonnegative, since\nkappa>4/3. Direct substitution, or (1) and the audited tail identity\nbelow, gives\n\n  V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij\n   =kappa { ||(g_1-g_2)-(g_2-g_3)/8||^2\n                         +(255/64)||g_2-g_3||^2 } >=0. (8)\n\nFor the tail alone, write u=g_0-g_1 and v=g_1-g_2 using local indices.\nIts signed multiplier list is\n\n  lambda_hat_01=5/2, lambda_hat_10=17/4,\n  lambda_hat_12=33/4, lambda_hat_21=12,\n  lambda_hat_02=-1/4, lambda_hat_20=0.\n\nThe value coefficients cancel, and expansion gives\n\n  V_(1,1)+sum lambda_hat_ij Q_ij\n      =||u||^2-<u,v>/4+4||v||^2\n      =||u-v/8||^2+(255/64)||v||^2.\n\nNevertheless lambda_13=(kappa-1)-kappa/4 is strictly below the\nproposed floor kappa-1. The restricted terminal coefficient is -1/4.\nThis refutes the inference of (7) from full nonnegativity and PSD.\n\nThere IS another ordinary primitive certificate for this tail:\nlambda_01=2,lambda_10=4,lambda_12=8,lambda_21=12, all other\nmultipliers zero, gives ||u||^2+4||v||^2. Accordingly this example\ndoes not disprove existence of a suitable re-selection, arbitrary-tail\nheredity, or the original asymptotic minimax problem. It identifies\nthe exact missing sign assertion rather than replacing it with PSD.\n\n## 5. A particular nonprimitive tail cannot exploit this credit\n\nThe previously considered b=(17/10,2,6/5) fails even E_theta for\nevery 0<=theta<=1. Here is a self-contained witness.\nPut m=7/34, ell=21/85. Define the continuous derivative\n\n  f'(x)=x                  for x<=m,\n         m                 for m<=x<=m+ell,\n         x-ell             for x>=m+ell,\n\nand integrate with f(0)=0. Its derivative is nondecreasing and\n1-Lipschitz, so f is convex differentiable 1-smooth with minimizer 0.\nStarting at x_0=106/85 gives\n\n  (x_0,x_1,x_2,x_3)=(106/85,-77/170,77/170,7/34),\n  (g_0,g_1,g_2,g_3)=(1,-77/170,7/34,7/34).\n\nThe endpoint is core: x_3=g_3=m, f_3=m^2/2. Define\nI_i=<g_i,x_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2. Direct scalar evaluation gives\n\n  I_0=ell(1-m)=567/2890, I_1=I_2=I_3=0,\n  sum_(i<3)b_i I_i=567/1700,\n  V_b=-13581/23120.\n\nAt a core endpoint, expansion gives Q_(i,3)=-I_i. Thus\n\n  E_theta(b)=-13581/23120+theta*567/1700\n           <= -29349/115600 <0.                       (9)\n\nThe value of V_b can also be checked without integrating every term:\ndistance telescoping gives\nV_b=x_0^2-D^2g_3^2-2sum b_i I_i, with D=59/10.\nThis proves (9) exactly.\n\nConsequently this specific tail cannot acquire a valid E_theta\ncertificate at any saturated join parameter. A rank-one coefficient\nmatching attempt on this same fixed tail also failed, but (9) is the\nstronger actual-function obstruction. Neither statement excludes other\nnonprimitive tails. No numerical schedule screen was reopened.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": "max4_saturated_certificate_credit", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "artifact": "local_memory/saturated_certificate_credit.md", "status": "complete_candidate_unverified", "verification_allocation": "no fact_submit call", "numerical_screen": "closed; only a fixed exact rational coefficient check"}, "glossary": {"V_h": "Intrinsic primitive energy 2sum h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_n||^2, s=sum h_i.", "A,D,T": "Capacities 1+step sum of the prefix, tail, and joined schedule, respectively, local to this interface.", "r": "Positive bridge excess with bridge step1+r and saturated relation rT=AD.", "kappa": "Positive scale (T/D)^2=(A/r)^2 in this saturated-cut interface.", "theta": "Terminal-interpolation credit fraction1-1/kappa, lying strictly between0 and1.", "L": "Bridge query index in this local normalized smoothness-one interface; not the general smoothness constant.", "J_b": "Weighted incoming-terminal sum sum_(j<m)b_j Q_(j,m) on the supplied tail.", "E_theta(b)": "Signed value state V_b-theta J_b, retaining actual interpolation slacks.", "C_cross": "Actual cross-inequality sum sum_(j<m)b_j Q_(j,L)+Q_(m,L)+r Q_(L,m).", "lambda_hat": "Tail multiplier array after dividing its full-parent weights by kappa and subtracting theta b_i on incoming-terminal edges."}}
{"id": "b3b46bcdbeb86b2c", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T15:11:16.500259+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "New universal schedule functional candidate: for EVERY nonnegative length-n schedule, W_n(h)>=max(K_n(h),D_n^two(h)), where D_n^two is the maximum over earlier h_j>1 of gamma_j^2[(h_j-1)(h_last-1)_+/((1+P_j)(1+S_j))]^2, gamma_j=(h_j-1+P_j)/(h_j-1+2P_j), P_j the mass before j and S_j the mass strictly between j and last. A fixed explicit two-dimensional direction realizes each term; 1/2<=gamma_j<=1. On the constructed full-horizon family D_n^two>=(25/36)M^(-1/2), while K, all quadratics and the universal lens are o(1/B_n).", "evidence": "# A quantitative exact terminal completion on nonperiodic schedules\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint proof candidate. No verifier or numerical-solver\nrequest. This is an explicit family at every sufficiently large horizon,\nnot the unrestricted all-schedule comparison. Every new calculation used\nbelow is included here; no unverified finding is a premise.\n\nVerified predecessors used:\n\n- `1252b8823bb5a290`: the complete smooth-convex interpolation inequality\n  and the pointwise maximal completion at a genuinely unqueried terminal.\n- `dfde2fa326c908bf`: the exact Chebyshev steps, their sum and polynomial,\n  and the universal complex-lens construction and polynomial estimate.\n- `b98bb807d514e80d`: the single-cut functional K_n, the exact benchmark\n  recursions, and B_n <= 1+4 n^p for p=log_2(3).\n\nWe use smoothness one and initial distance one throughout. Thus the\nnormalized objective is 2f(x_n). General smoothness follows by scaling.\n\n## 1. An arbitrary-block prefix with a completely specified fresh direction\n\nLet M>=2 and A>=4. Prescribe ANY two nonnegative ordinary lists of sum\nM-1 each, separated by the checkpoint AM+1. A final step b will be added\nafter the second list. Work in R^2 and put\n\n    nu=sqrt(2A-4),\n    p=(1,0)/M,                  q=(-2,nu)/M,\n    J(z)=max(0,<p,z>,<q,z>),\n    f_old=env_1 J,              x_init=(1,0).\n\nThe squared boundary gradient is exactly ||q||^2=2A/M^2. The fresh\ncomponent of q has squared norm (2A-4)/M^2; it is not dropped from the\nstate or normalization.\n\nIn the first block every gradient is p. To check this, a query after\nordinary partial mass u has proposed proximal point\n\n    z=(1-(u+1)/M,0),       0<=u<=M-1.\n\nHere <p,z>>=0 and <q,z>=-2<p,z>. Thus p is an active subgradient of J.\nAt the checkpoint query the position is p and its proximal point is 0.\nThe checkpoint AM+1 sends the trajectory to X=(-A,0).\n\nIn the second block every gradient is q. Write t=u+1 in [1,M]. Its\nproposed proximal point is X-tq. The current height is\n\n    <q,X-tq>=(2A/M)(1-t/M)>=0.\n\nIts excess over the p-plane is\n\n    <q-p,X-tq>=3A/M-(2A+2)t/M^2 >= (A-2)/M >0.\n\nThis checks both other planes at every actual query, even if an\nindividual ordinary step is very large. The comparisons are affine in\nt, and the displayed bounds hold on the whole interval. The old q-plane\nwas already checked at every first-block query, so both cross directions\nand every queried sample belong to the same fixed J.\n\nThe envelope justification is elementary: if g is an active-plane\nsubgradient at z and x=z+g, its subgradient inequality makes z the unique\nminimizer of J(w)+||x-w||^2/2. Adding the inequalities for two such\nproximal pairs proves\n\n    ||g(x)-g(x')||^2 <= <g(x)-g(x'),x-x'>.\n\nThe minimizing inequalities identify g as the derivative of the convex\nenvelope, which is consequently 1-smooth. All claimed GD updates and\nsample values follow from this fixed envelope.\n\nAt the last query, just before the final step b, the proximal point and\nposition are respectively\n\n    y=X-Mq=(2-A,-nu),         x_last=y+q.\n\nOne has <q,y>=0, <p,y><0, f_old(x_last)=||q||^2/2. At ALL old\nqueries the value and gradient are retained in the completion below.\n\n## 2. The exact four-anchor maximal interpolant\n\nSet\n\n    a=1-1/M,       z_A=(a,0),       delta_A=a/M,\n    z_B=X-q,\n    delta_B=(M-1)||q||^2.\n\nFor a retained jet (x,g,f), its proximal point z=x-g and height\ndelta=f-||g||^2/2 give the conjugate quadratic\n\n    ||v||^2/2 + <z,v> - delta.\n\nThis is exactly the quadratic H_j in fact 1252b8823bb5a290 after\nexpansion. In the first block (z,delta) traces the line segment from\n(z_A,delta_A) to (0,0). Both endpoints are actual queried samples. In\nthe second block it traces the segment from (z_B,delta_B) to (y,0),\nagain with both endpoints queried. Therefore the maximum of ALL retained\nquadratics, including the optimum jet, is exactly\n\n    H(v)=||v||^2/2 + max(0,\n                        <z_A,v>-delta_A,\n                        <y,v>,\n                        <y,v>+(M-1)(<q,v>-||q||^2)).             (1)\n\nNo constraint is deleted: intermediate quadratics are convex\ncombinations of these endpoint quadratics. By the accepted fact,\n\n    F(x)=sup_v [<x,v>-H(v)]                                    (2)\n\nis one fixed finite convex 1-smooth function, preserves every old query,\nhas F(0)=0=min F, and is the pointwise maximal such interpolant.\nIt is selected before running the complete prescribed schedule.\n\nThe actual closest-minimizer distance stays ONE. The initial jet is\n\n    x_init=(1,0),  g_init=p,  F(x_init)=1/M-1/(2M^2).\n\nFor any minimizer z of F, the accepted interpolation inequality between\nthis jet and (z,0,0) gives\n\n    0 >= F(x_init)+<p,z-x_init>+||p||^2/2 = <p,z>.\n\nHence z has first coordinate <=0 and ||x_init-z||>=1. Origin attains\ndistance one. This proves the genuine normalization for F, not merely\nfor the old envelope.\n\n## 3. Closed formula at the genuinely unqueried terminal\n\nLet\n\n    b=1+M epsilon,    0<=epsilon<=1,\n    d=(2,-nu)=-Mq,\n    e=z_A-y=(A-2+a,nu),\n    D=||e||^2=(A-2+a)^2+2A-4.\n\nThe actual terminal point is\n\n    x_n=x_last-bq = y+epsilon d.\n\nDefine\n\n    tau=a(2epsilon-1/M)_+/D,\n    v=epsilon d-tau e.                                       (3)\n\nHere 0<=tau<=2epsilon/D<=1/2 because D>=(A-2)^2>=4.\nThe elementary products that will be used are\n\n    ||d||^2=2A,   <d,e>=2a,   <y,d>=0,\n    <y,e>=-(A-2)(A+a)<0.                                    (4)\n\nWe now check ALL four affine terms in (1) at v.\n\nIf tau=0, the y-term and zero term both equal zero, and the z_A-term\nis a(2epsilon-1/M)<=0. If tau>0, then\n\n    <e,v>=2a epsilon-tau D=a/M,\n\nso the y-term and z_A-term tie. The y-term is nonnegative because\n<y,v>=-tau<y,e>>=0. Finally\n\n    <q,v>=-[2A epsilon-2a tau]/M <=0,\n\nsince tau<=2epsilon/D and 2A-4/D>0. Consequently the z_B-term is\nstrictly below the y-term, its difference being\n(M-1)(<q,v>-||q||^2)<0. This also handles epsilon=0.\n\nFor tau=0, y is an active subgradient of the max-affine part of H.\nFor tau>0, y+tau e is the convex combination (1-tau)y+tau z_A\nof its active slopes. In both cases\n\n    x_n=v+y+tau e\n\nis a subgradient of H at v. Strong convexity of H therefore makes v\nthe unique maximizer in (2), and it is the ACTUAL terminal gradient.\nSubstituting (3)-(4) gives the exact normalized value\n\n    V(M,A,epsilon) := 2F(x_n)\n       = 2A epsilon^2\n         - a^2(2epsilon-1/M)_+^2/D.                         (5)\n\nEvery parameter and endpoint is finite, and epsilon=0 is included.\nIn particular, since a<=1 and D>=4,\n\n    A epsilon^2 <= V(M,A,epsilon) <= 2A epsilon^2.           (6)\n\nThe precise relative loss from the upper bound is at most\n2/(A D)<=2/[A(A-2)^2], which tends to zero as A grows.\nEquation (5), rather than that limit, is used in the comparisons below.\n\nThere is a substantial completion effect here. For A>=4 and epsilon<=1,\nboth <p,x_n> and <q,x_n> are nonpositive. Thus J(x_n)=0 and\nf_old(x_n)=0, whereas (5) is positive for epsilon>0. No queried value\nhas changed: only the behavior away from the retained samples differs.\nThe original old function's unqueried zero set does not constrain the\nmaximal interpolant. Its actual closest minimizer remains at distance one.\n\nThe upper bound in (6) also has an intrinsic interpretation. Every\ninterpolant of the last query and optimum has F(y)=0 by the unit-step\ndescent inequality, because F(y+q)=||q||^2/2. Smoothness then gives\n2F(x_n)<=||x_n-y||^2=2A epsilon^2. The exact completion loses only\nthe displayed finite old-query correction from this upper bound.\n\n## 4. A fixed horizon-dependent choice\n\nFor each integer N>=4096 let Chi_N be the increasing list\n\n    c_j=sec^2((2j-1)pi/[2(2N+1)]),       1<=j<=N.\n\nWrite L_N for THREE successive copies of this list, and define\n\n    M=2N(N+1)+1,       A=M^(1/12),       epsilon=M^(-1/3),\n    h^(N)=(L_N, AM+1, L_N, 1+M^(2/3)),\n    n_N=6N+2.                                                 (7)\n\nThe exact sum formula in dfde2fa326c908bf makes each L_N have mass\nM-1. Also M>=4^12, so A>=4 and the preceding construction applies\nwithout a limiting argument. It gives the explicit value (5), and\n\n    W_(n_N)(h^(N)) >= V(M,A,epsilon) >= M^(-7/12).             (8)\n\nTo obtain a family at EVERY integer horizon n>=24578, take\nN=floor((n-2)/6), and insert n-(6N+2) zero steps at the very beginning\nof (7). These duplicate a retained query, change no trajectory or\nvalue, and preserve the same formula. No new terminal jet is queried\nbefore the final nonzero step. Denote this length-n schedule by h^[n].\n\nThe first bridge is of order M^(13/12), the final bridge is of order\nM^(2/3), and the ordinary lists depend on the horizon. This is not a\nfixed periodic schedule or a bounded-length legality example.\n\n## 5. Every quadratic and every specified single-cut Huber test is smaller\n\nLet Q_n(h) denote the maximum normalized terminal error of all convex\nquadratics with curvature in [0,1], over every dimension. By orthogonal\ndiagonalization this is\n\n    Q_n(h)=max_(0<=lambda<=1) lambda product_j(1-h_j lambda)^2.\n\nSet T=(2N+1)^2=2M-1, and let P_N be the polynomial of Chi_N. The\nverified Chebyshev identity implies\n\n    |P_N(lambda)|^2 <= min(1,1/(T lambda)),   0<lambda<=1.      (9)\n\nFor the first bound, put sqrt(lambda)=sin(theta): the numerator of\nP_N is, up to sign, sin((2N+1)theta), and\n|sin(k theta)|<=k|sin(theta)| follows by telescoping k unit complex\nnumbers. The second bound follows from |Cheb_(2N+1)(sqrt(lambda))|<=1.\n\nThere are six complete copies of Chi_N in (7). For z=T lambda, its\nquadratic error is at most\n\n    T^(-1) z (1+A z)^2(1+z)^2 min(1,z^(-6)).                 (10)\n\nIndeed (AM+1)/T<=A and (1+M^(2/3))/T<=1 for M>=2. If z<=1,\nthe expression following T^(-1) is <=16A^2. If z>=1, it is\n<=16A^2/z<=16A^2. Therefore\n\n    Q_(n_N)(h^(N)) <=16 A^2/T <=16 M^(-5/6).                 (11)\n\nNow consider the exact K_n in fact b98bb807d514e80d. We check every\nindex, not merely the two selected bridges. In the increasing Chi_N,\nthe first entry is <=4/3, and the ratio of successive entries is <=9.\nFor the latter write c_j=csc^2(theta_j), where\ntheta_j=(N-j+1)pi/(2N+1). Consecutive positive angles have ratio <=2,\nand concavity of sin on [0,pi/2] gives c_(j+1)/c_j<=4<=9.\nThe weaker constant 9 is retained in the bounds below. Thus every\nordinary entry satisfies h_j<=9(1+P_j), with P_j the full preceding\nmass. This remains true at the beginning of each repeated copy.\nAlso max c_j=csc^2(pi/(2N+1))<=T/4<M, by sin(t)>=2t/pi.\n\n- A cut in the first L_N has tail mass at least AM. Its single-cut\n  term is at most 81/(2AM) <=41 M^(-5/6).\n- At the bridge AM+1 the prefix mass is M-1 and the tail mass is\n  M-1+b. Its term is A^2/[1+2(M-1+b)]<=A^2/M=M^(-5/6).\n- A cut in the second L_N has prefix mass at least (A+1)M and tail\n  mass at least b>=M^(2/3). Its term is at most\n  1/[2A^2 M^(2/3)] = M^(-5/6)/2.\n- At the final bridge b=1+M^(2/3), one plus the prefix mass is\n  (A+2)M. Its term is M^(-2/3)/(A+2)^2<=M^(-5/6).\n- The global Huber term is <=1/M<=M^(-5/6).\n\nConsequently\n\n    K_(n_N)(h^(N)) <=41 M^(-5/6).                              (12)\n\nInserted zero steps contribute zero cut terms and change no mass, so\n(11)-(12) hold for h^[n] as well. The second-block estimate only needs\nthe maximum individual step, and hence survives arbitrary permutations\nthere. The first-block ordering assumption is used explicitly above.\n\n## 6. The universal complex lens also misses this family\n\nLet Lens_n and Gap_h be exactly those in accepted fact dfde2fa326c908bf.\nSince n>=N, Lens_n is contained in Lens_N. The Chebyshev ellipse\ncalculation in that fact gives\n\n    |z||P_N(z)|^2 <= cosh^2(2)/T,       z in Lens_n.\n\nFor T|z|<=1, the product formula instead gives\n\n    |P_N(z)| <= exp(s(Chi_N)|z|) <= exp(1/6) <= cosh(2).\n\nThe last inequality follows, for example, from exp(1/6)<=6/5 and\ncosh(2)>=3. Thus, with C=cosh(2),\n\n    |P_N(z)|^2 <= C^2 min(1,1/(T|z|)).                         (13)\n\nThe accepted upper bound on a legal polynomial transcript is\nGap_h(z)<=(2Re z-|z|^2)|Poly_n(z)|^2<=2|z||Poly_n(z)|^2.\nUsing (13) and the same two ranges as in (10), now with T|z| as the\nnonnegative scalar variable, proves\n\n    sup_(z in Lens_n) Gap_(h^[n])(z)\n        <=32 cosh^12(2) M^(-5/6).                            (14)\n\nThis bounds ONLY the universal-lens channel; it makes no assertion\nabout the full schedule-dependent legal complex region.\n\n## 7. Comparison with the actual full B_n sequence\n\nNo asymptotic profile constant or limit is assumed. First, the verified\nupper bound B_n<=1+4n^p, p=log_2(3), yields the explicit estimates\n\n    K_n(h^[n])/U_n^ZJ <=41(1+4n^p)/M^(5/6),\n    Q_n(h^[n])/U_n^ZJ <=16(1+4n^p)/M^(5/6),\n    [sup_(z in Lens_n) Gap_(h^[n])(z)]/U_n^ZJ\n        <=32 cosh^12(2)(1+4n^p)/M^(5/6).                      (15)\n\nHere M>=2N^2, n<=6N+7<=8N, and p<5/3 because 27<32. Every\nright-hand side tends to zero. Thus these three established channels\nall miss the exact benchmark on the very same family.\n\nFor a pointwise lower comparison, set s=1+sqrt(2) and\nd_N=floor(log_2 N). In the verified primitive mass recurrence,\n\n    C_j=S_circle(j)+1,\n    C_j=max_(k<j) Omega(C_k,C_(j-k-1)),\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,\n\none has C_j>=C_(j-1), and the balanced choice gives\nC_(2^r-1)>=s^r. The dominant recurrence with its final empty block\ngives B_n>=2C_(n-1)-1. Since n-1>=2^(d_N+2)-1,\n\n    B_n>=2s^(d_N+2)-1>=s^(d_N+2).                            (16)\n\nOn the other hand N<2^(d_N+1) and M<=5N^2, so\n\n    B_n V(M,A,epsilon)\n      >= [s^2/20^(7/12)] [s/2^(7/6)]^d_N\n      > (18/25)(16/15)^d_N >1,                               (17)\n\nfor d_N>=12. For the elementary constants: s>12/5,\n2^(7/6)<9/4 since 2^7<(9/4)^6, and 20^(7/12)<8.\nFinally (16/15)^d_N >=1+d_N/15>=9/5 for d_N>=12, so the\nlast product is at least 162/125>1.\n\nEquations (5), (15), and (17) concern the actual B_n at EVERY integer\nn>=24578, not just a dyadic comparison constant. They prove for this\nexplicit family\n\n    W_n(h^[n]) >= V(M,A,epsilon) > U_n^ZJ,\n    max(K_n(h^[n]),Q_n(h^[n]),sup_Lens_n Gap_(h^[n]))\n        = o(U_n^ZJ).                                         (18)\n\nThe ratio supplied by (17) in fact diverges. This is a concrete gain\nfrom retaining the two-dimensional geometry and completing the free\nterminal. It is not a lower bound of this strength for all schedules.\n\n## 8. A scalar comparison with the same retained first block\n\nThere is a useful further explanation of the dimensional mechanism.\nKeep the first block and all of its scalar jets fixed, including\nx_init=1, gradient 1/M, and optimum 0. After the bridge AM+1 the\nscalar position is -A. Consider ANY legal scalar continuation whose\ngradient is a constant -v throughout the second ordinary block,\nincluding its last query. Convexity gives v>=0. Its last queried\nposition is x_E=-A+(M-1)v. Smoothness and the optimum force\nx_E<=-v if v>0, hence 0<=v<=A/M (the v=0 case has value zero).\n\nInterpolation against the optimum bounds the last queried value by\n\n    f(x_E)<=Av-(M-1/2)v^2 <= A^2/[4(M-1/2)].\n\nAfter the final step b>=1 the position is x_T=-A+(M-1+b)v.\nIf x_T<=0, monotonicity on the negative ray gives f(x_T)<=f(x_E).\nIf x_T>=0, then x_T<=A epsilon. When A epsilon<=1, the retained\ninitial derivative and convexity imply 0<=f'(x)<=1/M on [0,x_T],\nso f(x_T)<=A epsilon/M. Therefore EVERY such scalar continuation has\n\n    2f(x_T)<=max(A^2/(2M-1), 2A epsilon/M).                     (19)\n\nFor (7), A epsilon=M^(-1/4)<=1, and (19) is <=2M^(-5/6).\nThe two-dimensional construction gives >=M^(-7/12), larger by at\nleast M^(1/4)/2. The second scalar block's values and gradient may be\nchosen freely subject to legality; this is more than a comparison with\none specific scalar example. It is still a constant-gradient block\nrestriction, and does not upper-bound every one-dimensional trajectory\non the full schedule.\n\n## 9. Exact optimization of the whole homogeneous second-gradient class\n\nThe parallel component need not be fixed at -2/M. There is an explicit\noptimal choice, and it applies to TWO UNEQUAL prescribed block masses.\nThis section includes its own prefix, active-set, and optimization proof.\n\nLet the two ordinary sums be M_0-1 and M_1-1, with M_0>1, M_1>=1.\nLet the first bridge be A M_0+1 for ANY A>0, and let the final step be\nb>=0. Start from (1,0), use p=(1,0)/M_0 in the first block, and for\n0<=kappa<=A put\n\n    q_kappa=(-kappa,sqrt(kappa(A-kappa)))/M_1,\n    X=(-A,0),\n    y=X-M_1 q_kappa.\n\nThen ||q_kappa||^2=A kappa/M_1^2 and <q_kappa,y>=0. This parameter\ncovers, up to an orthogonal rotation fixing the first coordinate, EVERY\npossible q in any finite dimension that satisfies\n\n    <q,X-M_1 q>=0.\n\nIndeed that equation forces q_1=-M_1||q||^2/A<=0. Setting\nkappa=-M_1 q_1 gives the displayed norm and fresh squared component\nkappa(A-kappa)/M_1^2, which is nonnegative exactly for kappa in [0,A].\nThus the class being optimized is precisely the homogeneous new-plane\nclass whose second block ends at proximal value zero, with this first\nblock fixed. It does not include arbitrary affine intercepts or positive\nendpoint levels.\n\nAll prefixes are legal for J=max(0,<p,z>,<q_kappa,z>). At an old\nfirst-block proximal point the q-plane is a nonpositive multiple of\nthe p-plane. At a second-block proximal point X-tq_kappa, 1<=t<=M_1,\nthe q-plane has value\n\n    A kappa/M_1 * (1-t/M_1)>=0,\n\nwhile its first coordinate -A+t kappa/M_1 is nonpositive. Hence the\np-plane is <=0. These verify every actual old/new comparison. For\nkappa=0 the second block is already at a minimizer and has gradient\nzero; no strict positivity is presumed at that endpoint.\n\nLet\n\n    c=1/M_0,  a=1-c,  L=A+2a,\n    epsilon=(b-1)/M_1,\n    d=(kappa,-sqrt(kappa(A-kappa))),\n    z_A=(a,0),  e=z_A-y,\n    D=(A-kappa)L+a^2.\n\nFor epsilon>=0 the terminal point is y+epsilon d. The complete retained\nconjugate maximum is again exactly four terms:\n\n    H_kappa(v)=||v||^2/2+max(0,\n                   <z_A,v>-ac,\n                   <y,v>,\n                   <y,v>+(M_1-1)(<q_kappa,v>-||q_kappa||^2)). (20)\n\nFor M_1=1 the last term duplicates the preceding term. The endpoint\ndata produce (20) directly: first-block proximal heights are <p,z>,\nand second-block proximal heights are <q_kappa,z>. All intermediate\nquadratics are convex combinations of the two endpoints of their own\nblock. Accepted fact 1252b8823bb5a290 gives the fixed maximal interpolant\nF_kappa=H_kappa^*. The initial-jet inequality <p,z><=0 at every\nminimizer proves the actual closest distance is one, for every kappa.\n\nDefine clip_[0,1](u)=max(0,min(1,u)), and put\n\n    tau=clip_[0,1](a(kappa epsilon-c)/D),\n    v=epsilon d-tau e.                                      (21)\n\nHere D>=a^2>0. The useful identities are\n\n    ||d||^2=A kappa,    <d,e>=a kappa,\n    <y,d>=0,           <y,e>=-(A-kappa)(A+a)<=0.              (22)\n\nIf tau=0, then kappa epsilon<=c, the y-term and zero term tie,\nand the z_A-term is <=0. If 0<tau<1, the y-term and z_A-term tie,\nsince <e,v>=ac, and the y-term is >=0. If tau=1, then\n<e,v>>=ac, so the z_A-term dominates the y-term. It is also >=0:\nthe inequality a(kappa epsilon-c)>=D implies\n\n    v_1=-(A-kappa)+kappa epsilon-a\n        >= (A-kappa)(A/a+1)+c >= c.\n\nFor 0<kappa<=A, the fourth term never exceeds the y-term. In the\ninterior case,\n\n    <q_kappa,v>=-kappa(A epsilon-a tau)/M_1<=0,\n\nbecause a tau<=a^2 kappa epsilon/D<=A epsilon; the last inequality\nuses a^2 kappa<=A D. For tau=0 it is immediate. For tau=1, the\ncondition above gives kappa epsilon>=D/a+c>=a+c=1, hence\nA epsilon>=1>a. At kappa=0 this fourth term simply equals the y-term.\n\nFor 0<tau<1, y+tau e is an active convex combination of slopes;\nat tau=0 or1 the appropriate endpoint is active. Since\nx_n=v+y+tau e, this proves the exact terminal gradient and value for\nEVERY kappa and EVERY epsilon>=0. The value is\n\n    V_kappa = min_(0<=t<=1) J_kappa(t),\n    J_kappa(t)=A kappa epsilon^2\n               -2t a(kappa epsilon-c)\n               +t^2[(A-kappa)L+a^2].                        (23)\n\nThis also proves that V_kappa is concave in kappa: it is a minimum of\naffine functions of kappa. There is no numerical or existence step in\nthe following optimization.\n\n### Exact global optimizer in kappa\n\nSet\n\n    epsilon_low=L/[A M_0(A+a)],\n    epsilon_high=L/A.\n\nWhen epsilon_low<epsilon<epsilon_high, choose\n\n    kappa_opt=A(A+a)/L + a/[M_0 epsilon(A+a)],\n    tau_opt=epsilon A/L.                                    (24)\n\nThese inequalities ensure 0<kappa_opt<A and 0<tau_opt<1. This is the\nglobal optimizer over the ENTIRE kappa interval. To prove it, fix\nt=tau_opt in (23). The coefficient of kappa is\n\n    A epsilon^2-2a epsilon t-L t^2=0.\n\nConsequently (23) bounds every V_kappa by the SAME number\n\n    V_opt=epsilon^2 [A(A+a)/L]^2 + 2epsilon Aac/L.             (25)\n\nSubstitution of (24) in the unconstrained minimizer of (23) gives\nexactly tau_opt, so this bound is attained. The argument is an explicit\nupper certificate on this parameter class, paired with its displayed\nrealizing direction; it assumes no minimax interchange.\n\nOutside that open epsilon interval, kappa_opt=A is a global optimizer.\nHere is a direct proof including cases when the interval is empty.\nAt kappa=A let\n\n    t_A=clip_[0,1]((A epsilon-c)/a).\n\nIf epsilon<=epsilon_low, then t_A<=epsilon A/L. If\nepsilon>=epsilon_high, then t_A<=1<=epsilon A/L. In either case the\ncoefficient A epsilon^2-2a epsilon t_A-L t_A^2 of kappa in\nJ_kappa(t_A) is nonnegative. For 0<=kappa<=A it follows that\n\n    V_kappa<=J_kappa(t_A)<=J_A(t_A)=V_A.\n\nThus the endpoint A attains the global maximum, and both transition\nequalities cause no loss. If epsilon_low>=epsilon_high, these two\noutside ranges cover every epsilon>=0 and no interior case exists.\n\nFor this scalar endpoint write x=A epsilon. Its exact value is\n\n    V_A=x^2,                   0<=x<=c,\n        2cx-c^2,               c<=x<=1,\n        (x-a)^2+2ac,           x>=1.                          (26)\n\nThis follows either from (23) or directly from the three possible\nclipped values of t_A. Formulas (24)-(26) are a complete constructive\nsolution for every finite b>=1, unequal M_0,M_1, and A>0.\n\nIf 0<=b<=1, the final point lies on the segment from y+q_kappa to y.\nEvery retained interpolant has F(y)=0 and gradient zero there, and has\ngradient q_kappa and value ||q_kappa||^2/2 at y+q_kappa. The unit\nLipschitz bound and the triangle inequality force gradient t q_kappa\nat y+t q_kappa for 0<=t<=1: the distances to gradients 0 and q_kappa\nare at most t||q_kappa|| and (1-t)||q_kappa||, whose sum is their\nmutual distance. Equality forces the collinear point. Integrating\ngives the exact final value\n\n    (1-b)^2 A kappa/M_1^2.\n\nIt is maximized by kappa=A. This handles zero final steps and b=1.\n\nFor the remaining degenerate first mass M_0=1, its sole nonzero\ninitial jet has gradient (1,0) and proximal point zero. Taking kappa=A\nmakes y=0 and reduces the maximal terminal function on the positive\naxis to x^2/2, because every negative-side retained conjugate affine\nterm is nonpositive there. It attains A^2(b-1)^2/M_1^2 for b>=1.\nFor any kappa the forced minimizer y and smoothness bound give the\nupper bound A kappa(b-1)^2/M_1^2, proving optimality. The b<=1\nsegment proof gives the same formula. Thus M_0=1 needs no division\nby a or omitted limiting argument.\n\n### Quantitative improvement on the same full-horizon family\n\nIn (7) take M_0=M_1=M, A=M^(1/12), epsilon=M^(-1/3). The strict\ninterior regime applies: epsilon<1<epsilon_high, and\n\n    epsilon_low <= (A+2)/(A^2 M) <= 2/(AM) < epsilon\n\nfor A>=4 and M>=4^12. Hence (24) is an explicit legal direction on\nEXACTLY THE SAME schedules, and its error is (25). Since\n(A+a)/(A+2a)>=5/6 for A>=4 and 0<a<=1,\n\n    V_opt >= (25/36) A^2 epsilon^2 >= (1/2) M^(-1/2).         (27)\n\nThis exceeds the earlier bound M^(-7/12) by at least A/2, which\ntends to infinity. All bounds (11)-(17) on the actual schedules stay\nunchanged. In particular (27) exceeds 1/B_n at every n>=24578,\nwhile the three baseline ratios still tend to zero. Against every\nscalar continuation in Section8 its gain is at least M^(1/3)/4.\n\nThe uncompleted envelope still has terminal value zero. Indeed, for\nthese parameters A epsilon=M^(-1/4)<=1/64 and a>=1/2. Also\n\n    A-kappa_opt = Aa/(A+2a)-a/[M epsilon(A+a)] >= a/2,\n\nusing A/(A+2a)>=2/3 and 1/[M epsilon(A+a)]<=M^(-3/4)<=1/6.\nThus epsilon kappa_opt<=A epsilon<=a/2<=A-kappa_opt. Both old\nplanes are nonpositive at the terminal point. This checks the claimed\ncompletion effect after optimizing the angle, rather than inferring\nit from the earlier fixed-angle example.\n\nFor an arbitrary supplied schedule, each choice of an earlier checkpoint\nb_0>1 and its final step determines M_0,M_1,A=(b_0-1)/M_0. Equations\n(24)-(26), the b<=1 formula, and the M_0=1 case therefore give a\nconcrete two-checkpoint lower bound whenever those blocks exhaust the\nschedule. Maximizing over the finitely many possible earlier checkpoints\nis another valid lower-bound functional; the formulas fully solve its\ncontinuous direction parameter. No comparison with B_n for every\nschedule is claimed. In particular, this zero-level second-block class\nhas value zero when the final step is exactly one. Positive endpoint\nlevels or a different prefix remain necessary for that part of the\nall-schedule problem.\n\n## 10. The unit-final-step obstruction survives positive levels and offsets\n\nThe zero value at b=1 in Section9 is not repaired at the benchmark scale\nmerely by allowing a positive second-block proximal level. A bound for\nthe ENTIRE constant-second-gradient class is available without choosing\nan envelope representation.\n\nLet an arbitrary convex 1-smooth f have minimum zero at the origin. Let\nthe start of its last ordinary block be X, let that block have sum M_1-1,\nand suppose its gradient is the same vector q at every actual sample in\nthat block, including its last query. Append a unit final step. Its last\nqueried position is x_E=X-(M_1-1)q. Interpolation with the optimum and\nthe unit-step descent inequality give, respectively,\n\n    f(x_E)<=<q,x_E>-||q||^2/2,\n    f(x_E-q)<=f(x_E)-||q||^2/2.\n\nConsequently\n\n    2f(x_E-q) <=2<q,X>-2M_1||q||^2\n       = ||X||^2/(2M_1)-2M_1||q-X/(2M_1)||^2\n       <= ||X||^2/(2M_1).                                   (28)\n\nThis holds in every dimension, with arbitrary positive values, arbitrary\naffine intercepts in any realizing envelope, and arbitrary behavior\naway from the retained samples. The constant-gradient hypothesis is\nthe only special restriction on the final ordinary block.\n\nFor the first-block geometry of Section9, X=(-A,0), and all retained\nfirst jets ensure the true closest initial distance is one. The single\ncut at the preceding checkpoint AM_0+1, on the same schedule ending in\na unit step, already gives exactly\n\n    K_cut=A^2/(2M_1+1).\n\nEquation (28) is only a factor 1+1/(2M_1) larger. Thus this whole class,\neven after freeing every second-block level, direction, intercept and\nthe maximal terminal completion, cannot beat that baseline by a growing\nfactor when the final step is one. On the modified schedules (7) with\nthe last step replaced by one, its bound is M^(-5/6)/2=o(1/B_n), using\nthe same exact benchmark upper estimate as in (15).\n\nThis closes only the proposed extension by positive levels INSIDE a\nconstant-gradient last block. It leaves varying gradients inside that\nblock, more chronological cells, and the unrestricted worst case open.\nIt identifies a precise missing mechanism for extending the new\nterminal gain to arbitrary schedules.\n\n## 11. A new universally valid terminal two-checkpoint functional\n\nThere is a simple schedule-level consequence, without any parameter\nfeasibility assumption. In Section9, for M_0>1, fix the direction\n\n    kappa_0=A(A+a)/(A+2a),\n\nindependently of the final step. For every epsilon>=0, (23) and ac>=0\ngive\n\n    V_(kappa_0)\n       >= min_(t in R) [A kappa_0 epsilon^2\n                        -2t a kappa_0 epsilon+t^2 D_0],\n    D_0=a(A+a).\n\nCompleting this scalar square yields\n\n    V_(kappa_0) >= kappa_0^2 epsilon^2.                       (29)\n\nSpecifically, A kappa_0-a^2 kappa_0^2/D_0=kappa_0^2. The relaxation\nto real t and omission of the nonnegative term go in the lower-bound\ndirection. M_0=1 has the same conclusion with kappa_0=A by its explicit\nscalar construction in Section9.\n\nNow let h be ANY finite nonnegative schedule of length n>=2. For an\nindex j<n-1 with h_j>1 set\n\n    P_j=sum_(t<j) h_t,\n    S_j=sum_(j<t<n-1) h_t,\n    gamma_j=(h_j-1+P_j)/(h_j-1+2P_j),\n    D_j^two=gamma_j^2 *\n       [(h_j-1)(h_(n-1)-1)_+/((1+P_j)(1+S_j))]^2.            (30)\n\nDefine D_n^two(h) as the maximum of these terms, or zero if there is\nno such index (also set it to zero for n<2). Every denominator for an\nincluded j is positive; no 0/0 convention is hidden.\n\nFor h_(n-1)>1 apply the actual two-block construction with\nM_0=1+P_j, M_1=1+S_j, A=(h_j-1)/M_0 and\nepsilon=(h_(n-1)-1)/M_1. All intervening ordinary entries are used\nexactly in their prescribed order. Formula (29), with the genuine\nnormalization one, becomes (30). If h_(n-1)<=1 the displayed term is\nzero and the bound holds directly. Thus\n\n    W_n(h) >= max(K_n(h),D_n^two(h))                           (31)\n\nfor every nonnegative schedule. The finite maximization over j is part\nof adversary selection; it changes no function during a trajectory.\nSince 1/2<=gamma_j<=1, a weaker bound replaces gamma_j^2 by 1/4.\nThis is a new chronological product term, not a putative equality for W.\n\nFor the schedules (7), select the large bridge between the two L_N\nblocks. Formula (30) gives\n\n    D_n^two(h^[n]) >= (25/36) M^(-1/2),                       (32)\n\nusing A>=4 and gamma_j=(A+a)/(A+2a)>=5/6. Thus the improvement over\nthe previously available K, quadratic, and universal-lens channels is\nalready visible in an explicit universally valid scalar functional of\nthe schedule. No optimization oracle is needed to evaluate (30).\n\n## 12. A counterexample to uniform comparison by the enlarged functional\n\nThe new functional (31) STILL does not solve the target. This can be\ntested constructively without making any claim about small true W.\n\nKeep N,M,A from (7). Let L_N^up be the three increasing copies of\nChi_N and L_N^down the three decreasing copies. Define\n\n    h_bad^(N)=(L_N^up, AM+1, L_N^down, 1).                    (33)\n\nIts length is still 6N+2; the same beginning-zero padding covers every\nlarge integer horizon. All of its D_j^two terms are zero because its\nlast step is one. Quadratic polynomials are independent of the order\ninside each ordinary block. The bound (10) still applies, with the\nsmaller final factor 1+lambda instead of 1+b lambda; the same is true\nof (13)-(14). Thus the quadratic and universal-lens channels are again\nO(M^(-5/6)).\n\nHere every single-cut term is small as well. The first increasing block\nhas the same bound 81/(2AM) as before. At the bridge the exact term is\nA^2/(2M+1). For a cut in the DECREASING block, denote by T_j its full\ntail mass. Within a decreasing copy, each entry is at most four times\nits successor, by the adjacent-ratio proof in Section5. A last entry\nin a copy is <=4/3; it is either followed by another copy or by the\nunit final step. Hence every entry in the second block obeys\n\n    h_j <=4(1+T_j),       h_j<=M.\n\nThe full prefix mass there is at least (A+1)M. Its single-cut term is\ntherefore at most\n\n    h_j^2/[A^2 M^2(1+2T_j)] <=4/(A^2 M).\n\nThe final unit-step cut contributes zero, and the global Huber term\nis <=1/M. Consequently K_n(h_bad^[n])<=41M^(-5/6), and the same\nexact-B_n estimate (15) proves\n\n    max(K_n(h_bad^[n]),D_n^two(h_bad^[n]),Q_n(h_bad^[n]),\n        sup_(z in Lens_n) Gap_(h_bad^[n])(z)) / U_n^ZJ ->0.    (34)\n\nThis is an explicit refutation of the proposed UNIFORM comparison by\nthat combined lower functional. It is not a counterexample to the\nunrestricted target and not an upper bound on W_n(h_bad^[n]). The\nunit-tail cap (28) additionally shows that allowing arbitrary positive\nlevels in one constant-gradient second block cannot repair this\nfailure. A repair must use internal gradient changes, more cells, or a\ndifferent source of retained terminal value.\n\n## Scope and remaining task\n\nThis joint proof supplies an exact arbitrary-block terminal policy, a\nfour-anchor active-set formula, all old/new comparisons, the actual\nboundary gradient, true distance normalization, and a quantitative\nfamily at every large horizon that defeats several baseline channels.\nIt does not assert that every schedule has an advantageous two-block\npartition, that the completed function remains adverse after an\narbitrary further suffix, or that a greedy intermediate completion is\nglobally optimal. The all-schedule exact-profile comparison remains open.\n\nTargeted Matlas searches returned no external theorem used in the proof.\nThe exact accepted predecessors above were read. A search hit for an\nolder lower-bound paper returned HTTP 503 and was not relied on. All\nnew results remain candidates because the current assignment allocates\nno additional verifier requests.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "dfde2fa326c908bf", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "artifact": "research/max_terminal_completion_profile_gap.md", "section": "11, with the complete proving construction included", "prior_findings": ["e2dad1cf3b95b8d7", "bc4ce8b7c164f3b4"], "status": "unverified joint proof candidate"}, "glossary": {"D_n^two": "Maximum of the explicitly defined terminal two-checkpoint terms in equation(30), zero when no earlier checkpoint exceeds1 or n<2.", "D_j^two": "The j-th term gamma_j^2[(h_j-1)(h_(n-1)-1)_+/((1+P_j)(1+S_j))]^2 for h_j>1.", "P_j": "The sum of schedule entries strictly before index j.", "S_j": "The sum of schedule entries strictly between j and the last index n-1.", "gamma_j": "(h_j-1+P_j)/(h_j-1+2P_j), always in[1/2,1] for an included checkpoint.", "kappa_0": "The fixed second-gradient geometry A(A+a)/(A+2a) used in equation(29).", "D_0": "The scalar squared anchor displacement a(A+a) for kappa_0 in Section11.", "K_n": "The verified global-Huber/single-cut functional from fact b98bb807d514e80d."}}
