{"id": "0e5db04bc615f528", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T15:14:32.212145+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C26 complete candidate removes core resonance from the arbitrary nonperiodic/asymmetric subpower class: every mu_core in(0,1], p_grow in[1/2,2/3], and ray ratio kappa_ray<=13/[8(p_grow/(1-p_grow))^p_grow] has full objective <=(c_sc/a_sc)c_sc^(-m) on Hsc_m, uniformly o(U_(Nsc_m)^ZJ). The new attraction recurrence treats all outside initial points and partial core entries; no actual iterate is presumed permanently captured at first entry.", "evidence": "# Nonresonant cores: a uniform attraction envelope\n\nStatus: complete candidate, NOT verifier-accepted. This was completed before\nreading the new projection assignment at 15:06 UTC. It is preserved rather\nthan pursued further. No new verification is allocated. All new ingredients\nare proved here; C23--C25 are not premises.\n\n## Statement and exact class\n\nSet c_sc=13/5, a_sc=c_sc^(-17), bsc_j=a_sc c_sc^j,\nHsc_0=(), Hsc_(j+1)=(Hsc_j,bsc_j,Hsc_j), and Nsc_m=2^m-1.\nLet ssc_j=sum Hsc_j=a_sc(c_sc^j-2^j)/(c_sc-2).\n\nChoose p_grow in[1/2,2/3], beta_grow=1-p_grow,\nK_grow=(p_grow/beta_grow)^(p_grow), and\n1<=kappa_ray<=13/(8 K_grow). Choose ANY mu_core in(0,1] and any\nR_core>0. No reciprocal-checkpoint or other resonance condition is imposed.\nFor each sign sigma choose a locally absolutely continuous radial function\nG_sigma such that\n\n    G_sigma(R)=R                         for 0<=R<=1,\n    0<=R G_sigma'(R)<=p_grow G_sigma(R)   almost everywhere for R>1,\n    1/kappa_ray<=G_+(R)/G_-(R)<=kappa_ray for R>=1.\n\nDefine one fixed function by\n\n    g(sigma R_core R)=sigma mu_core R_core G_sigma(R),\n    f(x)=integral_0^x g(t)dt.\n\nFor every horizon m and every nonzero initial point, the actual GD run on\nHsc_m satisfies\n\n    2f(x_(Nsc_m))/x_0^2 <= (c_sc/a_sc)c_sc^(-m).       (1)\n\nThe bound is uniform over all of these parameters and functions, which may\ndepend on m. Arbitrarily many nonperiodic knots and unbounded radial secant\nvariation are allowed. The class is NOT all scalar smooth convex functions.\n\nThe new ingredient is an attraction inequality for a growing interval. In\nnormalized coordinates write b_j=mu_core bsc_j, S_j=mu_core ssc_j,\neta=13/8, rho_core=1/8, k_env=1/8, and A_env=13/40. Let I be the first\nlevel with t=b_I>1 and put J=I+1. For j>=J and 0<=q<=rho_core, every\ninitial point of radius at most q b_j obeys\n\n    |T_j(x)| <= min(A_env q, k_env rho_core),          (2)\n\nwhere T_j is the actual normalized GD map for the whole word Hsc_j.\nThus this whole interval ends inside the quadratic core. This statement\nhandles partial-block entry and subsequent exit; it does not presume that\nfirst entry alone is permanent capture.\n\n## Legality and complete areas\n\nIntegrating the elasticity bound gives 1<=G_sigma(R)<=R^(p_grow)<=R\nfor R>=1 and 0<=G_sigma'(R)<=p_grow R^(p_grow-1)<=1 there.\nTogether with the core formula, each ray is monotone and 1-Lipschitz.\nAcross zero, G_+(R)+G_-(T)<=R+T proves the same Lipschitz property.\nConsequently g is monotone and mu_core-Lipschitz, and f is convex,\ndifferentiable and 1-smooth with its unique minimizer at zero.\n\nThe complete normalized function values are\n\n    F(sigma R)=R^2/2                         for R<=1,\n    F(sigma R)=1/2+integral_1^R G_sigma(u)du  for R>=1,\n    f(x)=mu_core R_core^2 F(x/R_core).\n\nNo plateau or previously traversed area is omitted.\n\n## An absolute clock bound valid even at small crossing steps\n\nIn this section abbreviate p=p_grow, beta=beta_grow, K=K_grow.\nFor one ray define Clock(R)=integral_1^R du/G(u). It equals log R\non the core. Set q_R=R/G(R). Outside the core,\n\n    q_R'>=beta/G(R),   Clock(R)<=(q_R-1)/beta.          (3)\n\nFor EVERY h>1 and every overshoot z=hG(r)-r>0, claim\n\n    Clock(z)<=K h-1/beta.                             (4)\n\nWhen z<=1, use the global bound G(r)<=r^p, valid also for r<=1.\nMaximizing h r^p-r over r>0 gives\n\n    z <= beta p^(p/beta) h^(1/beta)\n       = (beta K h)^(1/beta).\n\nThus log z <= log(beta K h)/beta <= K h-1/beta, proving (4).\nThis logarithmic case removes the earlier restriction h>=2.\n\nIf z>=1 and r<=1, then h>=2 and Clock(z)<=z-1<=h-2.\nThe inequality h-2<=K h-1/beta follows from\nK>=1/(2beta), which is equivalent to\np^p beta^beta>=1/2. This last expression equals 1/2 at p=1/2,\nand its logarithm has derivative log(p/beta)>=0 thereafter.\nIf r,z>=1 and z>=r, monotonicity and (3) give\n\n    Clock(z)<=h+(1/beta-2)q_R-1/beta\n             <=h/(2beta)-1/beta<=K h-1/beta,\n\nusing q_R<=h/2. If 1<=z<=r, the growth assumption gives\nG(z)>=G(r)(z/r)^p, and therefore\n\n    z/G(z)<=q_R^p(h-q_R)^beta<=p^p beta^beta h.\n\nThe maximum occurs at q_R=p h, as differentiating the logarithm verifies.\nEquation (3) again proves (4).\n\nWrite A=K h-1/beta. On a crossing to the other ray, if A>=0 then\nthe target clock is at most kappa_ray A: for z>=1 this follows by\nintegrating the ray comparison, and for z<1 the target clock is negative.\nIf A<0, (4) forces z<1, where BOTH ray clocks equal log z, so the\ntarget clock is at most A, with no multiplicative loss. Finally every\nnoncrossing nonzero step decreases its own clock by at least h, because\nthe integral of 1/G over its inward displacement is at least h.\n\n## Every crossing word ends in a fixed small core interval\n\nFor every j>=17,\n\n    S_j/b_j=[1-(2/c_sc)^j]/(c_sc-2)>=eta.              (5)\n\nFor the elementary numerical bound, (2/c_sc)^3<1/2 and\n(2/c_sc)^2<4/5 imply (2/c_sc)^17<1/40.\nAny strict crossing needs h>1, hence has level at least18, since\nmu_core<=1 and bsc_17=1.\n\nChoose the last strict crossing of a run on any Hsc_m, and call its\nlevel j and size h=b_j. Every occurrence of level j is immediately\nfollowed by a complete Hsc_j. There are no subsequent strict crossings.\nIf the path reaches zero it stays there and the conclusion holds.\nOtherwise all later steps are on the same ray and decrease its clock.\nUsing at least the following Hsc_j, the final clock is at most\n\n    (kappa_ray K-eta)h-kappa_ray/beta  if A>=0,\n    (K-eta)h-1/beta                   if A<0.          (6)\n\nFor A>=0 the first expression increases with kappa_ray, so its maximum\nat kappa_ray=eta/K is -eta/(beta K). The function\nbeta K=p^p beta^beta increases on[1/2,2/3] and is at most\n4^(1/3)/3<8/15. Thus this maximum is less than -195/64<-21/8.\nFor A<0, K<=eta and K=(p/beta)^p<=p/beta imply\n\n    (K-eta)h-1/beta <= K-eta-1/beta <=-eta-1=-21/8.\n\nSince exp(21/8)>1+21/8+(21/8)^2/2+(21/8)^3/6>8, every\ncrossing word ends with radius less than rho_core=1/8.              (7)\nThis conclusion holds for arbitrary input radii and all admitted\nnonresonant curvatures. It says small core capture, not annihilation.\n\nFor later use, on either ray Clock(R)<=R-1 for ALL R>0: use\nG>=1 beyond1 and log R<=R-1 below1. A word with no strict crossing\nand no zero iterate consequently has\n\n    Clock(R_end)<=R_init-1-S_j.                       (8)\n\n## Exact attraction recurrence\n\nLet I be the first level with t=b_I>1. Then I>=18 and 1<t<=c_sc.\nAll steps of Hsc_I are at most1. Its quadratic-core multiplier P\nis nonnegative and satisfies P<=exp(-S_I)<=exp(-eta t).\nEvery core-started prefix of Hsc_I remains in the core. At the central\nstep of Hsc_(I+1), the largest possible radius factor is\n(t-1)P<=(c_sc-1)exp(-eta)<1; the latter follows from\nexp(eta)>1+eta>c_sc-1. The following Hsc_I again stays in the core.\nIts complete absolute multiplier is Q=P^2(t-1).\n\nWith J=I+1 and b_J=c_sc t,\n\n    b_J Q <= c_sc t(t-1)exp(-2eta t)\n           <=3c_sc/(16eta^3)=1248/10985<1/8=k_env.   (9)\n\nIndeed exp(2eta t)>(2eta t)^3/6 and (t-1)/t^2<=1/4.\nSince rho_core b_J<=c_sc^2/8=169/200<1, every input of radius\nq b_J, q<=rho_core, stays in the core throughout this base word.\nIts endpoint is at most k_env q and hence satisfies (2).\n\nInductively assume (2) at level j-1, where j>J. Take an input\nradius at most q b_j with q<=rho_core, and call r_1 its radius after\nthe first Hsc_(j-1). The exact word has the form\nHsc_(j-1), b_(j-1), Hsc_(j-1).\n\nIf c_sc q<=rho_core, the induction hypothesis on the first block gives\n\n    r_1<=min(A_env c_sc q,k_env rho_core)<=q,\n\nbecause A_env c_sc=169/200<1. Thus r_1<=rho_core and the middle\nstep uses the quadratic core. Its output radius is\n(b_(j-1)-1)r_1<=b_(j-1)r_1; here b_(j-1)>2. Applying the induction\nhypothesis to the second block with parameter r_1 gives both\n\n    r_end<=A_env r_1<=A_env^2 c_sc q<=A_env q,\n    r_end<=k_env rho_core.\n\nIf c_sc q>rho_core, the first block still has r_1<=rho_core.\nWhen that block crosses this is (7). When it never crosses, (8) gives\n\n    Clock(r_1)<=rho_core b_j-1-S_(j-1)\n       <=-(eta/c_sc-rho_core)b_j-1=-b_j/2-1<-3,\n\nbecause b_j>=c_sc^2 t>4. Since exp(3)>8, this also gives\nr_1<rho_core. A zero output is harmless. The middle step again has\nradius at most b_(j-1)rho_core. The second induction application,\nnow with parameter rho_core, gives r_end<=k_env rho_core.\nSince q>rho_core/c_sc and A_env=c_sc k_env, this is at most A_env q.\nBoth parts of (2) follow. This completes the induction.\n\nThe recurrence is an inequality on actual fixed-function GD maps; it\ndoes not choose a different derivative at different times. In the\nasymmetric case it needs only the radius bounds for both initial signs,\nnot an assertion that the reachable image is a centered interval.\n\n## Uniform full-objective bound\n\nFor any path with no crossing, write u for its terminal gradient\nmagnitude in normalized coordinates and r for its terminal radius.\nMonotonicity yields R_init>=r+S_m u. The full terminal area satisfies\nF_end<=u r-u^2/2 because G(t)<=min(t,u) for 0<=t<=r. Completing\nthe square therefore gives\n\n    2F_end/R_init^2<=1/(1+2S_m).                      (10)\n\nSuppose m>=J and the path crosses. If R_init<=rho_core b_m,\napply (2) with q=R_init/b_m. Its endpoint is in the core and\n\n    2F_end/R_init^2<=A_env^2/b_m^2<=1/b_m^2.\n\nIf R_init>rho_core b_m, equation (7) instead gives\n2F_end/R_init^2<=rho_core^2/R_init^2<1/b_m^2.\nAs b_m>c_sc and S_m>=eta b_m, both this bound and (10), after\nmultiplication by mu_core, are at most\n\n    1/(c_sc bsc_m)                                   (11)\n\nfor m>=J; here 1/(2eta)<=1/c_sc.\nFor m<=I all steps are at most1 in normalized coordinates, so there\nis no crossing. The bound (10) is at most1 and\nmu_core<=c_sc/bsc_m, since b_m<=b_I=t<=c_sc.\nTogether these cases prove (1), including the empty word.\n\n## Exact benchmark comparison and scope\n\nUse the benchmark recursions in accepted fact b98bb807d514e80d:\nC_n=S_circle(n)+1, B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1, C_0=B_0=1,\n\n    C_n=max_j Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_j Gamma(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),\n    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n    Gamma(A,B)=2A+B/2+sqrt(B^2+8AB)/2.\n\nFor completeness, Omega(A,B)<=(A^(3/4)+B^(3/4))^(4/3).\nTo prove it, put t=A^(3/4)/(A^(3/4)+B^(3/4)); the positive-root\nformula reduces it to\nt^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1.\nWith u=t^(1/3), v=(1-t)^(1/3), the difference equals\nu^3 v^3[1/(1+u+u^2)+1/(1+v+v^2)-uv]>=0:\nboth reciprocals are at least1/3 and uv<=2^(-2/3)<2/3.\nInduction gives C_n<=(n+1)^(4/3). Since Gamma(A,B)<=B+4A,\nanother induction gives B_n<=1+4n^(4/3). Consequently (1) divided\nby U_(Nsc_m)^ZJ is at most\n\n    (c_sc/a_sc)c_sc^(-m)[1+4(2^m-1)^(4/3)] ->0,\n\nbecause c_sc^3>16. No external theorem is used.\n\nThis removes the resonance assumption from the earlier nonperiodic\nsubpower class and handles all outside initial points using (2).\nThe common quadratic neighborhood, outer growth restriction and\nbounded ray ratio remain load-bearing. No all-scalar or unrestricted\nmultidimensional upper bound, and no new accepted fact, is asserted.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "workers/max3/research/c26_nonresonant_attraction_envelope.md", "fact_ids": ["b98bb807d514e80d"], "finding_ids": ["1560c4b6a302dc15", "b647bce3e53eb9bb"], "status": "Complete self-contained unverified candidate; no fact_submit allocated. Saved before switching to new projection task.", "scope": "Common quadratic core of arbitrary curvature; nonperiodic outer elasticity<=2/3 and stated bounded asymmetry. Not all scalar, not unrestricted W.", "is_fragile": false, "fragility_reason": "", "suggested_followup": "none", "justification_type": "calculation", "confidence": 0.96}, "glossary": {"rho_core": "The fixed normalized capture radius 1/8.", "k_env": "The base envelope coefficient 1/8.", "A_env": "The linear attraction-envelope coefficient 13/40.", "T_j": "Actual normalized GD map of the complete word Hsc_j on the one fixed derivative.", "mu_core": "Arbitrary common core curvature in(0,1], without any resonance assumption.", "R_core": "Physical radius of the common quadratic neighborhood.", "p_grow": "Upper radial elasticity, in[1/2,2/3].", "beta_grow": "1-p_grow.", "K_grow": "(p_grow/beta_grow)^(p_grow).", "kappa_ray": "Pointwise comparison factor between the positive and negative radial derivatives.", "G_sigma": "Fixed normalized radial gradient on ray sigma, equal to radius up to1.", "c_sc": "13/5.", "a_sc": "c_sc^(-17).", "bsc_j": "Physical checkpoint a_sc c_sc^j.", "Hsc_m": "Ruler word Hsc_0=(), Hsc_(j+1)=(Hsc_j,bsc_j,Hsc_j).", "Nsc_m": "2^m-1, its length.", "ssc_m": "Its total physical step sum a_sc(c_sc^m-2^m)/(c_sc-2)."}}
{"id": "f3d96c901566e96b", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T15:19:22.831914+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Constructive short-tail re-selection candidate: for any nonnegative tail with at most two positive steps, even arbitrary signed incoming-terminal multipliers cannot enlarge the intrinsic primitive certificate class. Any PSD signed identity with other multipliers nonnegative can be replaced by an ordinary nonnegative certificate, including a valid lift to the original zero-step formal space. Thus genuine benefit from E_theta needs at least three positive tail steps.", "evidence": "# Saturated-cut certificates retain a signed terminal-interpolation credit\n\nStatus: self-contained candidate, NOT verifier-accepted. No solver or\nfact_submit was used. The full initial-distance objective theorem is not\nbeing restricted here: the statement concerns the explicitly specified\nintrinsic primitive energy. The only accepted background used is the\nnecessity of the interpolation inequalities in fact 1252b8823bb5a290.\n\n## 1. Definitions and an exact identity\n\nWork at smoothness one. On a GD trajectory for a list h of sum s and\ncapacity C=1+s, write g_i=grad f(x_i), f_i=f(x_i), and\n\n  Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <= 0,\n  V_h=2 sum_(i<n) h_i(f_i-f_n)\n          +sum_(i<n) h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sC||g_n||^2.\n\nTake two lists a,b of capacities A,D, respectively, and set\n\n  T=A+D+r,  r>0,  rT=AD,\n  kappa=(T/D)^2=(A/r)^2,\n  theta=1-1/kappa.\n\nIn particular 0<r<min(A,D), kappa>1 and 0<theta<1. The joined\nschedule is h=(a,1+r,b). Denote its bridge query by L and the tail\nqueries by j=0,...,m; z=m is the final query. Tail indices in Q always\nmean the corresponding original joined indices. Define\n\n  J_b=sum_(j<m) b_j Q_(j,z),\n  E_theta(b)=V_b-theta J_b,\n  C_cross=sum_(j<m) b_j Q_(j,L)+Q_(z,L)+r Q_(L,z).\n\nThe exact identity, including every value term, is\n\n  V_h = V_a + kappa E_theta(b) - (kappa-1) C_cross.       (1)\n\nAll coefficients in -C_cross are nonnegative on actual inequalities\n-Q. Thus universal nonnegativity of V_a and E_theta(b) is a sufficient\ncondition for universal nonnegativity of V_h. It is not necessary here\nto assume V_b>=0. Whether this genuinely enlarges the allowable tail\nclass is a separate question.\n\nHere is a direct audit of (1). Put p=g_L, q=g_z, and\nH=sum_(j<m)b_j g_j. Then x_tail,0=x_L-(1+r)p and x_z=x_tail,0-H.\nFor C=C_cross+J_b the value part is\n\n  C_f=2sum_(j<m)b_j f_j+(r-D)f_L+(2-D-r)f_z.\n\nExpand each Q and use\n2sum_j b_j<g_j,sum_(i<j)b_i g_i>=||H||^2-sum_j b_j^2||g_j||^2.\nThe gradient part reduces exactly to\n\n  C_g=sum_(j<m)b_j(1-b_j)||g_j||^2\n            +[(D-r)/2-r^2]||p||^2+(D+r)||q||^2/2.\n\nAll mixed p,H,q products cancel. The identities\n\n  (kappa-1)(D-r)=2(A+r),\n  kappa r^2=A^2,  kappa D^2=T^2\n\nthen show V_h=V_a+kappa V_b-(kappa-1)C coefficient by coefficient:\nthe f_L coefficient is 2(1+r), the f_z coefficient is -2(T-1),\nthe p-square coefficient is -r(1+r), and the q-square coefficient\nis -T(T-1). Substituting kappa theta=kappa-1 proves (1).\n\n## 2. Full intrinsic certificates force the bridge coefficients\n\nFor this part assume the entries of a and b are strictly positive;\neither list may be empty. Suppose an explicit intrinsic certificate is\ngiven:\n\n  M_h=V_h+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij,\n  lambda_ij>=0,\n\nwhere values cancel and M_h is PSD in the full independent formal\ngradient vector. Allowing the optimum index and x_0 initially does not\nenlarge this class. Indeed V_h has no x_0-square coefficient, so PSD\nforces the x_0 row to vanish. Its cross coefficients are -lambda_i,star,\nwhich forces every lambda_i,star=0. Summing the value-flow equalities\nthen gives sum_i lambda_star,i=0, so these multipliers also vanish.\nThis argument relies on the absence of an initial-distance term and\ndoes NOT apply to a full objective certificate.\n\nThere are two explicit equality trajectories for V_h. A scalar Huber\ntrajectory staying on one affine ray has every gradient equal to 1,\nall finite Q zero, and V_h=0. A second trajectory uses the derivative\n\n  f'(x)=min(1,max(x,-eta)),  eta=r/D,\n\nstarting at A. The prefix gradient is 1, its last query is 1, the\nbridge lands at -r, and the tail gradient is -eta until its endpoint\n-eta. Every incoming optimum residual is zero. Distance telescoping\ngives V_h=A^2-T^2 eta^2=0.\n\nFor completeness, if R_i is a prefix query's remaining prefix sum\nand R_j is a tail query's remaining tail sum, this second trajectory\nhas\n\n  Q_(prefix i,tail j)=-(1+eta)eta R_j,\n  Q_(tail j,prefix i)=-(1+eta)R_i.                       (2)\n\nEvery within-block Q is zero. Since M_h>=0 and all lambda Q<=0,\nequality of V_h forces multiplier complementarity and M_h to vanish\non each equality gradient vector. Those vectors span the two block\nindicators e_A,e_B, so both indicators lie in the kernel of M_h.\n\nBy (2), the only potentially nonzero cross multipliers are\n\n  nu_i=lambda_(prefix i,z),  mu_j=lambda_(tail j,L).\n\nFor a prefix index i<L, the corresponding row of M_h e_B is\n-D nu_i/2. Thus nu_i=0 for every i<L. Write nu=nu_L.\nFor a nonterminal tail index j, the corresponding row of M_h e_A\nis [r mu_j-nu b_j]/2; at z it is [r mu_z-nu]/2.\nConsequently\n\n  mu_j=(nu/r)b_j  (j<m),  mu_z=nu/r.\n\nThe sum of the prefix value-flow equations is\nsum_j mu_j-nu=2(A+r). Therefore all cross multipliers are fixed:\n\n  lambda_(j,L)=(kappa-1)b_j (j<m),\n  lambda_(z,L)=kappa-1,\n  lambda_(L,z)=r(kappa-1),                              (3)\n\nand every other cross multiplier is zero. The mixed gradient block\nof the full matrix vanishes, not merely its value on equality data.\n\n## 3. Exact cone interface and the sign that cannot be discarded\n\nLet lambda^A,lambda^B be the two within-block multiplier arrays of\nthe given full certificate. Define a signed tail array by\n\n  lambda_hat_ij=lambda^B_ij/kappa\n                 -theta b_i * 1_(i<m,j=z).             (4)\n\nCombining (1) with (3) yields the full block-diagonal identity\n\n  M_h = M_a + kappa M_b,\n  M_a=V_a+sum lambda^A_ij Q_ij,\n  M_b=V_b+sum lambda_hat_ij Q_ij.                       (5)\n\nEach summand has its own value cancellation, since its values are in\na disjoint block. Full PSD is equivalent to PSD of both summands.\nThe prefix multipliers are nonnegative. The exact tail constraints are\n\n  lambda_hat_ij>=0 except possibly i<m,j=z,\n  lambda_hat_(i,z)>=-theta b_i.                        (6)\n\nConversely, any prefix intrinsic certificate and any PSD tail identity\nwith the value cancellation and bounds (6) give a nonnegative full\ncertificate by (3),(4). Equivalently, the tail has an ordinary\nnonnegative certificate for E_theta(b), not necessarily for V_b.\n\nThus direct restriction to an ordinary primitive tail certificate would\nrequire the stronger bounds\n\n  lambda^B_(i,z)>=(kappa-1)b_i.                        (7)\n\nThey are not implied for each full certificate. The next exact example\nproves this, including full nonnegativity and PSD.\n\n## 4. Exact full certificate violating the proposed coefficient floor\n\nUse the empty prefix A=1, tail b=(1,1) of capacity D=3, and\n\n  r=sqrt(7)-2, T=sqrt(7)+2, kappa=(11+4sqrt(7))/9.\n\nThe full schedule is h=(sqrt(7)-1,1,1). Use original indices 0,1,2,3,\nwhere 0 is the bridge query and 1,2,3 are the tail queries. Set\n\n  lambda_10=lambda_20=lambda_30=kappa-1,\n  lambda_03=r(kappa-1),\n  lambda_12=5kappa/2, lambda_21=17kappa/4,\n  lambda_23=37kappa/4-1, lambda_32=12kappa,\n  lambda_13=3kappa/4-1,\n\nwith every unlisted multiplier zero. All are nonnegative, since\nkappa>4/3. Direct substitution, or (1) and the audited tail identity\nbelow, gives\n\n  V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij\n   =kappa { ||(g_1-g_2)-(g_2-g_3)/8||^2\n                         +(255/64)||g_2-g_3||^2 } >=0. (8)\n\nFor the tail alone, write u=g_0-g_1 and v=g_1-g_2 using local indices.\nIts signed multiplier list is\n\n  lambda_hat_01=5/2, lambda_hat_10=17/4,\n  lambda_hat_12=33/4, lambda_hat_21=12,\n  lambda_hat_02=-1/4, lambda_hat_20=0.\n\nThe value coefficients cancel, and expansion gives\n\n  V_(1,1)+sum lambda_hat_ij Q_ij\n      =||u||^2-<u,v>/4+4||v||^2\n      =||u-v/8||^2+(255/64)||v||^2.\n\nNevertheless lambda_13=(kappa-1)-kappa/4 is strictly below the\nproposed floor kappa-1. The restricted terminal coefficient is -1/4.\nThis refutes the inference of (7) from full nonnegativity and PSD.\n\nThere IS another ordinary primitive certificate for this tail:\nlambda_01=2,lambda_10=4,lambda_12=8,lambda_21=12, all other\nmultipliers zero, gives ||u||^2+4||v||^2. Accordingly this example\ndoes not disprove existence of a suitable re-selection, arbitrary-tail\nheredity, or the original asymptotic minimax problem. It identifies\nthe exact missing sign assertion rather than replacing it with PSD.\n\n## 5. A particular nonprimitive tail cannot exploit this credit\n\nThe previously considered b=(17/10,2,6/5) fails even E_theta for\nevery 0<=theta<=1. Here is a self-contained witness.\nPut m=7/34, ell=21/85. Define the continuous derivative\n\n  f'(x)=x                  for x<=m,\n         m                 for m<=x<=m+ell,\n         x-ell             for x>=m+ell,\n\nand integrate with f(0)=0. Its derivative is nondecreasing and\n1-Lipschitz, so f is convex differentiable 1-smooth with minimizer 0.\nStarting at x_0=106/85 gives\n\n  (x_0,x_1,x_2,x_3)=(106/85,-77/170,77/170,7/34),\n  (g_0,g_1,g_2,g_3)=(1,-77/170,7/34,7/34).\n\nThe endpoint is core: x_3=g_3=m, f_3=m^2/2. Define\nI_i=<g_i,x_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2. Direct scalar evaluation gives\n\n  I_0=ell(1-m)=567/2890, I_1=I_2=I_3=0,\n  sum_(i<3)b_i I_i=567/1700,\n  V_b=-13581/23120.\n\nAt a core endpoint, expansion gives Q_(i,3)=-I_i. Thus\n\n  E_theta(b)=-13581/23120+theta*567/1700\n           <= -29349/115600 <0.                       (9)\n\nThe value of V_b can also be checked without integrating every term:\ndistance telescoping gives\nV_b=x_0^2-D^2g_3^2-2sum b_i I_i, with D=59/10.\nThis proves (9) exactly.\n\nConsequently this specific tail cannot acquire a valid E_theta\ncertificate at any saturated join parameter. A rank-one coefficient\nmatching attempt on this same fixed tail also failed, but (9) is the\nstronger actual-function obstruction. Neither statement excludes other\nnonprimitive tails. No numerical schedule screen was reopened.\n\n## 6. Complete two-bridge identity with retained earlier anchors\n\nThe identity also provides a closed, explicitly signed value state\nthrough two bridges. Let a_1,a_2 be any nonnegative physical prefix\nlists of capacities A_1,A_2. Starting with the physical tail b of\ncapacity D_0, define\n\n  H_1=(a_1,1+r_1,b), D_1=A_1+D_0+r_1, r_1 D_1=A_1 D_0,\n  H_2=(a_2,1+r_2,H_1), D_2=A_2+D_1+r_2, r_2 D_2=A_2 D_1,\n  kappa_1=(D_1/D_0)^2, kappa_2=(D_2/D_1)^2,\n  K=kappa_1 kappa_2=(D_2/D_0)^2, theta_2=1-1/K.\n\nLet L_1,L_2 be the respective bridge query indices in the final\noriginal indexing and z the final query. For each physical step in\na_1 or a_2, its gradient/value index is also its original index in H_2.\nDefine the following sums of actual inequalities, without collapsing\nany old query or replacing it by a new anchor:\n\n  C_1=sum_(j in b steps)b_j Q_(j,L_1)+Q_(z,L_1)+r_1 Q_(L_1,z),\n  C_2=sum_(i in H_1 steps)(H_1)_i Q_(i,L_2)\n                                      +Q_(z,L_2)+r_2 Q_(L_2,z),\n  J_left1=sum_(i in a_1 steps)(a_1)_i Q_(i,z)\n                                      +(1+r_1)Q_(L_1,z).\n\nThen the complete identity is\n\n  V_(H_2)=V_(a_2)+kappa_2 V_(a_1)+K E_theta2(b)\n       -kappa_2(kappa_1-1)C_1-(kappa_2-1)C_2\n       -(kappa_2-1)J_left1.                            (10)\n\nEvery coefficient displayed on -Q is nonnegative. In particular the\nearlier a_1 queries still have their actual outgoing edges to z, and\nevery query inside H_1 has its actual edge to L_2. These are not removed\nby the unqueried-terminal theorem.\n\nTo verify (10) algebraically, apply the audited expansion in Section 1\nat each cut. The weighted incoming-terminal sum for H_1 is exactly\nJ_b+J_left1. Substitution gives a J_b coefficient\nkappa_2(kappa_1-1)+(kappa_2-1)=K-1=K theta_2; all other terms are\nthose displayed in (10). This derivation is part of this same candidate\nproof, not an invocation of an unverified finding as a theorem.\n\nConsequently, if explicit nonnegative intrinsic certificates are given\nfor a_1 and a_2, and an explicit nonnegative certificate is given for\nE_theta2(b), (10) assembles an intrinsic certificate for H_2. Its PSD\nremainder is the sum of the three input PSD remainders with scales\n1,kappa_2,K. All original actual multipliers are sums of nonnegative\nnumbers. No positive remainder is presumed for a benchmark block.\n\n## 7. Final objective certificate, including initial distance\n\nHere is the exact final conversion for any intrinsic certificate\nproduced by (10), and in particular for the example below. Write H=H_2,\nlet its terminal query be n, its capacity be D=1+sum_(i<n)H_i, and let\n\n  M_H=V_H+sum_(i!=j<=n)lambda_ij Q_ij >=0\n\nbe the assembled PSD identity, with all lambda_ij>=0. Define\n\n  t=(-1+sqrt(8D+1))/4, beta=1+t,\n  gamma=1/(2t+1), q=gamma^2,\n  x_(n+1)=x_n-beta g_n.\n\nThe arithmetic identities are t=gamma D, 1-gamma=2qD, and\nq=1/[1+2(sum H_i+beta)]. The accepted terminal-square parameterization\nin fact 1252b8823bb5a290(c) applies with terminal probabilities\nalpha_star=gamma, alpha_n=1-gamma, and all other alpha_i=0.\nGive the queried prefix the following additional optimum multipliers:\n\n  lambda_pre_(i,star)=2q H_i  (i<n),\n  lambda_pre_(n,star)=2q(D+1),\n\nand multiply every intrinsic lambda_ij by q. All these coefficients\nare nonnegative and their flows are exactly the required 2alpha_i.\nThere are no optimum-outgoing multipliers in this particular\nconstructed certificate; none is dropped from an arbitrary certificate.\nThe full identity supplied by the accepted parameterization is\n\n  q||x_0||^2-2f_(n+1)\n    +q sum_(i!=j<=n)lambda_ij Q_ij\n    +2q sum_(i<n)H_i Q_(i,star)+2q(D+1)Q_(n,star)\n    +2(1-gamma)Q_(n+1,n)+2gamma Q_(n+1,star)\n  =||g_(n+1)-[gamma x_n-(t+gamma)g_n]||^2+q M_H.       (11)\n\nThe optimum is translated to x_star=0,f_star=0 in this display.\nIt follows that W_(n+1)(H,beta)<=q. The factor on ||x_0||^2 is retained,\nso this is a certificate in the original objective normalization.\n\nTo audit its application rather than re-prove the accepted component,\nthe prefix remainder of that component is\n\n  R=q||x_0||^2-2(1-gamma)(f_n-||g_n||^2/2)\n       -||gamma x_n-(t+gamma)g_n||^2\n       +2q sum_(i<n)H_i Q_(i,star)+2q(D+1)Q_(n,star)\n       +q sum lambda_ij Q_ij.\n\nUsing t=gamma D and 1-gamma=2qD, its mixed <x_n,g_n> coefficient\ncancels. Its f_n and ||g_n||^2 coefficients are respectively\n-2q(D-1) and -qD(D-1). Distance telescoping then gives R=q M_H,\nwhich is exactly (11). Every queried-prefix inequality is retained.\n\n## 8. A full two-bridge calibration and its exact scope\n\nTake b=(1,1), and take both a_1,a_2 empty. Then\n\n  D_0=3, D_1=2+sqrt(7), r_1=sqrt(7)-2,\n  D_2=[1+D_1+sqrt((1+D_1)^2+4D_1)]/2, r_2=D_2-1-D_1.\n\nThus H_2=(1+r_2,sqrt(7)-1,1,1). For E_theta2(b), use the signed\ntail coefficients in Section 4 and add theta_2 b_i to each incoming\nterminal coefficient. The only initially negative coefficient becomes\ntheta_2-1/4>0: already kappa_1>4/3 and K>kappa_1. All other\ncoefficients are nonnegative. The PSD remainder is\n||u-v/8||^2+(255/64)||v||^2, because the added interpolation terms\nexactly cancel the -theta_2 J_b term in E_theta2.\n\nEquations (10),(11) therefore give a completely explicit certificate\nfor this five-step objective schedule after appending beta. This is a\ncalibration of signed coordinates with nonnegative actual multipliers,\nnot an improved asymptotic schedule. In particular, the physical tail\ncapacity 3 is already smaller than that of the legal two-step primitive\nschedule (3/2,3/2), whose capacity is 4. The old tail also has an ordinary\nprimitive certificate, so this example does not establish strict\nenlargement by the E_theta state.\n\nThe comparator's primitive property has a direct identity, so no\nunverified external premise is needed: at h=(3/2,3/2), take\nlambda_01=1,lambda_10=7/2,lambda_12=17/2,lambda_21=14,\nlambda_02=1/2,lambda_20=1. Expanding gives V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij=0.\nAll six multipliers are positive. Replacing the capacity-3 tail by this\ncapacity-4 tail in the same two saturated joins strictly increases both\nsuccessive capacities, since the positive root\nOmega(A,D)=[A+D+sqrt((A+D)^2+4AD)]/2 is increasing in D for A,D>0.\nThe terminal q in (11) strictly decreases. The displayed calibration\nis therefore strictly dominated already at its own finite horizon.\n\nThe unresolved bridge toward an improvement is concrete: find an actual\ntail with a nonnegative certificate for E_theta at some theta<1 and with\na capacity gain large enough to pay for the physical prefix lengths.\nNeither the existence of that tail nor the required infinite-horizon\ncomparison with U_n^ZJ is asserted here.\n\n## 9. Exact re-selection for tails with at most two positive steps\n\nThe new state cannot give a genuinely larger primitive certificate\nclass on tails with at most two positive steps. More strongly, suppose\nan identity V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij has value cancellation and is PSD,\nwith every multiplier nonnegative EXCEPT that incoming-terminal\nlambda_(i,n) may have arbitrary real signs, without any lower bound.\nFor at most two positive steps, an ordinary nonnegative intrinsic\ncertificate can always be constructed. This is a certificate statement;\nno unproved dual-attainment equivalence is used.\n\nFor one positive step u, value cancellation gives\nlambda_10=lambda_01+2u. An affine Huber trajectory has equal gradients\nand all Q=V=0. Thus PSD forces the all-one vector into the kernel.\nThe second row of the matrix times that vector is\nu(lambda_01-2)/2, so lambda_01=2, lambda_10=2+2u.\nThe remainder is (2-u^2)||g_0-g_1||^2; PSD requires u<=sqrt(2).\nBoth multipliers are already nonnegative.\n\nNow take two positive steps h=(u,v), with s=u+v. Put\n\n  c=lambda_02, d=lambda_20, a=lambda_01, b=lambda_12.\n\nOnly c,b can be signed; a,d,lambda_10,lambda_21 are nonnegative.\nThe value equations give\n\n  lambda_10=a+c-d+2u,\n  lambda_21=b+c-d+2s.\n\nThe same affine equality argument puts the all-one gradient vector in\nthe PSD kernel. Direct expansion of its first and last rows gives\n\n  u a+s c=2u,\n  v(b+c)+u d=2s(u+1).\n\nTherefore\n\n  a=2-(s/u)c,\n  b=2s(u+1)/v-c-(u/v)d,\n  lambda_10=2(u+1)-(v/u)c-d,\n  b=2(u+1)+(u/v)lambda_10>0.                           (12)\n\nSo b is actually positive. In gradient differences U=g_0-g_1 and\nV=g_1-g_2, the complete quadratic is\n\n  [2-u^2+v(u-1)c/u]||U||^2\n    +[c-(u-1)d]<U,V>+[C_0-(u/v)d]||V||^2,             (13)\n  C_0=s[2(u+1)/v-s].\n\nThese formulas can be checked from the six explicit pair residuals;\nno inequality replacement occurs. If c>=0, all the supplied\nmultipliers are nonnegative already, by (12).\n\nIf c<0, PSD implies both diagonal coefficients in (13) are\nnonnegative. Since d>=0, the last coefficient implies C_0>=0.\nIf u<1 then u<sqrt(2). If u>=1, the c contribution in the first\ncoefficient is nonpositive, so its nonnegativity implies u<=sqrt(2).\nNow replace the supplied multipliers by the explicit adjacent-only list\n\n  lambda'_01=2, lambda'_10=2+2u,\n  lambda'_12=2s(u+1)/v,\n  lambda'_21=2s(u+1)/v+2s,\n  lambda'_02=lambda'_20=0.\n\nAll are nonnegative. Substitution is (13) with c=d=0 and gives\n\n  V_h+sum lambda'_ij Q_ij\n       =(2-u^2)||U||^2+C_0||V||^2 >=0.                (14)\n\nThis constructs the re-selection, rather than inferring a false\ncoefficient bound for the original certificate.\n\nFor nonnegative lists with zero entries and at most two positive\nentries, first restrict the given signed certificate to the subspace\nwhere all jets in a zero-step run agree. Congruence preserves PSD;\nterms between coincident jets vanish; remaining coefficients aggregate\nby query class. Only coefficients entering the final class can remain\nsigned. The intrinsic energy becomes the positive-compressed energy.\nApply the result above (or V_empty=0). Lift the newly constructed\nordinary certificate to the original formal space by selecting each\npositive-step query as its representative and selecting the original\nterminal n as the terminal representative. Positions agree as formal\nidentities because every omitted step coefficient is zero. The lifted\nquadratic is a congruence of the compressed PSD matrix; all unused\nformal-gradient columns vanish. No equality between duplicate formal\ngradients is imposed in the final certificate.\n\nAccordingly, a strict advantage from the terminal-credit state, if one\nexists, requires at least three positive physical tail steps. The\nspecific three-step rational tail in Section 5 is already excluded by\nan actual-function witness. General three-step and longer re-selection\nis not proved by this calculation.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": "max4_terminal_credit_short_tail_reselection", "artifact": "local_memory/saturated_certificate_credit.md", "finding_ids": ["f7cee3034df8cb7f", "edbbe4af2a2ad5c2", "00244596ecb825bc"], "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "status": "complete_candidate_unverified", "next_gap": "strict credit advantage or a general re-selection proof for at least three positive tail steps", "fact_submit_calls": 0, "numerical_pep_calls": 0}, "glossary": {"u,v": "The two strictly positive compressed tail step sizes in this local re-selection argument.", "c,d": "The local tail multiplier coordinates lambda_02 and lambda_20.", "a,b": "The local tail multiplier coordinates lambda_01 and lambda_12; b is not a step list in Section9.", "C_0": "(u+v)[2(u+1)/v-(u+v)], the second diagonal coefficient after constructive re-selection.", "U,V": "Gradient differences g_0-g_1 and g_1-g_2 in Section9; V here is not the intrinsic scalar V_h.", "lambda'": "The explicitly constructed nonnegative adjacent-only multiplier array in equation(14)."}}
{"id": "846c8ad5e763dfb7", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T15:23:34.393788+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C27 complete strict-SIMPLEX reduction candidate: any intrinsic primitive failure for the SAME schedule with m positive steps has a negative projection-function witness in dimension m+1 on a simplex with exactly m+2 vertices, every nonduplicate sample projecting strictly onto its own distinct vertex. Gives full normal/Gram game and exact physical norm identity V_h=||x0||^2-||xn+S P_C(xn)||^2. Explicit triangle at h=3/2 has V=-3/80; a translation family on that SAME triangle preserves V while normalized objective with the CLOSEST minimizer tends to0. No capacity/profile theorem is inferred.", "evidence": "# Strict simplex witnesses for primitive failure\n\nStatus: complete candidate, NOT verifier-accepted. No new fact_submit or\nnumerical search is allocated. The proof uses the interpolation necessity\nin accepted fact 1252b8823bb5a290. The public projection/saturation findings\n20c6d21ee60a4cb0 and 4ad60ca9f3ec3e2e were leads, not premises.\nAll new steps, including the independent-gradient perturbation, are below.\n\n## 1. Exact statement\n\nFor a finite nonnegative physical schedule h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), put\nS=sum h_i and define, on any actual convex differentiable 1-smooth GD run,\n\n    V_h=2 sum_(i<n) h_i(f_i-f_n)\n        +sum_(i<n) h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n        -S(S+1)||g_n||^2.                            (1)\n\nCall h primitive when V_h>=0 for every such finite-dimensional run with\na minimizer. Let m be the number of strictly positive steps of h.\n\nThe following conditions are equivalent:\n\n* h is primitive;\n* for every finite-dimensional compact convex polytope C containing0,\n  the function F_C(x)=(||x||^2-dist(x,C)^2)/2 and its GD run satisfy\n  ||x_n+S P_C(x_n)||<=||x_0||;\n* the same inequality holds for every full-dimensional simplex in\n  R^(m+1) with vertices 0,v_0,...,v_m.\n\nMore strongly, if h fails primitivity, a negative witness can be chosen\non a simplex of the last form with its m+1 distinct compressed query\ngradients exactly v_0,...,v_m. Every projection normal inequality at\none of these query vertices is STRICT against each different vertex,\nincluding zero. Duplicates introduced by zero steps are simply restored.\nThus there are exactly m+2 vertices, dimension m+1, and no limiting\ninterpolant. The original step values and order are unchanged.\n\nThe construction preserves a strictly negative intrinsic energy. It\ndoes NOT preserve the normalized objective error, or the initial distance.\nSection7 gives a fixed triangle where the absolute negative energy stays\nconstant while even the objective normalized by the closest-minimizer\ndistance tends to zero.\n\n## 2. Projection functions and every normal inequality\n\nLet C be a nonempty compact convex set containing0. The unique maximizer\nof <x,v>-||v||^2/2 over v in C is its nearest point p=P_C(x), by completing\nthe square. Therefore\n\n    F_C(x)=max_(v in C)(<x,v>-||v||^2/2)\n          =(||x||^2-dist(x,C)^2)/2.                   (2)\n\nThe nearest-point condition is\n\n    <x-p,v-p><=0 for EVERY v in C.                   (3)\n\nNecessity follows by differentiating squared distance along the segment\nfrom p to v; sufficiency follows by expanding ||x-v||^2. Applied twice,\n(3) gives ||P_C(x)-P_C(y)||^2<=<P_C(x)-P_C(y),x-y>, so P_C is\n1-Lipschitz. Comparing the maxima in (2) gives\n\n    0<=F_C(x+d)-F_C(x)-<P_C(x),d>\n      <=<P_C(x+d)-P_C(x),d><=||d||^2.\n\nConsequently F_C is convex, differentiable, and 1-smooth, with gradient\nP_C. It is nonnegative and F_C(0)=0. Its minimizer set is precisely the\npolar cone C^polar={z:<z,v><=0 for every v in C}: use (3) with p=0.\n\nFor proposed query data X_i,G_i, define\n\n    Y_i=X_i-G_i,  F_i=<G_i,X_i>-||G_i||^2/2.\n\nOn C=conv{0,G_0,...,G_m}, the COMPLETE projection conditions are\n\n    <Y_i,G_j-G_i><=0 for ALL i,j,\n    <Y_i,-G_i><=0 for ALL i.                         (4)\n\nConvex combinations then give (3) for every retained point. No adjacent\nor chronological subset replaces (4).\n\n## 3. A finite strict perturbation with independent gradients\n\nCompress zero steps from the supplied negative witness. A zero step repeats\nthe point, gradient, and function value. Its coefficients in (1) vanish,\nand S and the final jet are unchanged, so V_h is unchanged. If m=0 then\nV_h=0, and there was no negative witness. Hence assume m>=1 and the\ncompressed steps are all positive; write their actual energy as -v_def<0.\n\nConstruct an auxiliary quadratic on R^(m+1): let\n\n    theta_l=(l+1)/(2(m+2)), 0<=l<=m,\n    A_aux=diag(theta_0,...,theta_m),\n    psi(z)=<z,A_aux z>/2,  z_0=(1,...,1).\n\nRun the same compressed schedule and denote its jets by z_i,w_i,psi_i.\nIf P_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z), its coordinate gradients are\nw_(i,l)=theta_l P_i(theta_l). These m+1 gradients are linearly independent.\nIndeed the P_i have distinct degrees 0,...,m and nonzero leading\ncoefficients. A linear combination vanishing at the m+1 distinct theta_l\nis a polynomial of degree at most m with m+1 roots, hence is zero; the\ndistinct-degree basis then makes every coefficient zero.\n\nEvery two sample positions z_i,z_j are distinct, since P_i-P_j is a\nnonzero polynomial of degree at most m and cannot vanish at all theta_l.\nFor the signed residual convention from accepted fact 1252b8823bb5a290,\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n\ndirect substitution for this auxiliary quadratic gives\n\n    Q_ij^aux=-<z_i-z_j,A_aux(I-A_aux)(z_i-z_j)>/2<0    (i!=j). (5)\n\nAll theta_l lie strictly between0 and1. Let E_aux be the finite energy\nof this explicitly given quadratic run. Choose\n\n    epsilon=v_def/[2(v_def+|E_aux|)] in(0,1/2].       (6)\n\nTake the fixed product function\n\n    f_mix(x,z)=f_original(x)+epsilon psi(z/sqrt(epsilon)).\n\nStart it at (x_0,sqrt(epsilon)z_0). Its sampled points and gradients are\n(x_i,sqrt(epsilon)z_i) and (g_i,sqrt(epsilon)w_i), with values\nf_i+epsilon psi_i. It is convex differentiable 1-smooth, and all GD\nequations are exact. Energy and every Q are additive across these\ncoordinates. Hence its energy is\n\n    V_mix=-v_def+epsilon E_aux<=-v_def/2<0,           (7)\n\nits gradients are independent, and all finite distinct-sample Q_ij\nare strictly negative by (5). Rename these mixed data x_i,g_i,f_i.\n\n## 4. One origin/value shift makes an actual simplex trajectory\n\nSet\n\n    f_* = min_i(f_i-||g_i||^2/2)-1,\n    r_i = f_*-f_i+<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2.\n\nLet A_gram=(<g_i,g_j>)_(0<=i,j<=m), which is positive definite.\nDefine z_*=sum_j (A_gram^(-1)r)_j g_j. Then <g_i,z_*>=r_i.\nSet X_i=x_i-z_* and F_i=f_i-f_*. The GD equations are unchanged,\nand the defining equalities give\n\n    F_i=<g_i,X_i>-||g_i||^2/2.                        (8)\n\nFor all finite ordered pairs, the Q residual is unchanged and is\nstrictly negative. Substituting (8) into it gives\n\n    Q_ij=<X_j-g_j,g_i-g_j><0 for i!=j.               (9)\n\nMoreover <X_i-g_i,g_i>=F_i-||g_i||^2/2>=1. These are ALL the\nnormal inequalities (4), including strict inequalities against zero.\nConsequently the fixed polytope C=conv{0,g_0,...,g_m} satisfies\nP_C(X_i)=g_i and F_C(X_i)=F_i at every compressed query.\nThus it is an actual globally legal projection-function realization;\nwe did not just assign independent values and gradients.\n\nThe g_i are linearly independent, so these m+2 points are all vertices\nof a simplex of dimension m+1. Orthogonally project all X_i onto\nW=span{g_0,...,g_m}. Gradients, values, GD equations, and all inequalities\n(9) are unchanged. The removed component is constant along the run.\nThis puts the entire realization in dimension m+1. All initial points\nare nonzero, since <g_0,X_0>=F_0+||g_0||^2/2>0.\n\nTranslation of the points and a common shift of the values leave (1)\nunchanged; orthogonal compression does too. Therefore its final energy\nis exactly V_mix<0. Restoring the original zero steps repeats the\ncorresponding actual jets and keeps the same polytope, all physical\nsteps, and the energy. This proves the claimed finite sign reduction.\n\n## 5. The norm identity, including the discarded-slack term\n\nFor arbitrary original legal data with minimizer0 and value0 define\n\n    I_i=<g_i,x_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2>=0,\n    J=sum_(i<n)h_i I_i.\n\nThe GD squared-distance telescope gives\n\n    V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_n+Sg_n||^2+2(S I_n-J).         (10)\n\nIndeed ||x_0||^2-||x_n||^2\n=2sum h_i<g_i,x_i>-sum h_i^2||g_i||^2; substitute\n<g_i,x_i>=f_i+||g_i||^2/2+I_i and expand the final square.\n\nFor projection functions, (2) makes every I_i exactly zero. Therefore\nthe proposed physical-step formula is exactly\n\n    V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_n+S P_C(x_n)||^2.             (11)\n\nIt is generally false without the extra slack in (10). Combining (11)\nwith sections2--4 proves all equivalences in section1. No missing\nfunction values were dropped: they determine r, z_*, and the resulting\npositions relative to the simplex.\n\n## 6. A precise full Gram game and its scope\n\nFor the compressed length m define the lower-triangular matrix T_h by\n(T_h)_ii=1, (T_h)_ij=h_j for j<i, and zero for j>i. Let A_gram be\nthe positive definite gradient Gram matrix and b_i=<g_i,X_0>.\nThe complete normal inequalities are precisely\n\n    b_j-b_i <= (T_h A_gram)_ij-(T_h A_gram)_ii   for all i,j,\n    b_i >= (T_h A_gram)_ii                      for all i. (12)\n\nThe strict simplex construction makes all nontrivial inequalities strict.\nConversely any positive definite A_gram and b satisfying (12) have a\nrealization: choose independent vectors with this Gram matrix, and set\nX_0=sum_j(A_gram^(-1)b)_j g_j. Equations (12) then prove all projection\nconditions for the specified simplex and prescribed GD trajectory.\n\nPut d_i=h_i for i<m, d_m=-S. Then sum_i d_i=0 and\n\n    V_h=2 b^T d-d^T A_gram d,\n    ||X_0||^2=b^T A_gram^(-1)b.                       (13)\n\nThese formulas retain every queried vertex. They are a lossless\nfinite-dimensional game for primitive validity, not yet a capacity bound.\nIn particular, no conclusion S+1<=(m+1)^(log_2(1+sqrt(2))) is inferred.\n\nAdding a common constant to every b_i preserves the finite differences\nand the primitive energy, and can enforce the origin inequalities. It\nchanges the initial norm in (13); it therefore cannot be used freely in\nan objective-normalized lower bound.\n\n## 7. An exact triangle, and a quantitative normalization failure\n\nTake the one-step schedule h=(3/2), the triangle\n\n    v_0=(1,0),  v_1=(-2/5,1/10), C=conv{0,v_0,v_1},\n    X_0=(2,267/20), X_1=(1/2,267/20).\n\nThe two query normals are Y_0=(1,267/20) and Y_1=(9/10,53/4).\nALL nontrivial projection comparisons are\n\n    <Y_0,v_1-v_0>=<Y_1,v_0-v_1>=-13/200,\n    <Y_0,-v_0>=-1,  <Y_1,-v_1>=-193/200.\n\nThus these are strict vertex projections, and their complete values are\nF_C(X_0)=3/2, F_C(X_1)=21/20. The norm game gives\n\n    V_h=72889/400-72904/400=-3/80<0.                  (14)\n\nFor every T>=0 put u=(1,14), X_i(T)=X_i+T u. Since\n<u,v_0>=<u,v_1>=1, every finite normal difference above is unchanged,\nand the origin comparisons only become more negative. Hence the SAME\nfixed triangle realizes the same gradient trajectory for every T,\n\n    F_C(X_i(T))=F_C(X_i)+T,\n    V_h(T)=-3/80.\n\nIts minimizer cone is {z:z_1<=0, z_2<=4z_1}. Both coordinates of\nX_0(T) are positive, while both coordinates of a cone point are\nnonpositive. Expanding squared distances proves that zero is the\nclosest minimizer to X_0(T). Therefore the genuine normalized terminal\nobjective is\n\n    [21/10+2T]/[(2+T)^2+(267/20+14T)^2] ->0.          (15)\n\nThis is not merely an arbitrary poor choice of minimizer. It proves that\nan absolute negative primitive margin alone cannot lower-bound the\nnormalized objective even on one fixed simplex and one fixed schedule.\nIt does not upper-bound the worst case for that schedule: other instances\nmay give a much larger value.\n\nThe mechanism holds on every independent-gradient simplex. Let u be\nthe unique vector in the gradient span with <u,g_i>=1 for all i.\nTranslation by T u preserves all finite normal inequalities and shifts\nall sample values by T, while improving the origin comparisons.\nSince sum d_i=0, it leaves V_h unchanged. The closest-minimizer distance\nis Theta(T): for each cone point z, <g_i,z><=0, so\n||X_0+Tu-z||>=(T+<g_i,X_0>)/||g_i|| for sufficiently large T;\nthe upper bound O(T) follows by choosing z=0. Thus (15)'s dilution\nis a general geometric feature, not a special coordinate artifact.\n\n## 8. A failed capacity-witness simplification\n\nA natural attempt to make the simplex game tractable is to require all\nnonzero visited vertices to have one common radius. This is NOT a\nuniversal class of primitive counterexamples, even at length1.\n\nSuppose 0<=h_i<2 and every nonzero sampled projection gradient has norm R.\nIf both consecutive gradients are nonzero, projection cocoercivity gives\n\n    ||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2\n      <=h_i<g_i,g_i-g_(i+1)>\n      =(h_i/2)||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2.\n\nThey must therefore be equal. If a zero gradient is ever reached, that\npoint is a minimizer and all later gradients are zero. Before that time\nthe gradient is constant. If no zero is reached, x_n+Sg_n=x_0 and V_h=0.\nIf a zero is reached, each h_i<2 step decreases squared distance to zero:\nthe vertex0 inequality gives <x_i,g_i>>=||g_i||^2, hence\n\n    ||x_(i+1)||^2<=||x_i||^2-h_i(2-h_i)||g_i||^2.\n\nThus (11) is nonnegative again. In particular equal-radius vertex walks,\nincluding regular-simplex vertex walks, cannot reject h=(3/2), although\nthe explicit strict triangle (14) does reject it. Allowing projections\nonto nonvertex faces is outside this failed simplification.\n\nThe exact general simplex reduction succeeds. The equal-radius capacity\nansatz fails, and the unrestricted capacity inequality remains open.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "workers/max3/research/c27_simplex_projection_reduction.md", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "finding_ids": ["20c6d21ee60a4cb0", "4ad60ca9f3ec3e2e"], "status": "Complete self-contained unverified candidate; no new verifier call allocated.", "scope": "Universal SIGN test for intrinsic primitive validity, with strict simplex witnesses; not preservation of normalized W and not a capacity upper bound.", "is_fragile": false, "fragility_reason": "", "suggested_followup": "none", "justification_type": "calculation", "confidence": 0.97}, "glossary": {"V_h": "Intrinsic primitive energy defined in equation(1) of the evidence.", "C": "Compact convex projection set; the constructed witness is the simplex conv{0,g_0,...,g_m}.", "P_C": "Euclidean nearest-point projection onto C.", "F_C": "The convex 1-smooth function (||x||^2-dist(x,C)^2)/2, whose gradient is P_C.", "A_gram": "Positive definite Gram matrix of the independent compressed sampled gradients.", "T_h": "Lower triangular matrix with diagonal1 and entry h_j in row i, column j when j<i.", "b_i": "Pairing <g_i,X_0> in the finite simplex Gram game.", "d_i": "Coefficient h_i for i<m, and -S for the compressed terminal i=m.", "C^polar": "The minimizer cone {z:<z,v><=0 for every v in C}.", "v_def": "Positive magnitude of the original negative primitive energy.", "epsilon": "Explicit weight v_def/[2(v_def+|E_aux|)] for the independent-gradient quadratic perturbation.", "E_aux": "Intrinsic primitive energy of the explicit auxiliary diagonal quadratic run."}}
{"id": "ec8567340a9f5e45", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T15:28:35.015573+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Scope correction for max's earlier Chebyshev examples: BOTH the terminal-large-step family and its final-unit-step counterfamily already satisfy the VERIFIED ray baseline F_ray>=M^(11/12)/5308416>1/B_n for every stated large horizon, with that ray value tending to infinity. Select only the last two adjacent large steps of the FIRST increasing Chebyshev copy. Thus those examples do not give a regime beyond every accepted baseline; the earlier exact completion formulas and narrower K/Q/lens separations remain unaffected.", "evidence": "# Audit: the earlier Chebyshev families already have large ray witnesses\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED exact comparison using accepted facts. This corrects the\nstrategic interpretation of earlier findings, not their displayed interpolation\nformulas. No verifier call or numerical computation was used.\n\nThe all-large-horizon families in findings `e2dad1cf3b95b8d7` and\n`54a304d61fe223b4` were compared with K, quadratics and the universal complex\nlens, but not with the stronger VERIFIED functional F_ray from fact\n`1fca3e5dd555cb4a`. They are already very bad schedules for F_ray.\n\nLet N>=4096, M=2N(N+1)+1, A=M^(1/12). The first ordinary list in either\nfamily begins with an increasing Chebyshev copy Chi_N. Its last two adjacent\nsteps are\n\n    b_0=csc^2(2theta), b_1=csc^2(theta), theta=pi/(2N+1).\n\nTheir exact identification follows from accepted fact `dfde2fa326c908bf`.\nSelect JUST these two steps as ray checkpoints. Let P be the total preceding\nmass and T the total following mass, so the middle ordinary block is empty.\nBeginning-zero padding changes neither P nor T. Both families satisfy\n\n    P+1<=M,\n    T<=total mass<=(A+3)M,\n    A>=4,\n    M>=2304A and M>=1152.                               (1)\n\nThe mass bound follows from the two lists of mass M-1, bridge AM+1 and\nlast step either 1 or 1+M^(2/3). For the numerical-free large-M checks,\nM>=2^25 and M/A=M^(11/12)>=2^22>2304, while A>=4 because\nM>=2^25>4^12.\n\nSince sin(t)<=t and pi^2<10,\n\n    b_1-1>=(2M-1)/10-1>=M/12,\n    b_0-1>=(2M-1)/40-1>=M/48.                           (2)\n\nThe last inequalities hold for M>=48. Set L_pair=(b_0-1)(b_1-1), so\n\n    L_pair>=M^2/576>=4AM.                               (3)\n\nThe exact accepted ray parameters are\n\n    m_0=1/(P+1), m_1=(b_0-1)/(P+1), O_1=P/(P+1),\n    z_term=(L_pair-P)/(P+1), m_old=m_0,\n    e_term=P/(P+1)^2.\n\nThere is no same-ray monotonicity condition on the two nonterminal slopes\nm_0,m_1, because each ray has been visited only once. Both are positive.\nMoreover\n\n    P+2T+1<=(2A+7)M+1<=4AM<=L_pair.\n\nConsequently q=z_term/(2T+1)>=m_old. Also q<=z_term/(T+1), so it is an\nadmissible terminal slope. In particular the full old-slope condition\nz_term>=(1+T)m_old holds. This checks every feasibility hypothesis of\nthe accepted ray fact, including its retained remote offset.\n\nIts exact normalized payoff is\n\n    2e_term+z_term^2/(2T+1).\n\nUsing (1)--(3),\n\n    z_term >= (M^2/576-M)/M >= M/1152,\n    2T+1<=4AM,\n\nso the complete ray functional satisfies\n\n    F_ray_n(h)>=M/(5308416 A)=M^(11/12)/5308416.           (4)\n\nThis tends to infinity on BOTH earlier families, including the family\nending with a unit step. It also gives a bound at every padded horizon.\nThe actual benchmark obeys B_n>=2n+1 by accepted fact `0be8b54e0a33381c`.\nFor a pointwise check already at N>=4096, n>=6N+2, M>=2N^2 and A<=N^(1/4)\n(since M<=5N^2 and N>=5). Thus\n\n    B_n F_ray_n(h)\n       >=24N^(11/4)/5308416>1.\n\nFor the last inequality N>=2^12 gives its numerator at least24*2^33,\nlarger than5308416. The ratio diverges with N.\n\nTherefore these examples do NOT demonstrate a geometric witness regime\nuncovered by all the project's accepted baseline witnesses. They remain\nvalid separations from the explicitly listed weaker channels K, Q and\nthe universal lens, and their exact terminal formulas remain separately\nuseful. The omission of F_ray must not be used to motivate more work\nmerely improving the payoff on these same schedules.\n\nThe last-unit obstruction for a constant-gradient final block is still a\ncorrectly scoped candidate; it is an obstruction to that selected prefix\nand continuation class, not to the full ray adversary on the schedule.\n\nThis audit neither refutes the all-schedule target nor supplies an upper\nbound on W_n. It identifies a previously unmade comparison against a\nverified predecessor and rules out a misleading claim of target-level\nnovelty for these families.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1fca3e5dd555cb4a", "dfde2fa326c908bf", "0be8b54e0a33381c"], "finding_ids": ["e2dad1cf3b95b8d7", "54a304d61fe223b4", "bc4ce8b7c164f3b4"]}, "glossary": {"M": "For this audited family, 2N(N+1)+1, where N>=4096 is the Chebyshev-copy length.", "A": "M^(1/12), the audited large bridge factor.", "L_pair": "Product (b_0-1)(b_1-1) for the two selected adjacent Chebyshev checkpoints."}}
{"id": "b80fa725238cc84a", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T15:28:35.137157+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Exact candidate invariant for ONE fixed maximal finite-prefix interpolant: its proximal value F(x)-||grad F(x)||²/2 is an active convex combination of the seed proximal values, at EVERY x. Hence every later unit step caps F by the largest seed height even after arbitrarily many internal gradient changes. Exact convex-roof formula identifies the true minimizer set and gives a quantitative arbitrary-suffix recurrence. Greedy maximal extensions leave the whole function unchanged and cannot create height. A Huber counterexample proves this is not an upper bound for other prefix interpolants or unrestricted W.", "evidence": "# Height budgets and a two-checkpoint construction with an arbitrary tail\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint proof candidate. No new verifier allocation and no\nsolver call. All new ingredients are proved below in the same document;\nunverified earlier findings are not premises. This does not establish the\nuniform comparison with the complete sequence B_n.\n\nVerified inputs: fact `1252b8823bb5a290` for smooth-convex interpolation and\nthe maximal finite-prefix interpolant; fact `1fca3e5dd555cb4a` for the ray\ncomparison in Section 5. The definitions of W_n, K_n and B_n are those in\nfact `b98bb807d514e80d`. Work at smoothness one, minimum zero.\n\n## 1. An invariant of one fixed maximal prefix interpolant\n\nTake finitely many legal jets (x_j,g_j,f_j), including (0,0,0). Define\n\n    y_j=x_j-g_j,       delta_j=f_j-||g_j||^2/2,\n    M_seed=max_j delta_j,\n    m_seed(v)=max_j(<y_j,v>-delta_j),\n    H_seed(v)=||v||^2/2+m_seed(v),\n    F_seed(x)=sup_v(<x,v>-H_seed(v)).                       (1)\n\nThe accepted fact proves that F_seed is the maximal convex 1-smooth\ninterpolant of these jets. Each delta_j is nonnegative by interpolation\nwith the optimum. For every x, put v=grad F_seed(x). Strong conjugate\noptimality gives\n\n    x-v=sum_j alpha_j y_j,\n    alpha_j>=0, sum_j alpha_j=1,\n\nwhere positive alpha_j occur only among affine terms active in m_seed(v).\nTo justify the convex-hull subgradient assertion directly, the directional\nderivative of a finite maximum of affine functions is the maximum of the\nactive slopes in that direction. A vector outside their convex hull can\nbe strictly separated from that hull and cannot satisfy the subgradient\ninequality. Every convex combination of active slopes does satisfy it.\nUsing their common active value in (1) now gives the exact identity\n\n    F_seed(x)-||grad F_seed(x)||^2/2\n        =sum_j alpha_j delta_j in [0,M_seed].              (2)\n\nThus arbitrary later changes of gradient on this ONE fixed function do\nnot create a proximal value above the maximum seed value.\n\nThere is an equivalent finite convex-roof formula. Let K_seed be the\nconvex hull of all y_j, and define, for z in K_seed,\n\n    J_roof(z)=min {sum_j alpha_j delta_j:\n                  alpha>=0, sum alpha=1, sum alpha_j y_j=z},\n\nand J_roof=+infinity outside K_seed. The coefficient simplex is compact,\nso the minima exist; 0<=J_roof<=M_seed on K_seed. Then\n\n    F_seed(x)=min_alpha [sum alpha_j delta_j\n                         +||x-sum alpha_j y_j||^2/2].       (3)\n\nFor a direct proof, minimize the displayed continuous convex function\nover the coefficient simplex and set v=x-sum alpha_j y_j. Its directional\noptimality conditions on the simplex say that positive coordinates\nminimize delta_j-<y_j,v>. These are exactly the active terms of m_seed(v),\ngiving equality with (1). This is an identity, not a numerical program.\n\nIn particular, with K_zero=conv{y_j:delta_j=0},\n\n    argmin F_seed=K_zero,\n    dist(x,K_seed)^2/2 <= F_seed(x)\n                         <= dist(x,K_seed)^2/2+M_seed.      (4)\n\nThe minimizer identity follows from (3): its two nonnegative summands\nvanish exactly when x=sum alpha_j y_j with support on zero-height jets.\nIt supplies the actual closest-minimizer distance, without substituting\ndistance to the generally larger set K_seed.\n\nFor any GD step t>=0 from x, let x_new=x-t grad F_seed(x). The elementary\nsmoothness inequality and (2), with delta=F_seed(x)-||grad F_seed(x)||^2/2,\ngive\n\n    F_seed(x_new)<=delta+(1-t)^2||grad F_seed(x)||^2/2\n                  =(1-t)^2 F_seed(x)+[1-(1-t)^2]delta\n                  <=(1-t)^2 F_seed(x)\n                      +[1-(1-t)^2]_+ M_seed.              (5)\n\nThis holds along any nonnegative schedule, irrespective of the number\nof gradient changes. A unit step gives F_seed(x_new)<=M_seed. Every\nfollowing step in [0,2] preserves that value cap. Iterating (5) gives,\nfor a suffix t_0,...,t_(r-1), the explicit bound\n\n F_seed(x_r) <= product_(i<r)(1-t_i)^2 F_seed(x_0)\n   +M_seed sum_(j<r)[1-(1-t_j)^2]_+\n                         product_(j<i<r)(1-t_i)^2.          (6)\n\nEmpty products and sums have their usual meanings.\n\nGreedily adjoining jets of F_seed at new points and recomputing the\nmaximal interpolant leaves the entire function unchanged. Indeed the\nnew conjugate quadratic is <=H_seed everywhere, by interpolation of\nF_seed with its actual new jet, whereas all old quadratics remain.\nTheir maximum is therefore still H_seed. In particular such greedy\nextensions cannot increase M_seed, by (2).\n\nThe scope is essential. The bound does NOT hold for arbitrary other\ninterpolants of the seed. Seed jets (0,0,0) and (1,1,1/2) have M_seed=0\nand maximal interpolant F_seed(x)=x^2/2. The symmetric Huber function\n\n    f_H(x)=x^2/2 for |x|<=1, and |x|-1/2 for |x|>=1\n\nhas the same seed. Starting at1 with schedule (R+1,1), R>=2, the maximal\nquadratic ends at0, whereas f_H visits -R and -(R-1), with normalized\nterminal value 2R-3. Its new proximal height at -R is R-1>0. Hence\ncreating new height requires a nonmaximal choice of the future function;\nthe invariant is not an upper bound on the unrestricted W_n.\n\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "finding_ids": ["b96d3f3a109d79d2", "a73e2d7143e09751"]}, "glossary": {"M_seed": "Maximum seed proximal value delta_j=f_j-||g_j||²/2.", "F_seed": "Pointwise maximal 1-smooth convex interpolant of the fixed finite legal seed jets.", "m_seed": "Maximum affine function max_j(<y_j,v>-delta_j) built from seed proximal points and heights.", "H_seed": "Strongly convex conjugate ||v||²/2+m_seed(v).", "K_seed": "Convex hull of the seed proximal points y_j.", "K_zero": "Convex hull of the seed proximal points with delta_j=0; the exact minimizer set of F_seed.", "J_roof": "Minimum convex-combination height above each point of K_seed, extended by infinity outside K_seed."}}
{"id": "1ed052539a27d4ff", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T15:29:25.041438+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "New explicit arbitrary-schedule candidate lower bound survives an arbitrary final tail: for ANY i<j with h_i,h_j>1, put A=(h_i-1)/(1+P_i), epsilon=(h_j-1)/(1+S_ij), and T_j=sum after j. One fixed four-plane Moreau envelope in dimension2, with true initial distance1 and all old/new constraints checked, gives W_n(h)>=A² min(epsilon²,epsilon)/[4(1+2T_j)]. No feasibility oracle is used. Exact angle optimization within the cap family is also explicit. A horizon-dependent unit-step-block example separates this functional from the ENTIRE verified F_ray by a factor at least sqrt(M)/12; that example itself is already above the target scale by K, so it is not advertised as resolving the difficult benchmark regime.", "evidence": "# Height budgets and a two-checkpoint construction with an arbitrary tail\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint proof candidate. No new verifier allocation and no\nsolver call. All new ingredients are proved below in the same document;\nunverified earlier findings are not premises. This does not establish the\nuniform comparison with the complete sequence B_n.\n\nVerified inputs: fact `1252b8823bb5a290` for smooth-convex interpolation and\nthe maximal finite-prefix interpolant; fact `1fca3e5dd555cb4a` for the ray\ncomparison in Section 5. The definitions of W_n, K_n and B_n are those in\nfact `b98bb807d514e80d`. Work at smoothness one, minimum zero.\n\n## 2. Fully explicit three-cell construction\n\nPrescribe arbitrary nonnegative lists B_0,B_1,B_2 of respective sums\nM_0-1, M_1-1, T, where M_0,M_1>=1 and T>=0. Put two checkpoints between\nthem:\n\n    h=(B_0, A M_0+1, B_1, 1+M_1 epsilon, B_2),\n    A>0, epsilon>0.                                      (7)\n\nTheir entries can be arbitrarily large individually; only their actual\npartial sums are used. Select any kappa satisfying\n\n    0<kappa<A,             kappa(1+epsilon)<=A.             (8)\n\nIn R^2, write\n\n    a=1-1/M_0,\n    nu=sqrt(kappa(A-kappa)),\n    p=(1,0)/M_0,              q=(-kappa,nu)/M_1,\n    X=(-A,0),                y=X-M_1 q=(kappa-A,-nu),\n    d=-M_1 q=(kappa,-nu),     e=(a,0)-y,\n    D=||e||^2=(A-kappa)(A+2a)+a^2>0,\n    w=d-(a kappa/D)e,\n    W_sq=||w||^2=A kappa-a^2 kappa^2/D,\n    lambda_cap=epsilon/(2T+1),\n    r_cap=lambda_cap w.                                  (9)\n\nThe following products are exact:\n\n    <d,y>=0, <d,e>=a kappa, <y,e>=-(A-kappa)(A+a),\n    <e,w>=0, <d,w>=W_sq, <q,w>=-W_sq/M_1,\n    w_1=kappa(A-kappa)(A+a)/D>0,\n    <y,w>=a kappa(A-kappa)(A+a)/D>=0.                     (10)\n\nHere w_1 denotes the first coordinate, not a time index. Define ONE\nfixed nonsmooth convex function and its Moreau envelope by\n\n    J_cap(z)=max(0,<p,z>,<q,z>,<r_cap,z-y>),\n    f_cap(x)=min_z[J_cap(z)+||x-z||^2/2],   x_init=(1,0).   (11)\n\nAll four planes are chosen before running the entire supplied schedule.\nThe fourth plane has intercept -<r_cap,y><=0; this intercept is retained.\n\nFor completeness, this envelope is convex, differentiable and 1-smooth.\nThe strictly convex coercive minimization has a unique proximal point\nz(x); optimality says g(x)=x-z(x) belongs to the subgradient of J_cap\nthere. Monotonicity of subgradients at two points gives\n<g(x)-g(x'),x-x'> >= ||g(x)-g(x')||^2. Thus g is 1-Lipschitz. The\ntwo minimizing inequalities show that it is the derivative of the\nenvelope, and joint convexity followed by minimization proves convexity.\nAn active affine-plane slope at z is a subgradient, so it suffices below\nto check activation at x=z+that slope.\n\n### First block and first checkpoint\n\nFor partial ordinary sum u in [0,M_0-1], propose gradient p and proximal\npoint z=(1-(u+1)/M_0,0). Its first coordinate lies in [0,a]. The p-plane\nis nonnegative, and the q-plane is nonpositive. Since <e,w>=0,\n\n    <r_cap,z-y>=lambda_cap (z_1-a)w_1<=0.\n\nThus p is active at every actual sample, including the first checkpoint\nquery at x=p. That checkpoint sends the iterate exactly to X=(-A,0).\n\n### Second block and second checkpoint\n\nFor t=u+1 in [1,M_1], propose gradient q and proximal point X-tq.\nIt equals y+(M_1-t)q. The q-height is\n\n    <q,X-tq>=(A kappa/M_1)(1-t/M_1)>=0.\n\nThe excess of q over p is affine in t, with endpoints controlled by\nits value at t=M_1:\n\n    <q-p,X-tq>=A kappa/M_1+A/M_0\n                         -t(A kappa/M_1^2+kappa/(M_0 M_1))\n                   >=(A-kappa)/M_0>0.\n\nThe fourth plane has value\n\n    <r_cap,X-tq-y>\n       =-lambda_cap(M_1-t)W_sq/M_1<=0.\n\nAll old and future plane comparisons therefore hold at every sample.\nThe position at the second checkpoint is y+q. Its step 1+M_1 epsilon\nsends the trajectory to\n\n    x_C=y+epsilon d.                                     (12)\n\n### Arbitrary final list\n\nFor every partial tail sum u in [0,T], propose gradient r_cap. Its\nproximal point is\n\n    Z(u)=y+epsilon d-(u+1)lambda_cap w.\n\nThe fourth plane there has nonnegative value\n\n    <r_cap,Z(u)-y>\n       =lambda_cap[epsilon-(u+1)lambda_cap]W_sq>=0,         (13)\n\nbecause (u+1)/(2T+1)<=1. The p-plane is nonpositive since\n\n    M_0<p,Z(u)>\n       =kappa-A+epsilon kappa-(u+1)lambda_cap w_1<=0\n\nby (8) and w_1>0. The q-plane is also nonpositive:\n\n    M_1<q,Z(u)>=-epsilon A kappa\n                         +(u+1)lambda_cap W_sq<=0,\n\nusing W_sq<=A kappa and (u+1)lambda_cap<=epsilon. Thus the fourth\nplane is active throughout the entire final list, including its endpoint.\nThis checks all directions of every old/new comparison using the same J_cap.\n\n### Exact value, boundary gradients and true normalization\n\nAt the terminal point x_C-T r_cap, adding half the squared gradient to\n(13) gives\n\n    2 f_cap(x_n)=epsilon^2 W_sq/(2T+1).                   (14)\n\nThe actual squared gradients are\n\n    ||p||^2=1/M_0^2,\n    ||q||^2=A kappa/M_1^2,\n    ||r_cap||^2=epsilon^2 W_sq/(2T+1)^2.                  (15)\n\nThe terminal proximal value is exactly\n\n    delta_end=T epsilon^2 W_sq/(2T+1)^2.                 (16)\n\nThe fresh component of q has squared norm kappa(A-kappa)/M_1^2; it\nhas not been dropped from any norm or scale calculation. The construction\nretains an explicitly positive value budget when T>0, in contrast to a\npure quadratic terminal completion followed by a unit-step reset.\n\nThe origin is a minimizer: its p- and q-planes are zero and its fourth\nplane is <=0 by (10). The minimum of J_cap and its envelope is zero.\nEvery minimizer z of J_cap satisfies <p,z><=0, hence z_1<=0. A minimizer\nof the envelope is exactly a minimizer of J_cap, since both terms in\nits defining minimum are nonnegative. Therefore the closest minimizer\nto x_init=(1,0) is genuinely the origin, at distance exactly one.\nEquation (14) is consequently the required normalized objective, not\nan evaluation using an arbitrarily distant optimum.\n\nAll endpoints M_0=1, M_1=1 and T=0 are included. In particular a=0\ncauses no singularity because 0<kappa<A makes D>0. Zero steps duplicate\nqueries and have no effect on the proof. No parameter limit is hidden.\n\n## 3. A schedule functional with no feasibility oracle\n\nFor any nonnegative schedule h of length n and any i<j with h_i>1,\nh_j>1, define\n\n    P_i=sum_(t<i) h_t,\n    S_ij=sum_(i<t<j) h_t,\n    T_j=sum_(j<t<n) h_t,\n    A_ij=(h_i-1)/(1+P_i),\n    epsilon_ij=(h_j-1)/(1+S_ij).\n\nChoose explicitly\n\n    kappa=A_ij/[2 max(1,epsilon_ij)].                      (17)\n\nIt is positive, at most A_ij/2, and satisfies (8), because\n1+epsilon<=2max(1,epsilon). From D>=a^2 one has\n\n    W_sq>=A kappa-kappa^2>=A kappa/2.\n\nThus (14) yields, for EVERY eligible pair,\n\n W_n(h) >= A_ij^2 min(epsilon_ij^2,epsilon_ij)\n                                      /[4(1+2T_j)].       (18)\n\nDefine C_n^tail(h) to be the maximum of the right side over all eligible\npairs, with zero when there are none. The construction proves the new\ncandidate universal lower bound\n\n    W_n(h)>=max(K_n(h), C_n^tail(h)).                      (19)\n\nThe pair selection is adversary selection before the run; there are no\nunproved existence parameters and no changes to the supplied schedule.\nUnlike a product term tied to h_(n-1), C_n^tail need not vanish when the\nlast step is one. Arbitrarily long, nonperiodic final lists are covered\nwith their exact mass loss 1+2T_j. No assertion that (19) matches 1/B_n\nuniformly is made.\n\n## 4. Exact optimization within the displayed cap directions\n\nFor fixed A>0, a>=0, epsilon>0, T>=0, all directions satisfying (8)\nare admissible in the preceding proof. The exact score is proportional\nto\n\n    W_sq(kappa)=A kappa-a^2 kappa^2/\n                         [(A-kappa)(A+2a)+a^2].            (20)\n\nIf a=0, this is A kappa, so its maximum on the admissible interval is\nat kappa=A/(1+epsilon). If a>0, differentiation with the positive\ndenominator gives\n\n    W_sq'(kappa)=A-a^2 kappa[2D+(A+2a)kappa]/D^2.\n\nPut b=A+2a and C=(A+a)^2, so D=C-b kappa. Expanding its numerator\nfactorizes as\n\n    D^2 W_sq'(kappa)\n        =(A+a)^2 [A(A+a)-b kappa]\n                       [(A+a)-kappa].                    (21)\n\nThe second bracket is positive for kappa<A. Hence the unique unconstrained\nmaximizer in (0,A) is\n\n    kappa_0=A(A+a)/(A+2a).\n\nIts squared direction norm is W_sq(kappa_0)=kappa_0^2. The exact optimum\nwithin the explicitly checked cap family is consequently\n\n    kappa_opt=min(kappa_0,A/(1+epsilon)) for a>0,\n    kappa_opt=A/(1+epsilon) for a=0.                       (22)\n\nAt the feasibility endpoint, kappa(1+epsilon)=A, the old p-plane is\nstill dominated, by the nonnegative subtraction in its comparison. The\noptimization therefore has no open-boundary loss. This is an optimum\nonly within this displayed family of cap planes, not over all prefixes\nor all legal interpolants.\n\n## 5. A strict comparison with the entire verified ray functional\n\nThe following family establishes that (18) contains information absent\nfrom F_ray, although the schedules are already above the target scale\nby the single-cut witness and hence do not solve the hard regime.\n\nLet an integer M>=16 be given, A=M^(1/8), epsilon=M^(-1/4), and choose\n\n    h=(1 repeated M-1 times, A M+1,\n       1 repeated M-1 times, 1+M epsilon, 1).              (23)\n\nOnly its two large checkpoints have entries >1, so the verified ray\nconstruction can select at most those two: a selected first checkpoint\nmust exceed1, and every later selected checkpoint must also exceed1\nbecause positive new gradient magnitude requires (b-1)m>O>=0.\nIf both are chosen, its exact z_term is\n\n    z_term=A epsilon-(M-1)/M\n          =M^(-1/8)-1+1/M<0.                             (24)\n\nIndeed M^(-1/8)<=1/sqrt(2)<3/4 and 1/M<=1/16. Thus this pair is\ninfeasible even before its terminal old-slope constraint. Every feasible\nray witness has at most one cut, and fact 1fca3e5dd555cb4a gives exactly\n\n    F_ray_n(h)=K_n(h).\n\nThe global Huber term is <=1/M. The first cut contributes\nA^2/[1+2(M-1+1+M epsilon+1)]<=A^2/(2M). At the second cut,\none plus the prefix mass is (A+2)M and the tail has sum1, so its value is\nepsilon^2/[3(A+2)^2]<=M^(-3/4)/3. Thus\n\n    F_ray_n(h)=K_n(h)<=M^(-3/4).                          (25)\n\nThe new cap, with T=1, has from (18)\n\n    2f_cap(x_n)>=A^2 epsilon^2/12=M^(-1/4)/12.             (26)\n\nIts ratio to the complete ray functional is at least sqrt(M)/12 and\ntends to infinity, with a genuinely unit final step and exact distance1.\nThis comparison is deliberately scoped: n=2M+1, so (25) itself does\nnot establish that F_ray is below the project benchmark. No such claim\nis used to market this family as a new solution of the all-schedule gap.\n\n## Remaining interface\n\nThe invariant (2) identifies a structural limitation of repeatedly using\none maximal interpolant. The new fourth plane explicitly creates the\nproximal value needed to survive an arbitrary tail, and (18) gives its\nquantitative schedule cost without unspecified feasible parameters.\nThe missing target-level step is still to compare a sufficiently rich\nlegal construction uniformly against the complete sequence B_n.\n\nMatlas searches supplied no external theorem used here. The previously\ndownloaded exact text of Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur, arXiv:1502.05666v6,\nRemark2 and its preceding interpolation proof, was read for context;\nall identities used above are proved from the accepted project fact and\nthe displayed elementary constructions, not from an external black box.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "1fca3e5dd555cb4a", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "finding_ids": ["b96d3f3a109d79d2", "b3b46bcdbeb86b2c", "b80fa725238cc84a"]}, "glossary": {"C_n^tail": "Maximum of A_ij² min(epsilon_ij²,epsilon_ij)/[4(1+2T_j)] over all eligible i<j, or zero.", "S_ij": "Total prescribed mass strictly between selected checkpoint indices i and j.", "A_ij": "Normalized first overshoot (h_i-1)/(1+P_i).", "epsilon_ij": "Second overshoot (h_j-1)/(1+S_ij).", "M_0": "One plus the prescribed ordinary mass before the first selected checkpoint.", "M_1": "One plus the prescribed ordinary mass between selected checkpoints.", "kappa": "Angle parameter in (0,A) satisfying kappa(1+epsilon)<=A.", "w": "Orthogonal projection of d=-M_1 q to the perpendicular of e=(a,0)-y.", "W_sq": "Actual squared norm of w, A kappa-a² kappa²/D.", "lambda_cap": "Explicit final gradient scale epsilon/(2T+1).", "r_cap": "Actual gradient lambda_cap w on every final-list sample.", "J_cap": "Fixed nonsmooth maximum of 0, p.z, q.z and r_cap.(z-y).", "f_cap": "Unit Moreau envelope of J_cap.", "delta_end": "Exact terminal proximal value T epsilon² W_sq/(2T+1)²."}}
{"id": "5b4359baa88bf3e4", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T15:33:15.117022+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Strict local obstruction at the three-step silver tail, even with unbounded signed terminal credit: some neighborhood of h=(sqrt2,2,sqrt2) contains no distinct schedule of equal or larger capacity whose E_theta is universally nonnegative, for ANY theta. Four explicit legal core-ended witnesses have every Q_(i,terminal)=0; their exact first-order cone rules out every nonzero direction of nonnegative capacity change. The fourth witness is a fully checked two-dimensional mixture. This is a local three-step statement, not global or asymptotic optimality.", "evidence": "# Three-step silver is locally maximal even with arbitrary terminal credit\n\nStatus: complete self-contained candidate, NOT verifier-accepted. No numerical\nPEP evaluation or fact_submit. The only accepted predecessor is\n1252b8823bb5a290, used for finite smooth-convex interpolation of one explicitly\nchecked two-dimensional data array. This is a finite local obstruction, not\nthe unrestricted asymptotic minimax theorem.\n\n## 1. Statement and definitions\n\nFor a positive three-step schedule h=(u,v,w), put s=u+v+w and T=1+s.\nOn any actual convex differentiable 1-smooth GD trajectory define\n\n  Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n  V_h=2sum_(i<3)h_i(f_i-f_3)+sum_(i<3)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                               -sT||g_3||^2,\n  E_theta(h)=V_h-theta sum_(i<3)h_i Q_(i,3).\n\nLet t=sqrt(2), lambda=1+t, and h_S=(t,2,t), whose capacity is\nT_S=3+2t=lambda^2.\n\nThere exists epsilon_0>0 such that, for every positive h satisfying\n\n  0<||h-h_S||<epsilon_0,  T>=T_S,\n\none of four explicit legal witnesses below has V_h<0 and Q_(i,3)=0 for\nall i<3. In particular E_theta(h)<0 on that same witness for EVERY real\ntheta. This excludes not only the bounded terminal-credit state but every\nPSD intrinsic certificate with arbitrary signed incoming-terminal\nmultipliers and nonnegative multipliers on the other actual inequalities.\n\nFor each theta>=0 it follows that h_S is a strict local capacity\nmaximizer in the E_theta class, and also in the class of certificates\nallowing arbitrary signed incoming-terminal multipliers. No claim of\nglobal optimality among all three-step schedules or of the project's\nall-horizon result is made.\n\n## 2. Core-ended equality data and its energy\n\nSuppose a finite legal GD transcript has optimum (0,0,0), and\n\n  f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2 for all i,  x_3=g_3.\n\nPut y_i=x_i-g_i. Direct expansion gives\n\n  Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>,  Q_(i,3)=0,\n  V_h=||x_0||^2-T^2||g_3||^2.                          (1)\n\nThe energy identity follows by telescoping squared distances and\nsubstituting the displayed value formula. All four witnesses below have\nthese properties, so all their incoming-terminal interpolation slacks\nvanish exactly.\n\nFor convenience put\n\n  a=u-1, A=u+1, r=v-1, D=w+1, eta=r/D.\n\nRestrict to a sufficiently small fixed open neighborhood of h_S in which\nu,v,w>1, eta a<1, and 0<r(w-1)<1.
